# TODO

See `docs/FEATURE_ROADMAP.md` for bigger tasks and `BUGS.md` for known compiler defects.

- **[ci][verify] First macOS nightly must confirm the BigInt-literal ambiguity fix compiles.** The macOS `const BigInt` overload-ambiguity fix now lives in ONE chokepoint: `int_literals.render_int_literal_value` pins a target-less `>int32` literal to an exact BigInt-ctor width (`static_cast<int64_t>` for `<=int64`, `static_cast<uint64_t>` up to uint64, `from_str` beyond), so a bare `long`/`ull` token never reaches an ambiguous `long -> BigInt` / `unsigned long long -> BigInt` conversion (int64_t == long only on Linux; uint64_t == unsigned long long only on macOS). Both codegen paths share the function (THIR pre-renders literals through it). Verified only by a C++ simulation of the macOS/Linux ctor layouts plus Linux-green -- no real macOS build here (osxcross not set up). So a red first mac-nightly on any BigInt-vs-literal site (`datetime.py`, `sys_maxsize`, the `int/bigint_compare_large_literal` / `int/bigint_list_large_literal` / `float/as_integer_ratio` / `builtins/sum_basic` / `stdlib/datetime_datetime` cases) is this fix's regression, not new breakage; conversely a green mac-nightly closes the verify loop. Rides the phase-2 mac row. Surfaced by /tpy-ready + /tpy-review of `fix-bigint-list-literal`.

- **[perf][cleanup] `render_int_literal_value`'s BigInt-TARGET arm has no `[2^63, 2^64-1]` case -- a threaded huge literal in that range renders `BigInt::from_str("...")` (runtime string parse) where the target-less arm now uses `static_cast<uint64_t>(...)` (compile-time, exact `BigInt(uint64_t)` ctor).** Add the symmetric `uint64` fast-path to the BigInt-target arm (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/int_literals.py`) so a value like `n: int = 18446744073709551615` avoids the parse. Rare range, correct either way -- pure perf/symmetry, not a bug. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the list-literal consolidation.

- **[ci] Nightly CI phase 2 -- macOS cross-compile row (osxcross, build-only).** IMPLEMENTED on branch `nightly-macos-row` (unmerged): the `macos-arm64` row cross-compiles the whole case set with osxcross and passes nothing but `--cxx`. The harness detects the darwin target itself (`<cxx> -dumpmachine`) and auto-degrades exec to build-only (a cross binary can't run on the host), so manual and CI runs behave identically with no flags; the `--build-only` harness mode also lands as the explicit just-check-it-links form. The row self-disables (status "unavailable", never red) until its `requires` paths exist (both osxcross wrappers), so a half-set-up box doesn't go red. Toolchain fixes on the same branch: darwin deployment-target pin (`-mmacosx-version-min`), Mach-O linker selection (`--ld-path` to the `<triple>-ld` sibling), cross-prefixed C-driver derivation, and an `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` env for osxcross's own libs. NOTE: the originally-planned `no_macos.txt` / `--macos-classify` per-case marker mechanism was built and then REMOVED -- real cross- and native-mac runs turned up only fixable bugs, zero genuinely-unportable cases, so the opt-out had no user; a future unportable case gets a targeted skip added then (the `no_cpython.txt`/`no_thir.txt` pattern is the template). REMAINING before/after merge: the `float/as_integer_ratio` container-element `>int32`-literal BigInt ambiguity is now FIXED (branch `fix-bigint-list-literal`, consolidated into `int_literals.render_int_literal_value`); the `async/reactor_connect_refused` runtime-parity divergence remains (a runtime bug the build-only cross nightly cannot catch, only a real-mac run does), then the row greens; user merges the branch. Both phase-1 smoke-run deferred fixes were already resolved: the `--mbedtls=system` glue-link/version issues (sysdeps row runs `pcre2=system,mbedtls=system` green) and the CPython-3.14 divergences (the ubuntu:26.04 rows' 7 cpy-parity cases made version-robust; a residual loop-less `asyncio.Future()` divergence remains a LOW note in `BUGS.md`, no longer a nightly blocker).
- **[cleanup] Replace the `lib/cpy/typing.py` full-module shadow with a targeted `typing.overload` monkeypatch.** The cpy stub shadows the ENTIRE stdlib `typing` (a `sys.path` dance to import the real module as `_real_typing`, an eager `dir()`-copy of every name, and a module `__getattr__` delegating lazily-served aliases like `Match`/`Pattern`) solely to supply a runtime-dispatching bodied `@overload` -- stdlib `typing.overload` discards bodies and raises `NotImplementedError` when called, and 79 cpy cases rely on the working dispatcher. Impersonating a whole stdlib module + the path dance is fragile: the eager copy already missed lazily-served names (broke `tpy_version` under 3.14, patched with the `__getattr__`). Cleaner: drop the shadow and patch ONLY `typing.overload` in the cpy subprocess via a `lib/cpy/sitecustomize.py` (auto-imported at startup; `lib/cpy` is already on the cpy-phase `PYTHONPATH`), leaving real `typing` otherwise untouched -- ~30 lines replacing ~166, removing the impersonation, the `sys.path` dance, and the `__getattr__` gap. Validate against all 79 `@overload` cpy cases + full suite (a mechanism swap they all ride on). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the py314 typing fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[ergonomics] Preflight-reject clang < 19 with libstdc++ -- today it auto-selects and fails with a wall of template errors.** clang 18 cannot build the TPy runtime against libstdc++ (`__cpp_concepts` 201907 keeps `<expected>` preprocessed away; found by the nightly CI, floor documented in README/CLAUDE.md), but `tpyc/toolchain.py` happily resolves it: `--cxx clang` picks the best-versioned clang++ with no version floor, and a clang-18-only box then dies mid-build with "no template named 'expected' in namespace 'std'" instead of an explanation. Fix shape, two parts: (1) after resolving a clang, check the version (or probe `__cpp_concepts` via `-dM -E`) and fail fast with a clear diagnostic naming the constraint and the alternatives (clang >= 19, gcc, zig); (2) teach `_find_best_versioned("clang++")` to skip sub-19 candidates when a viable one exists, so mixed-install boxes self-heal. gcc and zig (bundled libc++) are unaffected; a clang paired with libc++ would work but tpyc has no `-stdlib` plumbing, so version-gating is honest today (`-D__cpp_concepts=202002L` is the known force-enable workaround, compile-verified). Own `/tpy-add-feature` pass (touches toolchain resolution used by the warm build-cache path -- keep it import-light per `toolchain.py`'s header). Surfaced by the nightly CI clang-18 row failure.
- **[feature] Resume the blocking cross-thread MPSC channel (`tplib.channel`).** Design + working draft exist (see `docs/CHANNEL_DESIGN.md` "Blocking cross-thread MPSC channel"); the `Condvar` prerequisite already shipped (`tpy.sync`, `condvar_ping_pong`). Both compiler prerequisites are now fixed (the `with`-in-`__del__` rethrow gap -> destructors fail-fast on an escaping exception; and the `for v in rx:` const-receiver generator-`__iter__` gap -> readonly inference now accounts for a mutating `__iter__`), so the channel is UNBLOCKED. Next: re-add `lib/tpy/tplib/channel.py` (Arc[Mutex[_Buf]] + two Condvars, cloneable Senders, recv() + iterator, explicit-close), the `channel_mpsc` fan-in test (`no_cpython`), and update `docs/LANGUAGE_FEATURES.md` + `docs/SEND_SYNC_DESIGN.md`. Follow-ups deferred with it: MPMC (multi-consumer), `select`, unbuffered/rendezvous, closure-based producers.
- **[feature] Extend `unsafe_interior_mutable` to an inline (non-`Ptr`) field -- a C++ `mutable` member.** Today the hatch is `Ptr[T]`-only: `tpyc/sema/registration.py` rejects a non-pointer payload ("only supported on a Ptr[T] field"), so an interior-mutable primitive must live behind a raw pointer to a heap block (`unsafe_take`/`unsafe_release`). Allow `unsafe_interior_mutable[T]` (T a concrete, non-`readonly`/`Own`/nested type) to lower to an inline `mutable T` C++ member: registration accepts the non-`Ptr` inner and sets `is_interior_mutable`; codegen emits `mutable T name;` and suppresses const-projection on the field's access (the inline case of the flag the `Ptr` pointee already uses). Sound -- inline `mutable` is the same escape as the `Ptr` form (mutate through a shared readonly handle), just inline storage; the `Ptr`-only reject is conservatism/YAGNI, not a soundness gate, and the rename commit's own doc names "C++ `mutable` member" as an intended hatch form. Payoff: a `@nocopy` interior-mutable primitive with NO external pointer into it (nothing needs a stable/shared address, unlike `Mutex`'s guard cell or `Rc`/`Arc`'s shared refcount cell) becomes allocation-free. First consumer: `tpy.sync.Condvar` -- its `_cv` currently heap-allocates (`unsafe_interior_mutable[Ptr[_RawCondvar]]`) only because the hatch forces a `Ptr`; inline `unsafe_interior_mutable[_RawCondvar]` drops that alloc and keeps `_RawCondvar` an honest non-const wrapper (vs. the alternative of faking const ops in the C++ wrapper). Design decision on the escape hatch -> own `/tpy-add-feature` pass; do NOT bundle into the channel work. Surfaced discussing the Condvar allocation.
- **[cleanup] Consolidate the two receiver-mutation-recording sites.** `MethodAnalyzer` (`tpyc/sema/methods.py` ~849-929) + `CallAnalyzer._record_mutation_call_edges` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) record "calling this method mutates the receiver" for EXPLICIT calls; `_record_iter_receiver_mutation` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) mirrors a copy of the same logic for a for-loop's implicit `__iter__` (interior-field exemption, `mark_loop_var_mutated`, self-deferred-edge vs `mark_param_mutated`, parent-inclusive lookup). Two sites deciding the same question is a drift risk (the iter mirror already once shipped missing the parent lookup + `mark_loop_var_mutated`, caught by /tpy-review). Extract the shared receiver-mutation core into one helper both paths call -- the wrinkle is the explicit path records the Phase-2 edge separately in `calls.py` while the implicit path records it inline, so unifying also has to reconcile the edge-recording split. Own refactor pass; do NOT bundle into other in-flight work. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit + safety-model) of the const-receiver `__iter__` fix.
- **[test-gap] Two `__del__` fail-fast paths lack a witness.** (1) A throwing CALL in a wrap-free `__del__` (no `with`/`try`/`raise`, so `_body_has_literal_throw` leaves it unwrapped) aborts at the C++ `noexcept` boundary via the generic `tpy_terminate_handler` (`TurboPython panic: uncaught <Type>: ...`), a DIFFERENT message/mechanism than the wrapped path's `report_del_exception` (`... exception in __del__: ...`) -- the code comment asserts this but no case exercises it. (2) A `with`/`try` `__del__` that ACTUALLY throws at runtime (vs the current clean-run smoke cases) would confirm `__exit__`/`finally` still runs before the wrap aborts. Both are low-priority (path (1) is C++-guaranteed noexcept behavior, not TPy codegen); add cases only if this area churns again. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the `__del__` fail-fast change.
- **[thir][test-gap] Newly-reachable param-read fallbacks and guard-call temp boundary lack unit witnesses.** After the callable/param preflight removal (`_f1_param_eligible` / `_ctor_param_eligible` deleted), `_unrouted_binding_read`'s `name.own_optional_read` / `name.optional_ptr_read` reject branches became reachable from ordinary function params for the first time, but no unit routes a body READ of such a param (own-optional / non-F1 pointer-repr-optional) to prove the fallback fires rather than mis-lowering -- mirror the existing `test_own_str_body_read_stays_ast`. Same gap for a plain-function (not constructor) used-but-unsupported param (e.g. a `Callable`/`Waker`-typed param actually called). And with match guards now admitting calls, add a unit pinning the guard-call-needing-arg-temp boundary: the temp forms raise `ThirUnsupported` (not `assert`) since a guard is never a flush point, so the body must fall back cleanly. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage, architecture-fit).
- **[thir][test-gap] Str tuple-unpack targets inside comprehension generators lack unit coverage.** The `_scalar_or_str_unpack_elem` widening also newly admits str-typed unpack targets in comprehension generators (`{k: v for k, v in pairs}` where `k: str`), exercised only incidentally by marked (non-ratcheted) `tests/cases` (`dict_comp_unpack` / `set_comp_unpack`) with zero coverage in `tpyc/thir/test_thir_comprehensions.py`. Add a unit that routes a str-target comprehension generator and pins its emit. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the tuple-container branch.
- **[thir] Tuple-unpack non-str target rungs (scalar + str + standalone Own/const-ref landed).** Standalone and for-each tuple-unpack route value-scalar and str (view-form) targets (`_scalar_or_str_unpack_elem` / `_unpack_target_decl`). The STANDALONE arm additionally routes `Own[F1-record]` move-out targets off call/method rvalue sources (`std::move(std::get<i>)`, `_standalone_unpack_target_binds` / `THIRTupleUnpack.binds`), sema's `is_const_ref` expensive-copy targets (`const T& a = std::get<i>(...)`), nested value-tuple targets, and the `Final[tuple[...]]` global / class-constant / module-qualified-call source shapes. Deferred, each its own rung in `_gen_tuple_unpack`: (a) **record / borrow (`is_ref`) targets** -- the biggest residue (27 for-each bodies at `tuple.ref_target`); needs the `&std::get<i>(tmp)` / `auto&& = ::tpy::unwrap_ref(tuple_elem_ref(...))` alias arm + the pointer-repr source detection (`src_elem_types[i]` -> `T*`); (b) **for-each `Own` / const-ref targets** (the for-each head keeps the narrow all-value gate) and **Own targets off NAME sources** (the one-shot / last-use-move / copy bind arms); (c) **reused / predeclared targets** (`not all(is_new)`) -- the was-declared assign path; (d) **pointer-repr Optional / pointer-variant union elements** -- the `optional_to_ptr` / `to_ptr_variant` element arms; (e) **subscript sources** (`_, np = addrs[0]` -- the `auto __tup_N = ::tpy::__getitem__(...)` capture needs the container-subscript tuple-element read admitted).
- **[thir] Slot-hoist pointer-repr locals -- v1 landed (OPT_PTR_SLOT + union
  rvalue/addr kinds); deferred rungs.** The v1 slice routes Optional-ptr locals
  (None / exact-type F1 rvalue inits, None + rvalue reseats through the
  pre-declared rebind slot) and ptr-variant union locals (concrete-member or
  whole-union rvalue -> value-variant `__slot_N` + `to_ptr_variant`;
  concrete-member lvalue address `v{&(name)}`; rvalue reseat via `.emplace`).
  Deferred, each a named reject: (a) **hoisted / branch-first decls**
  (`pending_hoist_decls` preamble + the branch snapshot slot state); (b)
  **const indirection** (`decl.opt_slot_const` / `decl.union_addr_const` --
  readonly-rooted sources bind `const T*` / const-pointee variants); (c)
  **`Own[Opt[T]]` call/name lifts** (`decl.opt_slot_source` -- the
  `optional_to_ptr({slot} = ...)` is_opt_field forms); (d) **polymorphic
  subclass slots** (slot retyped to the rvalue's class; rebind case is a
  sema-error tier); (e) **lvalue-init reassigned optionals + lvalue reseats**
  (`decl.opt_reseat_source` -- pointer copy / storage-lift / address-of
  reseat arms); (f) **union reseats without a pre-declared slot** (the AST's
  inline-slot fallback) and **address reseats** (`decl.union_reseat_source`);
  (g) **non-record-member ptr-variant unions** (scalar/container members --
  `_eligible_ptr_union` keeps them out). The union decl/reseat emit arms are
  corpus-zero-witness until the call track lands union-returning calls and
  print-of-call args (their host bodies co-block there); unit-pinned in
  `test_thir_ptr_locals.py` meanwhile.

- **[ergonomics] Allow a `Final[enum]` module constant as a default parameter (and field) value.** The enum-MEMBER default half is DONE (`def f(order: MemoryOrder = MemoryOrder.SEQ_CST)` works; the `tpy.atomic` sentinel was retired). The remaining half is a `Final[EnumType]` module constant used as a default: still rejected because `tpyc/typesys.py:2631` restricts `Final` to "only primitive types and tuple". Relax that restriction to admit `Final[enum]` (an enum member is a compile-time constant with a scoped-enumerator C++ form), then the existing `Final`-name default path (`codegen_cpp/functions.py` `default_to_cpp` `TpyName` arm) already emits it. Separable and lower-value than the member half was. Surfaced writing `tpy.atomic`.
- **[feature] Mutable (non-readonly) property getter -- unblocks a faithful lazy-cached `.content`.** Every `@property` getter is unconditionally marked `auto_readonly` (`tpyc/parse/parser.py:1792`), so `sema/method_expansion.py` clones it into a const + mutable overload pair and the body must type-check as BOTH -- a getter that mutates/caches into `self` (the requests `Response.content` lazy-drain idiom) fails the const clone ("Cannot call non-readonly method on readonly reference"). No current spelling produces a mutable-only property. Add one: a property getter that mutates self skips the const clone (or an explicit opt-out marker). Semantic cost to weigh in design: a mutable-only property makes `readonly[T].prop` uncallable (the getter demands a mutable receiver) -- the readonly system forbids read-mutation on purpose, so this is a deliberate narrowing, not a free win. Touches parser + `sema/method_expansion` + registration (type-system-adjacent) -> architectural, own `/tpy-add-feature` design. Payoff: `tplib.requests` `Response.content`/`.text`/`.json()` could auto-drain a `stream=True` body (matching real requests) instead of the current declared divergence (a streamed `.content` stays permanently `b""` -- never auto-filled; full-body via `r.raw.read()` / `iter_content`). Generally useful for any lazy-cached property. Surfaced designing requests streaming.
- **[thir][hygiene] Pointer-indirection is a distributed fact -- fold it into a single name-resolution point.** A name read's indirection now lives in three places: the use-gated `container_ptr` flag in the generic name arm (`tpyc/thir/lower/expressions.py` ~890-900, excluded at RECEIVER/BORROW_BIND uses), a handful of scattered post-hoc `replace(lowered, deref=...)` patches at consumer sites, and `_lower_truthy` re-deriving pointer-ness independently (`e.name in lc.pointers`) then anti-stacking against the operand's own flag. Two composition bugs in one day came from exactly this (a method-receiver double-arrow during cell integration; the rebound-container truthiness double-deref at the master merge -- a real miscompile with no corpus witness, now unit-pinned by `test_rebound_container_truthiness_derefs_once`). Fix shape: one resolution point that answers "what does reading this name render as" (bare / `(*name)` / arrow-receiver), consumers consume the verdict instead of re-deriving -- adjacent to the form-as-IR-fact design, which resolved the borrow/storage analog of the same question. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective of `thir-match-dynattrs-args`.
- **[thir][hygiene] Classifier/helper param order is inconsistent across siblings -- swap-prone once `_WalkState` is gone.** Removing the `_WalkState` bundle spread its fields (`declared`, `pointers`, `rebind_slots`, `storage_tuple_locals`, `narrowed`) as separate positional params across the remaining expression/comprehension helpers and the `for`/`match` classifiers in `tpyc/thir/lower/`, but the order is not uniform between siblings (e.g. `checks.py:_record_field_write_ok(stmt, declared, analyzer, pointers, narrowed, prescan)` vs `_optional_record_field_write_ok(stmt, declared, pointers, analyzer, narrowed, prescan)` -- `analyzer`/`pointers`/`narrowed` swap positions). Since `pointers` and `narrowed` are both bare `set[str]`, nothing catches an accidental swap at a future call site. Options: (a) unify the arg order across the family, or (b) introduce a small FROZEN read-only carrier (a `NamedTuple`/frozen dataclass of the same fields, no `branch_copy`/mutation methods -- distinct from the deleted mutable `_WalkState`). The carrier-vs-flat call is a design choice; some of the ordering skew is pre-existing carry-forward. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of `thir-lower-reject-expressions`.
- **[thir][hygiene] `THIRGenExpr` stores pre-rendered C++ strings where the sibling node keeps facts structured.** `_lower_genexpr` (`tpyc/thir/lower/comprehensions.py`) pre-renders `binding_cpp` (via `loop_var_binding`) and both `iife_captures`/`inner_captures` lists (via `_genexpr_captures`, called twice) into `THIRGenExpr` at lowering time, whereas the sibling `THIRComprehension` carries the equivalent facts structured (`var`, `elem_type`) and defers the `loop_var_binding`/capture render to `_emit_comprehension` in `emit.py`. Both nodes already store SOME rendered cpp (`container_cpp`, `cpp_iterable`), so this is a lower/emit boundary-placement inconsistency, not a bug -- byte-identical today. Refactor to carry `var`/`elem_type` + a structured captured-names tuple and build the capture list once in `_emit_genexpr`. Non-trivial (moves the capture logic across the boundary). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the generator-expression cells.
- **[thir] Protocol boundary -- the rungs the param/arg cells deliberately left out.** The bare protocol param, the protocol-receiver method call, the protocol call-arg slot (Adapter / RefAdapter / concrete + `auto` rvalue temps) and `len(p)` are routed. Excluded, roughly by remaining mass: (a) **`Own[P]` params and returns** (~16 bodies, incl. 2 resumable) -- `T_p&&` / `std::unique_ptr<P>` slots whose reads move, whose method calls render `->` (`_receiver_is_own_dyn`), and whose call args take `::tpy::make_adapter<Base>` / `std::make_unique` (`_gen_dynamic_protocol_own_arg`); (b) **protocol RETURNS** (~8) -- `auto` (structural) / `Base&` (@dynamic); (c) **resumable protocol params** (21 blocked at `res.param_type`) -- need the two-phase `decltype` frame capture (`thir/lower/resumable.py`, `protocol_param_template_name`); (d) **`Optional[P]` / protocol-union params and arg slots** -- `_gen_protocol_arg`'s typed-null / `&(...)` / `optional_to_ptr` renders; (e) **protocol SUBSCRIPT** (~16 at `subscript.recv_type`) -- NOT uniform: `items[0]` emits `::tpy::__getitem__(items, 0)` while `items[i]` emits `items[static_cast<std::size_t>(i)]`, so the rung must first establish which dispatch the AST picks; (f) **protocol slots in the record-method arg loop** (`_record_method_call_supported` still rejects them as `method.arg_shape`, though `_gen_method_call`'s user-record loop does run the protocol pre-arms); (g) **for-each over a protocol iterable**, which rides the `stmt.for_each` rock (the corpus's #1 blocker, 333b/184sh). Landed on `thir-protocol-boundary`.
- **[thir][telemetry] `return`-of-record-source fallback reasons lost their sub-tags in the gate-removal inlining.** The inlined `TpyReturn` record-source reject in `tpyc/thir/lower/statements.py` (~2056) now routes both the `Own`-typed-source-at-a-borrow-slot case (old `return.record_source.own_at_borrow`) and the non-F1-record bare-name source (old `return.record_source.name_notf1`) through the generic `_record_source_reject_detail` bucket (`return.record_source.name.{slot}`). Correctness is unaffected (accept/reject boolean unchanged; byte-diff green), but `$THIR_FALLBACK_JSON` triage can no longer distinguish these two return-source fallback causes. Restore the two `note_detail` calls at the collapsed branch if the distinction is still wanted for porting triage. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness + architecture-fit) of `thir-read-only-admission`.
- **[thir][test-gap] Two protocol arg arms are unit-covered but never routed-and-exec-diffed by the corpus.** (1) The @dynamic structural-conformer **lvalue** RefAdapter arg (`::tpy::RefAdapter<Base,C> __tmp{p}`, `_protocol_arg_temp` `rvalue=False` / `is_dyn_protocol` branch): the case written for it, `tests/cases/protocols/protocol_dynamic_structural`, still carries `no_thir.txt` because its `main()` falls back wholesale on an unrelated var-decl (`pet: Pet = Parrot()`), so the `greet(p)` RefAdapter site is never emitted from THIR. (2) The ctor-protocol-param path (the `_ctor_param_eligible` doc update admitting a protocol param on `__init__`): `protocol_method_dynamic_param` falls back for pre-existing reasons (`ctor.mil_field.nominal.method`, `field.result_type`). Both arms are hand-verified correct and pinned by `test_thir_protocols.py` unit tests, so this is a corpus end-to-end blind spot, not a live bug -- flip these cases (or add a minimal exec case) once the unrelated blockers clear. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity + test-coverage) of `thir-protocol-boundary`.
- **[thir][test-gap] Resumable leaf-shape rejects moved into `_lower_stmt_dispatch` are exercised only incidentally.** The predictive `_leaf_shape_reject`/`_gate_leaf` scan in `tpyc/thir/lower/resumable.py` was deleted; its rejects (`res.leaf_return`, `res.leaf_field_write` gated on `scope.in_branch`, `res.unpack`, `res.leaf_try`, `res.leaf_with`, `res.leaf_match`, `res.nested_def`) now fire inline in `_lower_stmt_dispatch` under `lc.resumable_leaf_mode`. No dedicated case pins the moved reject (e.g. a `return`, or a name-write, nested two levels inside an `if` in an `async def` with no `await` in that branch); coverage rides on pre-existing `no_thir`-marked `tests/cases/async/*` cases being re-diffed by the overlay. Add a minimal case so the moved invariant is explicit, not incidental. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of `thir-structural-plans`.
- **[thir] Per-case migration harness -- remaining test guards.** DONE: the scoping test + the ratchet fire-side test (`tpyc/test_compiler.py` `TestThirScoping`), the `_apply_no_thir_marker` + `_thir_flag_conflict` unit tests (`tests/test_thir_harness.py`), the shared `_make_codegen` gate helper (both `generate_code` and `generate_code_to_strings` now scope THIR identically), the flag-conflict guard extracted into the testable `_thir_flag_conflict`, the overlay/ratchet decision extracted into the pure `_thir_case_mode` (+ its decision-table unit tests), and the integration guard that a marked case still gets an overlay (`test_marked_case_still_gets_an_overlay` -- the only thing that can catch a re-gating of the overlay on the marker, since a marked case byte-diffs identically either way). Remaining: (a) a `--thir-check-flip` detection test; (c) the `THIR_CLASSIFY_WRITE`/`THIR_CHECK_FLIP` call-site wiring in `compile_with_diagnostics` (the `fell > 0` -> `dirty=` and `no_thir and fell == 0` flip-candidate conditions) has no direct test; (d) `--no-thir` end-to-end (that it leaves `thir_codegen` false, skips overlay+ratchet, and renders its report-header line); (e) the comp-phase RATCHET FAILURE itself (`pytest.fail` in `tests/test_case.py`) has no committed test -- `TestThirScoping` only asserts the fallback counter on `Compiler`, bypassing the harness wiring, so an unmarked case with a fallback body failing comp is verified only by hand; (f) the fallback-breakdown footer lines are gated on `THIR_IGNORE_MARKERS` with nothing asserting they stay off by default and print under `--thir-codegen`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) x3 + the merge-review.
- **[thir] Surface stale `no_thir.txt` markers on a default run -- the measurement is already free.** The overlay now runs for every case, so `fell` (the per-case user-body fallback count) is computed for MARKED cases too, and `record_thir_case_marked()` throws it away. A marked case with `fell == 0` is an un-mark candidate -- exactly what `--thir-check-flip` computes on demand. Print it instead: `tpy| thir: N stale markers (run --thir-check-flip)`. That would have caught the 62-case drift automatically instead of waiting for someone to run the query. **Surface, never assert:** marked-but-clean is benign porting progress, so failing on it would turn every THIR-lowering widening into a red suite until the markers are re-synced; the ratchet already enforces the correctness-critical direction (unmarked => zero fallback). Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion on `thir-marker-resync`.
- **[thir] `THIR_IGNORE_MARKERS` does two unrelated jobs; `--thir-codegen` is misnamed.** The flag both (a) suppresses the ratchet corpus-wide and (b) enables the faces/shapes/fallback coverage metrics. They are independent axes -- a coverage sweep does not require dropping the ratchet, and a no-ratchet run does not require the metrics. The name is now wrong too: the default run already byte-diffs THIR over the whole corpus, so "thir-codegen" no longer names what the flag uniquely does. Candidate split: `--thir-metrics` (print faces/shapes/fallback) and `--thir-no-ratchet` (ignore markers), with `--thir-classify` / `--thir-check-flip` implying the latter. Cosmetic-but-load-bearing: `_thir_case_mode` already takes `classify`/`check_flip` separately from `ignore_markers` so the split has a landing spot. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) + the /tpy-ready second-opinion on `thir-marker-resync`.
- **[thir] Assert emit idempotence directly -- the harness depends on it but nothing states it.** The THIR overlay re-emits each local user module a second time, so "a module emits identically every time" is now load-bearing for the whole corpus -- yet it is an emergent property of the overlay, not a tested invariant. `recursive_alias_cpp_names` violated it (a definer rendered its own generic recursive alias qualified on a second pass) and the overlay caught it only incidentally; the bug was not a THIR divergence at all and reproduced with THIR fully off. `tpyc/test_compiler.py::TestCodegenStateIsolation` pins the one map. Generalize: double-emit every module on the AST path and byte-compare, so the next per-module codegen map that forgets its reset fails directly instead of masquerading as a THIR bug. Needs no THIR, and covers cases THIR never touches. Surfaced by `fix-recursive-alias-name-leak`.
- **[thir] Broad marker re-sync -- the dial under-counts after master merges widen routing.** Each master merge that widens THIR routing makes some already-`no_thir`-marked cases clean, but the merge resolution marks only the newly-FAILING cases (targeted, to keep the merge focused) and never un-marks the now-clean ones. So markers drift in the over-marked direction and the `N/M migrated` dial under-counts real progress. Fix: run `uv run pytest --thir-classify --no-exec` to re-sync the whole corpus (add markers for new fallbacks, remove for now-clean), review the marker churn, commit -- as a periodic maintenance pass, not bundled into a merge. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective after the second master merge (13 cases marked; an unknown number of now-clean cases left over-marked).
- **[thir] Top-level module code is outside the THIR candidate set -- invisible to the ratchet + inflates the migrated dial.** A case whose user module is only top-level statements (no def/class) routes 0 THIR bodies AND records 0 fallback (verified: `x=1; y=x+2; print(y)` -> routed 0, fallback `{}`), so it counts as "migrated"/clean by vacuity though 0% of its code goes through THIR. This is why the summary reads `504 migrated` but only `388 cases with routed bodies` -- the ~116 gap is top-level-only (and top-level-heavy) cases. Unlike every OTHER un-migrated construct (async, Optional param, ...), which IS a candidate and counts as fallback, top-level is not a candidate at all -- so it is the one thing the ratchet can't see: a case can add top-level code without ever needing top-level migrated, and a top-level codegen regression is invisible to the ratchet (the AST oracle + exec still catch correctness). Fix: make top-level a THIR candidate in the seeding loop (`tpyc/thir/`) so an un-routed top-level counts as fallback -> a top-level case becomes not-clean -> needs a marker (honest dial + ratchet coverage); this reclassifies the ~116 (markers arrive). Ultimately top-level codegen (globals via pointer slots, a distinct emit path -- see CLAUDE.md) must itself migrate to THIR, since AST codegen can't be deleted while top-level still needs it. Surfaced by the user reviewing the 388-vs-504 dial discrepancy.

- **[sema hygiene] Enum-member param-default validation isn't wired into every `ParamInfo`-building site.** `_validate_param_default_enums` (`tpyc/sema/registration.py`) runs at `register_function` and the record-method registration, but not at `register_overload_group` or `methods.py::_build_protocol_method_info`, so a wrong-enum default on an `@overload` stub (`def f(x: int, c: Color = Mode.A) -> int: ...`) passes registration with no diagnostic. Not a miscompile -- a stub's default is dropped by codegen (the impl's default is emitted, and the impl IS validated) -- just an inconsistent diagnostic surface for the same shape the enum-default feature targets. Route the remaining `ParamInfo`-construction sites through the same check when next touching that area. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the enum-member default-param feature.
- **[generics] Associated-type inference: multi-param protocol via an *extends* declaration resolves only the first param.** The direct-conformer and protocol-typed-param multi-param paths are DONE (`def take[K, V, T: Pair[K, V]](x: T)` solves K and V positionally; the structural helpers now return the full positional resolution). The residual gap is the *extends* route: `builtin_modules.get_extends_protocol_type_arg` is single-valued, so a conformer that satisfies a multi-param protocol through protocol *inheritance* (not its own methods) binds only the protocol's first param (the caller falls back to a length-1 `elem_types`, leaving later params unresolved). Unexercised in-tree (no corpus case reaches a multi-param protocol via extends). Fix shape: give the extends lookup a positional multi-param variant aligned to the parent protocol's type_params. Surfaced building the multi-param bound-inference feature.
- **[generics][test-gap] The `_infer_protocol_type_arg_from_protocol` (protocol-to-protocol) multi-param path has no end-to-end test.** Its return was generalized to a positional list alongside the record-structural path, but the only way to reach it -- forwarding a protocol-typed param into a generic bound -- hits the pre-existing codegen bug in BUGS.md (undeclared `T`), so it can't be a passing case. The record-structural multi-param path IS covered (`assoc_type_multiparam_bound`). Add coverage once that codegen bug is fixed. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of multi-param inference.
- **[sema hygiene] `_infer_protocol_type_arg_structurally` resolves the protocol by BARE short name.** `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py` uses `registry.scan_by_short_name(protocol_name)` where `protocol_name` comes from the param/bound NominalType's `.name` -- the same bare-name-as-identity class the enum/record match fixes retired (qname discipline). Two same-named protocols in different modules could resolve to the wrong one; downstream the damage is bounded (the solved type still passes through the real bound's conformance check, so worst case is rejection-of-valid, not a miscompile -- probed with a two-module `Container` collision). Route the lookup through the bound's qname / `protocol_info_of(bound)` instead of the short-name scan when next touching this area.

## Next
- **[sema hygiene] Consolidate the ctor-overload-group resolution duplicated between the raise and expression paths.** The oserror-exact branch grew two hand-synced copies of "resolve __init__ overload group (own or inherited via `get_method_overloads_with_parents`) + coerce args + own/readonly checks": `CallAnalyzer._resolve_ctor_overload_group` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) and the inline block in `StatementAnalyzer._analyze_raise` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`), with drift in ambiguity reporting (calls.py routes `_ambiguous_overload_error`'s candidate-listing message; statements.py re-raises the bare `OverloadAmbiguityError.__str__`). Extract the shared logic into `sema/overloads.py` parameterized on args + error context. While there: (a) fold `_attach_ctor_overload_info` into `_set_record_constructor_info` via an optional pre-resolved `winner` param (near-verbatim duplicate today); (The former item (b) -- the hardcoded `"builtins.OSError"` warning sites -- was retired wholesale: the warning was replaced by the `@virtual_raise` raise-time mapping, leaving no OSError-specific compiler code.) Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the oserror-exact branch.
- **[accepted divergence] Sibling submodule `__tpy_init` order is sorted, not CPython source order.** `from pkg import a, b` (and `from . import a, b`) store the imported names in a `set`, so source order is lost at parse time. Both submodule-injection paths (`_discover_absolute_submodule_imports`, `_resolve_relative_import`) now iterate `sorted(names)` for a *deterministic* (reproducible) build, but that is alphabetical, not left-to-right source order. Observable only if two sibling submodules have order-dependent top-level side effects (one's module body reads state the other's body sets) -- otherwise unobservable, and any real dependency is ordered by the discovery chain regardless. To match CPython faithfully, preserve name order through the parser (ordered container instead of a `set`) so the injected `TpyImport`s follow source order. Surfaced by /tpy-ready second-opinion of the submodule-init fix.
- **[sema hygiene] Verify or drop the `@property` sub-branch in `is_view_compatible_source`.** The property-access branch added to `is_view_compatible_source` (`tpyc/sema/local_deduction.py`) routes a property through `is_dangling_return(property_getter_call)`; its owned-str `-> False` arm is exercised (the @property owned-str-return fix), but the StrView `-> True` (view-safe) arm may be unreachable in practice because `_view_source_is_temporary` treats any `TpyFieldAccess` as non-temporary, so a StrView property source never reaches the pending-view machinery. Confirm the view-safe arm is reachable (some caller path -- walrus/annotated -- may hit it) or drop it as dead. Correct-if-reached, harmless if not. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model, low confidence) of the @property owned-str fix.
- **[CPython interop] Continue past rungs 0-2 (harness gate now landed).** The ext-exec gate is done -- a first-class `tpyc` `.so` build mode (`tpyc -b` on an `# tpy: ext_module` -> `-fPIC -shared`, no `main`, `<mod>.so`) plus `tests/test_interop_exec.py` (snapshot glue/module C++ always; cached `.so` build + import + cpy-parity + ext_checks; facade self-check), retiring `verify.sh`. Every scalar marshaller is done -- `float` (C++ `double`), `bool` (truthiness via `PyObject_IsTrue`/`PyBool_FromLong`), all fixed-width ints (`Int8`..`Int64` / `UInt8`..`UInt64`, per-width range checks) -- plus void return (`-> None` -> `Py_RETURN_NONE`) and `str`/`bytes` (copy-in: PyUnicode <-> `std::string`, PyBytes <-> `std::vector<uint8_t>`; the boundary marshals the owned form and the function's borrow-form param converts at the call, so the glue emitter is unchanged). Containers are now done too (list/dict/set/tuple as @export fn params/returns, O(n) recursive copy-in/out -- see the dedicated entry below). Numeric `Span[T]` buffer input (phase 3) is now done too as an @export function PARAM (copy-in via the buffer protocol; see the dedicated entry below). Exposed-class dunders (Q4) are now DONE too (see the dedicated exposed-classes entry below). Containers/Span at the exposed-class METHOD boundary are now DONE too (method/`__init__` params/returns admit the same boundary set as free @export functions; getset fields stay scalar-only). @property on exposed classes is now DONE too (computed getset; see the exposed-classes entry below). Inheritance between exposed classes is now DONE too (single exposed same-module base -> real `tp_base` via `PyType_FromSpecWithBases`; base-before-derived creation, own-members-only tables + CPython slot/MRO inheritance, richcompare per-op delegation to the ancestor dispatcher, ctor inheritance, BASETYPE + tp_init exact-type guard rejecting Python-side subclass instantiation). Next candidates: the remaining exposed-classes deferred matrix (public class-typed fields). Full phasing + locked design in `docs/CPYTHON_INTEROP.md`.
- **[CPython interop] exposed-class inheritance -- deferred slices.** (1) **Cross-module exposed base** (a base defined in another module is a located error, mirroring the cross-module enum/class-param guard; needs the general cross-module exposed-type story). (2) **Python-side subclassing of exposed bases** stays rejected at instantiation (tp_init exact-type guard) -- enabling it needs Python-override-aware dispatch from the C++ side (same root as the static-dispatch divergence; @dynamic territory). (3) **Multiple bases** are a PERMANENT reject (CPython refuses two bases with distinct C instance layouts), including one-exposed-base-plus-plain-record mixins (a second record base could displace the exposed base from offset 0). (4) **Value-type hierarchies at the boundary are untested** (sema allows a value-type record parent chain; the boundary composes per-class -- read-only getsets per value class -- but no interop case exercises it). (5) The `Instance<T>` payload static_asserts were relaxed for derived payloads (standard-layout -> !is_polymorphic + alignment cap); the non-movable-payload located-sema-pre-check TODO (filed at the internal-fields rung) now also covers polymorphic payloads reaching the static_assert as a raw g++ error when the @dynamic base is CONFORMED structurally rather than declared.

- **[CPython interop] container boundary -- deferred slices.** `list`/`dict`/`set`/`tuple` cross the `@export` fn boundary O(n) by-copy, recursively (`marshal.hpp` `list_from_py`/`list_to_py`/`dict_*`/`set_*`/`tuple_*` element-fn-parameterized helpers; `extension.py` `_marshal_in_expr`/`_marshal_out_expr` emit nested per-element lambdas bottoming out at `from_py`/`to_py`). Recursion is glue-driven, NOT C++-template-driven: the C++ storage type is ambiguous at the leaves (`list[bytes]` and `list[list[UInt8]]` both render `std::vector<std::vector<uint8_t>>`), so only the glue -- holding the unambiguous TPy element types -- can pick the leaf marshaller. `is_function_boundary_marshallable` recurses (new, split from the base `is_boundary_marshallable` which stays scalar/leaf-only; new `_container_element_types`/`_is_marshallable_element` in `type_def_registry.py`); the facade (`cpython_h.hpp`) gained the abi3 container C-API (type objects + List/Dict/Tuple accessors + iterator protocol for sets, since `PySet_Next` isn't in the limited API). Strict-by-container-kind IN (`PyType_IsSubtype` vs the exported type objects). Three acknowledged divergences documented (`docs/CPYTHON_INTEROP.md` "container cliff"): copy-in mutation (a mutated list/dict/set param **warns** via `mutated_params`; tuple exempt), strict-IN, per-element coercion (incl. dict-key collapse). Tests: `tests/interop/containers/`, `tests/cases/interop/{warn_export_container_mutation,error_export_container_exposed_element}`. **Deferred:** (1) **exposed class/enum types as TOP-LEVEL container ELEMENTS** -- DONE (branch `interop-container-element-exposed-types`): `list[Color]`, `list[Counter]`, `dict[str, Counter]`, `set[Color]`, `dict[Color, V]`, `tuple[Color, Int64]` cross -- the per-element converter (`extension.py` `_marshal_in_expr`/`_marshal_out_expr` leaf arms) marshals each element through its module type handle (`instance_payload`/`instance_to_py`/`enum_from_py`/`enum_to_py`), copy-in/out; `_is_marshallable_element` admits exposed enum/class elements; a container param with an exposed element declares its local via `auto` (the bare element render isn't visible in the glue namespace) and `_elem_cpp` qualifies it. An exposed enum crosses in any position; an exposed CLASS as a `set` element or dict KEY is rejected by the general Hashable check before the boundary. **DEFERRED sub-slices (each a located error via `_exposed_form_error`'s `_unsupported_exposed_element`, NOT a misbuild -- surfaced by /tpy-review):** (1-i) **an exposed type NESTED inside a container element** (`dict[str, list[Counter]]`) -- HIGHER PRIORITY, MODERATE: `_elem_cpp` only qualifies a DIRECT exposed leaf, so a nested exposed leaf renders a bare unqualified name that the glue namespace can't resolve; fix = a recursive qualified container-render (re-render the container C++ type with exposed leaves namespace-qualified, a parallel to `to_cpp_stored` with an exposed-leaf hook). (1-ii) **an exposed CLASS as a tuple element** (`tuple[A, Counter]`) -- LOWER, HARD: a tuple's non-value element uses BORROW form (`std::tuple<A, const Counter*>`) but the per-element marshal builds STORAGE form -- the borrow/storage duality (needs the tuple storage->pointer conversion); tuple-of-ENUM/value works. (1-iii) **`@nocopy` exposed class as a container element** -- rejected (each element is copied; @nocopy forbids the copy) -- stays rejected permanently unless a move-in-place element path is designed. (1-iv) once the general Hashable bug (BUGS.md) is fixed, add a PRINCIPLED interop guard rejecting an exposed CLASS in a set-element/dict-key position (copy-on-cross has no stable identity for a hash key) -- today the general Hashable check incidentally covers it (safety-model W3). (1-v) test gap: `dict[Counter, V]` class-KEY rejection message untested (low value). Cross-module exposed element (class + enum) = located error (`_foreign_exposed_element` recurses into element types). Tests `tests/interop/container_exposed_elements/`, `tests/cases/interop/{warn_export_container_element_mutation,error_export_set_exposed_class_element,error_export_container_cross_module_element}`. Observation (BUGS.md): an exposed class defining `__hash__`+`__eq__` is still not recognized as Hashable, so it can't be a set element even in principle. **(1b) faithful exception DATA-FIELD crossing (exc TODO 1a)** -- DONE (branch `interop-exc-data-fields`): a data-carrying user exc's fields (scalar/str/bytes/exposed-enum) cross as INSTANCE ATTRIBUTES via a per-type generated setter (`extension.py` `_emit_user_exc_setters`) threaded through the `ExcRegistry` (now `typeid -> PyObject* + ExcSetErr`); the setter constructs `pytype(e.what())`, `PyObject_SetAttrString`s each field via `_value_out_expr`, then `PyErr_SetObject`. Message-only excs use the shared `exc_set_err_message_only` default. `str()`/`args` reflect the message only (acknowledged divergence; the C++ exc holds fields, not the original arg tuple). Container/exposed-class exc fields = located error (`_validate_user_exc_data_fields`), deferred. Tests `tests/interop/user_exc_data/`, `tests/cases/interop/{error_export_exc_container_field,error_export_exc_class_field}`. (2) **`Optional[container]`** params/returns (`list[int] | None`) -- not admitted (the boundary set has no Optional support yet; orthogonal). (3) **`tuple[T, ...]`** homogeneous/variadic tuple -- only fixed-arity `tuple[A, B, ...]` admitted; `_container_element_types` returns the fixed `TupleType.element_types`. (4) **`frozenset`** -- not a boundary type (only `set`); a `frozenset` ARG to a `set[T]` param IS accepted IN (`PyAnySet_Check` covers both) but there's no `frozenset[T]` TPy type to declare. (5) **`Array[T, N]` / `Span[T]` as containers** -- `Array` is a value type (fixed C++ `std::array`, not yet a boundary type); `Span` is the separate phase-3 buffer path. (6) **containers at the exposed-CLASS boundary** -- the METHOD half is DONE (the method-boundary rung): method/`__init__` params/returns are gated by `is_function_boundary_marshallable` + `is_span_boundary_param` (same set as @export functions; the method glue already shared `_emit_marshal_in`/`_emit_call_return`, so no codegen change), the copy-in mutation warning covers methods, and a borrow-form list/dict/set return warns (copy-out) with `Own[...]` acknowledged -- tests `tests/interop/class_method_containers/`, `tests/cases/interop/{warn_export_class_method_container_mutation,warn_export_class_container_borrow_return,error_export_class_method_span_return}`. STILL DEFERRED: a CONTAINER getset field. Scalar/str/bytes fields, an exposed-ENUM field (DONE, branch `interop-class-typed-fields`), and `Final`-constant snapshots use the base `is_boundary_marshallable` (scalar/str/bytes/@export-enum leaves; a class field is rejected for the aliasing reason, not marshallability), which does not recurse into containers, so a `list[int]` field stays rejected (an exposed class cannot STORE a container) -- the getset + constant-snapshot paths have no container marshaller; real per-field container marshal emission is its own follow-up. (7) **zero-copy** -- always copies; the read-only zero-copy path is `Span`+buffer (phase 3.5), and write-back has no v1 escape hatch (a copy-back-out-on-return option was considered and declined as out-of-rung). (8) test gaps (from /tpy-review): the `set`-OUT failing-element `to_py` null-cleanup branch (set_to_py/dict_to_py/tuple_to_py) is only exercised via the list path; an empty-`set` round-trip (`set_size(set())`/`to_set([])`) is untested; the dict-key-collapse coercion (`dict[int]` from `{True: ...}` -> `int` key) is declared but has no `ext_checks.py` assertion; a `frozenset` arg to a `set[T]` param is accepted (item 4) but untested and undocumented in the user-facing "strict-by-kind IN" note -- add a test + a doc line that a set kind includes `frozenset`. (9) the `is_boundary_marshallable`/`is_function_boundary_marshallable` split has no mechanical guard: `_validate_exposed_class` now uses the recursive predicate for method params/returns but the base one for FIELDS (by role, in one `check()`), and `_constant_exposable` stays base-only -- fold into a single `allow_containers=False` predicate when the field-getset work lands, or leave the split with this note. (10) the tuple-of-container mutation case (`tuple[list[int], str]` param, mutate the inner list) is copy-in-invisible like every container but is NOT the same as the list/dict/set warning: a tuple is a value type, so the inner list is already copied on element access in pure-TPy (the param is never `mutated`), so the warning correctly can't/shouldn't fire -- pre-existing TPy value-vs-reference model, documented in the container-cliff section, not a new divergence. (11) **borrow-form `bytes` returns are un-warned** -- `bytes` is a reference type, so a `-> bytes` @export fn/method return borrowing an existing object (`RefType[bytes]`) copies out at the boundary exactly like a borrow-form list, but the copy-out alias warning (added with the method-boundary rung) deliberately covers only list/dict/set to avoid churning existing-test warnings; widening it to `bytes` is a small follow-up (same `warn_if_borrow_return` predicate in `analyzer.py`).
- **[CPython interop] Span buffer boundary -- deferred slices.** `Span[readonly[T]]`/`Span[T]` (`T` a fixed-width int or `float`) cross the `@export` FUNCTION boundary as a PARAM ONLY (never a return -- return numeric data via `list[T]`), via the buffer protocol: `span_from_py<T>` (`marshal.hpp`) calls `PyObject_GetBuffer(PyBUF_ND | PyBUF_FORMAT)` (1-D, C-contiguous), strictly validates itemsize/format against `T` (no coercion), copies into a fresh `std::vector<T>` that implicitly converts to the function's `std::span<T>`/`std::span<const T>` param (the same owned-copy-converts-to-borrow-param trick as `str`/`bytes`), and releases the view. New predicate `is_span_boundary_param` in `type_def_registry.py` (param-only, checked at the `_validate_ext_module_exports` call site and the `assert_marshal` drift-guard in `extension.py` -- NOT folded into `is_function_boundary_marshallable`, which stays direction-symmetric for every other boundary type). Facade (`cpython_abi.hpp`/`cpython_h.hpp`) gained `Py_buffer` + `PyObject_GetBuffer`/`PyBuffer_Release` + `PyBUF_ND`/`PyBUF_FORMAT`, verified against real `Python.h` in the facade self-check. **v1.0 is copy-in for BOTH `Span[T]` and `Span[readonly[T]]`** (no `PyBUF_WRITABLE`, no write-back) -- a mutated `Span[T]` (non-readonly) param **warns** (the renamed `_warn_export_copy_boundary_mutation`, same `mutated_params` mechanism and no-escape-hatch bar as the list/dict/set container-mutation warning); `Span[readonly[T]]` can never warn (writing through it is already a compile error). Tests: `tests/interop/span_numeric/` (round-trips per width + float via `array.array`/`memoryview`/`bytes`/`bytearray`; `ext_checks.py` for format/itemsize mismatch, non-buffer arg, non-contiguous buffer, copy-in-mutation-invisible), `tests/cases/interop/{warn_export_span_mutation,error_export_span_return}`. **Deferred:** (1) **zero-copy** (both read-only and writable) -- phase 3.5, IR-gated (the foreign-borrow primitive; a spike showed the escape machinery can't fence a foreign-source param at all sinks). (2) **`bool`/`int` (BigInt)/`Char` elements** -- excluded; `bool`'s buffer format (`'?'`) is inconsistent across exporters and rarely used as a numeric buffer, BigInt has no fixed-width buffer representation. (3) **`Float32` elements** -- excluded because the scalar `Float32` type itself isn't yet a boundary type at all (unrelated, orthogonal gap -- `Span[Float32]` will "just work" once that scalar rung lands, since the admission predicate reuses `is_boundary_marshallable`). (4) **multi-dimensional / non-C-contiguous buffers** -- rejected (`PyBUF_ND` without `STRIDES` requires 1-D contiguity); no `ndim > 1` support. (5) **Span at the exposed-class METHOD boundary** -- DONE (the method-boundary rung; a `Span` method/`__init__` PARAM is admitted via `is_span_boundary_param`, return stays rejected, the mutation warning covers it); a Span getset FIELD is not a thing (a view field doesn't marshal; stays base-rejected). (6) **Span as a container element** (`list[Span[float]]`) -- not admitted, not a design goal. (7) **format-code table completeness** -- `span_from_py_impl`'s per-width accepted-code lists (e.g. int32 accepts `'i'` and `'l'`, int64 accepts `'q'` and `'l'`) cover the common numpy/`array.array` cases per-platform but are not an exhaustive `struct`-module mapping; revisit if an exotic exporter's format code is rejected in practice. **Post-/tpy-review (2026-07-01):** the buffer-view exception-safety leak (a `bad_alloc` during the copy skipped `PyBuffer_Release`) and the silent non-native-byte-order acceptance (a `'>i4'`-style buffer copied verbatim instead of rejected) were both found and FIXED in review (RAII guard + a native-byte-order check in `span_format_matches`); the transitive-mutation-warning gap (a `Span[T]` mutated only via a helper call gets no warning, unlike `list`/`dict`/`set`) was filed as a `BUGS.md` defect (needs `/tpy-fix-bug`, touches shared `mutation_propagation.py` infrastructure) rather than patched inline. Remaining cleanups from that review: (8) **`span_elem_type()` dedup** -- `is_span_boundary_param` (`type_def_registry.py`) and `_span_elem_cpp` (`extension.py`) both duplicate the "strip Own/Ref, assert Span, pull `type_args[0]`, unwrap readonly" sequence; extract one shared helper. (9) **widen `span_numeric` test coverage** -- DONE in part: added `peek_int32` (a mutable, non-readonly `Span[Int32]` param that only reads), round-tripped in `driver.py` via `array.array`/`memoryview`, proving the mutable form marshals correctly and that a read-only *use* of it doesn't warn; mirrored with a `peek` case in `tests/cases/interop/warn_export_span_mutation` (declared-but-never-mutated `Span[Int32]` stays quiet, unlike `scale`). STILL OPEN: only 4 of the 9 `span_from_py<T>` specializations (`float`/`Int32`/`UInt8`, now also exercised via `peek_int32`) get a happy-path test; the dual-format-code branches for the untested widths (`int8_t`/`int16_t`/`uint16_t`/`uint32_t`/`int64_t`/`uint64_t`, e.g. `int64_t` accepting `'l'` alongside `'q'`) remain unexercised. (10) **the native-byte-order rejection (review fix, item above) has no regression test** -- and, unlike the width/format-mismatch cases already in `ext_checks.py`, it can't be added cheaply: `array.array`/`memoryview.cast()` only ever expose a NATIVE format string (no stdlib-only way to construct a Py_buffer with an explicit non-native `<`/`>`/`!` prefix), so covering this branch needs either a numpy test-only dependency (the one common exporter that does emit explicit byte-order codes) or a from-scratch C++-level unit test calling `span_format_matches` directly (`runtime/cpp/tests/` currently has only the facade self-check, no general header-logic unit-test harness). Small infra/scope decision either way -- pick one before adding the test, don't assume it's a five-minute addition. Surfaced by the `/tpy-ready` retrospective for `interop-span-buffer`.
- **[CPython interop] exception bridge follow-ups (built-in types DONE).** Built-in TPy exceptions raised in an `@export` body now cross as the matching `PyExc_*` with the message (`tpy/interop/exc_bridge.hpp`; glue catches `const tpy::BaseException&`). Remaining, all tracked Planned in `docs/CPYTHON_INTEROP.md`: (1) **user-defined exception classes -- TYPE crossing DONE** (`PyErr_NewException` per ext_module-defined exc class at `PyInit_`, registered `typeid -> PyObject*` in an `ExcRegistry` the glue passes to `set_py_err_from`; own importable type, inheritance chain, catchable as base). Still deferred under this rung: (1a) **faithful data-field crossing -- DONE** (branch `interop-exc-data-fields`): a data-carrying user exc's fields (scalar/str/bytes/exposed-enum) cross as instance attributes via a per-type generated setter threaded through the `ExcRegistry` (`typeid -> PyObject* + ExcSetErr`; `extension.py::_emit_user_exc_setters`, `exc_bridge.hpp`); the setter constructs `pytype(e.what())`, sets each field via `PyObject_SetAttrString`, then `PyErr_SetObject`. `str()`/`args` reflect the message only (acknowledged, unavoidable -- the C++ exc holds typed fields, not the original arg tuple). Container/exposed-class exc fields = located error (`Compiler._validate_user_exc_data_fields`), deferred. Full `args` fidelity (opt 1, needs `super().__init__` modeling) declined. The crossing is *transitive* (registry-driven, fires wherever raised) AND covers INHERITED user data fields (a subclass crosses its own + inherited fields via `TypeRegistry.user_declared_fields`, which skips native-base fields like `BaseException.message`; found+fixed in /tpy-review -- the first cut only marshalled own fields, a silent-drop divergence). The setter is `noexcept` + wrapped in try/catch degrading to `PyErr_NoMemory()` (a field marshaller can throw under OOM). Cross-module exposed-enum exc field = located error (routes through `_exposed_form_error` for the locality check). Two DEFERRED follow-ups filed from /tpy-review: (1a-i) **admit VALUE-TYPE exposed-class exc fields** -- the getset path admits an immutable value-type exposed-class field, but exc fields reject ALL exposed-class fields; admitting them reuses `_value_out_expr`'s `instance_to_py` arm (now safe under the setter's try/catch, since `instance_to_py` can throw) + needs a test; (1a-ii) **dedup the "is user exception" predicate** (`not is_native and implements_throwable and inherits_base_exception`) -- now spelled at 4 sites (`compiler.py`, `analyzer.py`, `registration.py`, `extension.py`); fold into a `RecordInfo.is_user_exception()` / registry helper. AND (higher value, /tpy-ready 2nd-opinion): the load-bearing drift is admission-vs-EMIT, not validator-vs-validator -- `compiler._validate_user_exc_data_fields` admits `is_boundary_marshallable(_, False) and not is_exposed_class`, but the setter EMIT is in `extension.py::_emit_user_exc_setters`; widening the shared marshallable predicate would auto-admit a type the setter cannot emit -> broken glue TU. Co-locate the "admissible exc-field type" predicate WITH the emit so admission+emit cannot drift (the exposed-class exc-field reject is already test-pinned by `error_export_exc_class_field`). Tests +`tests/interop/user_exc_inherited/` (hierarchy + transitive raise), +`tests/cases/interop/error_export_exc_cross_module_enum_field`; (1b) **the `str()`/`args` divergence is documented, not warned** -- a per-raise warning was shipped then REMOVED in /tpy-review: it was non-actionable (unavoidable under TPy's model, no escape hatch, no author-side fix -- making the exc message-only would drop the very fields, worse) and landed on the TPy author, not the Python consumer who reads `e.args`; so it is a doc-only divergence like the other no-escape-hatch ones (`_warn_export_user_exc_data` and its `warn_export_user_exc_data` test were deleted). (1c) **cross-module user exceptions** -- only classes DEFINED in the ext_module get a Python type; an imported user exc raised at the boundary still degrades to its built-in base (a data-field exc field typed as a cross-module enum is already a located error; the raise-through-an-imported-exc case is the remaining gap). (1f) **test gaps** -- bare user exc (no `__init__`), `py_exc_by_name` unknown-built-in-base fallback (e.g. inheriting `CancelledError` -> `PyExc_Exception`), and the `PyInit_` module-init (top-level) raise path are unexercised. (2) **`@error_return` Err -> raise** across the boundary. (3) **panic -> exception** (convert `tpy_panic` to a Python exception instead of `abort()`ing the host). (4) **exception chaining** (`__cause__`/`__context__`) not carried. (5) **`CancelledError` (and other `async.hpp` `BaseException` subclasses) degrade to `PyExc_BaseException`** -- no `PyExc_CancelledError` stable-ABI singleton exists (asyncio.CancelledError is Python-level), so bridging it needs importing `asyncio.CancelledError` at `PyInit` and stashing the type; deferred. (6) **test gaps**: the `PyInit_` (module-init raise) catch path needs a dedicated expected-import-failure interop case; broaden the cascade test with user-raised `RuntimeError`/`OverflowError`/bare `LookupError` (the latter exercises degrade-to-listed-base). (7) optional: extend the facade self-check to validate the new `PyExc_*` decls. (8) **low-prio perf:** `py_exc_for` is a linear `dynamic_cast` cascade (O(depth) RTTI probes). Fine -- it's a cold path (runs only when an exception crosses the boundary) -- but if ever revisited, the clean form is a virtual kind-tag on the `Throwable`/`BaseException` hierarchy switched on in the interop header (one vcall, keeps the Python C-API out of `core.hpp`), best bundled with the user-exc-class rung since that adds per-type metadata anyway. Not a faster cascade.
- **[CPython interop] exposed classes (`PyType_FromSpec`) -- deferred slices.** The baseline rung ships: `@export class` -> a CPython type built at `PyInit_`, instances embedding the TPy payload after the `PyObject` header (`tpy::interop::Instance<T>`, `class_bridge.hpp`); `tp_init` runs `__init__`, `tp_dealloc` runs the C++ destructor; plain instance methods + annotated fields as read/write getset; instances cross as free-fn/method params (a borrow of the live payload via `instance_payload` -- mutation writes through) and returns (`instance_to_py` copies into a fresh instance, so a borrow-form `-> Cls` return warns; `Own[Cls]` is acknowledged). The type is final (no `Py_TPFLAGS_BASETYPE`), no instance `__dict__`. The ext-module validator (`Compiler._validate_exposed_class`) rejects, with a located error, what the glue can't emit yet -- this is the deferred matrix: **(1) dunders** (Q4) -- **checkpoint 1 DONE** (`__repr__`/`__str__` -> `Py_tp_repr`/`Py_tp_str`; `__eq__`/`__ne__`/`__lt__`/`__le__`/`__gt__`/`__ge__` -> one `Py_tp_richcompare`, `__ne__` auto-derived from `__eq__`; `__hash__` -> `Py_tp_hash`, `PyObject_HashNotImplemented` wired when a richcompare dunder is defined without `__hash__`; new `_validate_export_class_dunders` in `analyzer.py` mirrors `_validate_dyn_dunder_kind`'s decorator/kind rejections plus per-dunder param-count/type/return-type checks; fixed a real pre-existing gap surfaced by the first class-typed non-self method param: `_boundary_inner` didn't strip `ReadonlyType`, so `def __eq__(self, other: Vec2)` -- whose `other` arrives as `readonly[Vec2]` -- failed `is_function_boundary_marshallable`/`is_exposed_class` even though `Vec2` is exposed; tests `tests/interop/class_dunders/`, `tests/cases/interop/error_export_class_dunder_{return,arity,staticmethod}`, `warn_export_class_dunder` repointed to `__call__`). **checkpoint 2 DONE** (arithmetic/ordering operators -> `Py_nb_*`: binary ops -- `__add__`/`__sub__`/`__mul__`/`__truediv__`/`__floordiv__`/`__mod__`/`__pow__`/`__lshift__`/`__rshift__`/`__and__`/`__or__`/`__xor__` -- each wire ONE wrapper handling both the forward dunder and its reflected counterpart if defined, since CPython invokes the same slot with `(a, b)` regardless of which side is `self`; the non-self operand marshals as whatever type the dunder declares -- another `@export` class OR a scalar/enum (e.g. `def __mul__(self, scalar: Int64)` for `vec * 3`), NOT restricted to the record's own type -- and a type mismatch downgrades to `NotImplemented` via a new `catch (const MarshalError&) { if (!PyErr_ExceptionMatches(TypeError)) return nullptr; PyErr_Clear(); }` pattern (a real error, e.g. `OverflowError` from a right-typed-but-bad-value operand, still propagates); `__pow__`/`__rpow__` use the ternary `nb_power` signature and reject a real 3-arg modulus; unary ops (`__pos__`/`__neg__`/`__invert__`) reuse the repr/str shape; in-place ops (`__iadd__`... the `CONST_PARAMS_METHODS` set) mutate self and return the SAME PyObject (`Py_IncRef`+return, no `instance_to_py` copy) since TPy already requires them to return `self` -- the borrow-return-copies warning is suppressed for this group specifically since identity IS preserved here (unlike every other dunder). New facade: `Py_nb_*` slot ids (25 constants) + `unaryfunc`/`binaryfunc`/`ternaryfunc` typedefs (`cpython_abi.hpp`, self-check-verified) + `PyErr_ExceptionMatches`/`PyErr_Clear` (`cpython_h.hpp`). Dunder-name tables (`_NB_BINARY_OPS`/`_NB_UNARY_OPS`/`_NB_INPLACE_OPS` in `extension.py`) are derived from the canonical `BINOP_TO_METHOD`/`BINOP_TO_RMETHOD`/`AUGOP_TO_IMETHOD`/`UNARYOP_TO_METHOD` tables (`modules/defs.py`), not a hand-rolled duplicate. Tests: `tests/interop/class_operators/` (same-type `__add__`, scalar-mixed `__mul__`/`__rmul__`, unary `__neg__`, in-place `__iadd__` with an identity check, ternary `__pow__`), `tests/cases/interop/error_export_class_dunder_arith_{arity,return}`. Verified end-to-end via a manual `.so` build+driver (forward/reflected/unary/inplace/wrong-type-TypeError/3-arg-pow-TypeError all matched). **checkpoint 3 DONE (Q4 fully landed)** (container protocol -> `Py_mp_*`/`Py_sq_*`/`Py_tp_iter*`: `__len__` wires ONE wrapper to both `Py_mp_length` and `Py_sq_length`; `__getitem__` -> `Py_mp_subscript` (a wrong-typed key's `TypeError` propagates directly -- no `NotImplemented` fallback, unlike arithmetic operators, since subscripting has no reflected side; slicing, `__getitem__(self, s: slice)`, is out of scope); `__setitem__`/`__delitem__` share ONE `Py_mp_ass_subscript` wrapper (`value == nullptr` means `del obj[k]`; if only one of the pair is defined, the other direction raises `TypeError`); `__contains__` -> `Py_sq_contains` (omitted entirely when undefined -- `in` then falls back to CPython's own iterate-via-`tp_iter` behavior for free); `__iter__` -> `Py_tp_iter` (reuses the checkpoint-2 unary-op emitter directly -- no self-identity special-casing needed: `__iter__` may legitimately return a fresh iterator object, not `self`, so it's marshalled like any other exposed-class-returning dunder, with the usual borrow-vs-`Own` warning); `__next__` -> `Py_tp_iternext` -- `__next__` is implicitly `@error_return(StopIteration)` (the parser default: `parse/parser.py`'s `if node.name == "__next__" and error_return is None: error_return = "StopIteration"`), so the compiled method returns `std::expected<T, StopIteration>` rather than throwing on exhaustion; the wrapper checks `.has_value()` and calls `set_py_err_from` on the `.error()` value directly (`StopIteration` is already in the exception bridge's built-in list, so no new bridging code needed) -- exempted from the blanket `@error_return` rejection the other dunders get. `list(x)`, `iter(x)`/`next(it)`, and a `for` loop all round-trip through CPython's own sequence/iterator machinery once these are wired -- verified end-to-end via a manual `.so` build+driver. New facade: `Py_mp_*`/`Py_sq_*`/`Py_tp_iter*` slot ids + `lenfunc`/`objobjargproc`/`objobjproc` typedefs (reuses `binaryfunc`/`unaryfunc` from checkpoint 2 for `mp_subscript`/`tp_iter`/`tp_iternext`), self-check-verified. Tests: `tests/interop/class_container/` (len/getitem/setitem-mutation/contains/iter+next via `list()`+manual+for-loop), `tests/cases/interop/error_export_class_dunder_{getitem_arity,setitem_arity,len_return}`. Never in scope for Q4 at all: `__call__`, `__bool__`, async iteration (`__aiter__`/`__anext__`), context managers (`__enter__`/`__exit__`), descriptors (`__get__`/`__set__`), and pickling (`__reduce__`) -- file separately if ever wanted. **Post-/tpy-review fixes (same session):** one Critical found and fixed, one Critical claim DISPROVEN by direct testing -- (a) [DISPROVEN] the review flagged `Py_tp_hash` being omitted whenever ANY richcompare dunder is defined without `__hash__` (not just `__eq__`) as a silent divergence from real Python's `__eq__`-specific unhashable-on-override rule. Building and running an actual `.so` showed this ISN'T a bug: `PyType_Ready` (the mechanism behind `PyType_FromSpec`, used for every exposed class) nulls `tp_hash` whenever `tp_richcompare` is populated AT ALL, with no way to tell which specific dunder populated it -- verified by removing the explicit `PyObject_HashNotImplemented` wiring entirely and confirming the type stayed unhashable regardless (a plain-`__len__`/no-richcompare class stays hashable, confirming richcompare-presence, not the explicit wiring, is what triggers it). So a `__lt__`-only class DOES become unhashable once exposed, unlike its plain-Python source (which stays hashable) -- this is a genuine, unavoidable, now-DOCUMENTED acknowledged divergence (comment in `extension.py`, `Ordered` class + `ext_checks.py` assertion in `tests/interop/class_dunders/`), not something fixable in TPy's glue. (b) [FIXED, confirmed real] richcompare codegen hardcoded the "other" operand's C++ type as the record's own type regardless of what the compare dunder actually declared, so a legal-per-sema `def __eq__(self, other: Int64)` passed comp but failed the `.so` build with a confusing internal g++ error; fixed by requiring a compare dunder's operand type equal the record's own type at sema (`_validate_export_class_dunders`), +`error_export_class_dunder_compare_wrong_type`. **Known limitation from this fix (documented in `docs/CPYTHON_INTEROP.md`/`LANGUAGE_FEATURES.md`, not yet filed as a TODO before now):** unlike arithmetic operators (checkpoint 2), a comparison dunder can only compare against the record's OWN exposed-class type -- `__eq__(self, other: Int64)` or `__eq__(self, other: SomeOtherExposedClass)` are both rejected at sema, even though sema's `is_function_boundary_marshallable` check would otherwise admit them. Lifting this needs richcompare codegen to marshal per-op-declared-type with a `NotImplemented`-on-mismatch fallback (mirroring `_value_in_decl_expr`'s already-built arithmetic-operator pattern) instead of the current single type-guard-then-switch shape shared by all 6 ops -- a real codegen redesign, not a quick follow-up. Also added from the review: `__delitem__`-actually-implemented coverage (`Slot` class in `tests/interop/class_container/`), an `OverflowError`-propagates-not-swallowed-by-NotImplemented test and an asymmetric `__sub__`/`__rsub__` pair (operand order matters, unlike the previously-only-tested commutative `__mul__`/`__rmul__`) in `tests/interop/class_operators/`, and `error_export_class_dunder_{contains_return,iter_return}` cases. **Post-/tpy-ready fixes (same session, second-opinion agent caught this before merge):** `compiler.py`'s per-method loop unconditionally skips all non-`__init__` dunders, so its `type_params`/overload/param-form rejections never ran against ANY dunder -- confirmed as a LIVE gap (not just theoretical) by direct reproduction: a generic dunder (`def __len__[T](self) -> Int32:`) and a defaulted dunder param (`def __eq__(self, other: C = None)`) both passed comp-phase cleanly then failed the actual `.so` build with confusing internal g++ errors (`no matching function for call to ... __len__() const` / `could not convert 'nullptr' to 'const C&'`) -- the exact failure shape already fixed once for richcompare in this same round. Fixed: `analyzer.py::_validate_export_class_dunders` gained a `type_params` check and a new `_unsupported_dunder_param_form` helper (duplicated from -- not shared with -- `compiler.py::_unsupported_param_form`, since the two validators live in different modules; unifying them is the remaining architectural item below). Tests: `error_export_class_dunder_{generic,default_param}`. The `@overload`-grouped-dunder case the second-opinion agent also flagged was checked and found to be ALREADY correctly rejected (the existing `is_overload_stub` check fires on the first stub before the impl is ever reached) -- not a live gap, no fix needed there.

**@export class single-value marshal duplication:** `extension.py`'s `_value_in_decl_expr`/`_value_out_expr` (expression-form, now shared by operator dunders AND getset fields) duplicate `_emit_marshal_in`/`_emit_call_return`'s (statement-form) branch structure -- two marshalling dispatch chains to keep in lockstep when a boundary type is added. Same-family: the `-1`-sentinel BaseException/catch-all block is hand-inlined three times (tp_init, field setter, property setter -- the field variant lacks the BaseException arm); a tiny shared `-1`-catch emitter would collapse it. (low confidence) `_value_in_decl_expr` always binds a `const&` for an exposed-class non-self operand regardless of Phase-2 mutation analysis; a dunder that legitimately mutates a non-self operand would hit a const-mismatch build failure rather than a clear diagnostic -- not confirmed against a concrete case. **(2) inheritance -- DONE** (branch `interop-class-inheritance`: single exposed same-module base -> real `tp_base` via `PyType_FromSpecWithBases`; see the dedicated deferred-slices entry); **(3) `@property` -- DONE** (branch `interop-class-properties`): a property crosses as a *computed* getset -- the getter is a zero-arg method return site (reuses `_emit_call_return`, so it admits the full method boundary set incl. containers), the setter a one-param method param site (reuses `_emit_marshal_in` via an `a0` alias; a non-value setter param is Own-auto-wrapped, so the marshalled owned local is `std::move`d in). Getter-only property = `nullptr` setter (`AttributeError` on write, as CPython -- message text differs). Shape checks route through `export_method_shape_error(allow_property=True)` (async/generator/generic/`@error_return` accessors still reject); a setter typed as an exposed class -- value-type OR reference (the property Own auto-wrap runs before a user record's ValueType flag is known, so both arrive Own-wrapped) -- is a dedicated located error steering to a plain method; a `Span[T]` setter is a located error (a setter stores, and the buffer copy-in dies at the call -- store `list[T]`); `del` on any property or field raises `AttributeError` via a `value == nullptr` guard in both setter emits (CPython's getset delete protocol; previously segfaulted); `_`-prefixed properties are internal (uniform `_`-attribute rule, borrow-return warn loop included); a borrow-return getter warns via the existing `_warn_export_class_return_alias` (labeled `property 'x'`, const-clone deduped). Tests `tests/interop/class_properties/`, `tests/cases/interop/{error_export_class_property_class_setter,error_export_class_property_value_class_setter,error_export_class_property_span_setter,error_export_class_property_setter_unmarshallable,error_export_class_property_error_return,error_export_class_property_unmarshallable,warn_export_class_property_borrow_return}`. Deferred follow-ups: (3a) **enable value-type exposed-class property setters** -- rejected today only because `_apply_property_setter_wrapping` (sema/method_expansion.py) runs before the protocol pass sets a user record's ValueType flag, so the param is Own-wrapped and the borrowed argument payload can't feed it; fix = defer/reorder the wrap decision (plain-TPy-wide, own design pass) or copy-emit at the boundary for a value-class `Own` setter; (3b) property accessors skip `unsupported_boundary_param_form` (a defaulted setter value param passes unvalidated -- harmless today, the getset protocol always supplies the value; add for uniformity with the method path); (3c) test gap: no runtime (ext-exec) witness of an exposed-class-VALUED getter (`-> Own[Inner]` copy-out read; the borrow-form warn case is comp-only) -- needs a second exposed class in an interop case. The warning-dropped-on-error residual is now its own BUGS.md entry; **(4) class-typed fields** -- the ENUM half is DONE (branch `interop-class-typed-fields`: an exposed-enum getset field round-trips value-correctly, singleton-preserving -- see the enums entry item 2c). The CLASS half stays *rejected* with a located error and is **foreign-borrow-gated**, not just unimplemented: a nested exposed class is stored inline by value, so a getset getter could only copy it out -- `outer.inner` would fabricate a fresh object per read and `outer.inner.x = 5` would silently no-op where CPython aliases the real object (a worse instance of the copy-divergence family than the `-> Cls` return, because it masquerades as plain attribute access and the consumer writing Python sees no compile warning). Faithful write-through aliasing needs the deferred **foreign-borrow primitive** (a `PyObject` pointing into the parent's storage; the same phase-3.5 primitive gating zero-copy str/buffer), so it groups with the zero-copy deferrals rather than being a near-term rung; the diagnostic steers to making the field **internal** (a `_`-prefixed name -- see the internal-fields entry below -- kept as payload state, never a Python attribute) plus an accessor method (mutating through `self`) or an explicit `copy()`. A *public* `@nocopy` or cross-module exposed-class field stays rejected for the usual reasons (internal fields have no interop gate, so a `_`-named `@nocopy` member is fine). **Internal (`_`-prefixed) fields DONE** (branch `interop-internal-fields`): a field whose name begins with `_` never crosses as an attribute at EITHER site (exposed-class getset via the `extension.py` getset loop + `_validate_exposed_class`; exception data-fields via `_collect_user_exc_classes` + `_validate_user_exc_data_fields`), gated by one shared predicate `type_def_registry.is_internal_boundary_field`. It stays live payload state, so an exposed class can now hold ANY-typed internal member (reference class, container, `@nocopy`) reached through a method -- the pattern the reference-class-field diagnostic already recommended, now actually reachable. Divergence (acknowledged, like `e.message`): a `_`-field is an ordinary attribute under plain Python but absent on the `.so`, so drivers must not read one. Test `tests/interop/internal_fields/`; migrated `class_container`'s `_i` cursor (its pointless mutable getset drops).
