FILED-NOT-BUILT: replace the C++ `nothrow_move` static_assert on the payload with a located sema pre-check for a non-movable exposed payload (a `_`-field can now make a class non-movable, hitting the assert as a raw build error rather than a diagnostic). **(4a) exposed VALUE-TYPE fields -- DONE** (branch `interop-class-typed-fields`): a field typed as an @export `ValueType` record (`origin: Point`) is admitted -- value types are immutable, so copy-out is behaviorally invisible (the same safe bucket as enum, not the reference-class footgun). Two parts landed: (i) an exposed value-type class emits READ-ONLY getset (getter only, `nullptr` setter -- gated on `info.is_value_type` in `extension.py`; also fixed the pre-existing r/w-mutable fidelity gap that was filed in BUGS.md, now removed); (ii) the field reject narrowed to `is_exposed_class(typ) and not _boundary_inner(typ).is_value_type()`, so a value-type field falls through to admission -- getter copy-out via `instance_to_py`, the OUTER holder's field-set stays r/w (whole-value replacement). CPython parity: chose read-only `.so` + ext-only divergences (mutation-raises-AttributeError and copy-out `is`-identity in `ext_checks.py`, driver stays parity-clean) over freezing the `lib/cpy` `ValueType` stub -- no `no_cpython.txt` needed, consistent with the shipped strict-by-type/final/no-`__dict__` pattern. Equality gate resolved: value types have NO implicit structural `==` (it's a compile error without a defined `__eq__`), and a user-defined `__eq__` is already wired by the Q4 richcompare rung. Test `tests/interop/value_typed_fields/`. Char fields (no marshaller) and tuple fields (value type, but the getset has no container/tuple emit -- pairs with the container-getset-field work) remain separate small gaps; **(5) static/classmethods**; **(6) generic exposed classes / generic methods**; **(7) faithful exception data-field crossing (exc TODO 1a)** -- DONE (branch `interop-exc-data-fields`); reused the getset single-value marshal helper (`_value_out_expr`) for each exc data field, not an exception-only path; container/exposed-class exc fields deferred (located error); **(8) `copy.copy`/`pickle`** (a C type with no `__reduce__`/`tp_copy` fails both where the source class succeeds -- track as a declared divergence); **(9) overloaded methods / `__init__`** (now *rejected* -- the glue emits one positional wrapper, so an `@overload` group would silently expose only the first arity; supporting it needs per-arity dispatch in the wrapper); **(10) `tp_traverse`/GC** -- not emitted (fields are scalar/str/bytes/enum or a nested value-type class, all held by value with no `PyObject` reference, so no reference cycle is possible -- an enum field holds an interned int, a value-type field an inline immutable value, not a Python ref). NOT triggered by the enum-field work (the earlier claim that class-typed fields would force GC assumed a *reference* model); it becomes mandatory only if a future *aliasing* class-typed field lands via the foreign-borrow primitive (item 4), which would hold a real `PyObject` ref -- the current copy model would not. Wire it in with that (deferred) work; **(11) `Py_TPFLAGS_BASETYPE`** -- a LEAF exposed type stays non-subclassable from Python (honest `TypeError` at the class statement); a class that is another exposed class's base carries BASETYPE (required by `PyType_FromSpecWithBases`) with a tp_init exact-type guard rejecting Python-subclass instantiation, and the `PyType_IsSubtype` branch in `instance_payload` is now LIVE (a base-typed param accepts a derived instance); **(12) throwing re-`__init__`** leaves the instance with a destroyed payload reachable via getset (UB if then touched) -- exotic (catch an `obj.__init__()` failure, then read a field) and CPython is also ill-defined there; revisit if it bites. Code organization: DONE -- the glue emitter is now `codegen_cpp/extension.py` (`ExtensionGenerator`, wired as `CodeGenerator.extension`; `generate_extension_glue` is a thin delegator), and `boundary_cpp_type`/`boundary_unmarshallable_msg` moved to `type_def_registry.py` (next to `is_boundary_marshallable`), so both the sema validator and the glue import them from one neutral home. Byte-identical codegen. Full design in `docs/CPYTHON_INTEROP.md` phase 4. **Review follow-ups (from /tpy-review):** DONE in round 2 -- non-`__init__` dunders now *warn* (`_warn_export_class_unexposed_dunders`); `Own[<exposed class>]` params, `@nocopy` exposed-class borrow-returns, and class-typed fields are now *located errors* (`Compiler._exposed_form_error`, renamed from `_exposed_class_form_error` when it gained the enum cases) instead of opaque C++ failures (the `@nocopy` borrow-return suppresses the duplicate identity warning). Round-3 (ready gate): `@overload` method/`__init__` groups now reject (item 9) -- the silent-first-arity drop the rest of the branch was eliminating. (A plain *duplicate* `def __init__` was already rejected upstream; the new check covers the `@overload` group case codegen's `[0]` would otherwise truncate.) STILL OPEN: (a) keyword arguments -- DONE (see the dedicated `[CPython interop] keyword-arg boundary` entry below). (e) cross-module exposed classes have no same-defining-module guard -- DONE (branch `fix-cross-module-exposed-class`): `Compiler._exposed_form_error`'s class branch now checks the class is locally `@export`-defined in the current module (mirrors the pre-existing enum-branch guard), resolved via `registry.imported_record_qualification_for_type` (qualified identity, not a bare short-name set -- a name-collision variant of this exact check was caught live by `/tpy-review`'s `cpython-parity`/`architecture-fit` passes and fixed before merge), so a foreign exposed class used as a param/field/return is a located error instead of a misbuild; tests `tests/cases/interop/error_export_class_cross_module_{param,return,name_collision}`. **Cleanups:** rename `type_def_registry._boundary_inner` -> `boundary_inner` (now a cross-module API); dedupe the dunder-exclusion predicate spelled at 3 sites (compiler/generator/analyzer); unify `is_exposed_class` (`td.record.exposed_to_host`) and `is_boundary_marshallable` (`td.boundary_marshal`) onto one canonical check (the `boundary_cpp_type`/`boundary_unmarshallable_msg` relocation is DONE -- now in `type_def_registry.py`). **Test gaps:** round 2 added `error_*` cases for the Own-param / class-typed-field / @nocopy-borrow-return rejects + a `warn_export_class_dunder` case; still missing `error_*` for the remaining `_validate_exposed_class` reject paths (throwable/generic/static/async; the inheritance rejects got their own cases on the `interop-class-inheritance` branch) + the `@export`-outside-`ext_module` ParseError + `@export("renamed") class`, and a happy-path for multiple exposed classes in one module or an empty class (no fields/no non-init methods).
- **[CPython interop] keyword-arg boundary -- deferred slices.** Positional + keyword (and mixed) args cross at the `@export` boundary: every >=1-param wrapper (free fn, method, `tp_init`) uses `PyArg_ParseTupleAndKeywords` + `METH_VARARGS | METH_KEYWORDS` with a `kwlist` of the Python param names and a `:name` format suffix; zero-arg stays `METH_NOARGS`; the 3-arg keyword wrapper casts into the `PyCFunction` slot via facade `as_pycfunction` (`_PyCFunction_CAST` idiom). Param forms the unpack can't cross -- defaults, `*args`/`**kwargs`, positional-only (`/`), keyword-only (`*`) -- are *rejected* with a located compile error (`Compiler._unsupported_param_form`, used by both `_validate_ext_module_exports` and `_validate_exposed_class`) rather than silently mishandled (a default was silently *required*, a `*args` silently *dropped*). Tests: `tests/interop/kwargs/` (happy-path + `ext_checks.py` for the unknown/duplicate-keyword `TypeError`), `tests/cases/interop/error_export_{default_param,varargs,posonly,kwonly,class_default_method}`. Deferred: (1) **default param values + keyword-only support** -- the natural next rung; defaults need an optional-slot format (`|`) plus passing the C++ default when an arg is omitted, keyword-only needs the `$` marker (it usually pairs with defaults). (2) **`_unsupported_param_form` reads raw `TpyFunction` AST attrs and forces an `ast_by_name` workaround in the class-method loop** that already holds a `FunctionInfo` (THIR "consumer re-derives the fact" smell); `FunctionInfo`/`ParamInfo` carry equivalents (`has_variadic`/`has_keyword_only`/`kwarg_name`/`ParamInfo.positional_only`/`has_default`), but the free-fn call site iterates AST nodes too, so converting the helper to `FunctionInfo` is a small refactor across both sites, not a one-liner -- fold in when this entry's next rung is built. (3) **test gaps** -- only the free-fn forms (default/`*args`/posonly/kwonly) and a default-on-method have `error_*` cases; a `**kwargs` reject needs the `Unpack[TypedDict]` form (a bare `**kwargs` is rejected upstream), and the other forms on a method/`__init__` reuse the shared helper so they're covered indirectly. (4) **error-message text divergence** (declared, cosmetic): the C parser's unknown-keyword / arg-count message wording differs from CPython's own (same `TypeError` type); `ext_checks.py` asserts the type only and `docs/CPYTHON_INTEROP.md` notes it.
- **[CPython interop] enums + constants boundary -- deferred slices.** An `@export` enum in an ext_module is recreated at `PyInit_` as a **real** CPython enum (`enum.IntEnum`/`enum.Enum` via the stdlib functional API with `module=` set; `runtime/cpp/include/tpy/interop/enum_bridge.hpp` `make_enum`/`enum_dict_add`) -- CPython-parity-verified observably identical to a source-level class-statement enum. Module-level `Final` constants of a boundary scalar (`int`/`IntN`/`bool`/`float`/`str`) become init-time module-attribute snapshots (read after `__tpy_init`; Final => immutable so the snapshot can't go stale). Enums are `@export`-gated (consistent with classes/functions; the enum parser accepts bare `@export` in an ext_module); constants are auto-exposed via `Final` (no decorator syntax exists for a binding, and Final is the only snapshot-safe global form). Validator (`Compiler._validate_exposed_enums`) rejects `@export` on a `@native` enum or a nested enum; the glue collectors `ExtensionGenerator._exposed_enums`/`_exposed_constants` filter the exposable surface. Facade += `PyImport_ImportModule`/`PyObject_GetAttrString`/`PyObject_Call`/`Py_BuildValue`/`PyDict_New`/`PyDict_SetItemString`; `marshal.hpp` += `to_py(std::string_view)` (a `Final[str]` resolves to `StrView`). Tests: `tests/interop/{enums,constants}/`, `tests/cases/interop/error_export_{enum_outside_ext,native_enum,nested_enum}`. Deferred: (1) **enum *values* as `@export` function param/return types** -- DONE (branch interop-enum-values): an exposed enum crosses as a fn param/return via `enum_bridge.hpp` `enum_from_py`/`enum_to_py` against the module-static enum-type handle (promoted from a PyInit-local). OUT reconstructs the member (`EnumType(value)`, identity preserved); IN is strict-by-type (bare int -> TypeError; documented int-vs-member-vs-source divergence, ext_checks-tested). Admission = `boundary_marshal` on the exposed enum's TypeDef (sema registration) + `is_exposed_enum`. `int(enum)` inside a body is still unsupported (orthogonal TPy conversion gap, not the boundary). (2) **nested enums** (inside a class) and **cross-module enums** -- nested still rejected; a cross-module exposed-enum param/return is a located error (`_exposed_form_error`, checks the enum is locally @export-defined) rather than a misbuild -- the exposed-CLASS cross-module path now has the same guard too (DONE, branch `fix-cross-module-exposed-class`; see the exposed-classes entry's item (e)). From /tpy-review (treat as ONE deferred cross-module unit, all low-reachability today): (2a) the cross-module guard branch was UNTESTED -- DONE (branch `fix-cross-module-exposed-class`): a 2-file `src/main.py` + `src/enum_mod.py` fixture under `tests/cases/interop/` works fine (the "single-source-per-case" limit only applies to the `tests/interop/` ext-exec harness, not the plain comp-phase `tests/cases/` one); see `error_export_enum_cross_module` (and the class analog `error_export_class_cross_module_{param,return}`). (2b) DONE for both classes and enums: the class guard resolves by qualified identity (`registry.imported_record_qualification_for_type`, the same type-object-based "is this local?" helper codegen already uses to qualify a cross-module record) instead of a bare short-name set -- a module that imports a foreign exposed class under an alias while also defining its own same-named local one no longer defeats the guard; regression test `error_export_class_cross_module_name_collision`. The ENUM guard was similarly hardened (reads `EnumInfo.module_name` off the resolved type object rather than `registry.imported_enum_qualification`'s name-keyed lookup) and the underlying general enum name-resolution bug that initially blocked verifying it (an aliased cross-module enum import getting confused with a same-canonical-name LOCAL enum, no `@export` needed to reproduce) is now fixed too (branch `fix-enum-alias-collision`: `parse/type_resolver.py`'s enum branch now mints a qualified `NominalType` from the import tuple instead of trusting a bare-name registry lookup, mirroring the pre-existing record branch; `codegen_cpp/generator.py`'s `native_cpp_names` population gained the qname-keyed entry records already had plus a local-shadow guard on the bare-name compensating write). Regression tests `tests/cases/enum/enum_alias_local_collision` (general) and `error_export_enum_cross_module_name_collision` (interop). (2c) **enum getset field on an exposed class** -- DONE (branch `interop-class-typed-fields`): a locally-defined exposed enum is a valid read/write getset field; the getter/setter reuse the same single-value marshal helpers (`_value_out_expr`/`_value_in_decl_expr`) the operator dunders use, so the field round-trips `enum_to_py`/`enum_from_py` against the module enum handle (member singleton preserved, strict-by-type set). A cross-module exposed-enum field stays a located error (the same cross-module guard, now covering the field role -- retargeted `error_export_class_enum_field`). Tests: `tests/interop/enum_typed_fields/`. (2d) `is_exposed_enum` reads `td.boundary_marshal` as an exposed proxy rather than a first-class `EnumInfo.exposed_to_host`; correct today (boundary_marshal set only when exposed) -- fold into the existing "unify is_exposed_class / is_boundary_marshallable onto one canonical check" cleanup, don't add the field standalone. (2e) DONE: asymmetric enum signatures (enum-IN-only `is_red(c: Color) -> bool`, enum-OUT-only `default_color() -> Color`) now exercise each marshal side independently in tests/interop/enum_values. (3) **`bytes`/`BytesView` and `Char`/`tuple` constants** -- a `Final[bytes]` resolves to a view with no `to_py(span)` overload, and `Char`/`tuple` aren't boundary types; all are silently left unexposed (like a non-`@export` function), documented in `docs/CPYTHON_INTEROP.md`. A `bytes` constant would need either `to_py(BytesView/span)` or forcing the owned form. (4) **the auto-exposed-`Final` surface is implicit** -- there is no per-constant opt-in marker (unlike `@export` on enums/funcs/classes); a user intending to export a `Final[tuple]` gets silent non-export. Acceptable as the opt-in-parallel framing (only boundary-typed Finals form the surface), but if it bites, a future explicit constant-export spelling (or a warning on a skipped non-boundary `Final`) is the escape hatch. (5) **`_exposed_constants` re-resolves the Final type** via `resolve_stmt_type_cascade` (the generator's `seen_globals`/compiler's `ModuleVarInfo` already hold it) -- a small "consumer re-derives the fact" smell; fold onto a shared resolved-globals source when one is threaded to the glue. (6) **test gaps** (from /tpy-ready retrospective): an unannotated `X: Final = 5` constant (may carry an unresolved int-literal type that fails `_constant_exposable` where the annotated `Final[int]` form passes -- the item-5 materialized-type fix would also erase this) and an `auto()`-valued `@export` enum (the parser materializes `auto()` to concrete values at parse time, so the path is correct but untested) are both unexercised.
- **[CPython interop] str/bytes boundary -- deferred slices.** The str/bytes rung ships only the owned, immutable value forms by copy. Excluded, by design: (1) **`StrView`/`BytesView` (zero-copy view input)** -- a view param would borrow into the live PyObject for the call's duration; making that escape-sound for a *foreign* source is the phase-3.5 foreign-borrow primitive (IR-gated). Currently rejected with the standard "not marshallable" diagnostic; `str`/`bytes` (copy) are the workaround. (2) **`bytearray` (mutable buffer)** -- shares `bytes`' C++ representation (`std::vector<uint8_t>`) but is a reference type; marshalling it by copy as a `PyBytes` would silently drop the aliasing a CPython caller expects, so it is rejected (admission judges the `Own`-unwrapped inner type's `is_value_type`, so `bytearray` and `Own[bytearray]` both reject). Could later be supported as an explicit copy with a documented "no aliasing" note, or mapped to a real `PyByteArray` if a mutable round-trip is wanted. (3) **`str.upper()`/concat etc. produce fresh owned strings** -- fine today; no action. All three are in `docs/CPYTHON_INTEROP.md`.
- **[CPython interop] ext-exec .so build can't reuse the non-PIC stdlib `.o` cache.** The exec phase reuses a shared precompiled stdlib `.o` set, but the ext-exec `.so` build is `-fPIC` so those objects don't apply -- `tpyc -b` recompiles the runtime + imported stdlib fresh on every cache-miss build (correct, just slower; ccache warms most of it). With one interop case the cost is small; if more land, add a PIC-`.o` stdlib cache variant (or build the `.so` from the non-PIC set where the linker permits) so ext-exec re-verifies as cheaply as exec. Deferred until interop case count or build time justifies it.
- **[CPython interop] macOS `.so` build support.** The first-class ext build links `-fPIC -shared`, which is the Linux recipe: it works because the dynamic linker resolves the undefined `Py*` symbols against the host interpreter at import. macOS (Mach-O) won't leave those symbols undefined under plain `-shared` -- the canonical recipe is `-bundle -undefined dynamic_lookup` (what CPython's own `LDSHARED` uses; `-fPIC` is a harmless default there, and a bare `<mod>.so` is still the right import name). So `build_cpp_commands(shared=True)` in compiler.py needs a platform branch for the link flags, and the `_EXT_BUILD_SUPPORTED` gate in `tests/test_interop_exec.py` (currently Linux-only -- the ext-exec build+run skips on other platforms while snapshot/cpy-parity/facade-self-check still run) should widen to macOS once the recipe lands and is validated on a Mac. Windows is separate and larger (MSVC + no statement-expression extension). Interop is documented Linux-first; this lifts it to macOS.
- **[CPython interop] follow-ups from /tpy-review.** (1) **Facade function-signature self-check.** `cpython_facade_selfcheck.cpp` validates struct layouts + constants but NOT the `extern "C"` function decls in `cpython_h.hpp`, so a wrong signature surfaces only at link/ABI corruption. Add `static_assert(std::is_same_v<decltype(&::PyFn), decltype(&tpy::cpy::PyFn)>)` per decl. (2) **Boundary-marshalling render helpers live in `codegen_cpp/generator.py`** (`boundary_cpp_type`, `boundary_unmarshallable_msg`) but are consumed upward by `compiler._validate_ext_module_exports` via a local import -- a layering smell; move to a neutral `tpyc/codegen_cpp/ext_boundary.py` (or similar) both sides import. (The admission predicate itself already moved to `type_def_registry.is_boundary_marshallable`, and `BOUNDARY_CPP_TYPES`/`boundary_type_ok` were deleted, so only these two diagnostic-render helpers remain.) (3) **`_Py_NoneStruct` is not a named stable-API symbol** -- it is binary-exported by every CPython and the only way to reach `Py_None` from the no-`Python.h` facade at the abi3 3.12 floor (the documented `Py_GetNone()` lands in 3.13). Marked "don't use directly" upstream; swap to `Py_GetNone()` once the abi3 floor rises to 3.13. (Done in this round: void-return `@export` and the `to_py` overload-ambiguity, now resolved by a `requires`-constrained integral `to_py` template that never competes with the `int64_t`/`double`/`bool` overloads.)
- **[design question, open -- discussion stage, no decision] Revisit the `# tpy:` directive surface: comment pragmas vs in-language spellings.** Comment directives (`# tpy: ext_module`, `macro_module`, `namespace(...)`, `link("pcre2", managed=True)`, ...) are poorly visible in code (dim highlighting) even though typos already warn (parser.py's unknown-directive ParseWarning). Candidates discussed 2026-07-12: (a) status quo -- honest "metadata, not semantics", matches `# coding:`/`# type:` pragma precedent; (b) marker import `from tpy import __extension_module__` -- the `from __future__` precedent, loud typos, highlighted as code; no parameter channel, and linters flag it as an unused import (F401) unlike the special-cased `__future__`; (c) dunder assignments `__ext_module__ = True` / `__tpy_namespace__ = "..."` / `__tpy_link__ = [...]` -- declarative, statically scannable pre-execution (required by `macro_module`, which decides whether the module RUNS under CPython at compile time), CPython-inert without stubs, param-capable via literals, linter-quiet; (d) a call form (`tpy.extern.ext_module()`) -- rejected in discussion: looks like an import-time side effect but would never execute (a declaration in a call costume). Constraint: whatever is chosen must cover the WHOLE surface in at most two coherent forms (flags + parameterized), not fork per-directive; migration would touch every directive consumer, all tests, and docs. Whole-surface redesign, own /tpy-add-feature design pass if picked up.
- **[CPython interop] ext-exec harness robustness gaps (from /tpy-review).** (1) `discover_interop_cases` (conftest) silently drops a `tests/interop/<case>/` dir that has a `driver.py` but no `# tpy: ext_module` source, and picks only the first such `.py` -- a mis-named or future multi-module case is underbuilt with no diagnostic; warn/fail loudly instead. Relatedly, if discovery ever returns nothing, `_INTEROP_CASES` is empty and the module collects zero tests silently -- add a module-load guard asserting at least one case so a broken discovery fails loudly instead of no-op'ing. (2) `compute_ext_exec_fingerprint` doesn't key on the Python version (`sys.version_info`/`sys.executable`): the ext-exec marker stays green across a CPython minor-version switch, so a driver-output divergence between versions stays invisible until `--force-exec`. Fold the version into the fingerprint. Both deferred -- low bite with one case.
- **`urllib.parse` v2 follow-ups (deferred from the v1 stdlib add).** Tracked per-item in STDLIB_ROADMAP.md's urllib.parse table: `parse_qs` (dict-of-lists; needs `dict[str, list[str]]` build), bytes variants (`quote_from_bytes`/`unquote_to_bytes`/`SplitResultBytes`), `urldefrag`, and the extra params/forms (`urlencode(doseq=)`, `quote(encoding=)`). Also the namedtuple-compatibility gap: results are records, so integer indexing (`r[0]`) and unpacking (`a, b, ... = urlsplit(u)`) are unavailable -- all-str components mean `__getitem__(int) -> str` is feasible to add if a parity need surfaces. Each is a focused `/tpy-add-feature`. Surfaced building urllib.parse v1.
- **`urllib.parse.unquote` invalid-UTF-8 -> U+FFFD divergence (silent, documented).** `unquote`/`unquote_plus` of a percent-sequence that decodes to invalid UTF-8 (e.g. `unquote("%FF")`) returns the raw bytes; CPython defaults to `errors='replace'` and substitutes U+FFFD. Matching it needs a lossy UTF-8 decode (`bytes.decode(errors=...)` or equivalent) the runtime does not yet expose -- a small runtime/stdlib feature, not a urllib fix. Well-formed URLs never hit it. Documented in the module header + LANGUAGE_FEATURES. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of urllib.parse v1. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` (lossy UTF-8 decode).
- **[codegen perf] A constant container of literals should lower to a static/compile-time structure, not a runtime-rebuilt or runtime-initialized heap container.** Two rungs: (a) a set-literal membership in a function body (`x in {"a", "b", ...}`) rebuilds a fresh `ordered_set<std::string>` on EVERY call; (b) hoisting it to a module-level `set[str]` global (the current urllib.parse workaround) builds the heap set once at `__tpy_init` but is still a runtime-initialized heap object, not a compile-time constant. For a container of compile-time literals (esp. string sets used as lookup tables), codegen could emit a truly static structure -- a sorted `constexpr std::array<std::string_view>` + binary search, a perfect hash, or a `string_view` switch -- with zero per-call and zero startup heap cost. Surfaced by /tpy-review of urllib.parse's scheme tables. Needs `/tpy-add-feature`.
- **[feature] Support `Final[set[...]]` / `Final[frozenset[...]]` (container constants) for module-level constants.** `Final` is currently restricted to primitives + tuple (`Final[set[str]] is not supported; only primitive types ... and tuple are allowed`), so a constant lookup table must be spelled as a plain mutable module global `set` -- which works but loses the immutability/intent `Final` documents (`frozenset` would be the natural CPython spelling). Pairs with the constexpr-lowering item above: a `Final` constant container is exactly what should lower to a static structure. Surfaced building urllib.parse's scheme tables (had to use a non-`Final` global). Needs `/tpy-add-feature`.
- **[feature] `collections.namedtuple` / `typing.NamedTuple` support.** A type that is simultaneously a tuple (indexable `r[0]`, unpackable `a, b = r`, iterable) AND carries named fields + methods/properties. TPy has no such type today, which forces stdlib results CPython returns as namedtuples -- `urllib.parse`'s `SplitResult`/`ParseResult` (and future `os.stat_result`-shaped results) -- to be plain records, losing indexing/unpacking (a deliberate, compile-visible divergence). Namedtuple support would let those be true namedtuples and close the divergence. Likely a `@class_macro` synthesizing `__getitem__`/`__iter__`/field properties over a backing tuple, or a first-class type-system construct. Surfaced building urllib.parse v1 (user: "eventually we should handle namedtuples"). Needs `/tpy-add-feature`.
- **Multi-base ctor inheritance with a generic init parent -- test-coverage gap.** `_find_unique_init_parent` now accepts a generic-base instantiation as the unique init parent, and the `inheritance/generic_base_inherited_ctor` case covers single-base + generic-subclass shapes, but the multi-base mixin shape (`class Sub(Base[14], Mixin)` where `Mixin` contributes no `__init__`) is untested -- it exercises the `found is not None` uniqueness path with a generic init parent specifically. Add a case. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the generic-base inherited-ctor fix.
- **[IR migration] Keep a hoisted str/bytes tuple view target zero-copy when its source provably outlives the hoist scope (precise lifetime analysis).** Today a str/bytes tuple-unpack target hoisted out of a branch is unconditionally owned (`_promote_predecl_view_targets`, the Option-A conservative rule): the hoisted slot outlives the per-branch `__tup` temp and any branch-local by-ref source, so a view would dangle. But when the by-ref source itself outlives the hoist scope, owning is an unnecessary copy. Two instances: (a) the source is a pre-existing function-scope local (`tuple_unpack_stable_name_keeps_view`, just hoisted); (b) the source is assigned on all branch paths under one shared name (`if c: t = make(); a, b = t else: t = make2(); a, b = t`), so the predecl machinery hoists `t` to the *same* outer scope as `a`/`b` -- the source is then function-lifetime and a view stays valid (every read of `a`/`b` happens while `t` is alive). The conservative rule over-owns both; the precise rule -- own iff the source does NOT outlive the target's hoist scope (accounting for same-scope declaration/destruction order) -- is place/loan escape analysis, which belongs on MIR's `Place`/`LoanInfo` model, not a codegen/sema heuristic. Until then the conservative copy is correct, just occasionally non-optimal. Surfaced fixing the tuple-unpack hoisting bug (Option B deferred).
- **[cleanup] Materialize the tuple-unpack "owned-temp source" fact as a `TpyTupleUnpack` AST field.** Sema (`_analyze_tuple_unpack`, `source_binds_by_ref`) and codegen (`_gen_tuple_unpack`, the bind-by-ref guard `isinstance(stmt.value, TpyName) and not any(stmt.is_owned)`) independently re-derive the same "does the source materialize an owned `__tup` temp vs bind by-ref" predicate; a comment pins the pairing today, but it is a sema/codegen mirror that will drift if codegen's bind conditions evolve. Set it once on the node (alongside `is_owned`/`is_ref`) and have both read it -- matches the "first-class AST facts over consumer-side re-derivation" rule + the THIR direction. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the tuple-unpack-view UAF fix.
- **[THIR migration] reach analysis excludes `enum_member_of` by field NAME -- make the exclusion structural before the next stamped type fact.** `sema/reach_analysis.py::_iter_typed_children` walks every `TpyType`-valued node attribute; `TpyFieldAccess.enum_member_of` (the sema-stamped binding fact for `E.A`) is skipped by a hardcoded key check, because surfacing it re-adds the defining module's include past the aliased-re-export dedupe (`tests/cases/imports/package_alias_reexport_enum`). It is the FIRST stamped fact that is an actual `TpyType`; the next one (any binding/codegen fact stamped as a type) will silently get walked and over-include unless someone remembers to extend the skip list. Options: a field-naming convention the walker checks (e.g. a `_binding` suffix), a wrapper marker type, or an explicit per-node allowlist of reach-relevant fields. NB the skip is by KEY over every node's `__dict__`, so the inverse failure exists too: a future unrelated attribute coincidentally named `enum_member_of` gets silently reach-skipped (under-include). Both failure modes surface as visible include diffs in snapshots, not silent miscompiles. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the value-binding frontier.
- **[THIR migration] value-binding hygiene one-liners (spend when the next enum/scalar cell lands).** (a) `_lower_call_arg` carries three boolean context flags (`temp_args`/`readonly_target`/`method_arg`); bundle into a small ArgCtx-style dataclass if a 4th arrives. (b) ~10 gate sites hand-write the `_eligible_scalar(t) or _eligible_enum(t, analyzer) is not None` disjunction -- fold the plain-disjunction sites into one combined value-scalar predicate when the next enum cell (cross-module/@native/nested) re-touches them anyway. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective of the value-binding frontier.
- **[THIR migration] THIR emit duplicates three AST-codegen leaf decisions verbatim (transitional, do NOT extract yet).** `tpyc/thir/emit.py` mirrors the AST path byte-identically by design (dual-mode during migration), duplicating: `_is_elif` source-column elif detection (vs `StatementGenerator._is_elif`), the `div_check`->`div_floor` / `mod_check`->`mod_floor` swap (vs `expressions.py` `_gen_binop`'s `divisor_non_zero` branch), and the comment-emission ordering. These collapse when the AST codegen path retires (THIR becomes the only emitter), so extracting a shared helper now would couple two paths meant to diverge-then-one-dies -- premature. Until then a change to an AST heuristic must mirror into emit.py; the byte-identical corpus run catches a divergence. The `Mirror ...` comments in emit.py are the intentional signposts -- keep them. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the THIR scaffold.
- **[THIR migration] THIR lowering depends on `codegen_cpp/type_resolution`.** `tpyc/thir/lower.py` imports `resolve_stmt_binding_type` from `codegen_cpp/` to capture a local's sema-deduced type onto `THIRVarDecl`. That helper is a sema-query (reads the analyzer, emits no C++) misplaced under codegen, so THIR -- the sema->codegen boundary -- inverts the layering by reaching into the codegen package. When the IR boundary hardens, move `resolve_stmt_binding_type` (and siblings) to `sema/` or `typesys`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the THIR scaffold.
- **[THIR migration] `--thir-codegen` non-vacuity gate's filtered/full heuristic is coarse.** `_thir_gate_verdict` (`tests/conftest.py`) decides fail-vs-warn from `bool(config.option.keyword) or bool(config.option.file_or_dir)`. Two non-canonical invocations mis-verdict: `pytest tests tpyc --thir-codegen` (explicit paths that still cover the configured `testpaths`) reads as a subset and only WARNs on a zero-routing wiring regression instead of failing; `--thir-codegen --collect-only` routes nothing (collection compiles no bodies) yet reads as full and FAILs. The canonical gate is path-less (`uv run pytest --thir-codegen --no-exec`) and verdicts correctly, and no CI consumes the exit code, so impact is low. Tighten by comparing positional paths against `config.getini("testpaths")` (testpaths-covering paths count as full) and exempting `--collect-only`; mind path normalization (`tests` vs `tests/` vs `./tests`, nodeids). Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the non-vacuity guard.
- **[THIR migration] range-`for` slice deferred cells (increment 2).** `tpyc/thir/lower.py`'s `_for_range_eligible` covers only `range(stop)` / `range(start, stop)` step-1 counter loops over a fixed-int loop var not used after the loop. These richer shapes stay on the AST path and are the next breadth for range-`for`: (1) **3-arg / stepped ranges** (the `literal_pos != 1` / `literal_neg` / variable-step branches of `_gen_range_counter_loop`, incl. `range_check_step_nonzero` + `range_check_overflow`); (2) **hoisted loop var** (`hoist_loop_var` -- the `__range_N` hidden-counter + body-assign form); (3) **`for/else`** (the `__after_else_N` label + `orelse` block); (4) **bounds that are binops / calls / `Int32(k)` ctors / negated literals** -- need the byte-identical net to confirm `gen_range_args`' `_gen_expr_deref(arg, ptype)` matches `_emit_expr` with the param-type context (the literal/name shapes are confirmed); (5) **loop var shadowing an outer local** (`was_declared` handling); (6) **non-`_eligible_scalar` element types** (e.g. BigInt, Float32 range -- different overflow/`__step_N`/`f`-suffix handling; the gate is `_eligible_scalar`, which already admits bool/float, so only these stay out). Each is additive in `_for_range_eligible` + `THIRForRange` emit; the byte-identical net (`TPY_THIR_CODEGEN=1`) gates each. Surfaced building THIR increment 2.
- **[THIR migration] expression-statement slice deferred cells (increment 32).** The expr-statement cell (`tpyc/thir/lower.py` `_stmt_eligible` `TpyExprStmt` branch) routes `print(<scalar/str-literal args>)` + bare same-module free-function call statements. Deferred, additive behind the byte-identical net: **(1) docstring-opening bodies** -- a function/method whose body OPENS with a docstring stays fully AST, because the `TpyExprStmt` branch has no docstring carve-out, unlike the ctor M3c-trivia rung which treats `is_docstring(stmt) or TpyPassStmt` as inert `THIRNoOpStmt` trivia (`lower.py` ~2130). Reusing that precedent (a docstring/`pass` expr-statement -> `THIRNoOpStmt`) would route the common `def f(): """doc"""; print(...)` pattern -- a real routing gain, the self-contained tail of this cell. Deferred (not just "cheap, later") because it carries a design fork CLAUDE.md forbids settling autonomously: scope the no-op arm to docstring-position-only (a leading string-literal expr-statement) vs ANY bare string-literal expr-statement -- they differ on a mid-body bare `"str"`. **(2) bare numeric-literal print args** (`print(0)` / `print(1.5)`) -- an `IntLiteralType`/`FloatLiteralType` fails `_eligible_scalar`, so only named/typed scalar exprs route as print args; the literal-arg boundary is intentional (same reason bare-literal locals stay out). **(3) print f-string / container / BigInt args** -- ride the str/BigInt/form rungs. The `sep=`/`end=` kwargs LANDED (wave-2 tails: str-literal + resolved-str-NAME sources, `_print_kwarg_token`); still out there: `file=`/`flush=`, kwargs on an empty arg list, and non-literal/name kwarg sources -- note the emit renders sep-then-end (AST renders end-then-sep; unobservable until a temp-hoisting source is admitted, flagged in `_emit_print`). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness + test-coverage) of the print cell. Drive the docstring carve-out via `/tpy-add-feature`.
- **[consolidation debt] Always fully-qualify local type references in generated C++ -- retire the conditional shadow-qualification (and CLOSE the member/type collision class uniformly).** Today local record/enum/@dynamic-protocol type *references* render bare (`day`) and only qualify (`::tpyapp::mod::day`) inside a record whose member shadows the type -- the `RecordInfo.shadows_local_type` sema fact + `_qualify_shadowed_nominals` flag + per-render-path flag-awareness (`_cpp_base_name` for records/enums, `get_dynamic_base_name` for protocols) + the THIR whole-record reject (`docs/THIR_COMPLETION_LEDGER.md`). This is the "parallelize" side of generalize-vs-parallelize: it added a SECOND qualification toggle beside the existing cross-module one (`native_cpp_names`, which ALREADY always-qualifies), and it needs a NEW flag-aware branch for every shadowable type kind -- proven by the recursive-union-alias-wrapper residual (BUGS.md), a 4th kind whose separate render path (`recursive_alias_cpp_names`) the conditional fix does not cover. Always-qualifying local references (extend the cross-module path to local) would DELETE the shadow fact + flag + all per-path branches + the THIR fallback cell AND close the collision class for every type kind at once -- collision-proof by construction, one uniform rule that subsumes the alias residual. Costs to weigh: whole-corpus snapshot churn; generated-C++ verbosity; it stays "qualify references, not DECLARATIONS" (struct/forward decls + out-of-line-def class name stay bare); and the THIR win needs THIR emit changed to bake qualified names too (coordinated AST+THIR). Verify no emit site breaks under blanket qualification (friend operators / ADL, templates, CRTP). This is the RIGHT closure of the collision class -- prefer it over adding a 5th flag-aware render path. Route via `/tpy-add-feature`. Surfaced by the user + the /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion of the member/type-name collision fix.
- **[THIR migration] F5 generic-record follow-ups (from /tpy-review of the F5 branch).** Three deferred items: **(1)** the "bodyless binding" predicate (`fn.native_function or fn.native_name is not None or fn.cpp_template is not None`) is now spelled in `generator.py`'s `_is_bodyless_binding` AND inline 3x in `codegen_cpp/builtins.py:135,143,152` -- hoist to one shared helper / `TpyFunction` property per [[feedback_prefer_full_unification]] (touches builtins.py, out of the F5 branch's scope). **(2)** scaffold test: a generic derived record inheriting a generic base parameterized by the derived's own type param (`class Derived[T](Base[T])`) -- exercises a `TypeParamRef` base arg, distinct from the concrete/non-slice base args already tested. **(3)** scaffold test: a negative guard for the `T`-field-init reject (`self.value = self.other` where `other: T`) covering the fall-through `return False` in `_ctor_field_init_ok`'s TypeParamRef branch. All low-risk, byte-diff-gated. **Not yet routed generic-record cells** (deferred, byte-identical fallback): a `T` LOCAL decl (`x = self.value`) and `Own[T]` METHOD params (not in `_f1_param_eligible`, so an `Own[T]`-param move-write falls back). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit + test-coverage) of F5.
- **[THIR migration] Extract the repeated type-unwrap prologue in `lower.py` (all sites or none).** `tpyc/thir/lower.py` repeats the `unwrap_readonly(unwrap_ref_type(unwrap_send_sync(t)))` + `if t is None` + `OwnType` reject + `getattr(t, "type_args", None)` prologue inline ~12x across the `_container_*` / `_f1_*` type predicates (e.g. `_container_scalar_read`, `_container_record_iter`). A prior /tpy-review flagged the 2-predicate duplication; the decision (correct) was NOT to extract 2-of-12 against the uniform inline convention. The clean move per [[feedback_prefer_full_unification]] is to extract a single `_unwrap_container_type(t) -> (bare_type, args) | None` helper and route ALL sites through it in one pass -- so a future change to the unwrap/Own-reject chain can't drift between copies. Low-risk (byte-diff-gated, no emit change). Surfaced by /tpy-review + /tpy-ready retrospective of the record-for-each + print cells.
- **[THIR migration] Audit the remaining `_eligible_scalar(analyzer.get_expr_type(...))` gate sites toward `_resolved_scalar`.** Increment 34 added `_resolved_scalar` (`tpyc/thir/lower.py`: readonly/ref unwrap + `resolve_int_literals`) and migrated the ~7 sites the container-literal cell provably needed (method ret/args/slots, subscript results, print args, binop result, for-each elem); ~14 other `_eligible_scalar(get_expr_type(...))` sites remain (return values, assign RHS checks, condition operands, ...) that could equally see an unresolved `IntLiteralType` from a literal-seeded container's use sites. Safe today -- a stale site just returns False and the function conservatively stays on the AST path -- but it drifts: a future gate reaching for `_eligible_scalar` out of habit silently under-routes. NOT a mechanical rename: each conversion WIDENS what routes and needs its own byte-diff scrutiny (the increment-34 no-paren literal-operand binop reject is exactly the kind of emit fork such widening surfaces), so do it as a deliberate audit pass -- classify each site (needs resolution / genuinely literal-free), migrate the needed ones in one batch under the whole-corpus byte-diff. **Trigger: run the audit before opening the F3 form-ladder resumption** so drift doesn't accumulate across more statement-shape cells. Deeper framing (retrospective second opinion): `_resolved_scalar` and `resolve_pending_container` are consumer-side re-derivations of facts sema already decided -- per CLAUDE.md's "prefer first-class type/AST facts over consumer-side dispatch", the audit should also weigh having sema materialize resolved types at use sites (one write in `_resolve_pending_list_types`/finalize) so every future THIR gate stops paying the re-derivation tax. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the container-literal-locals cell. NB: THIR deferral notes of this kind belong in `docs/THIR_COMPLETION_LEDGER.md`'s deferred-cell registry -- migrate the THIR paragraph-entries there when next touching them, keeping TODO.md a priorities list.
- **[THIR migration] `THIRName.result_type` carries the analyzer seed type, not the resolved type.** `nodes.py` documents `THIRExpr.result_type` as "the fully-resolved type", but `_lower_expr` sets a name's `result_type = analyzer.get_expr_type(e)`, which for a retro-widened literal-seeded local (`offset = 0` later used as `UInt64`) is the pre-widen seed (`Int32`). Harmless today -- `emit.py` never reads `result_type` on a `THIRName`, and the mixed-sign comparison gate (increment 5) resolves operand types from the lowering `declared` map instead. Fix when name nodes need accurate types (MIR / the form design): thread the `declared` (name -> resolved type) map into `_lower_expr` so a `THIRName` gets `declared.get(name)`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the THIR scalar-breadth batch.
- **[THIR migration] truediv (`/`) eligibility decision -- the `/` in `_ARITH_OPS` is dead.** The parser emits true-division as `TpyBinOp` op `div`, not `/`, so the `/` token in `tpyc/thir/lower.py`'s `_ARITH_OPS` never matches; `_binop_eligible` rejects truediv via the op-name miss, NOT a missing cpp_template (float and int truediv both carry `::tpy::truediv({self}, {0})`). Float increment 3 exposed this: pre-float, truediv's float result was rejected by `_eligible_scalar`; now only the op miss keeps it out. Decide: (a) **enable** truediv -- replace `/` with `div` in `_ARITH_OPS`, byte-verify via the net (float `a / b` emits `(::tpy::truediv(a,b))` identically per probe; ALSO verify int `a / b` -> float and any div-by-zero handling), or (b) **drop** the dead `/` token. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective (the float-`/` exclusion comment had the wrong reason).
- **[THIR migration] Unify the mixed-sign comparison predicate.** `tpyc/thir/lower.py`'s `_mixed_sign_compare` reproduces `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`'s `_mixed_sign_fixed_int` (both over the shared `int_traits_of`). They reach the same verdict by different literal-handling -- codegen has an `IntLiteralType` skip clause (`expressions.py` ~2131), while the THIR side resolves operand types first (`_operand_type`), so literals never reach the predicate. Net-gated today, but the divergent reasoning bites if either side's literal handling shifts. Hoist one predicate into the type layer (`type_def_registry`, where `int_traits_of` already lives) and have both phases call it. Surfaced by /tpy-review meta + /tpy-ready retrospective.
- **[THIR migration] The fallback-purity invariant is load-bearing and unguarded.** Generate-and-assert rests on lowering being side-effect-free w.r.t. shared analyzer/codegen state: when a body raises `ThirUnsupported` partway through, the partial attempt is discarded and the AST path re-emits that body. This holds today (`_LowerCtx`/`declared` are per-attempt; `movable_locals` is copied at `tpyc/thir/lower/context.py`; lowering only reads analyzer state), but nothing enforces it -- a future lowering arm that writes back into an analyzer set would corrupt the AST oracle on every fallback, with no test to catch it (fallback emits byte-identical AST, so the corruption surfaces as an unrelated miscompile elsewhere). Add an explicit invariant note at the three `except ThirUnsupported` boundaries (`lower_function` / `lower_constructor` / `lower_resumable`) and a guard test that lowers a body which rejects mid-way and asserts the analyzer state is unchanged. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion of the gate removal.
- **[THIR migration] Defense-in-depth test: a later match arm / with-body / try-body falls back.** The sequential-lowering commits replaced the batch preflight walk over arm/handler bodies with `_lower_stmts`, which raises on the first unroutable statement after earlier ones already lowered. The mechanism is shared with flat bodies and covered by `test_sync_lowering_reports_first_reject_in_source_order` + its ctor twin (`tpyc/thir/test_fallback.py`), so this is a guard against a future local-catch regression, not a coverage hole -- optional. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the gate removal.
- **[THIR migration] Positive-routing unit tests for the arg call sites that dropped their `_expr_eligible` conjunct.** The `_expr_eligible` removal widened admission at ~60 call sites; only two (`str()` scalar-ctor result, record-ctor comprehension element) got a matching positive-routing unit-test flip. The sites most likely to admit a genuinely NEW shape rather than defer an already-redundant check -- `_float_pass_through_arg` / `_record_pass_through_arg` / `_instantiation_call_eligible` -- rely solely on the whole-corpus byte-diff. Add positive-routing unit tests analogous to the two flipped, so a widened-but-mis-lowered shape at those sites is pinned at unit level, not only by the corpus diff. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the gate removal.
- **[THIR migration] form rung F1/F2 deferred cells (F2 remainder finished 2026-06).** F1/F2 (`tpyc/thir/lower.py` + `emit.py`, the `_f1_*`/`_f2*`/`_borrow_local_binding`/`_is_record_rvalue_source`/`_is_move_source` helpers + `THIRFormConvert`/`THIRFieldAccess`/`forms.LocalBinding`) now admit, byte-identical in the free-function slice: single-assignment non-value **record** locals (`T&` alias / `optional_to_ptr`), **reseatable `T*` pointer-locals** -- lvalue field reseat `&(...)` (F2a) and the **rvalue `__slot_N` rebind machinery** (F2d, `T __slot_n = init; std::optional<T> __slot_{n+1}; T* p = &__slot_n;`, reseat `p = &*(__slot_{n+1} = rvalue)`) -- `->` reads off never-null pointer-locals, scalar field reads off F1-record receivers, F1-record reference params, the **Optional borrow<->storage write** (`recv.field = ptr_to_optional[_move](p)`, copy F2b + move F2e), the **storage-Optional return** (`Own[T]|None`: `return ptr_to_optional[_move](p)`, F2c/F2e), and **`None`** write/return (`std::nullopt`, F2c -- the literal's `form` selects nullopt vs nullptr). Still deferred to the AST path, additive behind the byte-identical net (`uv run pytest --thir-codegen --no-exec`): **(A) ctor member-init-list `ptr_to_optional` -- FRONTIER, not a form cell (M3; method frontier M1 landed 2026-06):** the MIL is emitted by the record driver (`records.py` `_extract_field_inits` -> the ` : Field(val)` list), structurally outside `gen_body` where the THIR dispatch lives, and only for constructors. M1 landed the method prereqs; **M3a (increment 14) then landed the ctor scaffolding** -- the whole-ctor `THIRConstructor` node (MIL split from body), the `emit_thir_constructor_tail` emitter, and the ctor feed (`iter_module_constructors` -> `lower_constructor` -> `ctx.thir_constructors`, the `gen_record_decl` tail seam) -- for the pure-MIL **scalar** slice. This cell (the `opt(::tpy::ptr_to_optional(p))` MIL line) is **CLOSED by M3b-copy** (increment 15): a pointer-repr `Optional[F1-record]` field from a non-own `Optional[record]` borrow param lifts via `ptr_to_optional` in MIL position, reusing the F2b conversion -- only the MIL context was gated, now opened. Remaining ctor form work (own-param `std::move`, ctor-call rvalue sources, the hoist/demotion mirror, base inits) is M3b-move / M3b-rvalue / M3c / M3d (see the method-frontier cell). Orthogonal to the F1->F-final type-family ladder. **(B) call-arg cascade -- LANDED (increments 56-65; tracked in `docs/THIR_COMPLETION_LEDGER.md`'s call-arg row):** the `gen_call_arg` cascade is mirrored -- arg temps (`THIRArgTemp` + the TempSink seam), Own copy+move (`THIRMove`), the pointer-repr Optional faces (`THIROptionalPtrArg`, incl. narrowed subjects), union lifts, method/self-receiver rows. The remaining sub-rows (the **call** (`T|None`-returning) / **`copy()`-acknowledged** optional-field write sources, `Own[T]|None` arg slots, coerce-wrapped lvalues, protocol slots) live in the ledger's deferred list, not here. **(C) container locals** (list/dict/set) -- needs `_normalize_decl_type_for_cpp`'s pending/int-literal resolution in the decl-type render (only bare records admitted via `type_to_cpp` today); rides F3 with tuples. **(D) cross-module / `@native` / generic records** (`_f1_record` excludes them -- `to_cpp()` qualification, native field rename, generic-arg recursion differ from the bare render). **(E) subscript sources** (`xs[i]` storage reads -- `is_storage_form_optional_source` covers them, the gate just needs the receiver/bounds shape). **(F) `->` reads needing narrowing** -- a field read off an OPTIONAL_TO_PTR (nullable) pointer-local needs a deref check (the never-null pointer-local `->` read landed in F2a). **(G) name-alias / REF_ALIAS-source write/reseat sources** -- a pointer-local reseat from another borrow NAME (`x = y`) and an optional-field write/return whose source is a `T&` REF_ALIAS local: `_f2_reseat_ok` requires a field source, `_is_record_rvalue_source` a ctor/call, and `_is_borrow_ptr_local` excludes REF_ALIAS (`T&`, not a pointer), so all stay on the AST path. Tuple form (per-element pointer/optional mask) is F3, union form (`to_ptr_variant`) F4, generic-slot form F5 per the `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` ladder. Surfaced building THIR form rungs F1-F2.
- **[THIR migration] method frontier deferred cells (M1 landed 2026-06).** M1 (`tpyc/thir/lower.py`: `_function_eligible(self_type=...)`, `iter_module_callables`, `_method_self_type`, the `self_receiver`/`THIRSelf` model) admits **plain instance methods** of same-module non-generic records, byte-identical, routing F1/F2 over real corpus code (1313 bodies / 510 cases under `--thir-codegen`). Restricted to **value-scalar params** and excludes the constructor. Deferred, additive behind the byte-identical net (`uv run pytest --thir-codegen --no-exec`): **(M2) record params on methods** -- DONE (increment 12): `_param_is_const` gained a `record_name` arg and looks up the method's `const_borrow_params` on the owning record (`get_record(name).get_method_overloads(...)`), threaded via `_LowerCtx.record_name`; `_function_eligible` admits F1-record params on methods uniformly (`_f1_param_eligible`). No readonly carve-out -- for a plain F1-record (ref) param the readonly forced-const and inferred-const verdicts coincide (`decide_param_const`), so `const_borrow_params` is exact; a carve-out would re-exclude the common auto-readonly getter (verified: carve-out -> +0 corpus bodies). Routing 2835 -> 3163 bodies / 534 -> 576 cases. **Readonly free functions** -- DONE (increment 13): removed the `is_readonly and not is_instance_method` exclusion; the forced/inferred const verdicts coincide for free functions too (a readonly callable cannot mutate a param, so its record params are uniformly const), byte-identical. Small gain (3163 -> 3165 bodies / 576 -> 577 cases) -- most readonly free functions stay AST-side for other reasons (native, generic, non-F1 params, calls in body) -- but it clears the last readonly asymmetry in the gate. **(M3a) pure-MIL scalar constructors** -- DONE (increment 14): a whole-ctor `THIRConstructor` (member-init-list split from body, `tpyc/thir/nodes.py`), a dedicated MIL-tail emitter (`emit_thir_constructor_tail`), and a separate ctor feed (`iter_module_constructors` -> `lower_constructor` -> `ctx.thir_constructors`, populated in `generator.py`; the seam is the `gen_record_decl` tail branch in `records.py`). Slice: flat (no-base) same-module non-generic record whose `__init__` is entirely hoistable own-scalar field inits, so every init hoists to the MIL and the body is `{}` (no demotion mirror -- a non-init ctor body is <1% of corpus ctors, the M3c rung). The signature stays AST (M1 precedent). Docstring/`pass` ctors are rejected (they make non_init_stmts non-empty -> a `{\n ... }` body, M3c). Routing 3165 -> 4652 bodies / 577 -> 1160 cases (+1487 ctors; the non-vacuity tally in `compiler.py` now counts ctors). **Remaining ctor rungs, all on the path to deleting `_extract_field_inits` / `_extract_base_inits` / `_get_non_init_stmts` + the inline MIL emit -- that deletion (corpus still byte-identical) is the ctor-frontier completeness gate: (M3b-copy)** record / `Optional[record]` MIL fields, copy arm -- DONE (increment 15): a pointer-repr `Optional[F1-record]` field routes the F2b optional-write helper (`None` -> `std::nullopt`; a non-own borrow source -> `ptr_to_optional`, the cell (A) above, now closed) and a plain `F1-record` field admits a non-own record param (`copy()`-unwrapped via `_unwrap_copy`) as an implicit MIL copy; the ctor param gate gained pointer-repr `Optional[F1-record]` (`_ctor_param_eligible`), the per-field MIL value is built by `_lower_ctor_mil_init` (`THIRFormConvert` STORAGE / STORAGE `None` literal / plain copy). Reuses F2b/F2c, no new emit node. Routing 4652 -> 4691 bodies / 1160 -> 1169 cases (+39 -- most record-field ctors take `Own` params, so land in M3b-move). Remaining: **(M3b-move)** own-param `std::move` sources -- DONE (increment 16): an `Own[F1-record]` param at its last use moves into a record / Optional[record] field (`f(std::move(p))`, the common ownership ctor). THIR's first `std::move` emit -- a `move` flag on `THIRMilInit`; `_is_move_source` generalized to take a `movable_names` set (mirroring the AST's parameterized `_is_last_use_movable`), the ctor MIL passing `own_param_names`. The move arm never combines with `ptr_to_optional`: lowering checks move first and emits a plain source before the Optional arm (matching the AST cascade, where the AST's `source_is_own_optional` skips `ptr_to_optional` for any own param). Routing 4691 -> 4703 bodies / 1169 -> 1172 cases (+12 -- most `Own`-param records are entangled with deferred features: a non-init body, base class, generic record, or non-F1 field). NB `THIRConstructor.params` / `record_name` are unread by the tail-only emitter (own_param_names is derived from `init_method.params` at lowering); KEPT as faithful-IR fields (a ctor has a name + params) for a future signature-emit increment -- decision recorded on the node docstring. **(M3b-rvalue)** ctor-call rvalue / param-field-read sources + own-optional params + the Optional `copy()`-source asymmetry -- DONE (increment 17): a new `_is_record_value_source` admits a non-own F1-record param name, an F1-record ctor-call rvalue (`self.rec = Inner(v)`, reusing `_is_record_rvalue_source`), and an F1-record field-read off a *param* receiver (`self.rec = b.inner`); for an `Optional[F1-record]` field these construct the optional directly (`opt(Inner(v))`) -- `ptr_to_optional` is reserved for borrow-`T*` sources, so `_lower_ctor_mil_init` splits the Optional arm on `_is_borrow_ptr_local`. `_ctor_param_eligible` gained the own-optional shapes (`Own[Inner|None]` / `Optional[Own[Inner]]` via `unwrap_optional_own`), which move into the Optional field through the existing move arm (`opt(std::move(m))`). `copy()` is unwrapped before the Optional check, so `self.opt = copy(m)` routes like the record arm (`opt(::tpy::ptr_to_optional(m))`). Deferred (self-contained): `self.<record field>` read sources (the pointee may be uninitialized at MIL time -- ordering-sensitive; `test_self_field_record_read_source_is_ineligible` pins the boundary). Routing 4703 -> 4739 bodies / 1172 -> 1182 cases (+36). **(M3c-trivia)** docstring / `pass` non-init bodies -- DONE (increment 18): the first non-empty ctor body. Docstring / `pass` emit NO code (a docstring's `gen_body` code is None, `pass` is `""`), so they break no hoist chain -- every field init still hoists -- but their presence keeps the body non-empty (` {\n    }`, not ` {}`). New `THIRNoOpStmt` (THIR's first body-statement shape); `lower_constructor` collects them into `THIRConstructor.body` in source order without breaking the walk. The comment asymmetry is byte-exact: `pass` keeps its `loc` so `_emit_stmts` emits its `// pass` source comment, a docstring lowers with `loc=None` (the AST emits NO comment for a docstring). The emitter's `if ctor.body:` brace branch was pre-built in M3a. Routing 4739 -> 5030 bodies / 1182 -> 1210 cases (+291 -- docstring/`pass` ctors are common). **(M3c-demotion)** the non-trivia body + the hoist/demotion split -- DONE (increment 19): a field init that can't hoist demotes into the body alongside the non-init statements, lowered via the shared `_body_eligible` / `_lower_stmt` machinery (the same path method bodies use). KEY: studying `_extract_field_inits` showed THIR's existing `_ctor_field_init_ok` gate (declared = params + self) already subsumes the bare-name / body-local / nested-def / temp-rollback demote triggers (a field init whose source isn't resolvable from params isn't hoistable -> naturally demotes), and `_body_eligible` rejects any demoted init it can't lower (whole ctor -> AST, byte-safe); so only the **`chain_broken` ordering cascade** needed explicit reproduction (a hoistable init after a non-init statement must demote -- the MIL runs before the body). `lower_constructor` splits the body into (hoisted MIL inits, body stmts) + `_lower_ctor_body_stmt`; no new node/emit. Tiny routing gain (5030 -> 5031 bodies / 1210 -> 1211 cases, +1 -- body-eligible demotion is <1% of corpus ctors, so 5 hand-written units per behavior are the real validation). **Deferred (self-contained):** demoted **record**-field writes (not body-eligible -- `_stmt_eligible` admits only scalar / Optional field writes), and non-trivia body statements outside the statement-shape slice (match / with / try / for-container / method calls / ...; print + bare free-function calls now route, incr 32). **(M3d-1)** single-base inheritance -- DONE (increment 20): a single same-module F1 base routes; `super().__init__(args)` lowers to a structured `THIRBaseInit` (base C++ name via `super_parent_type.to_cpp()` + lowered eligible-scalar arg exprs, the placeholder `base_inits: tuple[str]` becoming `tuple[THIRBaseInit]` rendered at emit) prepended to the field MIL, mirroring `_extract_base_inits`. The base-init breaks no hoist chain; the body reuses M3c-demotion; the derived signature stays AST (identical to a flat one, so the `gen_record_decl` tail seam works unchanged). KEY guard: an inherited-field write (`self.<base field> = expr`) goes to the body WITHOUT breaking the chain in the AST (the base ctor owns the MIL slot), which the M3c-demotion chain logic can't reproduce -> `_is_self_nonown_field_assign` rejects any such ctor (plus multi-base, the explicit `BaseN.__init__` form, non-F1 bases) to AST. Routing 5031 -> 5057 bodies / 1211 -> 1216 cases (+26 -- single-base ctors are common). **(M3d-2)** multi-base + inherited-field writes -- DONE (increment 21): `_lower_base_inits` collects every `super().__init__` / `BaseN.__init__` call (`super_parent_type or unbound_self_parent_type`, `self` already stripped by sema) and sorts by parent declaration order (mirroring `_extract_base_inits`'s -Wreorder-safe ordering); the single-base gate relaxes to "every parent is a same-module F1 record." A direct inherited-field write (`self.<base field> = expr`) goes to the body via `_is_self_nonown_field_assign` (the base ctor owns the MIL slot, breaks no chain) + is tracked in `body_written_self_fields` so a later own-field hoist reading it demotes (`expr_reads_self_field`), completing the `_extract_field_inits` walk; the M3d-1 reject-guard became that body branch. `_reject_nondef_ctor_field_in_body` is not reproduced -- it fires only for a demoted own non-default-constructible (record-typed) field, whose body-write is not body-eligible, so the ctor rejects first. Routing 5057 -> 5071 bodies (+14, same 1216 cases). **The ctor frontier (M3a-M3d) is COMPLETE.** Remaining ctor cells are cross-axis-blocked (not self-contained): `str`/`list`/`dict`/`tuple`/`union` MIL fields ride the form ladder (F3+); demoted record-field writes need the record body-write rung; generic/native records ride their frontiers. **THIR hygiene (non-blocking, from /tpy-review):** `_lower_stmt`'s `TpyAssign`+`TpyFieldAccess` else-branch builds a `THIRFormConvert(STORAGE)` for any non-scalar non-None field write -- `_emit_form_convert` only handles `OptionalType` in the STORAGE direction, so a plain-record field write would crash at emit. Unreachable today (`_body_eligible` rejects record-field writes), but if the eligible-field-write set ever grows, add an `assert isinstance(ftype, OptionalType)` before that node so the invariant is self-documenting and a gate gap fails as an AssertionError, not a cryptic emit crash. **scalar field writes** (`recv.field = <scalar>`) -- DONE (increment 10, `_scalar_field_write_ok`): a value-scalar field off any F1-record receiver lowers to a plain `THIRAssign`. **augmented assignment** (`recv.field += x` / `x += y`) -- DONE (increment 11, `_scalar_aug_assign_ok`): lowers to `target = (target OP value)` via `THIRBinOp` (+ a `paren_wrap` flag for the AST aug-assign's no-paren RHS), gating out in-place dunders / `str +=` / `FixedInt += BigInt` / subscript / class-constant targets. NB the `FixedInt += BigInt` gate became reachable when increment 75 admitted BigInt to `_eligible_scalar`; it is covered by `test_fixed_target_aug_bigint_value_stays_ast` (tpyc/thir/test_thir_bigint.py). The non-template `resolved_binop` gate stays defensive-only. **static / property / dunder-operator methods** -- DONE (increment 48); **generic-record methods** (templated `self`) -- DONE (F5 stages A-C, see `docs/THIR_COMPLETION_LEDGER.md`; residual: `T` local decls + `Own[T]` method params); **nested-record methods** still deferred (`iter_module_callables` walks top-level `module.records` only -- nested-record methods stay on the AST path, safe-by-omission). Surfaced building the THIR method frontier (M1).
- **[THIR migration] method frontier reuses the `_f1_is_const` / `_is_move_source` reproduce-mirrors unchanged (corpus net now restored).** M1 routes the F1/F2 mirror helpers (`_f1_is_const` vs `_is_const_indirect`, `_is_move_source` vs `_is_last_use_movable`; TODO entries above) over real corpus method bodies, so they are now corpus-validated, not just unit-tested -- the better moment to hoist them into shared analyzer-pure helpers (per "Hoist reproduce-style mirrors into shared helpers per-rung, NOT at F-final" above). M1 deliberately reused them as-is (routing, not refactoring); the extraction is a follow-on, lower-risk now that the net catches drift. Surfaced building the THIR method frontier (M1).
- **[THIR migration] `_f1_is_const` mirrors `_is_const_indirect` (transitional).** `tpyc/thir/lower.py`'s `_f1_is_const` re-derives the field-source subset of `StatementGenerator._is_const_indirect` (the ReadonlyType reads on the optional inner / the init's raw sema type / the var_types entry, plus the storage-optional const bump) and uses a `const_locals` analog of the walk-built `const_indirect_locals`. Documented mirror needing manual lockstep if `_is_const_indirect` grows a new branch applicable to F1 field sources -- lowering can't call the ctx-bound method directly. Same transitional-mirror family as the emit.py increment-mirror entries above; dissolves when the AST form-codegen retires (F-final) or `_is_const_indirect` is hoisted to a shared analyzer-pure helper. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of F1.
- **[THIR migration] Hoist reproduce-style mirrors into shared helpers per-rung, NOT at F-final.** The byte-identity-to-AST contract is the oracle that validates THIR -- and it is DELETED at F-final (when the AST form-codegen retires). So the *reproduce*-style mirrors (`_f1_is_const` vs `_is_const_indirect`; the F2b `_gen_assign` optional-field-write branch; `_is_move_source` vs `_is_last_use_movable` -- F2e, with the extra sema-set-vs-walk-built-`movable_locals` equivalence to re-audit on each slice expansion (M3b-move generalized `_is_move_source` to take a `movable_names` param mirroring the AST helper, the ctor MIL passing `own_param_names` -- so the two now share that shape and must move in lockstep); `_unwrap_copy` vs `ctx.unwrap_copy` (a NEW mirror added by M3b-copy; since lowering is analyzer-pure the clean extract is a shared free `unwrap_copy(expr, imported_names)` both delegate to, which touches the AST-side `context.py` -- a "born-shared vs mirror-then-extract" choice for the extraction pass); `_mixed_sign_compare` vs `_mixed_sign_fixed_int`; `_positional_only_template` vs `expand_cpp_template`'s brace grammar (incr 37 -- a gate-side re-parse, so a grammar miss only under-routes or fails loudly, never mis-emits); `_str_name_form`/`_resolved_str_value` vs `_is_str_view_source`/`_resolve_view_storage` (incr 38 -- EMIT-load-bearing: the name-form tag drives the owned-sink `std::string(x)` copy, so this pair is in the high-risk mirror class; when the next str owned sink lands, also fold the twice-spelled decl-init/return wrap condition into one `_wrap_view_to_owned` helper); the emit.py increment-mirrors incl. the F2d two-slot `__slot_N` emit vs `_gen_pointer_local_init`) do NOT "die" at F-final -- they become the canonical and only implementation exactly when their validating diff vanishes, baking in permanently any subtly-wrong behavior that neither a unit test nor an organic corpus case exercised. Contrast the *extract* pattern (`classify_local_binding`, `resolve_stmt_binding_type` -- both paths call one helper, identical by construction), which carries no such risk. Action: prefer extract over reproduce-mirror for each new sema->codegen fact, and hoist each existing mirror into a shared analyzer-pure helper as part of the rung that needs it, so reconciliation is incremental-and-oracle-checked rather than a big-bang at F-final after the net is gone. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion of F2.
- **[THIR migration] `--thir-codegen` gate exercises zero F2-rung shapes; non-vacuity tally is global.** After dropping the F2 corpus exhibits (redundant with the `TestF2*` unit tests), no corpus case routes an F2 shape (reassigned `T*` pointer-local / Optional borrow->storage write) through THIR -- the existing `&(...)`/`ptr_to_optional` emissions all sit in async/method/subscript/init contexts the gate excludes. So the whole-corpus byte-diff gate's F2 coverage is zero, and the non-vacuity guard (`_thir_gate_verdict`, bodies>0) cannot detect a rung silently ceasing to route (F1/scalar bodies keep the count >0). The unit tests are the actual F2 safety net (they assert routing AND byte-identity). This held for the **F2 remainder** (F2c return/`None`, F2d rvalue `__slot_N`, F2e `_move`): all route zero corpus cases (the corpus tally stayed 172/131), so each rung's `TestF2{c,d,e}*` unit tests are its only net -- the decision is **unit tests, not per-rung exhibits** (consistent with the dropped F2a/b exhibits). Durable fix still open: a per-rung routed-shape tally in the gate that fails if a previously-routing rung drops to zero -- which needs at least one minimal exhibit per rung to tally against, trading against suite size. Revisit if a future rung routes corpus cases (which would make the global tally meaningful again) or if rung-silent-drop bites. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion of F2.
- **[THIR migration] `THIRAssign.target` is a bare `THIRExpr` (no lvalue guarantee) -- weigh a Place-precursor for F3/F4.** F2b widened `THIRAssign.target` from `THIRName` to `THIRExpr` to carry the optional-field-write target (`recv.field` / `recv->field`), chosen on byte-identity grounds (emit renders both via `_emit_expr`). It is looser than MIR's `Place(Local/Field/Index)` and gives no structural guarantee the target is an lvalue. F3 tuple-unpack and subscript-write targets will pile onto the bare-expr target; revisit a thin lvalue-shaped target node (a Place-precursor) when those land, rather than accreting more shapes onto a bare expr. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion of F2.
- **[THIR migration] F3 storage-tuple alias decision is reproduce-mirrored, not extracted.** `forms.is_storage_tuple_alias_decl` (the `auto&& t = <storage tuple field>` arm) is co-located with the binding classifiers as the shared home, but the AST path (`statements.py` `_gen_var_decl_code` ~2157-2186, the `storage_form_tuple_locals` `auto&&` return) still decides this arm inline -- so two implementations co-exist, with the whole-corpus byte-diff as the only anti-drift net. Extending the THIR subset (Subscript / storage-form-Name alias sources) requires updating BOTH the predicate and the inline AST arm in lockstep. Per the "prefer extract over reproduce-mirror" rule (above), the clean fix is to refactor the AST arm to call `is_storage_tuple_alias_decl` (a verified zero-diff extract-method, like `classify_local_binding`); fold it in when the next F3 alias-source cell lands, or at F-final. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of F3.
- **Nullable static-protocol param in a ctor member-init field initializer -- test-coverage gap.** `seed_param_locals` now seeds `pointer_locals`/`const_indirect_locals` for a nullable static-protocol ctor param (e.g. `Optional[Sized]`) in the member-init-list context, but no test exercises a MIL-eligible ternary field-init on one (`self.count = len(items) if items is not None else 0`); existing protocol-optional-param ctors branch via `if/else` in the body, which is body-emitted not MIL-hoisted. Same `pointer_locals` deref mechanism the optional-ptr/union cases cover (`records/ctor_field_init_*`), distinct type path. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the ctor-MIL-deref fix. `seed_param_locals` now seeds `pointer_locals`/`const_indirect_locals` for a nullable static-protocol ctor param (e.g. `Optional[Sized]`) in the member-init-list context, but no test exercises a MIL-eligible ternary field-init on one (`self.count = len(items) if items is not None else 0`); existing protocol-optional-param ctors branch via `if/else` in the body, which is body-emitted not MIL-hoisted. Same `pointer_locals` deref mechanism the optional-ptr/union cases cover (`records/ctor_field_init_*`), distinct type path. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the ctor-MIL-deref fix.
- **`raise E(own_arg)` Own[T]-move -- test-coverage gap.** The raise path now shares the bare-name ctor arg-lowering loop, so the protocol / covariant / optional-ptr / union / mutated-temp arms are covered from a raise site (`exceptions/raise_ctor_*`). Still untested: a `raise E(own_arg)` where `__init__` takes an `Own[T]` param -- the move-at-last-use lives in the `gen_call_arg` fallback, not a dispatch arm, so it's a separate cell; make `T` `@nocopy` so a silent copy is a build error, cover both throw and return tier. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the raise-arg coercion fix.
- **`unsafe_interior_mutable` fields -- deferred scope (v1 shipped Ptr-only, field-level).** The interior-mutable field marker (`unsafe_interior_mutable[Ptr[T]]`, dual of `readonly`; landed for `Rc`/`Weak` refcount cells so `clone`/`downgrade`/`upgrade` are readonly-callable and yield readonly-payload clones) deliberately ships a subset: (a) **value-field interior** -- `unsafe_interior_mutable[T]` on a non-pointer field (a memoized/cached value mutated through a readonly getter) is rejected today; supporting it needs codegen to emit the C++ `mutable` keyword on the member (the Ptr case needs no codegen change since const doesn't cross a raw pointer). (b) **type-level interior metadata** -- instead of a per-field marker, declare a *type* as interior-mutable (e.g. `@unsafe_interior_mutable` on `_RcCellBase`) via library-declared `TypeDef` metadata, so every `Ptr[InteriorType]` field inherits it with zero per-field annotation (more CLAUDE.md-aligned, but broadens the hatch to every appearance of the type -- weigh against the field-level opt-in's auditability). Both are `/tpy-add-feature`-sized. (c) **reject `unsafe_interior_mutable[Ptr[readonly[T]]]`** -- the validation accepts a `Ptr` to a readonly pointee, but `unsafe_interior_mutable` on a const-pointer field is meaningless (the pointee is already inside the readonly boundary); add a reject branch + an `error_` case. Surfaced building the interior-mutable readonly-`Rc.clone` feature.
- **Zero-copy `json.dumps` over typed container shapes -- BLOCKED on protocol-conformance coverage.** Goal: dumping a *typed* container variable (`d: dict[str, Int32]`) serializes in place with no `JsonValue` wrapper (the current behavior is a clean rejection -- `error_json_dumps_concrete_container` -- after the implicit `to_union` deep-copy was reverted as a footgun). The clean way is a pure-stdlib type-directed serializer (a `ToJson` protocol + `dumps[T: ToJson](obj: T)`), but that needs built-in/container types to conform to a protocol, which TPy can't express today -- see "Protocol conformance for built-in and generic-container types" under Open feature gaps. A json-specific C++ writer + compiler routing was considered and rejected: it just duplicates the same compiler coupling we want to remove from `print`. So this stays blocked on that foundational capability; keep the clean rejection until then. Surfaced designing zero-copy json.dumps (reverted `to_union`).
- **`PostSemaFunctionMacroContext.note_param_mutated` is shaped for the single-overload free-function host.** Function macros only run on free functions today, so the method handles exactly that case -- but three assumptions become wrong once function macros gain method-body or multi-overload hosts (docs/MACRO_DESIGN.md lists method-body macros as not-yet-built): (a) it writes `direct_mutated_params` on `registry.get_function(name)[-1]`, the last overload by position -- wrong target for a multi-overload host; (b) the no-FunctionInfo guard is defensive and untested because it is unreachable through supported usage today (only the out-of-range guard has an `error_*` case); (c) the docstring's "over-report only pessimizes `const&`->`&`" understates impact -- for a *method* host, seeding `direct_mutated_params` can flip Phase-2 `is_readonly` inference and structural-protocol conformance, not just local const-ness. Revisit overload targeting, the readonly/conformance interaction, and the no-FunctionInfo coverage when that host surface lands. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective of the post-sema note_param_mutated addition.
- **Unify the two empty-literal/generic-constructor annotation blocks (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py` ~457 and `tpyc/sema/statements.py` ~3430).** The empty-`[]`-against-a-hint logic is duplicated near-verbatim across the call-arg/hint path (`analyze_expr_with_hint`) and the annotated-assignment path (`x: T = []`): both compute `hint_matches`/`annotation_matches`, both build the `ListLiteralInfo` with `has_explicit_annotation`/`explicit_type`, and both now carry the same recursive-union list-member branch (`needs_wrapper()` + `_find_list_member`). They must be kept in lockstep -- the union branch was added to expressions.py first and the statements.py copy was the source of a half-fixed bug (`x: JsonValue = []` rejected while `json.dumps([])` worked). Fold into one shared helper (statements.py could delegate to the expression path, or both call a common `_resolve_empty_literal_against(target)`). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the empty-list recursive-union fix.
- **Coordinate the two "generic container vs recursive union" dispatches in `compatibility._check_compat`.** The concrete-std-container rejection guard and `_check_union_member_type_args` (immediately below it) share the same outer condition (same-name `NominalType`, differing `type_args`) and are non-overlapping only by construction (the guard requires a std container + a `needs_wrapper()` union element + `a_res != e_res`; the other handles the accept path). Any future change to either has to know about the other. Consider folding the std-container deep-conversion check into `_check_union_member_type_args` (or a shared classifier) so the accept/reject decision lives in one place. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the cross-module recursive-union literal fix.
- **Unify non-generic recursive-union (`AliasRef`) C++ rendering onto the qname-keyed map.** The non-generic path renders via the short-name `native_cpp_names` channel (`AliasRef.to_cpp` + the imported-recursive-union registration in `generator.py`), which is collision-prone when two different modules export same-short-named recursive unions both reached cross-module without import -- mitigated today by a first-write-wins skip-guard + the documented same-short-name limitation. The generic path (`RecursiveAliasInstanceType`) is already collision-proof via the qname-keyed `recursive_alias_cpp_names` map. Clean end-state: route non-generic `AliasRef` rendering through the same qname-keyed map (key `{module}.{name}`), retiring the short-name channel for recursive-union wrappers.
