Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective of the cross-module recursive-union literal fix.
- **[LOW perf] str/bytes view->owned wrap is redundant in direct-init contexts.** The shared `_view_source_to_owned` chokepoint wraps a view source uniformly (`std::string(view)` / `::tpy::bytes_copy(view)`) because it can't see its consuming context. In a *direct-initialization* context -- a tuple literal `std::tuple<std::string,...>{view, ...}` -- the str element's explicit `string_view` ctor is already reachable, so the old `{tag, ...}` built the element IN PLACE; the wrap now materializes a temporary + move-constructs it (`{std::string(tag), ...}`), adding one cheap `std::string` move (buffer steal, no extra allocation/char-copy). Function-arg sinks (`push_back`, etc.) genuinely NEED the wrap (the explicit ctor doesn't fire on implicit conversion -- that was the original build failure). Skipping the wrap in tuple-literal str contexts would reintroduce a str-vs-bytes asymmetry (bytes still needs it: `std::vector` has no ctor from `span`), so a clean fix is context-aware (the chokepoint, or its callers, knowing direct-init vs copy-init). Negligible; only worth it if tuple-of-str-from-view turns up on a hot path. Surfaced reviewing the view->owned unification snapshots.
- **[LOW codegen readability] A bytes literal coerced to owned bytes/bytearray still renders the nested `::tpy::bytes_copy(::tpy::bytes_literal("...", N))`.** The storage-form literal path now emits the single `::tpy::bytes_literal_owned(...)`, but a bytes literal reaching an owned slot via a COERCION (`bytesview_to_bytes` / `bytesview_to_bytearray` in `tpyc/coercions.py`) -- notably `bytearray(b"...")` -- still gets the nested form: the literal renders as a view (`bytes_literal(...)` span) and the coercion lambda, which only sees the already-rendered string, wraps it in `bytes_copy(...)`. Correct, just verbose. Clean fix: render a bytes-literal source whose target is an owned bytes/bytearray slot directly via `cpp_bytes_literal_owned` (so no view->owned coercion fires) -- a sema/coercion-insertion change, NOT a codegen-helper swap, and NOT a string-sniff of the coercion arg. Surfaced finishing the readable-bytes-literal work (~8 snapshots still show the nested form, all `bytearray(b"...")`). Needs `/tpy-add-feature`.
- **[LOW] Fold the str/bytes view->owned-into-Optional-slot decision into the one chokepoint.** The view->owned unification routed three sites through `_view_source_to_owned`, but a FOURTH decider remains: `_maybe_convert_opt_str_param` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:486`, called at :1318) handles an `Optional[str]` param flowing into an `Optional[str]` slot (emits `s ? std::make_optional(std::string(*s)) : std::nullopt`), and it is str-ONLY (no bytes sibling). Not buggy today -- the bytes-Optional-into-Optional-slot shapes build and run via the optional/coercion machinery -- so this is cleanliness, not a defect. The clean end-state: the chokepoint's bare-slot arm uses `_is_*_view_source OR _expr_uses_optional_*_param`, while the `_wrap_for_owned_slot` Optional residual uses only `_is_*_view_source` (to avoid double-wrapping a whole-optional source); both could instead key uniformly on `_is_narrowed_value_optional(expr)` (`:570`, declared-Optional + analyzed-non-Optional = a narrowed deref vs a whole optional), collapsing the param-name augmentation and `_maybe_convert_opt_str_param` into the single chokepoint and giving bytes parity for free. A consumer-side re-derivation today; folding it onto the first-class `_is_narrowed_value_optional` fact is the per-CLAUDE.md direction. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion of the view->owned unification.
- **[cleanup] The str/bytes view->owned copy spelling lives in two helpers.** `view_to_owned_conv` (`tpyc/type_def_registry.py`, the bare `std::string` / `::tpy::bytes_copy` callable, wrapped by `view_owned_copy_init` for the coro/generator-frame ctor-init + init-capture) and `_view_owned_copy_expr` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`, the expression-site chokepoint + optional-param assign) both encode the same str->`std::string` / bytes->`::tpy::bytes_copy` mapping. Collapse to one source -- `_view_owned_copy_expr` could delegate to `view_to_owned_conv`, or the mapping could become a `copy_conv` field on `ViewTypeFamily` that every consumer reads. The two serve different layers (registry frame-capture vs codegen expression-site), so it is a cross-module consolidation, not a mechanical swap; relates to item folding `_maybe_convert_opt_*_param` onto `_is_narrowed_value_optional`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the bytes view-family param unification.
- **Compound view-source residual optimization (core DONE).** The COMPOUND view-deduction work (ternary / `and`-`or` / nested) is done: a view deduced from a compound owned-borrow source now registers EVERY storage root via the shared `walk_view_source_leaves` traversal (`tpyc/sema/local_deduction.py`), so mutating any root demotes it to owned -- this closed a silent UAF (a compound view tracked no roots before) -- and a narrowed `str | None` param deref is now a zero-copy view (the `std::string(std::string(...))` double-wrap is gone). One residual *optimization* remains, currently resolving OWNED (sound, just not zero-copy): **mixed pending-view + owned-borrow compound arms** (`x = a if c else d[0]`, `a` a pending-view local) conservatively resolve owned (the pending arm forces owned in the fresh-from-owned branch) -- a view tracking both the `source_var_ids` and the borrow-root channel would reclaim the copy. Folds naturally into the provenance-classifier consolidation (next item). Surfaced finishing the compound-view-roots fix.
- **[cleanup] Unify the view-source structural recursion shape.** THREE independent walks of the same TpyCoerce / TpyBinOp `&&`-`||` / TpyIfExpr arm shape: `walk_view_source_leaves` (`tpyc/sema/local_deduction.py`, drives pending-source + borrow-root collection), `is_view_compatible_source`'s own and/or+ternary recursion (same file, the view-safety predicate -- guarded today only by a keep-in-sync INVARIANT comment), and `addr_taken_roots` (`tpyc/sema/context.py:46`). A future node kind (e.g. walrus) needs all three updated in lockstep; a miss between the predicate and the leaf walk yields a stale view (UAF). Route `is_view_compatible_source` through `walk_view_source_leaves` (a leaf-fn returning view-safe-ness combined with `all()` -- non-trivial: the predicate uses AND/short-circuit + PendingViewType early-exits vs the walk's list return, so it's a small design step, not mechanical), and move the shared walker into `context.py` (it only needs parse nodes; `local_deduction` imports `context`, so verify no import cycle) so `addr_taken_roots` shares it too. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit, safety-model) of the compound-view-roots fix. (Breadcrumb: routing reassigned `str`/`bytes` PARAMS through this same view-source machinery -- so a param reassigned only to borrow-valid sources stays a view like a local does, retiring the coarse `param_needs_copy_for_reassign` for view types -- was probed and DEFERRED as not-worth-standalone: near-zero corpus payoff and a silent-dangle risk if mis-classified (a `span` param is implicitly constructible from an owned vector, so a wrong call dangles with no build error). Only fold the param-reassign path in if doing this consolidation anyway.)
- **[cleanup] DRY the for-loop / comprehension iterable-render call sites onto one `(code, type)` helper.** The narrowed-value-Optional fix routes every iterable-render site through the pair `_maybe_unwrap_narrowed_optional(expr, gen_expr_deref(expr), is_indirect_name(expr))` + `narrowed_value_optional_iter_type(expr, get_resolved_type(expr))` -- repeated inline at ~3 comprehension sites in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py` (`_gen_comprehension_iife`, `_gen_comp_begin_end_loop`, the array/general comp paths), plus the sync for-loop's `_for_iterable_deref` and the resumable `gen_async._for_src_access` / `gen_generators._analyze_for_strategy`. A single helper returning `(rendered_code, dispatch_type)` would collapse the repetition and make a future missed iterable-render site (the recurring decay pattern) impossible. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the narrowed-Optional for-loop fix.
- **[cleanup] `_enter_comp_scope` queries the un-narrowed iterable type when registering the comprehension loop var.** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py` -- `_enter_comp_scope` calls `get_resolved_type(gen.iterable)` (not `narrowed_value_optional_iter_type`), so for a narrowed value-Optional iterable `get_iterable_element_type` returns None and the comp loop var is left unregistered in the var-type table. No live symptom for `str`/`bytes` (the element is `Char`/`UInt8`, never an Optional needing a deref), so it is latent; route it through the same narrowed-type helper for consistency with the corrected outer dispatch. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the narrowed-Optional for-loop fix.
- **[cleanup -- HARDER THAN IT LOOKS] The `match_borrow_captures` override in the borrow snapshot is LOAD-BEARING, not a redundant epicycle.** An aliasing match-capture of an lvalue subject records a stmt-borrow (`sema/match.py`) AND adds the name to `match_borrow_captures`, which `analyzer.py`'s function-end snapshot OR's back in to cancel the `nonstmt_bound_names` exclusion. It LOOKS like add-to-exclusion-then-cancel, but the override is function-level on purpose: the match-arm codegen emits the capture *unconditionally in borrow form* (`q = &subject`), so the variable's single C++ declaration must stay pointer form for the WHOLE function -- even if the same name is later bound by an owned `with...as` / for-loop var (which re-adds it to `nonstmt_bound_names`). A naive cleanup that drops the override (or lets the later owned binding win) makes the decl owned (`Box q`) while the arm still binds a borrow -> the two disagree and codegen reads garbage. Verified: removing the override regresses `tests/cases/match/borrow_capture_then_with_rebind` from `99/7/99` to `0/7/0`. A real cleanup must unify the match-arm capture's emitted FORM with the variable's decl form (make the arm defer to owned when the name is later owned-bound, accepting the copy/parity implications), not just simplify the snapshot bookkeeping -- bigger work, likely IR-era (the place/loan model makes "this name's form" a single fact). Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the attempted cleanup, which a regression test caught. The guard test now exists.
- **[codegen readability] `print(v)` of a narrowed `str|None`/`bytes|None` name renders `print_optional_val(v)` instead of dereferencing the proven-non-None value.** The print path (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/builtins.py` ~332) branches on `get_resolved_type(arg)` -- the *declared* Optional type -- not the sema-narrowed analyzed type, so inside `if a is not None:` a `print(a)` still routes through `print_optional_val`. NOT a defect: output is correct (the optional is present, so the value prints). Cleanliness only -- a narrowed NAME arg could deref + print directly, but the fix must preserve the deliberate field-case `print_optional_val` (field storage stays `std::optional<T>`). Sibling of the (now-fixed) dict-value narrowing skip, same root (declared-vs-narrowed type at a value sink). Surfaced fixing the dict-value narrowed-Optional bug.
- **[codegen readability] `_emit_guarded_arm_tail` emits a dead `goto __match_end` after a terminating arm body.** The shared guarded-arm tail emitter (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py`) unconditionally appends `goto __match_end_N;` even when the arm body ends in `return`/`raise`, producing unreachable dead code (e.g. `record_guard` / `nested_type_pattern_edges` snapshots emit `return "other"; goto __match_end_2;`). Pre-existing in the helper; newly reachable for unguarded wildcard/capture arms now that the guarded-record capture branch routes through it. Compiles, no g++ warning -- purely cosmetic. Fix: gate the emit on `not stmts_terminate(case.body)`. Churns the str/float + record guarded-match snapshots, so do it on its own small branch. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the match-capture-indirect-subject fix.
- **[codegen readability] for-over-generator/iterator loop body is under-indented by one level.** A `for x in <generator-or-iterator>:` lowers to `{ auto __src; auto&& __itr; for(;;){ next; if break; <binding>; <BODY> } }`, but the loop BODY statements emit at the enclosing block's indent instead of the `for` body indent -- e.g. the loop-var binding sits at the correct depth and the next line (`print(x)`) drops back two levels. A plain `for x in <list>:` indents correctly, so the bug is specific to the iterator/generator for-lowering (the body emit doesn't account for the extra `{ ... for(;;) { } }` nesting). Compiles and runs correctly (C++ is whitespace-insensitive) -- purely cosmetic in `--dump-code` / snapshots. Reproducer: `for x in g(): print(x)` over any generator (`/tmp/agents/rev/genloop.py`). Pure cosmetic -> churns many generator/iterator-loop snapshots, so fix on its own small branch. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the audit-#11 view_of_param_yielded test snapshot.
- **Borrow/storage chokepoint -- test-coverage gaps.** Two over-trigger / regression guards not yet written (no observed failure): (a) a B56 reference-type ternary with BOTH arms fresh rvalues (`c = [1] if flag else [2]`) -- guards that the new "ternary copies a reference type" diagnostic does NOT over-fire when neither arm aliases; (b) a `Send[T | None]` / `Sync[T | None]` marker over a pointer-repr Optional -- guards the `_make_marker` -> `with_inner` `force_pointer_repr` preservation (no `send_sync/` case exercises a pointer-repr Optional inside a marker). Add under `tests/cases/control_flow/` and `tests/cases/send_sync/`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the chokepoint branch.
- **await-result tuple unpack -- test-coverage gaps.** The move-out fix (`async/await_tuple_own_unpack` + the CPython-parity `await_async_tuple_unpack`) covers 2-element Own/reference/value tuples, whole-assign, and loop re-await. Add: (a) a 3+-element await tuple with two `Own` elements (multiple move-outs from one `auto&& __tup`); (b) an await-tuple unpack inside a `try`/`except` body across a suspension (frame cleanup interleaving with owned-element destruction). Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of `fix-await-tuple-nocopy`.
- **owned-tuple PARAM -- test-coverage gaps.** The owned-tuple-param feature (`tuple[Own[A], ...]` -> `std::tuple<...>&&`) is covered for 2-element sync functions (unpack/forward/return, never-consumed warn+suppress, use-after-move, mixed exclusion). Add: an owned-tuple param on a **generator/async** function (interaction with frame-struct promotion -- the param is seeded into `movable_locals` + `storage_form_tuple_locals`) and on a **class method**. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the owned-tuple-param feature.
- **owned-tuple never-consumed warning is per-tuple, not per-element.** `_warn_unconsumed_own_params` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`) suppresses the warning for a `tuple[Own[A], ...]` param when ANY owned element is `@nocopy`/value-typed (`any(...)`), so a mixed `tuple[Own[NocopySock], Own[BigInt]]` that is never consumed is silenced even though the `BigInt` element is genuinely unconsumed -- weaker than the scalar per-param model. Whether to tighten to `all(...)` (warn unless EVERY owned element is drop-legitimate) is a warning-semantics design question, not a mechanical flip: tightening could newly warn on legitimate consume-one/drop-the-other patterns. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit, safety-model) of the owned-tuple-param feature.
- **Consolidate the `movable_locals` var-decl seeding idiom.** `if name in self.ctx.sema_movable_locals: self.ctx.movable_locals.add(name)` is repeated at ~7 var-decl sites in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` (each in its own early-return branch, a couple keying on `name` vs `stmt.name`), plus the param-block seeding. A single promote-point or a small helper would cut the drift risk and shrink the THIR lowering surface (each `id`-keyed seeding site is a future lowering touch-point). Care needed: the branches differ in surrounding context, so a naive helper must preserve each site's gating. Surfaced by /tpy-ready (retrospective second-opinion) of the owned-tuple-param feature.
- **Tuple-element BigInt-overflow panic -- test gap.** `int/panic_int_to_fixed_aggregate_overflow` covers an out-of-range `BigInt` element in a *list* literal, but the *tuple*-literal element path is a separate sema route (`_analyze_tuple_literal` via `_materialize_narrowing_element_coercion`, vs the array path's inline check). Add a panic case for an overflowing `BigInt` element in a `tuple[..., Int32]` literal/return so the tuple route's runtime `to_fixed_check` is guarded too. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of `fix-aggregate-literal-elem-coercion`.
- **Variadic tuples (`TypeVarTuple` / `*Ts`, and variadic-arity tuple returns).** TPy has fixed-arity tuple types + unpacking (FEATURE_ROADMAP A10) but no variadic-tuple generics: a function can't take/return a tuple of statically-unknown arity, nor splat `*args` of heterogeneous types into a fixed-arity tuple. Substantial type-system + codegen feature (the C++ side is `std::tuple` with parameter packs); warrants `/tpy-add-feature`. **Blocks `itertools.chain` / `product` / `permutations` / `combinations`** (chain/product take `*iterables`; product/permutations/combinations yield tuples whose arity depends on the inputs) and the *variadic* `starmap` (the fixed two-argument `starmap` is separately blocked on the simple-peephole dangling-capture bug -- see BUGS.md). Surfaced scoping itertools v2.
- **asyncio I/O reactor follow-ups (post-M2 + smaller gaps).** The M1 epoll reactor + M2 `sock_accept`/`sock_connect` + the client streams layer (`open_connection` -> `StreamReader`/`StreamWriter`) shipped (`EpollReactor` + `get_running_loop().sock_recv`/`sock_sendall`/`sock_accept`/`sock_connect`; see `docs/ASYNC_DESIGN.md` "I/O reactor" + `examples/net/async_echo_*`). Remaining, roughly in priority order: (1) **Swap-in reactor** -- make the executor's `reactor` field `@dynamic Reactor`-typed (it's concrete `EpollReactor | None` today; the `Reactor` Protocol already exists in `_executor.py`) and let `asyncio.run` take a reactor factory, so a user-provided / alternative backend can replace it. NOTE: cross-platform readiness polling is already handled *below* the TPy line -- `epoll_impl.cpp` is dual-backend (epoll on Linux, kqueue on macOS/BSD) behind the flat `tpy_epoll_*` ABI via `#ifdef`. So this is NOT needed for kqueue; the real motivator is a fundamentally different model (io_uring, completion-based) or a user-pluggable pure-TPy reactor. Low priority / speculative until such a need is real. (2) **Independent read+write waiters on one fd** -- the reactor is single-waiter-per-fd (one direction at a time); two coroutines awaiting different directions on the same fd needs `_waiters` keyed by (fd, direction) + a combined epoll interest mask with MOD. (3) **Scaling**: `EpollReactor._waiters` is a `dict[Int32, Waker]` and the executor `runnable_q` is still O(n) `pop(0)` (pre-existing, tracked separately) -- fine for now, revisit under a 10k-connection workload.
- **asyncio reactor M1 -- EINTR handling.** Two gaps, both low-frequency (EINTR is rare on non-blocking fds): (a) `tpy_epoll_wait` (`runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/epoll_impl.cpp`) retries `epoll_wait` on EINTR with the *original* `timeout_ms`, not adjusted for elapsed time, so a timer can fire late by up to one timeout per EINTR -- recompute the remaining time from a deadline on each retry; (b) a signal-interrupted `recv`/`send` in `_SockRecv`/`_SockSendAll` (`lib/tpy/asyncio/__init__.py`) returns EINTR, which falls to the error branch and raises `SocketError`, where CPython retries -- treat EINTR like a retry (re-issue the syscall) rather than a fatal error. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness + cpython-parity).
- **asyncio reactor M1 -- test-coverage batch (residual).** Most of this batch is now covered (cases under `tests/cases/async/`): cancel of a parked `_SockRecv`/`_SockSendAll` (`reactor_cancel_parked_io`), `sock_sendall` non-EAGAIN error (`reactor_sendall_error`), `get_running_loop()` outside `asyncio.run` -> `RuntimeError` (`get_running_loop_outside_run`), multi-fd `EpollReactor.poll` batch n>1 (`reactor_multi_fd_poll`), and negative `sock_recv` -> `ValueError` (`socket_recv_negative_size`). Two internal branches remain untested because they are not deterministically reachable from user-level TPy code: (d) `EpollReactor.register_fd` MOD re-arm (an fd re-registered while still armed -- the reactor unregisters on wake, so the same-fd re-arm path needs an internal scenario, not a user program), and (e) `_next_timer_timeout_ms` "already due" (`delta <= 0` -> 0) -- timing-dependent (a timer already past when the timeout is computed), so any test would be flaky. Add if a deterministic harness for internal reactor state lands. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage).
- **asyncio reactor M2 -- test-coverage batch (residual).** Most of this batch is now covered (cases under `tests/cases/async/`): sync `socket.recv(-1)` -> `ValueError` (`socket_recv_negative_size`, alongside the async path), `BlockingIOError` catch semantics -- catchable, caught by `except OSError`, not by `except ValueError` (`socket_blocking_io_error`), cancel of a parked `_SockAccept` (`reactor_accept_cancel_timeout`), and a direct `getsockopt_int`/`setsockopt_int` roundtrip on `SO_REUSEADDR` (`socket_getsockopt`, `no_cpython` -- TPy-only method surface). The `_SockConnect`-side paths remain untested because loopback connect behavior can't be steered from a portable test: (c-connect) cancel of a parked `_SockConnect` needs a connect that stays EINPROGRESS without completing (an unresponsive remote -- network-dependent/flaky), and (d) the `_SockConnect` synchronous-success branch (connect returns 0 on the first poll) is dead on loopback, which always returns EINPROGRESS for a non-blocking connect. Add if a deterministic connect-stall harness lands. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the asyncio M2 branch.
- **asyncio reactor M2 -- hardening / known gap.** A non-blocking stdlib gap surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion: **socket closed mid-await** -- `_SockRecv`/`_SockSendAll`/`_SockAccept`/`_SockConnect` hold a raw `Ptr[socket]` across suspension with no tie to the socket's lifetime, and `socket` has an explicit `close()`/`__del__` that flips `fd` to -1 -- a socket closed while its awaitable is parked leaves the awaitable polling `fileno() == -1` into the reactor (sharper than the generic `Ptr`-across-await exposure that `Ptr[Lock]`/`Ptr[Queue]` already carry). No compile-time enforcement; v1 accepts it. (The other two gaps from this entry are now fixed: the `socket._accept_fd` fd-leak on a peer-resolve failure closes the accepted fd before re-raising; `_SockSendAll` sends via a growing offset through `socket._send_from` instead of re-slicing a fresh `bytes` per park -- regression-guarded by `reactor_sendall_large`.) Surfaced by /tpy-review + /tpy-ready of the asyncio M2 branch.
- **asyncio streams -- deferred pieces.** The streams layer shipped `open_connection` -> `StreamReader` (`read`/`readexactly`/`readline`/`at_eof`) + `StreamWriter` (`write`/`drain`/`close`/`wait_closed`) + `IncompleteReadError`, and `start_server(handler, host, port)` -> `Server` (`serve_forever`/`close`/`wait_closed`/async-with + `server.sockets[0].getsockname()`; background accept loop spawning the async `handler` per connection, on the async-fn->Callable coercion). Deferred: (a) **`StreamReader.readuntil` `limit` / `LimitOverrunError` + multi-fill coverage** -- `readuntil(sep)` itself SHIPPED (the `bytes` coro-param gap is fixed via the `OWNED_COPY` capture kind); like `readline`/`read` it enforces no buffer limit, so CPython's `limit=`/`LimitOverrunError` surface is still absent. Also untested: the `readuntil` fill loop *iterating* (separator split across two `sock_recv` chunks) and a multi-byte separator spanning a chunk boundary -- the shipped `stream_readuntil` test sends all bytes in one `sock_sendall`, so the separator is found on the first buffer and the `while` loop body never runs (the loop logic is shared-shape with `readline`, which is also single-chunk in tests). Deterministic multi-fill over loopback TCP needs the server to send in pieces with a client read between -- add when there's a harness for it. (b) **`start_server` remaining CPython-parity gaps** -- both lifecycle tests now run under real CPython asyncio. Two documented `Server` divergences remain (no test exercises them, so neither is behind `no_cpython`): (i) **`wait_closed()` is a no-op** -- CPython awaits in-flight connections to drain; matching needs `Server` to track its spawned per-connection tasks (a task registry populated by `_accept_loop`) and await them in `wait_closed()`/`__aexit__`. (ii) **`__aenter__` returns None** (not self), so `async with server as s` is unsupported -- blocked on the async-return-of-`self`-copies-`@nocopy` codegen gap (BUGS.md). Also: `Server.sockets[0]` hands out a *readonly* socket borrow (only `getsockname`-style reads; mutating socket methods aren't exposed). The interior-mutable readonly-`Rc.clone` has since landed, but by design a clone from a readonly receiver yields a *readonly*-payload handle (no readonly->mutable laundering), so exposing a *mutable* socket still needs the proxy to hold and clone a *mutable* `Rc` via a non-readonly accessor path, not the readonly clone. A CPython `sock=` param to `start_server` is also still unimplemented. Sub-cells of the coercion still open: a bound async *method* ref (`obj.handler`) is the general no-method-value gap; the `Fn`-template target + intermediate `Own[Cancellable]` local are in BUGS.md; a sync (non-async) fn passed where a coroutine-factory `Callable` is expected should get a sema diagnostic (today only a downstream C++ error -- add an `error_` case once the diagnostic lands); `docs/CLOSURES_CALLABLE_DESIGN.md` should gain an async-fn->Callable coercion section (covers the coercion semantics + the bound-method/`Fn`-template limits). Also deferred: `StreamReader.readuntil` limit / `LimitOverrunError`, `StreamWriter.get_extra_info`, `start_server` backlog (multiple listening sockets, `reuse_port`, `ssl`), the streams buffer's O(n) per-fill `bytes` concat (mirror `_SockSendAll`'s offset gap). Surfaced building the asyncio streams layer.
- **asyncio: graceful SIGTERM shutdown (TPy enhancement; CPython-divergent).** `asyncio.run` now handles **SIGINT** (Ctrl-C) gracefully -- cancel the root task, run its `finally`/`__aexit__`/`wait_closed` cleanup, then raise `KeyboardInterrupt` -- matching CPython's asyncio.run (signal layer in `runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/signal_impl.cpp` + `lib/tpy/signal.py` + the executor's `_SignalScope`/`shutdown_armed`). SIGTERM is deliberately NOT handled, matching CPython (default terminate). Servers under k8s/Docker typically want graceful SIGTERM too; adding it is a deliberate divergence from CPython's asyncio.run, so it needs the acknowledged-divergence treatment (doc note + ideally an opt-in spelling like a `shutdown_signals=` arg to `run`) rather than silently installing a SIGTERM handler. The C signal layer already saves/restores via sigaction and the eventfd wakeup is signal-agnostic, so wiring a second signal is small once the divergence is acknowledged. Surfaced building the SIGINT graceful-shutdown feature.
- **Uncaught `KeyboardInterrupt` (and BaseException generally) exits 1 via the panic path, not 130.** TPy renders any uncaught `BaseException` through `tpy_terminate_handler` as `TurboPython panic: uncaught ...` and exits 1. CPython exits **130** for an uncaught `KeyboardInterrupt` (and a pre-signal-feature Ctrl-C terminated via SIGINT, also 130), so a server that doesn't catch KI now exits 1 instead of 130 -- a parity regression for the uncaught case. Consider special-casing an uncaught `KeyboardInterrupt` at the top-level/terminate path to exit 130 with a Ctrl-C-appropriate message (less crash-like than "panic"). This is about TPy's generic uncaught-exception rendering, not asyncio -- decide it on its own. Idiomatic programs catch KI (the server examples now do), so this only affects the uncaught path. Surfaced testing the asyncio SIGINT feature end-to-end.
- **asyncio: deadlock guard ("no progress possible") is suppressed during a signal-armed run.** `asyncio.run` registers the SIGINT wakeup eventfd in the reactor, so `wait_for_event`'s fd count is always >= 1 and the "no coroutine made progress, no timers, no I/O" RuntimeError never fires while shutdown handling is active -- a genuinely deadlocked root now blocks until Ctrl-C instead of raising. This matches CPython (which has no such guard), but loses a useful debugging signal. Restoring it would need the executor to distinguish the control eventfd from real work fds (the "dedicated executor wakeup fd" design, vs the chosen "eventfd in the reactor"). Low priority. Surfaced building the SIGINT graceful-shutdown feature.
- **asyncio: double Ctrl-C during shutdown is ignored (CPython aborts cleanup on the second).** TPy's SIGINT shutdown cancels the root once (`_check_shutdown_signal` guards on `already`); a second SIGINT delivered while cleanup is running is coalesced and ignored, so cleanup always runs to completion. CPython's `_on_sigint` raises `KeyboardInterrupt` directly from the handler on the second interrupt, aborting an in-progress (possibly hung) cleanup -- a deliberate escape hatch. TPy's behavior is arguably nicer (cleanup completes) but diverges; matching CPython would need the handler/consume path to track an interrupt count and force-raise on the second. Decide whether to match or keep the always-complete-cleanup behavior (and document it). Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the SIGINT feature.
- **asyncio SIGINT shutdown -- test-coverage gaps.** The shipped tests cover finally-cleanup + KeyboardInterrupt, suppressed-cancel-returns-value, and cleanup-raises-a-different-exception. Untested: (a) `async with` `__aexit__` running because a SIGINT cancelled the root mid-body (the motivating server shape -- distinct from the `finally` path, though both ride the same CancelledError unwind); (b) SIGINT delivered while a spawned background task is live, exercising `drain_spawned_with_cancel` on the signal-interrupted branch. Add when next touching this area. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the SIGINT feature.
- **asyncio streams -- coverage + small parity gaps (from /tpy-review).** Non-blocking follow-ons on the shipped streams layer: (a) test gaps -- `readline` multi-fill (newline arrives in a later chunk), `readline` at EOF with no trailing newline (partial-line return), `at_eof()` with EOF flagged but data still buffered (returns `False`), `is_closing()`, `drain()` with an empty buffer; (b) `IncompleteReadError` message text differs from CPython's "N bytes read on a total of M expected bytes" (the `.partial`/`.expected` fields match -- only the formatted message diverges); (c) `drain()` on an empty buffer returns without yielding a scheduling slot (CPython's `_drain_helper` is near-noop here too, so marginal). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the streams layer.
- **`async with StreamWriter` ergonomics (TPy-only enhancement).** Give `StreamWriter` `__aenter__`/`__aexit__` (returning self / `close()` + `await wait_closed()`) so users can write `async with writer: ...` instead of the explicit `writer.close(); await writer.wait_closed()` two-step. NOTE: this is a deliberate divergence -- CPython's `asyncio.StreamWriter` has NO async-context-manager support (no `__aenter__`/`__aexit__`; the documented cleanup is exactly the two-step), so code/examples using it would not run under stock CPython. Weigh against the streams module's CPython-faithful-mirror goal before building. Surfaced reviewing `examples/net/stream_client.py`.
- **io.BufferedReader / FileIO / socket.makefile v2 follow-ups.** v1 shipped `io.FileIO` (fd adopt) + `io.BufferedReader` (concrete, wraps a `FileIO`) + `socket.makefile("rb")` -- the binary-read layer `http.client` builds on. Deferred, each a separate add: (1) **`BufferedReader.readinto(buf)` / `FileIO.readinto(buf)`** -- write into a caller `bytearray`; blocked on mutable-buffer write support (slice-assignment / memoryview not on the surface), so http.client v1 should use `read()`. (2) **`BufferedReader.peek(n)`** -- non-consuming lookahead. (3) **Text mode** -- `makefile("r")` / a `TextIOWrapper` over a binary buffer (encoding/newline translation); v1 `makefile` rejects non-binary modes with `ValueError` (TPy-only, `no_cpython` case `socket_makefile_unsupported`). (4) **`makefile(buffering=0)` unbuffered** -- CPython returns the raw stream; our fixed `Own[BufferedReader]` return can't, so unsupported. (5) **`makefile` dup vs CPython shared-fd -- the faithful fix is a SOCKET-LAYER design, pure library, NOT THIR-gated.** v1 dups the socket fd (reader/socket close independently); CPython shares the fd via an internal refcount (`_io_refs`) and keeps the socket object alive while a makefile reader exists. Observably equivalent for request/response reads, so dup is fine for v1. (The sharper divergence than fd-count/refcount: the socket and the dup share ONE kernel byte-stream -- dup'd fds share the open file description -- so interleaving reads on both handles steals bytes from the other; benign for http.client's write-via-sock / read-via-fp split, a footgun if a user reads from both.) The blocker on a faithful share is purely an ownership-shape problem (no compiler gate): `makefile(self)` only *borrows* `self`, so it can't move the caller's owned `@nocopy` socket into an `Rc[socket]` -- the async `StreamReader` only gets to share `Rc[socket]` because `open_connection` *constructs* the socket into the Rc. The clean fix is to refcount the fd in a shared cell (CPython `_io_refs` model) so `makefile` clones it -- but forcing that `Rc`/heap cell on EVERY socket fights `@noalloc`/primitive-cleanliness (the cost is the per-socket alloc + losing the trivial-RAII value, not throughput -- socket I/O is syscall-bound). Preferred shape (design, build when a workload needs faithful shared-fd or a lean primitive -- per the don't-build-infra-ahead-of-demand stance): a two-layer split -- a lean **`tplib.net.Socket`** raw-fd `@nocopy` zero-overhead primitive (reactor + hot paths + `@noalloc` target) and the stdlib **`socket.socket`** as an `Rc`-backed CPython-faithful facade on top (where `makefile` shared-fd + refcount-close live). The lean primitive has value independent of makefile. Until then dup stands. (HTTP keep-alive has since landed -- SSL_DESIGN.md's former precondition for the Rc-unify cleanup -- and dup remains sufficient: the drain-before-next-request discipline never reads through socket and reader concurrently.) (6) **`readline(size)` `_MAXLINE` cap usage** -- the `size` arg works; http.client should pass it to bound header length once built. (7) **`makefile` return-type overloads (the eventual full-parity shape, once TextIOWrapper/BufferedWriter/BufferedRWPair exist)** -- mirror the existing `open()` pattern in `lib/tpy/tpy/_builtins/_io.py`: `@overload` on a `Literal[...]` mode arg returns the precise concrete type (`"rb"`->`BufferedReader`, `"wb"`->`BufferedWriter`, `"r"`->`TextIOWrapper`, `"rwb"`->`BufferedRWPair`, `buffering=0`->raw `SocketIO`), so a literal call needs no narrowing; a runtime `mode: str` call hits the catch-all overload returning the union of those types, which the user narrows/asserts. v1 returns only `BufferedReader` and raises on the rest. (8) **Audit `BufferedReader._take` vs `asyncio.StreamReader._take` for borrow-materialization drift** -- both clone the same `_buf`/`_fill`/`_take` shape, and `_take` relies on `bytes(self._buf[:n])` / `bytes(self._buf[n:])` to materialize an owned head BEFORE reassigning `_buf` (a no-wrap slice is a borrow into the buffer being reassigned -> UAF). The two copies can drift: a fix or regression in one won't track the other. A shared helper is awkward (sync vs async + read-blocks-to-size vs StreamReader's return-early), so the action is a periodic invariant audit, not forced unification. Surfaced building `io.BufferedReader` for the REST track. (9) **`BufferedReader` raw source is `Box[RawBinaryIO]` unconditionally** -- the `@dynamic` raw protocol (added for `ssl.SSLRawIO`/`makefile`) type-erases EVERY source via `make_adapter`, so the common concrete-`FileIO` path (the http.client/requests read loop) now pays a heap alloc per reader + a vcall per ~8KB fill it didn't before (was a concrete inline field). The decision is right -- a generic `BufferedReader[S]` would push a viral type param up http.client/requests, and the slicing wall blocks a bare protocol field -- so this is a perf-tracking note, not a back-out. If an HTTP read-throughput workload ever demands it, de-erase the concrete path via a static-generic + dyn hybrid (`BufferedReader[S]` for a statically-known source, the `Box[dyn]` form only when the source is polymorphic). Negligible at 8KB granularity for now. Surfaced by /tpy-ready of the ssl makefile increment.
- **http.client / http.HTTPStatus v2 follow-ups.** v1 shipped `http.HTTPStatus` (full CPython code set; `.value`/`.name`/value-lookup/int-compare) + `http.client` (`HTTPConnection`, `HTTPResponse`, `HTTPException`/`BadStatusLine`) -- plaintext HTTP/1.1 over a `socket.makefile`-backed `io.BufferedReader`, byte-parity with CPython on the implemented subset (tests `stdlib/http_status`, `http_client`, `http_client_framing`, `http_client_bad_status`, all gold parity via socketpair + `conn.sock` injection). Deferred, each a separate add: (1) **HTTPS / `HTTPSConnection` -- SHIPPED** (`HTTPSConnection(host, port=443, timeout=None, context=None)` runs the HTTP/1.1 flow over an `ssl.SSLSocket`; secure-default context, caller `context=` captured by value; a *sibling* of `HTTPConnection` -- not a subclass, since TPy method dispatch is static -- both satisfying a `@dynamic _Connection` protocol so a caller holds either behind one `Box[_Connection]`; request-building extracted to module free functions; test `stdlib/https_client`, `no_cpython`, real TLS handshake via the `_tls` injection seam). Residual: `http.client` now imports `ssl`, so every program importing it links mbedTLS -- see the use-driven-linking follow-up below. Test gap: the `@dynamic _Connection` protocol ships with NO committed test of `Box[_Connection]` virtual dispatch, and `_Connection` currently has zero consumers (a structural-conformance forward-declaration for Increment 5). DECISION (with user, post-merge): Increment 5 switches HTTPConnection/HTTPSConnection to NOMINALLY inherit `_Connection` (`class HTTPConnection(_Connection)`) -- explicit conformance, direct base storage in `Box[_Connection]` (no `Adapter`, so it sidesteps the structural-conformance incomplete-`Adapter` bug in BUGS.md entirely; no factory workaround needed). Cost is a vtable pointer per instance (concrete calls devirtualize -- no per-call cost) + regenerating the `http_client*` snapshots (vtable added to HTTPConnection's emitted C++). SHIPPED in Increment 5. (2) **Low-level incremental request API** -- `putrequest`/`putheader`/`endheaders`/`send`; v1 has only high-level `request()`. (3) **Non-bytes request bodies** -- v1 takes `bytes | None`; CPython also accepts `str` (encoded), file-like (`.read`), and iterables (auto-chunked) -- needs str->bytes at the boundary (and the `String.encode()` gap, BUGS.md) plus chunked *sending*. (4) **`HTTPResponse.headers` as an `email.message`-style object** -- v1 exposes `getheader(name, default)` (case-insensitive, dup-join) + `getheaders()` only; `.headers[...]` subscript / `HTTPMessage` methods are absent (a class can't reproduce `email.message.Message`). (5) **`HTTPStatus.phrase`/`.description`/`.is_success`-style helpers** -- TPy enums can't carry per-member data; accessing them is a compile error (signaled, not silent). Needs the per-member-data enum feature or a parallel `phrase(status)` free function. (6) **`str(HTTPStatus.OK)`** -- CPython 3.11+ returns `"200"`; align TPy enum `str()` if a test prints a member directly (tests use `.value`). (7) **Header continuation/folding** -- `_read_headers` treats each line independently; obs-folded continuation lines aren't joined (rare, legal). (8) **`Content-Length` > 2GB single read** -- `_length` is `int` but `read()` narrows to `Int32` for `BufferedReader.read`; loop the read for very large bodies. (9) **connection reuse -- SHIPPED** (HTTP/1.1 keep-alive: the socket persists across request()/getresponse() cycles; `HTTPResponse.will_close` mirrors CPython's `_check_close` -- Connection-header substring match, HTTP/1.0 default-close, unframed-body fallback; the caller drains each response and closes on will_close; `request()` after `close()` reconnects; test `stdlib/http_client_keepalive`, gold parity). Declared divergence: `getresponse()` does NOT auto-close on will_close as CPython does -- TPy's `SSLSocket.close()` sends close_notify immediately, which would race a still-unread TLS body; the close happens at the caller after draining. Residuals, each a separate add: no `CannotSendRequest`/`ResponseNotReady` state machine (a next request on an undrained connection silently corrupts the stream where CPython raises; needs the connection to see response state it hands away -- an Rc back-ref or a drained-callback shape), no pipelining, no `Proxy-Connection` (HTTP/1.0 proxy) keep-alive token. **Successor design (user-endorsed direction): will_close as ownership transfer.** CPython's `getresponse()` auto-close ("this effectively passes the connection to the response" per its own source) is a MOVE expressed via refcounts; TPy can express it directly -- on will_close, `getresponse()` moves the transport out of the connection into the response (owned closable slot beside the reader), so the connection divests immediately (`sock is None`, next request() reconnects = CPython observables restored), close stays eager everywhere, and the close-after-drain happens at the response owner with no TLS close_notify race. Removes the declared getresponse-no-auto-close divergence AND enables the misuse-guard residual above (the connection knows it has no transport). GATED on the `take()` Optional-field-move builtin (Ownership & Consuming Iteration section); once that lands this is a localized http.client change. Remaining corner after the redesign: an explicit user `close()` of a keep-alive connection mid-read (document as "you closed it"). `set_tunnel`/proxy still missing; redirect following ships at the requests layer. (`timeout=` now ships: `HTTPConnection(host, port, timeout=)` threads a socket timeout through `create_connection`.) (10) **status-line whitespace tolerance** -- v1 splits on single spaces (`split(" ", 2)`); CPython splits on whitespace runs (`split(None, 2)`). (11) **`http.HTTPMethod` enum** -- CPython exposes it alongside `HTTPStatus`; not implemented. (12) **perf** -- the `_read_chunked` O(total*chunks) accumulation is FIXED (bytearray + extend, amortized O(n)). Remaining: `_build_request` allocates a fresh `bytes` per header line (`data + ln.encode() + b"\r\n"`, two `bytes_concat` + a `bytes_literal_owned("\r\n")` each) -- bounded (few headers), fine. Surfaced building `http.client`.
- **`tplib.requests` / `urllib.request.urlopen` v2 follow-ups.** v1 shipped `tplib.requests` (`get`/`post`/`put`/`patch`/`delete`/`head`/`request` + `Response` + `Session` + Basic `auth` + `RequestException`/`HTTPError`/`ConnectionError`) and a simplified `urllib.request.urlopen` (GET/POST shim, `URLError`), both pure TPy over `http.client` (tests `tplib/requests_{get,post,session,errors}`, `tplib/error_requests_json_inline`, `stdlib/urlopen`; all `no_cpython` -- `requests` has no CPython module and the offline test seam injects a socket, so the wire-layer parity is covered by `http.client`'s gold tests). Deferred, each a separate add: (1) **`timeout=` -- SHIPPED** (`get`/`post`/.../`request`/`Session.*` take a `timeout: float | None`; threads through `_connect` -> `HTTPConnection(timeout=)` -> `socket.create_connection`; a slow connect/read raises `Timeout(RequestException)`, the socket layer's `TimeoutError` re-wrapped in `_request_on`; test `tplib/requests_timeout`). Residual: the requests `(connect, read)` two-element timeout tuple is not supported (single float only); and `Session.request(timeout=)` is silently ignored when `Session._connection` is pre-set (the injected connection is used as-is, so the per-call `timeout=` never reaches `_connect`) -- TPy-only module, no parity contract, but a silent no-op worth closing if the pre-set-connection path outlives the test seam. (2) **redirect following -- SHIPPED** (`allow_redirects=` on `get`/.../`request`/`Session.*`, default True; `head()` defaults False; follows 301/302/303/307/308 via `Location` resolved with `urljoin`; `Response.history` holds the intermediate responses, `Response.url` is the final URL; 301/302/303 coerce non-HEAD method to GET and drop the body, 307/308 preserve both; a redirect across a host/scheme/port change drops `Authorization` (`should_strip_auth`); `Session.max_redirects` default 30 -> `TooManyRedirects`; http->https redirects are followed and a redirect to a non-http/https scheme (e.g. `ftp`) raises `ConnectionError`; recursion-based, since a loop-carried `Own` local trips the return borrow check; tests `tplib/requests_redirect{,_disabled,_method,_too_many,_cross_host,_bad_scheme,_https}`). Residuals: the per-hop connection-queue seam `Session._redirect_connections` is offline-test-only (now underscore-prefixed, internal); `params` apply only to the first hop (redirect targets carry their own query); the build needed the recursive-record copyability fix (`is_type_non_copyable` guard, separate branch) because `Response.history: list[Response]` is self-referential. Test-coverage gaps (filed by /tpy-review, low value -- logic is cpython-parity-verified): the HEAD-exception path (a 302/303 redirect of a HEAD request keeps HEAD) and the Location-absent-on-3xx branch (a 3xx with no `Location` header returns as-is) have no dedicated case. (3) **cookies -- SHIPPED** (`cookies=` dict on `get`/.../`request`/`Session.*`; a `CookieJar` + `Cookie`; `Set-Cookie` parsed into `Response.cookies` and accumulated in `Session.cookies`, resent domain/path/secure-matched -- so a cookie set by one host never leaks to another across a redirect; tests `tplib/requests_cookies_{send,set,session,domain,delete}`). Deferred cookie sub-items, all declared narrowings: (a) `cookies=` accepts a `dict[str, str]` only (not a whole `CookieJar`) -- a reference-type union param `CookieJar | dict` was avoided; (b) the jar is keyed by name only, so a same-name/different-domain collision is last-wins (CPython keeps both, keyed by (domain, path, name)); (c) the RFC 1123 and RFC 850 `Expires` forms are parsed (matching CPython's `http.cookiejar`); the asctime form is not (an asctime `Expires` is ignored -> the cookie is session-lifetime). Real expiry is otherwise honored (Max-Age precedence over Expires, past-expiry/`Max-Age<=0` delete, malformed `Max-Age` discards the whole cookie like CPython, expired cookies withheld from send; via `time.time()` + `datetime.strptime`); (d) the default cookie path is `"/"`, not the request-URI directory. Compiler bugs surfaced building this (all worked around in the library): two new BUGS.md entries -- `k in` a const/readonly dict with a non-Equatable value type is rejected; a named reference-type local bound off a const container read (`c = d[k]` / `d.get(k)`) drops const -- plus a new dict reproducer of the pre-existing "resumable generator METHOD that iterates a `self` field" entry (a generator over a const dict can't store the const key-iterator in its frame; `__iter__` yields from a materialized `keys()` list instead), and a Safety/borrow-checker entry (a const method call on a container-subscript element blocks const inference for the enclosing container, so `update`/`header_for` are emitted mutable). Test-coverage residuals filed by /tpy-review (low value, logic is exercised elsewhere): no case supplies a comma-bearing `Expires=` Set-Cookie (guards the `_ingest` split-before-CID-join rationale) or a negative `Max-Age` (only `Max-Age=0` covered). (4) **`json=` inline-literal ergonomics** -- today the caller must bind `payload: JsonValue = {...}` first (the compiler rejects an implicit concrete-dict -> `JsonValue` conversion; clear error, documented); closing this is the recursive-union literal-coercion direction, not a requests fix. (5) **`Response.headers` case-insensitive -- SHIPPED** (`Response.headers` is now a `CaseInsensitiveDict` defined in `requests.py`: case-insensitive `[]`/`in`/`get`/`del`, original casing preserved in `items()`/`keys()`, `__eq__` does case-insensitive content compare; test `tplib/requests_headers_ci`). Now a full mapping: duplicate response headers joined with `", "` (at the `getheaders()` collect site, like urllib3's `HTTPHeaderDict`), `__iter__` (generator over original-case keys, so `for k in headers` works), and the mutable-mapping surface (`update`/`copy`/`pop`/`setdefault`/`popitem`/`clear`) all shipped. Sole residual: `headers == {plain dict}` is a type error (only CID==CID compares); CPython CID `==` accepts any `Mapping` -- a signaled compile error, not silent, low value. (6) **form-dict `data=` + multipart `files=` -- SHIPPED** (`data=` now accepts `bytes | dict[str, str]`: raw bytes sent verbatim, a dict urlencoded as `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`; `files=` (`dict[str, FileField]`) uploads `multipart/form-data` -- each value a `FileField(filename, content, content_type="application/octet-stream")`, so a file part always carries a `Content-Type` (unlike a requests bare 2-tuple, which omits it); any dict `data` folds in as plain form parts; wire format byte-verified against real `requests._encode_files`; tests `tplib/requests_form_data`, `tplib/requests_files`). Declared narrowings: no str body / file-like / iterable / list-valued form field; the files value is a `FileField` record, not a bare `(filename, content)` / 3-tuple like requests (a `tuple | FileField` union was prototyped to keep the bare tuple but dropped: the union element type read badly AND a bare-tuple literal won't coerce through the `dict[str, ...] | None` param -- the terse case wouldn't compile); `data=bytes` is ignored when `files=` is given (only a dict `data` folds in); an empty dict `data` is falsy so it is treated as no body (matching requests' `elif data:`); the multipart boundary is a fixed token (not randomized) with no content-collision scan (deterministic wire bytes, like urllib3's no-scan). Compiler bug surfaced while prototyping the dropped `tuple | FileField` shape (filed anyway, real defect): literal-index subscription (`v[0]`) of a tuple narrowed out of a variant emits raw `operator[]`; BUGS.md. Perf residual (from /tpy-review, deferred): `_encode_body` returns a `tuple[bytes|None, str|None]` that `_request_on` destructures without a move (Optional-wrapped tuple slots miss the whole-tuple-unpack `Own` optimization -- a pre-existing compiler gap, repro `tests/cases/tuple/own_tuple_per_element_param`), so the body bytes are copied once more on the hot path; same body-copy family as (13)(a). Not worth distorting the lib (inlining `_encode_body` into `_request_on`) to dodge a compiler gap; fix the tuple-unpack move opt instead. (7) **`.text` charset sniffing** -- v1 decodes UTF-8; `.encoding` is fixed (no `Content-Type` charset / chardet). (8) **streaming -- SHIPPED** (`stream=True` on all verbs + `request`/`Session.*`; the `Response` holds the live `HTTPResponse` and exposes `iter_content(chunk_size)` (required chunk_size, no default -- dodges the generator-default-drop bug in BUGS.md), `iter_lines()`, `.raw`, `.close()`, `with resp:`; only the terminal hop streams -- a followed redirect is fully drained + poolable, faithful to requests; a streamed connection is dropped, never pooled; the reader keeps its own dup'd fd so it survives the connection close; tests `tplib/requests_stream{,_lines,_redirect}`). Declared divergence: `.content`/`.text`/`.json()` do NOT auto-drain a streamed body (requests' lazy-`.content` needs a mutating `@property`, which TPy's readonly model forbids -- see the mutable-property-getter feature item above); the body is reachable via `iter_content` / `iter_lines` / `r.raw.read(-1)`. Compiler bugs surfaced building this (all worked around; filed in BUGS.md): a generator method narrowing an Optional reference-type `self` field is mis-inferred const (drops its default param + const-poisons the narrowed local); a `with EXPR as v:` whose call materializes a union-typed default arg emits the temp in an invisible scope; storing a generator's yielded reference-type value to an outer variable aliases the dead frame (silent UAF -- copy with `bytes(chunk)`). `/tpy-review` residuals: `iter_content`/`iter_lines` now also chunk over `.content` on a NON-streamed response (matching requests, added post-review); `iter_lines` strips a preceding `\r` so CRLF bodies don't leak a trailing `\r` (lone-`\r`-as-terminator is a declared divergence from full splitlines). Deferred parity follow-ups (declared in the `Response` docstring): (8a) a mid-stream socket failure surfaces as the RAW socket exception, not a wrapped `requests.ConnectionError`/`ChunkedEncodingError` -- wrapping needs a `try/except`+`raise` inside `iter_content`'s generator (the fragile resumable-frame surface with open BUGS.md entries), so it is `/tpy-add-feature` work, not a review fix; it also interacts with the qualified-`except`-name-shadow compiler bug (BUGS.md) that made the raw exception mismatch reachable; (8b) a second iteration after exhaustion silently yields nothing rather than raising requests' `StreamConsumedError` (narrow misuse, low value). Also filed to BUGS.md this round: close/`with`-exit UAF of a `.raw`/`iter_content` handle taken before close (Safety); try-scoped-`Own`-local can't be moved (worked around via the `_send_recv` helper); borrow-form-`Optional[bytes]`-param forwarding copies the body per send (perf, the `_send_recv` extraction newly triggers it). (9) **`Session` connection pooling / keep-alive -- SHIPPED** (`Session._pool: dict[str, Box[_Connection]]` keyed `scheme|host|port|verify-token` via the `_pool_key` free function; pop -> use -> put back; a will_close response closes the socket but the pooled entry stays as a lazy-reconnect handle, since `HTTPConnection.connect()` reconnects when `sock is None`; same-host redirect hops reuse the pooled connection within one call; `__exit__` closes the pool; module-level `get`/`post` use a fresh Session per call like CPython requests; test `tplib/requests_pool`, which seeds `_pool` directly as the pooling seam). Residuals: a pooled connection keeps its creation `timeout=` (a later per-call value does not re-apply -- same family as the `_connection`-seam residual in (1); closing it = a `set_timeout` on the `_Connection` protocol, re-applied at pop time); the `_connection` injection seam stays single-use and unpooled (a Box cannot be moved out of an Optional field into the pool -- probe-verified reject). Test-coverage gap (filed by /tpy-review; low value -- the pop/put-back logic is hop-agnostic and covered at hop 0): the hop>0 pool branch (redirect target served from the pool) has no case -- feasible offline via dual seeding (`_connection` for hop 0, `_pool[_pool_key(redirect_target)]` for hop 1; two sockets, so no reader read-ahead conflict). (The pool FAILURE path -- a raise on a popped entry drops it instead of restoring -- is covered by `requests_pool`'s `failed_request_drops_entry`, a SHUT_WR peer -> `BadStatusLine`.) (10) **proxies -- TLS SHIPPED** (`verify: bool|str` + https routing + `requests.SSLError`, Increment 5; tests `tplib/requests_https`, `tplib/requests_verify_context`, `tplib/requests_redirect_https`); `proxies=` still missing. (11) **auth schemes beyond Basic** -- digest, bearer-token helper, custom auth objects. (12) **`urlopen` response surface** -- `.geturl()`/`.info()`/`.getcode()` (v1 returns a bare `HTTPResponse`; use `.status`/`.reason`/`.read()`/`.getheader()`). (13) **exception tree roots at `OSError` -- SHIPPED** (`RequestException` re-based on `OSError`, so the whole tree -- `HTTPError`/`ConnectionError`/`Timeout`/`SSLError`/`TooManyRedirects` -- is caught by `except OSError`, matching CPython requests' `IOError`/`OSError` root; `urllib.request.URLError` re-based likewise; `_request_on`'s own `except` arms are unaffected -- its try-body raises only wire-level exceptions and sibling handlers don't catch each other's raises; test `tplib/requests_errors` pins the `except OSError` arm). Surfaced building the REST-track ergonomic layer. Two code-quality follow-ups from /tpy-review (both low priority): (a) **request-body bytes are copied at each call layer** (`post`/`put`/... -> `request` -> `_request_on`), the context-blind `bytesview_to_bytes` coercion (pre-existing, tracked separately) plus `_request_on`'s initial `body = data` copy that could be deferred to the json-only branch -- fine for small JSON payloads, revisit before a large-body path; (b) **`_request_on` binds `reason = resp.reason` as a `string_view` local** then constructs `Response` after `conn.close()` -- safe today (the local `resp` outlives the view and `close()` only nulls the socket), but a fragile reused-view pattern; passing `resp.reason` directly to the constructor removes the intermediate view. (The offline test seams -- `urlopen`'s private `_urlopen(..., injected: Box[_Connection])` impl and `Session._connection` / `Session._redirect_connections` -- are underscore-prefixed / off the public surface; a stricter method-based entry point was considered and rejected as over-engineering for a test seam.)
- **Use-driven (symbol-granularity) managed-lib linking.** Module discovery and managed-lib linking are import-driven at MODULE granularity with no tree-shaking: `reach_analysis.py` seeds every imported module as reached regardless of use, and `collect_third_party_deps` (`compiler.py`) walks the full discovered module set and links every `managed=True` directive. Consequence surfaced by HTTPSConnection: `http.client` imports `ssl` (for `HTTPSConnection`), so EVERY program importing `http.client` -- even pure-plaintext ones -- links the vendored mbedTLS objects (build-size cost only; ccache + the cached stdlib `.o` set amortize build time; no correctness/runtime impact). CPython avoids this via lazy `import ssl` inside the method; TPy has no lazy import. The fix is use-driven linking: link a managed lib only when a *used* symbol pulls it in, not merely an imported module -- reverse the "imports => reached" seeding, track re-exported-but-unused symbols, and preserve the transitive TYPE closure (a module whose type appears in a signature must still link even if no call uses it). Generalizes to any `managed=True` lib (pcre2/re, mbedtls/ssl); the THIR/MIR migration is the proper foundation for the reachability machinery (don't build it ahead of demand -- plaintext binary size has to actually bite first). When it lands, `http.client`'s `ssl` import becomes free for non-HTTPS users with no API change. Surfaced building HTTPSConnection (Increment 4). The bundled CA store (`cacert_data.c`, ~230 KiB of `.rodata`) rides the same no-tree-shaking payload: it links into every binary whose module set reaches `ssl`, including plaintext-only `http.client` users -- use-driven linking would drop it from non-HTTPS binaries too.
- **Bump mbedTLS to 3.6.7 and drop the local RSA-PSS backport.** We vendor 3.6.6 (latest as of 2026-07) and carry `runtime/cpp/third_party/mbedtls.patches/001-tls12-accept-rsa-pss-rsae-sigalgs.patch` for the upstream #10668 regression (TLS 1.2 clients rejecting `rsa_pss_rsae_*` ServerKeyExchange -- breaks PSS-picking servers like Windows/Schannel with RSA certs). When the release carrying the fix ships (expected 3.6.7), bump `VERSION`+`SHA256` in `scripts/vendor_mbedtls.py` and drop the patch (the vendor procedure in `third_party/README.md` walks the patch-review step). Blocked on the upstream release; recheck periodically.
- **`socket.settimeout` v2 follow-ups.** v1 shipped `settimeout`/`gettimeout`/`getblocking` timeout mode (recv/send via `SO_RCVTIMEO`/`SO_SNDTIMEO`, connect via the poll-based `tpy_connect_timeout`; a timeout raises `TimeoutError("timed out")`, now an `OSError` subclass to match CPython where `socket.timeout is TimeoutError`; the `makefile`/`BufferedReader` read path is timeout-aware via a `FileIO(timeout_mode=)` flag that re-raises `os.read`'s EAGAIN-`BlockingIOError` as `TimeoutError`). Tests `stdlib/socket_{timeout,gettimeout,connect_timeout}` (CPython-parity), `tplib/requests_timeout`. Deferred: (1) **`setdefaulttimeout`/`getdefaulttimeout` + the `_GLOBAL_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT` sentinel** -- CPython's `create_connection`/`HTTPConnection`/`urlopen` default to that process-wide sentinel, distinct from explicit `None`; TPy defaults to plain blocking (`None`), so a process-wide default can't be set. (2) **accept() under a timeout** -- server-side, not needed for the REST client; `SO_RCVTIMEO` does not reliably cover `accept`, so it would need the same poll wrap as connect. (3) **connect-timeout *expiry* is not deterministically tested** -- the success path is covered on loopback (`socket_connect_timeout`), but triggering an actual connect timeout needs an unresponsive remote (network-dependent/flaky), same gap as the asyncio `_SockConnect` cancel test; add if a deterministic connect-stall harness lands. (4) **`SO_LINGER` (struct linger) setsockopt** -- still int-only (`setsockopt_int`); add a struct path when needed. (5) **`socket.timeout` alias** -- CPython aliases `socket.timeout` to `TimeoutError`; TPy doesn't export it, so `from socket import timeout` fails (and `except socket.timeout:` fails on the qualified-type-in-except limitation regardless). Low value -- code should use `except TimeoutError`. (6) **`timeout=` threading above `HTTPConnection` is compile-only-tested** -- `HTTPConnection(timeout=)` -> `create_connection` -> `settimeout` reaching the socket is now exec-tested over loopback (`stdlib/http_client_timeout`, parity), but the one-line forwardings above it (`urlopen(timeout=)`, `requests.get/post(timeout=)`, `Session.*(timeout=)` -> `_connect` -> `HTTPConnection`) are verified only by compilation: their offline test seam (`_sock=` / `Session._connection`) makes `connect()` a no-op, so an end-to-end requests/urlopen exec test would need a loopback HTTP server, which the http test suite avoids by policy (socketpair seams only). (7) **structured `.errno`/`.strerror` on `OSError` -- SHIPPED** (fields on `tpy::OSError` exposed via `native_field` renames -- `errno` is a C macro, the C++ members are `error_number`/`strerror_text`; needed a codegen fix so `native_field` renames resolve through TPy-level subclasses of `@native` records, walking `mro_ancestors`; populated by `_raise_errno`/`_raise_io`/`_maybe_raise_connection_error` and the asyncio `sock_connect` completion, which now routes through the shared PEP 3151 mapping and raises `ConnectionRefusedError` on a refused async connect like CPython asyncio; `gaierror(OSError)` ships for name-resolution failures with the EAI_* code in `.errno` via the `tpy_last_resolve_code()` shim; a minimal `errno` module (`EAGAIN`/`EWOULDBLOCK`/`EINPROGRESS`/`EPIPE`/`ECONNRESET`/`ECONNREFUSED`/`ECONNABORTED`, host-correct via the `tpy_const_*` globals) enables `e.errno == errno.ECONNREFUSED`; tests `stdlib/socket_errno` (parity), `exceptions/os_error_attrs`). The "CPython-exact OSError" follow-up SHIPPED (branch `oserror-exact`): `(errno, strerror[, filename])` ctor overloads on the whole native family (per-overload `@cpp_template` stubs; `raise X(...)` and inherited-`__init__` construction resolve overload groups sema-side), ctor-time CPython-exact `str(e)` formatting, `.filename`/`.filename2` fields, os/file C++ raise sites populated via the shared `tpy::raise_mapped_os_error` PEP 3151 table (os error messages drop the `op:` prefix), socket factories retired to direct ctors, file-domain errno constants (`ENOENT`/`EEXIST`/`EACCES`/`EPERM`/`EISDIR`/`ENOTDIR`/`EBADF`/`ETIMEDOUT`), and a warning on errno-form construction of the BARE `OSError` class (CPython's `__new__` subclass mapping divergence); tests `exceptions/os_error_ctor`, `stdlib/os_error_exact`. Remaining residuals: post-hoc attribute assignment doesn't reformat `str(e)` (declared; ctor-time formatting), message-kwarg construction (`SSLError(message="x")`) of an inherited-overload-ctor subclass no longer resolves (kwargs skip the overload-group path; loud arity error, rare form); user subclasses keep a single `(errno, strerror)` `__init__` (user-class `@overload __init__` is accepted-then-silently-dropped, so it can't express the per-form ctor pair -- see BUGS.md). Coverage gap: `ConnectionResetError` (ECONNRESET) and `ConnectionAbortedError` (ECONNABORTED) are mapped in `_maybe_raise_connection_error` but have no exec test (hard to trigger deterministically without a cooperating peer / `SO_LINGER` RST); a compile-phase-only case exercising `except ConnectionResetError:`/`except ConnectionAbortedError:` would at least guard the names compile and are recognized as `OSError` subclasses. Surfaced building the REST-track reliability layer; (5)/(6) and the coverage gap by /tpy-review.
- **Annotation validator: explicit `__deref__()` calls aren't recognized as Ptr deref sites.** `# tpyc: nullable(p)` on `print(p.__deref__())` fails with "no ptr dereference found" -- the validator only matches auto-deref field/method access. Affects test authoring only (pointers/panic_ptr_deref_null stays unannotated because of it). Note: annotations are skipped in `--update-snapshots` mode, so a bad one only surfaces on the next normal run.
- **Subscript-keyed narrowing (`if items[i] is not None: use(items[i])`).** No narrowing fact is established for subscript expressions -- `_expr_to_narrowing_key` returns None for `TpySubscript`, so the guarded re-read warns and re-checks even for value-type elements with an unchanged index (verified by probe; the `warn_subscript_narrowing_*` test names notwithstanding, the unmutated sibling also warns). Sound but pessimistic; the documented bind-to-a-local pattern is the workaround. If added, the fact key must include the index expression identity and be killed by ANY container mutation, index reassignment, or alias-group mutation (the kill-set machinery from the flow-fact work covers the meets). CPython-semantics note: a repeated `items[i]` read is a real second `__getitem__` call, so eliding is only sound for builtin containers with no user `__getitem__` side effects.
- **Move an owned reference-type local into a comprehension element at last use.** NARROWED: container *method* inserts (`xs.append(x)`, `s.add(x)`, `d[k] = x`), the list/dict/set *literal* element forms, AND list/set/dict comprehensions over an `Own[T]`-yielding source now move a bare owned element -- the comprehension's last-evaluated sink (list/set element, dict value, including the filtered `[x for x in g() if p(x)]` shape) moves unconditionally (`_comp_elem_moves(is_last_sink=True)`, `_move_comp_sink`). Two gaps remain: (a) **consuming a temporary owned container** like `[w for w in [a, b]]` where `a`/`b` are owned locals -- iterating the fresh list literal does not yield `Own[T]` (the elements are borrows of the temp's slots), so the move-gate (`gen.owns_elements`) does not fire and the elements copy. Closing it needs consuming-iteration of the temporary source (move out of `[a, b]`), distinct from the Own-yielding-source path already handled; (b) a **sub-expression sink inside a filtered comprehension** -- `[take(node) for node in g() if p(node)]` where `node` is consumed by `take`'s `Own[T]` param: the element is the call result (not the bare loop var), so the move runs through `_maybe_move`'s `all_last_uses` gate, which the filter's extra `node` occurrence defeats, so `node` copies into `take`. Rare (the bare-var last-sink cases are covered); closing it needs the sub-expression move to recognize the post-filter last use too. Surfaced by the owned-Optional-local-move-out feature and the comprehension-sink move work.
- **[LOW] Materialize the comprehension bare-loop-var sink fact instead of re-deriving it in sema and codegen.** `_comp_elem_moves` (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`) and `_move_comp_sink` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) each re-derive "this sink is the bare loop var" with the same `TpyCoerce`-unwrap + `inner.name == gen.var` shape check. The two are adjacent and well-commented (and gate on the same `is_last_sink`/`all_last_uses` facts, so they don't drift today), but per the CLAUDE.md "prefer first-class AST facts over consumer-side re-derivation" direction this could be one shared util (or a fact on the comprehension node set during sema). Low urgency; THIR lowering will force it. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit, codegen-correctness) of the dict-comprehension move.
- **Match codegen `_SwitchEntry` tuple -> dataclass.** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py`'s `_SwitchEntry` is now an 8-field positional tuple (`guard, body, cap, as, raw_names, loc, type_facts, bind_modes`) unpacked at ~15 sites; growing it positionally is fragile (a file-local `@dataclass` already exists as precedent). Convert to a dataclass so field adds don't touch every unpack. Pre-existing debt, widened by the match-binding-form fact. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the match scalar-copy fix.
- **Match guarded-switch dedups bindings using the first entry's bind mode.** In `_emit_switch_groups`' multi-entry guarded arm, a binding shared across same-label entries is emitted once with `entries[0]`'s `bind_modes`; correct today because sema enforces same-value/same-label arms have a compatible binding type, so the free-copy-scalar verdict is identical across entries. Latent if that sema rule ever loosens to allow heterogeneous types per label. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the match scalar-copy fix.
- **Borrow-alias branch-decl follow-ups (from the @error_return borrow-alias fix).** (a) Prescan classifies a borrow-returning `TpyMethodCall` as an rvalue, so a borrow-rebound local still gets `std::optional<T>` rebind slots that the alias path never touches (dead stack; see `__slot_1`/`__slot_3` in `error_return/borrow_result_source_intact` generated code) -- teach the prescan rvalue classification the `call_returns_cpp_ref` distinction or drop the slots post-hoc. (b) Test gaps: a dict/set borrow from an `@error_return` callable (list+record covered); a borrow-then-borrow rebind (two branches binding different borrow sources, mutate after, observe the right source); a match-capture of a non-value subject guarding the `nonstmt_bound_names` exclusion (existing match tests pass with zero churn, a weak implicit guard only). (c) Const-predicate drift: sema's `record_stmt_borrow_binding` (`sema/context.py`) computes borrow const from ReadonlyType + readonly-fi only, while codegen's `_is_const_indirect` additionally consults codegen-populated const sets (`deep_const_borrow_params`, `const_ref_params`); a mismatch is a LOUD C++ compile error (`&const` into `T*`), not silent -- unify when those const facts move sema-side. (d) Single-target for-loop vars are not in `nonstmt_bound_names` (only with-as/unpack/match are); a loop-var-only name never enters the stmt-borrow fact so today's behavior is correct, but a name that is BOTH a loop var and stmt-borrow-bound elsewhere in the function now gets cross-statement pointer-form leakage at the loop pre-decl (the deleted walk was stmt-scoped); decide whether loop vars should be marked (which would change the old in-body-reassign behavior) or the fact made per-stmt. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the borrow-alias fix and the borrow-fact materialization.
- **Unify cross-module C++-name qualification (sema overlay vs codegen's native_cpp_names).** `_cross_module_cpp_overlay` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) re-derives a slice of codegen's `native_cpp_names` population (`generator.py`, ~130 lines) to qualify cross-module records at sema-time cpp_template rendering -- the two paths can drift (the @native subcase already had to be added to match codegen). Root cause is that `NominalType.to_cpp()` renders cross-module names via a codegen-populated ContextVar map, and sema renders C++ before that map exists. THIR-aligned end-state: push cross-module qualification out of `to_cpp` into a shared renderer that takes an explicit module-qualification context, so both phases call one path. Pre-existing smell (sema renders C++ at all); IR-migration-adjacent. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of `fix-cpp-template-cross-ns-qualify`.
- **Cross-module cpp_template qualification: `set`/`dict`/`{cpp}` coverage.** The sema overlay that qualifies cross-module element types in `@cpp_template` substitution is covered for `list()` (`imports/list_cross_module_own_iter`) and the same-module non-regression (`iterators/list_same_module_own_iter`). Untested through the same code path: `set(cross_module_iter())` (`set_construct<{T}>`), `dict(...)` (`dict_construct<{K},{V}>` -- the two-param path), and the `{cpp}` substitution branch (`result_type.to_cpp()`) with a cross-module result type. Same `{T}`/`{cpp}` render, lower risk, but a regression would silently emit unqualified C++. Also a cross-module `@native` record in a cpp_template arg (the overlay now mirrors codegen's native_name handling, but it's untested). Add guards when next touching this area. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage + architecture-fit) of `fix-cpp-template-cross-ns-qualify`.
- **`heapq.merge` manual-append guard for a reference-type `Own[T]` yield.** `tests/cases/stdlib/heapq_merge` exercises the reference-type (`Item`) yield only via `list(merge(...))`; the `for x in merge(...): r.append(x)` manual-append path is covered for value-type `Int32` but not for a reference-type `Own[T]` element, so a codegen regression in that append branch would be invisible. Add a small ref-type manual-append case. `list()` already covers the same yield boundary, so this is guard-completeness, not a known gap. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the merge `Iterator[Own[T]]` flip.
- **`Iterable[Own[T]]` conformance test-coverage gaps.** The Own-symmetric-strip fix is covered by `iterators/own_iter_to_iterable` (`list()` + a user `Iterable[Own[T]]` param fed an `Iterator[Own[T]]`) and `iterators/iter_copy_into_owned_warns` (the still-warns guard for a non-Own actual). Untested: `set(iter_of_own)` / `dict(iter_of_own_pairs)` sinks, and the concrete-container exact-match branch in `compatibility.py` with an actual carrying `Own` in a type arg (e.g. `dict[K, Own[V]]`). Add when next touching that area. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the symmetric-strip fix.
- **Share the receiver-mutation marking between method calls, `await`, and `with`.** `_mark_await_operand_mutated` (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`) and `_mark_with_manager_mutated` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) both copy MethodAnalyzer's non-readonly-receiver marking (the `mark_loop_var_mutated` + `mark_param_mutated` + `mark_all_view_borrowers_mutated` sequence in `methods.py`) -- `await x` is an `x.__poll__(waker)` call and `with mgr:` is an `mgr.__enter__()/__exit__()` pair, for mutation purposes. Extract a shared `ctx.mark_receiver_mutated(obj_root)` all three sites call so the sequence can't drift. Also `_root_name_of_expr` is local-imported inside `_mark_await_operand_mutated` (and `_register_call_result_borrow` inside `_analyze_named_expr`) to dodge the `expressions` <-> `statements` circular import; move them to a shared util (e.g. `sema/context.py` or a small `sema/expr_roots.py`) so both import at top level. Low value; do when next touching any site. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the await-self-mutation fix and the with-manager-borrow fix.
- **Fold `with_borrowed_fields` into the `with_fields` tuple (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/resumable_cfg.py`).** The async with-region prescan tracks borrowed (`T*`) vs owning (`frame_slot<T>`) context-manager frame fields via a parallel `with_borrowed_fields: set[str]` keyed by field name, alongside `with_fields: list[tuple[str, str]]`. A string-keyed side-set is the "string-key => not designed yet" smell; widen `with_fields` to `list[tuple[str, str, bool]]` (or a named triple) so the borrow flag is self-contained and the `fname in with_borrowed_fields` membership test at emit time disappears. Both are populated in the same prescan loop, so no ordering obstacle. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the with-manager-borrow fix.
- **Disentangle `FunctionInfo.is_readonly` for callable-value calls (`tpyc/sema/methods.py`, `tpyc/sema/calls.py`).** The synthetic FunctionInfo for a callable-value call is `is_readonly=False` so the borrow/mutation consumers treat the callback's ARGS as mutable -- but `is_readonly` is also read as "the RECEIVER may be mutated", so `methods.py` needs two `and not expr.is_callable_field` guards (at the readonly-receiver rejection and the receiver-mutation marking) to stop a callable field from demoting its holder. Each new `is_readonly` consumer that cares about receiver vs arg mutation will need another `is_callable_field` patch. Cleaner: split the two facts -- either a separate `receiver_readonly` flag on FunctionInfo, or set the synthetic FI `is_readonly=True` and carry arg-mutation conservatism through a dedicated channel. Relatedly, `analyze_callable_value_call`'s eager opaque-callee mutation loop re-expresses the per-param filtering (`ReadonlyType` / `param_has_mutable_borrow_surface` / `TypeParamRef`) that the call-edge recorder already has; an `_eager_mark_opaque_callee_mutations(expr, param_types)` helper would give the "opaque mutating callee" pattern (also wanted by unknown-body constructors) one home. Effort: S. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit + safety-model) of the callable-values fix.
- **Borrow-conflict warning is not last-use aware (`BorrowTracker.has_element_borrow`, `tpyc/sema/context.py:271`).** The "passing borrowed container to non-readonly parameter" warning (`_check_borrow_arg_conflicts`) fires whenever the container has ANY element/iter/ptr borrower registered in scope, regardless of whether that borrow is still live at the call site. So `p = xs[0]; print(p.v); mutate(xs)` warns even though `p`'s last read precedes the call and the mutation is provably safe -- a false positive. Sound (it never misses a real cross-call invalidation, only over-approximates), but noisy on the dead-borrow shape, which is common (`tests/cases/calls/warn_callable_borrow_invalidation` exercises exactly it). **Fix shape:** gate the warning on the borrow being live across the call -- intersect `has_element_borrow` with the existing last-use set (`all_last_uses`), or expire element borrows from the BorrowTracker at the borrower's last use. The latter is the cleaner end-state and aligns with the MIR loan-expiry model; the former is a cheap intersection at the warning site. Either way verify it doesn't suppress the genuine live-across-call case (read after the call). Effort: S. Surfaced reviewing the callable-values warning coverage.
