- **Callable-value test backlog (`tests/cases/calls/`).** Coverage additions from the callable-values fix, none guarding a known live bug: (a) `error_` case for the readonly-contract rejection from the callable-value direction -- a function with a non-readonly list param stored into a `Callable[[readonly[list[T]]], None]` field/param should reject at the store; (b) a `Fn[[T], R]` generic combinator forwarding a reference-typed arg, asserting it does NOT over-warn (the generic-slot eager-mark exemption); (c) return-covariance positive test (callee returns a concrete subtype, contract expects a supertype -- current variance tests use `None` returns on both sides); (d) a mutate-through-and-read-back case for the `star_unpack_generic_fn` / generic `Callable[[Box[T]], R]` shape (the const->mutable flip is currently exercised read-only); (e) a Callable-field-from-`@readonly`-method happy path (the `is_callable_field` receiver exemption is exercised only indirectly). Effort: S. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage + cpython-parity) of the callable-values fix.
- **Self-iterator `Iterable[T]`-param test-coverage gaps (`tests/cases/iterators/gen_selfiter_param`).** The resumable self-iterator fix (drive a move-only generator source in place rather than copying it into the iterator slot) is covered for a sync generator consuming a generator via a for-loop + comprehension, a temporary source, and the `Iterator[T]`/list inverses. Untested, none guarding a known bug: (a) an **async** consumer (`async def f(it: Iterable[T]): for x in it: ...; f(gen())`) -- low priority, the async coro's *sync* `for` reuses the identical `resumable_iter_*` emit already covered by the sync test, so this is guard-completeness for the 8 regenerated `async_*proto_param*` snapshots, not an untested path; (b) a generator **method** consuming an `Iterable[T]` whose arg is a self-iterator (the `record_name` frame adds a `__self` field + distinct struct name); (c) **two-level chaining** (`take(take(repeat_n(...), m), n)`) -- a resumable generator consuming another resumable generator; (d) an **empty** source (`times=0`, loop body never entered). Add when next touching this area. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the self-iterator-copy fix.
- **Simple-generator peephole codegen hygiene (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_generators.py`).** Two cleanups surfaced by /tpy-review of the peephole fixes (the third -- the duplicated optional-wrap emit that produced the missed-`yld` Critical -- is now consolidated into `_emit_iter_slot_return`): (1) `_gen_simple_while_generator` / `_gen_simple_for_generator` manually save/restore `self.ctx.current_ns` (and `generator_self_ref`) around the loop-body emit -- hand-written save/restore pairs that are easy to desync; fold setup+teardown into a small context manager (or a `_with_body_scope(...)`) that owns both. `_setup_body_scope` leaving `current_ns` set as a returned side effect is part of the same smell. (2) `_yield_optional_arg` derives the "move this owned yield" decision from the `val_binding` string (`"auto"` vs `"auto&&"`) plus an `OwnType` check; `val_binding` encodes the same fact as the canonical `yield_uses_borrow_slot(elem_type)` (for `OwnType` always `False`), so the string guard is redundant -- route through the canonical predicate. Neither is blocking; do before the next peephole-touching change.
- **`match` sequence patterns (`case [a, b]:` / `case (a, b):`).** The biggest user-expected pattern-matching gap and fully feasible in TPy's static model: `tuple` has compile-time arity, `list`/`Array`/`Span` need a length check + element bind, `str`/`bytes` excluded (matches CPython). No representation change, no RTTI. Listed under `MATCH_CASE_DESIGN.md` "Future Extensions"; this is the one worth scheduling first (mapping `{"k": v}` and star `[x, *rest]` patterns can follow -- star needs a rest-binding decision: sub-tuple for tuples, view-vs-copy for `list`/`Span`). Steering note: prefer growing `match`'s pattern surface over back-porting expression subjects into `isinstance`, so each surface keeps a clear job (isinstance = bare-name test-and-narrow; match = expression-subject test-and-destructure).
- **Checked downcast for polymorphic / `@dynamic` sources.** Today the only runtime downcast is `isinstance`-narrowing inside a branch; there is no one-shot "assert this is a `Dog` or panic." Feasible by mirroring `any_cast_or_panic` (emit a checked `dynamic_cast` / `dyn_adapter_cast`). Open decision: `typing.cast(T, x)` is a static no-op in CPython, so overloading it to mean "checked downcast" diverges -- a distinct spelling (e.g. a `downcast()` helper) may be cleaner. Pairs with the `Any`-extraction path that `cast()` already has. Lower priority than sequence patterns; revisit when a real use appears.
- **Materialize vararg slot const-ness on `TpyVarargPack` at sync time.** `_gen_vararg_pack` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:5308`) takes an optional `slot_type` and re-derives whether to wrap the element type in `ReadonlyType` at codegen by combining the pack's substituted base with the slot's current readonly-ness. Cleaner THIR-aligned shape: when `_sync_inferred_vararg_readonly` flips a slot, also walk the module's call sites and update `TpyVarargPack.element_type` for each call to that callee -- codegen then reads a single fact, no consumer-side re-derivation. Needs a TpyCall/TpyMethodCall walker that knows which packs target which callee FI. Not blocking; the current shape works.
- **Auto-readonly inference for `*args` (parallel to non-vararg auto-const).** Non-vararg reference-type params auto-const-infer based on body mutation (`def take_one(item: Box)` with a non-mutating body emits `const Box& item`); varargs don't -- `*items: Box` always emits `tpy::varargs<Box>` (mutable elements) regardless of whether the body mutates. The asymmetry is the underlying reason the caller-side marking fix landed on `fix-vararg-const-arg`: a non-mutating mutable vararg slot forces every caller to provide non-const sources, instead of the callee's slot collapsing to `varargs<const T>`. The proper symmetric fix is to track vararg-element mutation in the body (mirror the param-mutation tracking for non-vararg params) and have `_vararg_span_type` / the codegen render path emit `varargs[readonly[T]]` / `varargs<const T>` for inferred-readonly slots. That subsumes both the recent caller-marking fix (which becomes unnecessary for non-mutating callees) and the const-source family in BUGS.md. Real feature work -- design surface (interaction with the explicit `*items: readonly[T]` form, with generic varargs, with mutation propagation), and many existing snapshots would shift (mutable vararg signatures flip to `varargs<const T>` for non-mutating bodies). Probably warrants `/tpy-add-feature`. Surfaced when reviewing the `fix-vararg-const-arg` test outputs.
- **Mutating property getter has no escape hatch.** Every `@property` getter is forced `auto_readonly` (`tpyc/parse/parser.py` sets `auto_readonly=True` on getters unconditionally), so the getter *body* cannot mutate `self` or call a non-readonly method -- a lazy-memoizing getter (`if not self._cached: self._cached = compute()`) is rejected. CPython getters mutate freely. (Cloning an `Rc` from a getter body now works -- `Rc.clone` is `@auto_readonly` since the interior-`_cell` change -- so that is no longer an example of the gap; body-side mutation of a plain field still is.) (Returning a mutable object from a getter and modifying *that* DOES work -- auto_readonly's dual const/mutable overloads hand back a mutable reference on a mutable receiver; the gap is purely body-side mutation.) Possible: a `@mutating_property` / non-const-getter form, or a getter-body opt-out of auto_readonly. Interacts with the auto_readonly dual-overload clone (probably `/tpy-add-feature`). Effort: M.
- **Vararg address-take marking: consolidate sites + use `effective_storage`.** Five sites in `tpyc/sema/{calls.py,compatibility.py}` share the "this expression's address is taken into a `T*` slot, so the source must be a non-const lvalue -- mark its root mutated" pattern: `_validate_lvalue_params` (~calls.py:2549), `coerce_expr`'s `record_to_ptr`/`upcast_to_ptr` (compatibility.py:1252), the `value_ptr_coercion` path (~calls.py:4606), and the two vararg packing branches added on the readonly-varargs / fix-vararg-const-arg work (`_analyze_and_pack_varargs` non-unpack and *unpack branches). A small `_mark_addr_taken_root` helper on `CallAnalyzer` would tighten them. Additionally, the two vararg branches use raw `_root_name_of_expr(arg)` rather than `borrow_tracker.effective_storage(...)` -- the other three sites resolve alias chains first, so the vararg pair currently misses element/field/ptr borrows back to their source param. No real bug surfaced yet, but the asymmetry will rot. THIR-friendly final form: tag `TpyVarargPack` with `address_takes_elements: bool` at construction so codegen + the mark sites consult a single fact instead of each re-deriving from `elem_type.is_value_type() and not TypeParamRef`.
- **Codegen-side vararg lvalue/hoist dispatch consolidation.** `_gen_vararg_pack`'s non-unpack branch (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:5337`) now classifies each arg as lvalue/rvalue inline (`TpyName`/`TpyFieldAccess`/`TpySubscript` after `TpyCoerce` peel) to decide between direct `&arg` and temp-hoist. The classifier duplicates `_match_subject_is_lvalue` in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py:25`. Two follow-ups: (a) push the per-arg hoist decision back to sema as `TpyVarargPack.args_need_hoist: list[bool]` -- THIR-aligned, single source of truth (codegen reads, doesn't re-derive a structural fact); (b) extract a shared `codegen_cpp/lvalue.py:is_addressable_lvalue` helper once a third caller appears. Worth doing before the next varargs-touching feature. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-vararg-generic-ref`.
- **Resumable generator yielding `tuple[..., RefType]` with 2+ yields.** No test exercises a resumable-frame generator (multi-yield) yielding a tuple that contains a pointer-form element -- the interaction of the new borrow-form-loop-var deref at the yield-return and `_maybe_wrap_tuple_to_pointer`. The tuple path is unchanged by the deref fix (gated on `generator_borrow_form_loop_vars`, scalar names only), so it's coverage hardening, not a known defect. Add under `tests/cases/iterators/`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the generic-vararg-iteration work.
- **Materialize the owning-tuple generator-local fact instead of re-deriving it in codegen.** `CodeGenContext.owning_generator_tuple_locals` body-scans `func.body` to classify which resumable-frame tuple locals are owning (`Own[tuple]` call, never reassigned) vs aliasing, and is called twice per async function (the struct field-decl in `gen_async.py` and `setup_resumable_frame_locals`). Sema already tracks the owning fact in `owning_storage_tuple_vars`; THIR-aligned shape: materialize an owning-tuple-locals set on `TpyFunction` (analogous to `generator_locals`) so codegen reads one fact, no body rescan or double-derivation. Not blocking. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the tuple owning/alias-mix work.
- **Materialize the borrow-alias frame-local classification on the AST instead of the codegen prescan.** `_classify_pointer_alias_locals` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py`) body-scans the resumable body to classify which frame locals are borrow aliases (single-assign of an lvalue, tuple-unpack `is_ref` targets from a safe source) -> `T*`/`const T*` frame slots, re-deriving the borrow-vs-own decision via `_is_plain_nonvalue` / `is_rvalue_source` / `_is_const_indirect`. Same family as the owning-tuple item above: sema's `_analyze_var_decl` / `_analyze_tuple_unpack` already decide indirection (`ref_bound_locals` / `pointer_locals`); THIR-aligned shape is an `is_borrow_alias` (+ const) fact on `TpyVarDecl` / per-target on `TpyTupleUnpack` so codegen reads one fact. The for-loop `pointer_form_unpack_targets` machinery is also a codegen prescan, so the eventual unification covers both. Not blocking. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the borrow-alias-across-suspension fix.
- **THIR: consult the `native_field_name` rename fact in field-access lowering.** The networking-parity branch materialized `TpyFieldAccess.native_field_name` (sema-resolved `native_field()` rename, inherited through TPy-level subclasses of `@native` records -- e.g. `SocketError.errno` -> `error_number`); the AST emit reads it, but THIR's `THIRFieldAccess`/`THIRMilInit` construction sites use `escape_cpp_name(e.field)` and `_field_markers_clean` doesn't gate on the fact. `_f1_record`'s docstring assumes the F1 slice sees "no native name/field rename" -- falsified now that plain same-module records (`SocketError`, `gaierror`, user `OSError` subclasses) inherit renamed fields; the shape sits in the `_scalar_field_write_ok` slice. LATENT today: the whole-corpus byte-diff gate is green (nothing currently routes a renamed access), and if routing grows to reach one, the gate fails loudly naming the function. Fix is one line per site (`e.native_field_name or e.field`) or a marker-gate entry. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion on the networking-parity branch.
- **Fold the `no_source_comment` AST flag into a synthetic-statement / comment-provenance model.** `TpyStmt.no_source_comment` (`tpyc/parse/nodes.py`) is a codegen-*presentation* flag on the shared statement base: a multi-statement desugar (tuple-literal unpack today) sets it on all but the first lowered statement so the shared source comment emits once. It works and is minimal, but a presentation hint on the AST base accretes -- the next desugar wanting "emit this synthetic statement without a comment" reaches for the same boolean with no single owner. THIR-aligned shape: a synthetic-statement provenance notion (which source statement a lowered statement came from) that the comment emitter consults, so N lowered statements share one source-comment token rather than each carrying a suppression bool. Transitional until then. Not blocking. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion on the tuple-literal-unpack desugar.
- **Only hoist resumable-frame locals that are live across a suspension.** `_collect_generator_locals` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py:1250`) hoists EVERY function-level local into a frame field -- its only exclusions are params, `self`, and `global`-declared names -- so a local that is born and dies entirely within one straight-line segment (no `await`/`yield` between its last write and last read) still becomes a frame field. The docstring claims "locals living across a yield/await suspension become frame fields", but there is no cross-suspension liveness filter; the claim is aspirational. Example: a tuple-literal unpack desugared in an async body emits hidden `__unpack_N_*` temps that bind the targets before the first `await` and are dead by the suspension, yet they appear as `T* __unpack_0_0 = nullptr;` frame fields (`tests/cases/async/tuple_literal_unpack_alias_across_await`). Cost is frame-size only (extra pointer/value slots, written once) -- zero runtime cost -- so not blocking. Proper fix: compute per-local cross-suspension liveness (the resumable CFG in `resumable_cfg.py` has the suspension points) and field only locals live across >=1 suspension; the rest stay block-scoped locals in their segment. Correctness-sensitive (a local used after a suspension that is wrongly NOT fielded becomes stale/UAF), so design it as its own `/tpy-add-feature` pass, not an inline tweak. Benefits all async/generator bodies, not just unpacks. Surfaced reviewing the tuple-literal-unpack desugar's async frame fields.
- **Materialize alias-local const-ness as a sema fact instead of re-deriving the source's const-ness in codegen.** `_is_const_indirect` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`) decides a non-value alias local binds `const` by inspecting whether its init reads a const-inferred source (name in `const_ref_params` / `const_indirect_locals` / `deep_const_borrow_params`), and the `T&`-branch propagation at the call site (statements.py ~2119-2139) re-derives the same for subscript/name sources. The two are NOT redundant today -- `_is_const_indirect` tests the un-unwrapped `TpyName` and adds `deep_const_borrow_params`, while the inline `T&`-branch block unwraps `TpyCoerce` and handles subscript -- so neither can be deleted in isolation; they need manual lockstep if either const set grows. This is the consumer-side re-derivation the IR migration aims to remove: sema already knows (post-Phase-2) whether the alias local is ever mutated through its borrow chain, so the const verdict should be a single fact on `TpyVarDecl` (and per-target on `TpyTupleUnpack`) that codegen reads once, replacing both sites. Folds into the `is_borrow_alias (+ const)` fact in the item above. Not blocking. Surfaced completing the single-assign alias const-precision fix (BUGS.md const-source family).
- **Tuple-literal-unpack desugar test-coverage gaps.** The aliasing desugar (`a, b = (e0, e1)` -> per-element temps + binds) is covered for sync function and async bodies, but: (a) no snippet exercises it inside a SYNC GENERATOR (`yield`) body, where the temps/binds interact with the resumable-frame ABI (pointer-form frame locals, frame-slot hoist) -- add a `tests/cases/iterators/` case that aliases a reference element across a yield and observes a mutation at the source; (b) the const-precision half (`_is_const_indirect` const-inferred Optional/pointer-local source) is regression-guarded only by the `warn_auto_move_optional` snapshot -- add an exec case where a read-only `Optional[T]` alias is passed to a `@readonly` method, so a silently-dropped `const` becomes a C++ build failure; (c) add a `TestDesugarShape` unit case for an arity-mismatched tuple-literal RHS (`a, b = (Int32(1),)`) to pin that the length guard skips the desugar (falls through to TpyTupleUnpack + sema arity error). Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage).
- **Tuple-literal-unpack desugar does not reach macro-generated statement fragments.** `parse_fragment(kind="statements")` parses via `_parse_body` at `_fn_body_depth == 0`, so a flat tuple-literal unpack emitted by a macro fragment won't desugar (stays a copying TpyTupleUnpack). No current macro emits such a fragment; revisit if the macro API grows one. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit).
- **Generator-delegation limitation family: unified iterator-source typing.** All current delegation/iteration rejections in resumable frames share one root cause -- the frame must embed the source *by value*, and several source shapes have no spellable (or finite) C++ type. One work item lifts them together; the individual symptoms stay tracked where filed:
  - *Cross-module callee* rejected (BUGS.md MED small; `error_gen_delegate_cross_module`).
  - *Protocol-param callee* rejected -- the callee struct carries deduced `T_<pname>` template args the field type can't spell (`error_gen_delegate_proto_param`; tracked only here).
  - *Generator method on a generic class* rejected -- owner template args unspelled (`error_gen_delegate_generic_method`; tracked only here).
  - *Local bound to a generator call* rejected across a suspension (BUGS.md LOW small) -- same spelling gap at the hoisted-local emit.
  - *Named `Iterator[T]` source with non-value T* spells an ill-formed `std::expected<T&, ...>` result slot (BUGS.md LOW small).
  - *Bounded recursive delegation* (tree walks) rejected -- infinite-size embedded frame, needs heap indirection, not spelling (noted in the BUGS.md cross-module entry).

  Fix direction, two prongs: (1) a single "iterator-source C++ type" resolver used by `__for_src`, the hoisted-local emit, and the `__for_r` slot -- `std::decay_t<decltype(<qualified-callee>(<declval per param>))>` works cross-module (the callee's `inline auto` definition or concrete declaration is in the included header) and deduces generic / `T_<pname>` template args by overload resolution, subsuming the struct-name spelling for everything except protocol-typed *param* declvals (concepts can't declval -- derive from the arg's concrete type instead); (2) an opt-in boxed `__for_src` (heap-indirected sub-frame) for recursive delegation, which is a small feature of its own. Doing (1) retires the first five bullets in one pass; warrants `/tpy-add-feature`. Surfaced consolidating the fix-generator-for-src limitation set. **Blocks `itertools.compress`** (`for d, s in zip(data, selectors): ...` -- a for-loop with `yield` over a cross-module `zip` Iterator is rejected by exactly this limitation).
- **Cross-kind resumable-ordering test: `async def` consuming a sync generator call.** The unified `_emit_resumable_structs` sort orders coro structs after the sync generator structs they embed (`for x in gen_call():` with `await` in the body allocates a `__for_src` field typed as the generator struct), but no test exercises the cross-kind edge -- the coro-before-generator seed order would mask a missing edge until declaration order flips it. Add under `tests/cases/async/`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-generator-for-src`.
- **Two-consumer forced-callee test.** A simple-shaped generator delegated to by two independent resumable consumers is forced off the peephole once and embedded twice; no test pins the multi-consumer path of `_prescan_for_src_embedding`. Add under `tests/cases/iterators/`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-generator-for-src`.
- **Unify `sema/compatibility.py::is_lvalue` with `codegen_cpp/context.py::is_temporary_expr`.** Logical complements classifying the same expression taxonomy with one diverging TpyCoerce sub-case; the for-src materialization work deepened the codegen-side usage. A shared lvalue predicate would keep the two from drifting. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-generator-for-src`.
- **Shared coro-param frame-slot classification predicate.** `tpyc/sema/frame_traits.py::param_slot` is a hand-maintained conservative mirror of `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py::_classify_param_kind`: every new coro param storage shape must be added to both, and a missed sema-side update silently classifies the new shape non-Send (the safe direction, but degrades Send coverage). Extract a shared classification (a kind enum or an "is stored owned" predicate over types) both sides consult -- natural to fold into the generator-onto-resumable-frame migration, which restructures the codegen side anyway. While there, refine the over-conservative (never-unsound) cases that degrade Send coverage: (a) value-type `Optional`/`Union` slots (`Int32 | None` lowers to owned `std::optional<int32_t>` but classifies non-Send); (b) `_value_slot_traits` gates the tuple-borrow check on `TupleType.has_ref_elements()`, wider than codegen's actual borrow test `has_pointer_repr_element()` -- a tuple with a *union* element is stored owned by codegen but classified non-Send; (c) **str/bytes params are now stored OWNED in the frame** (codegen `OWNED_COPY` / the simple-generator owned init-capture, gated on `is_owned_in_coro_frame`), but `param_slot` still classifies a `str` param by `StrView` traits (non-Send) -- so an owned-str/bytes-param coro/generator frame is conservatively non-Send when it is actually Send. The shared predicate to consult here is `is_owned_in_coro_frame` (already used by codegen + `generator_borrow_param_indices`); `param_slot` should classify those by the owned type's traits. All three are safe-direction; tightening any is the risky direction (a wrong tightening reintroduces a false-Send), so verify against codegen shapes, not casually. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `send-sync-phase2` (a,b) and the str/bytes coro-param owned-copy fix (c).
- **Share the sync `_gen_call` arg-coercion pipeline with async `_gen_coro_emplace_arg`.** `_gen_coro_emplace_arg` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py`) hand-mirrors a subset of sync's `_gen_call` arg cascade -- `_gen_optional_ptr_arg`, `_gen_dynamic_protocol_arg`, `_gen_union_arg`, then `gen_call_arg` -- but omits `_gen_protocol_arg` (sync's nullable/multi static-protocol coercion). Not a reachable defect today: nullable/multi-protocol static-protocol async params are rejected up front by `_protocol_template_parts` (gen_async.py:458, clean "not yet supported" diagnostic), single required protocols flow through the `gen_call_arg` fallthrough, and rvalue ref-param temps are now hoisted by `_lift_borrowed_rvalue_args`. The hazard is drift: the manual mirror can silently fall behind when sync gains a new coercion. THIR-friendly final form: one shared arg-coercion entry point both the sync call site and the async emplace path call, so neither re-derives the cascade. Touches the hot sync `_gen_call` path -- warrants its own `/tpy-fix-bug`/`/tpy-add-feature` pass, not an inline patch. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-async-emplace-rvalue-dangle` (was the "shape half" of a BUGS.md entry whose silent-miscompile framing didn't hold up once the lifetime half was fixed and the protocol case shown to be guarded).
- **Async-def factory param for an `Own[T]` param emits `T` by-value, not `own_param_t<T>`.** An `async def` method/function with an `Own[T]` param (e.g. `asyncio.Queue.put(self, item: Own[T])`) generates the coro-factory wrapper param as `T item` rather than `::tpy::own_param_t<T>` -- unlike the sync side and unlike `put_nowait`/`get_nowait` which use `own_param_t<T>`/`own_return_t<T>`. Non-failing today: `Own[T]` args are moved (not copied), so `@nocopy`/reference element types still work (`Queue[Box[Int32]]` compiles + runs). It's a codegen consistency gap that could bite a future genuinely-uncopyable-AND-unmovable type, or diverge from the sync param shape in some edge. Fix: route the async factory's param lowering through the same `own_param_t<T>` path the sync method path uses. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `asyncio.Queue`.
- **Unify MRO-bound-ancestor resolution between `coro_struct_owner` and `protocols.py::resolve_ancestor_instantiation`.** Both walk `RecordInfo.mro_ancestors` to find the bound ancestor `NominalType` for a given defining record. They differ in how they match (`coro_struct_owner` in `tpyc/typesys.py` matches by qname string and stays registry-free; `resolve_ancestor_instantiation` uses a registry for per-ancestor `get_record_for_type` identity checks). Not unifiable as-is, but if the MRO-rebase pattern grows a third caller, hoist a single qname-based free function into `typesys.py` both consult. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the generic-base inherited-async fix.
- **`asyncio.BoundedSemaphore.__init__` duplicates `Semaphore.__init__` instead of `super().__init__()`.** The subclass re-runs the `value < 0` guard and re-initializes `_value`/`_waiters` by hand (then sets `_bound`), because TPy's MI `super()` support is only partial. If `Semaphore` ever gains a field, `BoundedSemaphore` silently misses it. Low-risk today (2-field parent); switch to `super().__init__(value)` once MI super() resolution is dependable. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `asyncio.BoundedSemaphore`.
- **Support nullable / multi-protocol static-protocol params on `async def`.** `async def f(x: SomeProto | None)` / `async def f(x: ProtoA | ProtoB)` (static protocols) is rejected at codegen (`_protocol_template_parts`, gen_async.py:458) -- only single required static protocols like `Own[Awaitable[T]]` are supported in coro frames. The sync side handles these via `_gen_protocol_arg`; lifting the async restriction needs the coro struct's template-header + frame-field shape to encode the nullable/multi-protocol case, plus the matching emplace-arg coercion (see the pipeline-sharing item above). Feature work, no current demand; the diagnostic is clean so it's not blocking. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-async-emplace-rvalue-dangle`.
- **Group generator-frame-state save/restore in `gen_async.py`.** The generator-body scope contextmanager manually saves/resets/restores ~12 fields (`generator_field_names`, `generator_optional_fields`, `generator_frame_slot_locals`, `generator_borrow_form_loop_vars`, `generator_for_loop_info`, ...) at three separate sites with no mechanical check that all three were updated -- each new frame-scoped field extends the list in three places. Collapse the generator-specific fields into a single `GeneratorFrameState` dataclass with one save/restore pair. (These fields are populated mid-setup by `setup_resumable_frame_locals`, so they stay mutable sets -- not a frozen snapshot; the win is the single save/restore pair, not immutability.) Not blocking; do before the next frame field lands. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the yield-deref narrowing. **Related incomplete-save/restore hazard** (surfaced by /tpy-review of the await-arg field-type-spelling fix): `_resumable_frame_ctx` routes through `setup_body_scope` -> `reset_scope`, which wipes ~30 scope fields (`declared_vars`, `var_types`, `optional_locals`, `ptr_variant_locals`, `slots`, `reassigned_vars`, ...), but the ctx manager only saves/restores ~12 of them. Harmless today because the only build-time entry (`_build_resumable_cfg` wrapping `builder.build()` in the ctx so the await-arg field-type `gen_expr` gets `self`->`__self` / `frame_slot` deref) runs at top level where those fields are empty, and the CFG is cached. But it is not nesting-safe: a future call site that builds a resumable CFG from *inside* an active body-emit scope would silently corrupt the outer scope's narrowing/declared-var state. Fix: make `_resumable_frame_ctx` save/restore the full `reset_scope` field set (the durable root fix; preferred over relocating the build-time entry, which does not remove the build-vs-emit phase mixing anyway).
- **`_is_templated_coro` omits `_fn_template_parts` despite its "single source of truth" docstring (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py`).** The predicate decides header-vs-cpp placement for coro/generator structs from `func.type_params`, `_protocol_template_parts`, and the enclosing record's params -- but NOT `_fn_template_parts`, so an `Fn`-only generator (no type/protocol params) returns `False`. Behavior is currently correct: such generators land in the header via another path (verified -- single-module AND cross-module `Fn`-only generators compile/run, nothing leaks to the `.cpp`), so this is a latent inconsistency, not a live defect. The hazard is a future refactor consolidating placement on this predicate would reintroduce a cross-module gap (an `Fn` generator's template body emitted out-of-line in the definer's `.cpp`, un-instantiable by importers). Fix: add `or bool(self._fn_template_parts(func))` once the placement paths are understood/consolidated (verify no snapshot churn -- should be a no-op for current placement). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the Fn-param-in-generator fix.
- **Borrow-form-yield-deref regression guard (BLOCKED on the bare-Optional-yield compile gap).** The yield-deref is gated on `generator_borrow_form_loop_vars` specifically so pointer-repr Optional/Union frame locals are NOT deref'd (a broad `pointer_locals` gate would `(*name)`-strip their None/variant case -- a Critical caught in review). Today the only guard against re-widening that gate is the comment at `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` `gen_yield_value`; a real regression test (a resumable generator yielding a bare `P | None` local) can't be green because direct `yield opt` hits the pre-existing bare-Optional-yield compile gap (see the `std::expected<std::optional<P>, ...>` narrowing entry in BUGS.md). Add the guard once that gap is fixed. Surfaced by /tpy-review test-coverage.
- **Reference-element exec coverage for generic generator/vararg iteration.** `gen_generic_for` only instantiates the generic generator with a value-type `T` (int) at exec; `varargs_generic_iter_ref` only does pass-through counting (no `args[1:]`). Add: a ref-type `T` call site for `gen_generic_for` (behavioral guard for the borrow-form ref-yield path beyond the snapshot), a `varargs_generic_iter_ref` variant exercising `args[1:]` for a reference-type `T`, and a direct `yield it` on a `pointer_form_unpack_target` (tuple-unpack loop var) to cover that membership of `generator_borrow_form_loop_vars` directly rather than via snapshot. Surfaced by /tpy-review test-coverage.
- **Regression guard for the call-macro `expr_type_hint` clearing.** `_run_call_macro` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) clears `ctx.expr_type_hint` while analyzing the macro's own args so the slot type can't leak into arg sub-analysis, then restores it for `CallMacroContext.expected_type`. The `call_macro_expected_type` case only passes string-literal args, whose `analyze_expr` is hint-independent (int literals are `IntLiteralType` at analyze time too -- the IntN coercion is later), so the clearing is currently a no-op in every test. A faithful guard needs a *hint-sensitive* arg whose `MacroArg.type` would differ if the hint leaked -- e.g. a nullary generic call whose `T` is pinned by the return hint (`poly(empty())` where `def empty[T]() -> list[T]`), plus a purpose-built reporting macro that emits something observable from `arg.type`. Non-trivial test scaffolding (needs a new reporting macro + a hint-sensitive callee). Surfaced by /tpy-review test-coverage of `feature/call-macro-expected-type`.
- **`@function_macro` test + API coverage gaps.** The spike has no `error_*` cases for its diagnostic paths: unknown function macro (a resolved decorator that isn't a registered `@function_macro`), a macro raising `MacroError` / an unexpected `Exception` (`tpyc/sema/function_macros.py`), `FunctionMacroContext.annotate_local`'s two failure modes (no introducing statement / local already typed), and `replace_expr`'s "node not found in body". Also missing: a regression guard for the parser behavior change (an unknown *resolved* free-function decorator now defers to sema's "Unknown function macro" instead of erroring at parse); a `-> None` function to exercise the `return_type is None` branch the smoke test never hits; `test_macro_loader.py` unit coverage for `MacroRegistry.get_function_macro` / `_is_function_macro` registration; an `annotate_local` target nested inside an `if`/`while`/`for` body (the `_annotate_in` recursion is untested -- all cases have flat bodies); and a `FunctionMacroContext.module_qname` exercise. Separately, `macro_api.py` re-exports `VarDecl`/`Name`/`StrLiteral`/`BoolLiteral` but not an `IntLiteral` alias, so a macro inspecting integer literals must reach for the internal `TpyIntLiteral`, breaking the "macros import only from `tpyc.macro_api`" contract -- add the alias. The frontend function-macro path (lowered `Function.decorators` -> `TpyFunction.pending_macros` -> sema pass-5.5) has no end-to-end test exercising the seam: `tpyc/test_frontend_lower_macros.py` stops at `lower_module` output and the `function_macro_resolve_type` snippet uses the source-decorator path, so the lowered->sema integration is untestable until a frontend actually emits decorated functions -- add a guard when the first such frontend lands. Surfaced by /tpy-review test-coverage of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **Unify `warning`/`error` on the remaining macro contexts.** `_MacroContextBase` (macro_api.py) hosts the shared diagnostics + registry introspection for `CallMacroContext` and `FunctionMacroContext`; `BuilderContext` (anchors at `_call_loc`) and `ClassInfo` still carry duplicated `warning`/`error`. Mechanical follow-up: alias their loc into `_loc` and inherit -- they would also gain `get_field_type`/`get_method_return_type` for free.
- **`lookup_imported_name` / `enum_members` / `lookup_function_signatures` coverage gaps.** (a) `lookup_imported_name` lives on `_MacroContextBase` so it is reachable from `CallMacroContext`/`BuilderContext`, but only the `@function_macro` path is exercised; add a `@call_macro` (or builder) case that resolves a module-visible record/enum by name. (b) `enum_members` is only tested on a module-local plain `Enum`; the imported-enum route (an enum imported from a sibling module) and the `IntEnum` subclass route are untested. (c) `lookup_function_signatures` has the same shape -- on `_MacroContextBase` but only exercised from the `@function_macro` path; add a `@call_macro`/builder case that reads a callee's signatures. (The per-module visibility contract -- a sibling-module type not imported into the host resolves to `None` -- and the `resolve_type_info` dedup are now done.) Surfaced by /tpy-review of `feat-function-macro-lookup-name`.
- **`lookup_function_signatures` param-shape gaps + `Signature.params` typing.** (a) `Signature.params` projects each param to `(name, TypeInfo.from_tpy_type(p.type))`, silently dropping `ParamInfo.keyword_only` / `is_variadic`; no test covers a callee with a keyword-only or `*args` param, so a macro can't tell those shapes apart and the projection could lose them unnoticed -- add a case and decide whether the flags belong on `Signature`. (b) `Signature.params`' element type is `tuple[str, TypeInfo | None]`, but `lookup_function_signatures` always sources non-optional `ParamInfo.type`, so the `None` slot is unreachable from this caller (inherited from `FunctionMacroContext.params`, the parser-phase shape where an unannotated param really is `None`). Decide whether to keep the type uniform against a future method-overload lookup or tighten it for the registry-phase caller. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `feat-function-macro-lookup-params`.
- **Refine vararg lvalue classifier to use `ctx.is_temporary_expr`.** The current ad-hoc include-list is over-permissive on two unreachable edges: `make_obj().field` (rvalue-rooted field access -- `&make_obj().field` would fail), and a user-record subscript whose `__getitem__` returns by value (`&call_returning_value()` would fail). The canonical `ctx.is_temporary_expr` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py:1916`) already handles the subscript case explicitly via its `TpySubscript` arm. Switch the classifier to `if not ctx.is_temporary_expr(arg): &arg else hoist` to absorb those edges. Current code is correct for everything reachable; this is hardening for future cases. Surfaced by /tpy-review safety-model.
- **Residual vararg arg-marking test variants.** Three primary variants are now covered (`method_varargs_nonmutating_ref_arg`, `varargs_generic_nonmutating_ref_arg`, `varargs_loop_var_ref_arg`). Lower-priority gaps remaining: mixed individual + `*unpack` args in the same call (currently rejected by sema -- could only be tested as a positive case if the rejection is relaxed); field/subscript source as a vararg arg (`f(self.box, items[0])` -- `_root_name_of_expr` returns the root, so marking would flow to `self` / `items`; works but unguarded).
- **Readonly-vararg test coverage hardening.** New code paths from the readonly-`*args` work lack regression guards (all routed and working, just untested): (a) the value-type const-view converting ctor in `runtime/cpp/include/tpy/varargs.hpp` (value specialization) -- forwarding a mutable value-type vararg into a readonly value-type vararg; (b) the "Cannot unpack into *args: element type ... needs a conversion" diagnostic branch in `tpyc/sema/calls.py` -- confirmed reachable (e.g. unpacking `Span[Int32]` into `*args: Int64`), not dead, but no test hits it; (c) generic readonly vararg `def f[T](*args: readonly[T])`; (d) method readonly vararg `def m(self, *xs: readonly[Box])`; (e) keyword-only + readonly vararg; (f) empty unpack into a vararg (`xs: list[Box] = []; f(*xs)`). Add `tests/cases/calls/` cases. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the support-star-unpack-varargs branch.
- **De-duplicate frame/local movable-registration in `_gen_var_decl`.** The two-liner `if stmt.name in self.ctx.sema_movable_locals: self.ctx.movable_locals.add(stmt.name)` now appears in three var-decl control-flow branches in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` (sync pointer-local, sync value-decl, and the frame-promoted generator/async branch). Collapse into one guarded helper. Not blocking. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the async frame-local-move fix.
- **Regression guard for `raise <expr>` last-use (blocked).** The liveness fix tracks reads in both `raise Foo(args)` and `raise <expr>` (raise_expr) forms, but only the arg form is tested (`error_raise_read_after_consume`). A clean raise_expr test (`raise make_err(h)` where `make_err` reads a consumed `h`) is blocked by the `Own[<Exception>]` return diagnostic bug in BUGS.md; add the guard once that lands.
- **Type alias re-export through intermediate module.** General TPy limitation surfaced while adding generic-alias tests: `lib_a` defines `type Foo = ...`; `lib_b` does `from lib_a import Foo`; `main` then does `from lib_b import Foo` and fails with `'Foo' not found in module 'lib_b'`. Affects both generic and non-generic type aliases. Root cause: `compiler.py::_extract_decl_exports` iterates `compiled.ast.type_aliases` (parser-output) which only contains locally-declared aliases, not imported ones; re-exporting modules don't add imported aliases to their `ModuleExports.type_aliases`. Records / functions / enums have separate re-export paths that work (see `tests/cases/imports/*_reexport`). The line-less module-level diagnostic shape (`main.py: error: ...`) means today's `# tpyc: error()` annotation can't pin it as a regression guard; once the re-export path is fixed, add a `generic_alias_reexport` happy-path test.
- **`Own[Optional[Tree[T]]]` field/param coverage.** Only the *return* shape is pinned today (`generic_recursive_own_optional_return`, `error_generic_recursive_optional_return_dangle`). No test exercises `Own[Optional[Tree[T]]]` as a record field or a function parameter, so the chosen storage/borrow rep for those positions can shift silently. Add field + param cases (likely under `tests/cases/union/`) to lock the rep. Surfaced by audit of the generic-recursive-alias followups list against tracked items.
- **Unify the recursive-alias-wrapper emit-ordering predicate sites in `generator.py`.** Three coordinated sites in `generator.py` key on `recursive_union_names` + `type_params` + `module.protocols`: (1) the pre-concept loop in `_generate_forward_decls_and_concepts` emits fwd-decls for ALL wrappers (generic template header / non-generic `struct X;`) when `module.protocols`; (2) the before-records loop in `_generate_definitions_and_reexports` emits the same fwd-decls when NOT `module.protocols` (sites 1 and 2 are complementary on the `module.protocols` axis); (3) the post-records type-alias loop emits non-generic wrapper *full definitions* and unconditionally skips generic ones (those emit before records). The split risks predicate drift if `_gen_recursive_union_struct` or the criteria change. Collapse into a single classifier (`GENERIC_RECURSIVE` / `NON_GENERIC_RECURSIVE` / plain) with explicit "fwd-decl before concepts / before records; full def before records (generic) / after records (non-generic)" intent in one place; also consider tightening the pre-concept fwd-decl to only wrappers actually referenced by a protocol signature (records use the precise `protocol_referenced_records` set; wrappers over-approximate). Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-recursive-alias-wrapper-gates` (codegen-correctness + architecture-fit).
- **Single-home the per-module codegen render-map resets in `clear_codegen_state()`.** Four maps carry per-codegen-module perspective and must reset on every `CodeGenerator.generate()` pass: `native_cpp_names`, `union_alias_names`, `recursive_alias_cpp_names` (all three reset in `clear_codegen_state()`, `tpyc/typesys.py`) and `local_qualified_cpp_names` (reset inline at `generator.py:236`, immediately above its own populate loop, and unmentioned in `clear_codegen_state`). A reader who takes `clear_codegen_state` as "where per-module maps reset" will add a fifth map there and miss nothing -- but one who copies the `local_qualified` pattern scatters the invariant further. That scattering is exactly how `recursive_alias_cpp_names` came to be un-reset (it rendered a definer's own alias qualified on any second emit of the module). Nothing reads `local_qualified_cpp_names` between `clear_codegen_state()` (`generator.py:173`) and the inline clear, so moving the clear up is behavior-neutral. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion on `fix-recursive-alias-name-leak`.
- **Unify the generic-recursive finalize-coverage sites into one registry-signature walk.** `_finalize_generic_recursive_aliases` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`) rewrites `AliasRef -> RecursiveAliasInstanceType` across a hand-maintained list of registration-time signature snapshots: AST function/method/field annotations, then `RecordInfo.init_params`, then record method `FunctionInfo`, then protocol `MethodSignature` (AST + registry). The **same omission recurred three times** while building `fix-recursive-alias-wrapper-gates` -- each missed snapshot category was a distinct reachable bug (ctor args, method return/params, protocol signatures). A per-category list is structurally footgun-shaped: the next signature snapshot added anywhere in sema silently reintroduces the bug class. Replace with a single pass that finalizes every registration-time signature snapshot (ideally driven off one place that enumerates them), so a new snapshot store can't be forgotten. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective on `fix-recursive-alias-wrapper-gates`.
- **`@dynamic` protocol method typed with a *non-generic* recursive union coverage.** The generic-alias cross-module case is now covered (`dyn_recursive_alias_method`). Still untested: an `@dynamic` protocol whose method takes/returns a non-generic recursive union (`Expr`). Probe whether it works before writing the case. (Note: the *same-module* protocol-method-with-recursive-alias emit-ordering failure found while covering the generic case is filed as a compiler bug in `BUGS.md`, not here.) Surfaced by /tpy-review of the W1 + F2 commits (test-coverage).
- **FEATURE_ROADMAP D19 status refresh.** `docs/FEATURE_ROADMAP.md:96` still labels D19 "generic recursive in progress" and the detail section (~line 707) lists generic recursive aliases under "Not yet supported". With qualified/aliased cross-module use landed, the remaining generic-recursive gaps are narrow follow-ups (`isinstance(x, Tree)`, `Tree[list[Int32]]` nested-ambiguous, mutual recursion across generic aliases) rather than "in progress" -- refresh the row + detail bullet to reflect substantial completion. Surfaced by /tpy-review (docs-sync); pre-existing, broader than the cross-module diff.
- **Precise generic-recursive-alias render registration (THIR cleanup).** Codegen registers *every* imported user module's generic recursive aliases into `recursive_alias_cpp_names` by qname (`generator.py`, the per-module setup loop), not just the ones an actual use site references -- tracking exact uses would need a finalize-time side-table. Correctness-safe (idempotent, qname-keyed, harmless over-registration), but it is a "consumer re-derives the fact" pattern: the finalize pass already knows which generic-recursive aliases a module instantiates, so the registration could be driven from there instead of a blanket import scan. Tighten when the THIR migration gives finalize a natural place to record per-module wrapper uses. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective (second-opinion flip).
- **Refactor `_resolve_own_source_type` to reuse `substitute_type_params`.** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:_resolve_own_source_type` (Box[P] Phase 3) does manual `dict(zip(...))` + `.get(name)` lookups in two places (function `inferred_type_args` and record-receiver `type_args`) instead of delegating to the established codegen helper `substitute_type_params` (used at line 2461). Not blocking; the duplication will rot as the helper grows. Cleanup: collapse both branches into one call through `substitute_type_params(fi.return_type, subst)` and let the existing helper handle the TypeParamRef walk. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Box[P] Phase 1-5 work.
- **Box[P] test coverage gaps.** Missing test cases identified during tpy-review: `tests/cases/protocols/dyn_box_generic_protocol` (Box[Container[Int32]] where Container[T] is `@dynamic` and generic -- exercises the partial-template `is_dyn_protocol_base` specialization for generic protocols + unique_ptr<Container<Int32>> construction); `tests/cases/protocols/dyn_box_return_from_function` (`def make() -> Own[Box[Pet]]: return Box(Dog(...))` -- pins return-site coercion for structural conformers, not just inheritance). The multi-hop inheritance case landed as `dyn_box_nested_dyn_inherit`; Rc[Box[Pet]] now resolves through both the intermediate-local form (`rc_dyn_box_workaround`) and the nested-call form (`rc_box_pet_nested`); the explicit-typearg form is still pinned by `error_rc_dyn_explicit_typearg`.
- **Bounded factory / single-allocation Rc loose ends.**
  - **`Own[U] -> Own[B]` / bare `U -> B` coercion.** Not implemented -- single-cell `Rc.new` only needs `Ptr`. Add only when a real use appears.
  - **Complete the `satisfies_bound` migration + unify the substitute-then-check step.** The inference path (`type_ops.py::infer_type_params_for_function`) and now `validate_type_param_bounds` (explicit-args path, fixed by `fix-generic-bound-substitution`) both substitute the bound by the resolved params before validating via `satisfies_bound` -- but they hand-inline the *same* substitute-then-check logic separately (`type_ops.py:1436` vs `calls.py`'s `validate_type_param_bounds`); collapse them into one shared helper. Remaining sites still call `type_conforms_to_protocol` on the *raw* bound (so a sibling-referencing bound like `[R, T: Proto[R]]` mis-checks there): `calls.py` overload pre-check + the three record-ctor sites, `expressions.py` Fn/Callable inference, `bound_check.py` class-param-shadowed method. Route them through the shared helper. The shared helper must also guard `substitute_type_params`'s `SemanticError("Unknown type parameter")` and rewrap it as a located `ctx.error` (today both fixed call sites are handed a complete subst, so it can't fire, but a future caller with a partial subst would crash unlocated). Correct for protocol bounds (all that reaches them today -- record-level class bounds are still rejected at registration), so the record-ctor sites are only latently affected; verify reachability of `class Stack[R, T: Proto[R]]` in the fix pass. Also guard the bound-collection loop in `codegen_cpp/generator.py` (it adds non-protocol bound names to protocol-include lists -- harmless no-match today) with `is_protocol_type`.
  - **Imprecise diagnostic for an inference-path bound violation.** When an inferred type arg fails its bound, `infer_type_params_for_function` returns `None`, so the call site reports the generic "Cannot infer type arguments ..." rather than a precise "'X' does not satisfy bound 'Y'". The `None` return is intentional (lets overload resolution try other candidates); a clean fix surfaces bound-miss as a distinct outcome. Pinned by `tests/cases/generics/error_bound_factory_inference` AND `tests/cases/protocols/error_rc_dyn_nonconformer` (both accept either wording via regex).
  - **Test-coverage gap: parametric protocol bound in inference.** No test exercises the substitution edge where the bound itself names another inferred param (`T: Iterable[U]` with U live in inference) -- the most subtle case for the new substitute-before-check logic. Existing `for_iterable_bound` uses a concrete type-arg, so substitution is a no-op there. Add when a real use surfaces.
  - **`@dataclass` macros applied to `Rc` factories.** Macro-emitted constructors that internally call `Rc.new[U: T]` may not see the LHS-hint propagation that the seed-substitute path provides for direct call sites. Untested; surface when a real use appears.
- **Cross-cell `Weak[OtherT]` test coverage.** Current `weak_cycle_breaks` / `weak_outlives_payload` / `weak_basic` all exercise self-referential cells (a `Weak[Node]` field inside `Node`). The new `_RcCell[U]::release_strong` comment claims "a nested `Weak.__del__` triggered by payload destructor sees `weak >= 2`" -- but no test exercises the case where U's destructor fires a `Weak.__del__` on a *different* cell (e.g., `Rc[A]` whose A holds a `Weak[B]`). The cross-cell sequencing should be correct by construction (each cell tracks its own counters), but the gap means a future change to the destruction order would not be caught by the canary suite. Add a focused case under `tests/cases/tplib/`. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of `feat-single-alloc-rc` (test-coverage specialist).
- **Extend `@dynamic` protocols to allow fields.** Currently `@dynamic` protocols can declare methods but not fields (gap noted in `docs/DYNAMIC_PROTOCOL_DESIGN.md:559-561`). That forced `_RcCellBase` in `lib/tpy/tplib/rc.py` to expose strong/weak counters as virtual methods. The fused-release API on the cell keeps each Rc/Weak operation to at most one vcall, so the perf delta from this is small (~the indirect-call cost on the final cell teardown, plus an icache + vtable-slot reduction from 5 methods to 1). The bigger payoff is shape clarity: a `@dynamic` base with `strong: UInt32` + `weak: UInt32` fields + a single virtual `release_payload(self) -> bool` matches `std::shared_ptr`'s control-block shape exactly. TODO comment in `lib/tpy/tplib/rc.py` flags the migration site. Also unlocks other polymorphic-cell patterns (any future tagged-union / type-erased control block).
- fix Box/Rc TODOs
- Add generic-holder (`class Holder[U]: shared: Rc[U]`) and Weak-via-downgrade variants of rc_field_default_init -- pin the broadened using-decl invariant.
- Protocol-side parallel handling of `IMPLICIT_AUTO_READONLY_METHODS`. Today `sema/registration.py::register_protocol` applies `IMPLICIT_READONLY_METHODS` to protocol method signatures (so e.g. `Deref[T].__deref__` becomes implicitly `is_readonly=True`), but doesn't parallel-treat `IMPLICIT_AUTO_READONLY_METHODS`. Harmless currently because protocol matching only asks "does the type have a signature with this name/shape" -- the type's dual mutable/const overload pair from method_expansion offers both, and the protocol's single const signature matches the const one. Becomes load-bearing if protocol-driven dispatch is ever extended to pick between the two overloads (e.g., `&p_borrow.__deref__()` resolving to the const overload vs `&p_owned.__deref__()` resolving to the mutable one). Surfaced by the parallel review of the `IMPLICIT_AUTO_READONLY_METHODS` split.
- Strict reachability for module compile + include set (workstream B of the include-propagation fix). Today: `import a` adds `a` to the build set and to consumer hpps regardless of whether anything from `a` is referenced. Goal: a module is built/included iff one of its symbols is reached, with implicit stdlib (tpy, typing, builtins) still always in. Three concerns to keep separate per Codex's review: semantic visibility (sema discovery is import-graph-driven today and changing it breaks `_register_user_module_import`'s "module already in registry" invariant), header/include visibility (already reach-driven post-fix, but currently still seeds from `user_module_imports`), and runtime side effects (`__tpy_init` is emitted from literal `TpyImport` AST nodes in `codegen_cpp/statements.py`, so dropping unused imports changes init semantics). Add `--keep-unused-imports` escape hatch for callers that depend on top-level side effects.
- CallMacroContext API redesign: current API grew ad-hoc from test cases (get_field_type, get_method_return_type, qualified_name, first_param, self_type, etc.). Needs a principled design pass: call site context (function params, method vs free), type introspection (fields, methods, qualified identity), and diagnostics. Consider whether type introspection belongs on TypeInfo (needs registry access) or stays on ctx. Unify get_record_fields (string name, match_args gated) with the TypeInfo-based path. See MACRO_DESIGN.md future extensions.
- `@overload` impl without annotations: arity-variant stubs + impl currently require the impl to carry full annotations. Could infer impl param types by merging stub signatures, or re-analyze the impl body per stub so the stubs become the sole source of type info. Blocked on restructuring sema body analysis; nice-to-have follow-up to the arity-overload work.
- range function/type defined in .py and generic
- range Sequence API: `Range[T]` is iterable but missing CPython's Sequence surface -- `__len__`, `__getitem__(Int32)` (O(1) `start + i*step`), slicing (returns another `Range[T]`), `__contains__` (O(1) arithmetic check, currently falls back to linear scan which is wrong-performance for `x in range(10**10)`), `__eq__`, eventually `__reversed__`. Consider folding with "range defined in .py" to avoid adding methods twice. Quick win independent of the rest: add O(1) `__contains__` now.
- Overloads with bodies (no dispatch function): allow `@overload` variants to have bodies directly, removing the need for a separate implementation function. TPy dispatches at compile time so this is straightforward. For CPython compatibility, `tpy.overload` could build a runtime dispatch table (pick by arity/types). Would simplify stdlib modules that mix `@native` and TPy overloads.
- Overloaded generic methods: free functions support a mixed generic/non-generic `@overload` set under one generic impl (each overload emits per its own type params), but methods reject any overloaded-generic outright (`sema/methods.py`: "Overloaded generic methods are not supported", three sites). Lift the method guard to reach parity with free functions -- the codegen path (`_gen_overload_specialized_method`) has the same impl-vs-stub type-param asymmetry the free-fn path resolves, so it needs the same stub-derived template-ness. While there, unify the free-fn emitter's `in_header` flag with the method emitter's `MethodEmitMode="def_hpp"` -- both encode "emit with `inline` because the definition lands in the header". `/tpy-add-feature` (it removes a guard + extends codegen, not a pure bug fix).
- Ptr null-provenance warning: consider warning when accessing through a Ptr with unknown provenance (similar to Optional access warnings). Design question: warn on all unknown-provenance access (noisy for function params) vs only when provenance is lost (was non-null, then reassigned from unknown source)?
- Source-order conflict resolution for module-scope name binding. Today, when a module both imports and locally defines `X`, the local definition always wins regardless of source order (the local `register_*` re-binds the `module_attributes` cell to `defining_module=None` and clears any prior `registry.functions[X]` entry). CPython's "last module-scope binding wins" requires per-statement order tracking: `class X: ...; from c import X` -> imported X wins; `from c import X; class X: ...` -> local X wins. Affects the `source_order_local_then_import` and `source_order_import_then_local` tests deferred from the per-module attribute table refactor (Phase 8). No current user impact (the "local wins" rule is sane and predictable); a Python-faithfulness improvement. Likely lives at the `register_*` sites + bind_imports interaction; needs a per-name source-line ordering map and a final pass that picks the latest binding.
- Reach analysis migration to bindings. Pairs with the strict-reachability entry above. Today's reach seeds from `module.user_module_imports` (the AST-level import statements). Migrating to consume a per-binding `requires_include` flag would let reach be driven by *which symbols are actually referenced*, not by which modules were import-statemented. The flag was trimmed when no reader existed; re-add when this work starts.
- Cycle-peer enum re-export `using` emission. Codegen currently suppresses cycle-peer enum re-exports (`generator.py` re-export emit loop) while cycle-peer record re-exports do emit a `using` through `<peer>_fwd.hpp`. Forward-declared enum classes are declared with their underlying type in `_fwd.hpp`, so the `using` would be safe; the suppression is a holdover from the legacy extraction-ordering accident. Flipping it means a small snapshot delta in `mutual_cycle_with_enum` (and possibly siblings) -- not blocking until a concrete use case forces consumer-side qualified enum-type access through a cycle peer.
- Define decorators as .py functions: readonly/noalloc/nocopy/pure/dynamic/error_return (tpy), native/cpp_template/builtin_type (tpy.extern), overload/override (typing) are currently parser keywords with empty stub files. Want them as real function definitions eventually so the parser doesn't need special handling. Blocked on: functions need type annotations to compile, and decorator signatures have no meaningful type (identity function over any callable).
- ContextManager[T] protocol
- **Consolidate the isinstance lowering-mode flags on `TpyCall` into one kind/enum.** `isinstance` lowering is now selected by several mutually-exclusive booleans/ints on `TpyCall` -- `isinstance_is_protocol`, `isinstance_deref_depth > 0`, `isinstance_type_param`, plus the default variant/typeid/dynamic_cast paths -- each consumed by a new branch at every consuming site (`_emit_call`'s isinstance lowering in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`, and `_extract_isinstance_facts`). The fact-extraction site already missed one mode (`isinstance_type_param`) and would have emitted `std::get` on a non-variant had sema not independently blocked the trigger -- a single `isinstance_lowering` enum/Literal field would make the per-mode handling exhaustive-by-construction and kill that class of omission. THIR-aligned (one materialized fact, no consumer-side mode re-derivation). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the type-param-isinstance trait lowering; same family as the cpp_shape/`has_field_condition` materialization items above.
- Reject `isinstance(self, ...)` transitively reachable from `__init__` / `__del__` bodies (low priority). The direct case is rejected at sema by the `self-isinstance-dispatch` branch (`tpyc/sema/calls.py::_analyze_isinstance`), but a method called *from* `__init__` that internally does `isinstance(self, Sub)` would compile and run -- and silently diverge from CPython, since the C++ ctor/dtor dynamic-type rule sets `*this`'s dynamic type to the enclosing class for the entire ctor/dtor call graph. **Detection shape:** walk the intra-module call graph during Phase 2 (mutation-propagation fixpoint already does this) flagging methods reachable from any `__init__` or `__del__` body; emit a warning (or error) when one of those methods contains a polymorphic `isinstance(self, ...)`. Cross-module calls are out of scope for the local call graph; document as a known transitive footgun. Low priority -- unusual pattern, sema rejection on the direct case catches the obvious mistakes. Filed during /tpy-review of the `self-isinstance-dispatch` branch.
- Migrate sync function/method emit sites in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/functions.py` (`gen_function_def`, `_gen_literal_specialized_function`, `_gen_overload_specialized_function`, `_gen_method`) to call `FunctionGenerator.compute_body_const_sets(func, record_name)` instead of inlining the same `_get_reassigned_params` + `_get_*_mutated_params` + `_build_param_const_sets` + readonly-self-add dispatch. The helper was extracted on the `async-scope-reset` branch to share the dispatch with async; sync sites still inline it (half-migration). Watch out for the method site at `functions.py:1648` -- it uses `gmp = _get_method_genuine_mutated_params` for *signature* const inference but `mp = _get_method_mutated_params` for the body const sets (the helper covers the body path); don't accidentally swap them. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `async-scope-reset`.
- Explicit upcast to silence the narrowing warning: currently the "upcast narrows 'Child' to 'Parent'" warning has no escape hatch. Add an explicit cast syntax (e.g. `cast(Parent, child)` or `Parent.upcast(child)`) that acknowledges the intent and suppresses the warning. Needed once users have legitimate "I know this narrows, I only want the Parent view" patterns. Pairs with the warning added for implicit value upcast in `compatibility.py`.
- `@dynamic` on concrete classes: the upcast-narrowing warning and the method-hiding warning both suggest "make '{Parent}' a @dynamic protocol for runtime polymorphism", but `@dynamic` currently applies only to protocols. Users with a concrete base class must refactor it into a `Protocol` first, which the warnings don't acknowledge. Options: (a) allow `@dynamic` on concrete classes (opt-in virtual dispatch, vtable for that hierarchy only); (b) update the warning wording to say "convert '{Parent}' to a @dynamic protocol" and accept the refactor burden. Option (a) is closer to Python semantics but adds vtable overhead to any hierarchy that opts in.
- **Polymorphic + wrappers + isinstance: broad composition landscape.** Investigated 2026-05-25. **Phase 1 landed** (bare `Pet` / `Ptr[Pet]` borrows dispatch `isinstance` via `dynamic_cast`; `Optional[Pet]` at parameter position; fresh `Own[...]` rvalue lent to a borrow param). **Phase 2 landed** (`/tpy-add-feature`, on `dyn-protocol-wrapper-compose`):
  - **Deref-view narrowing through owning wrappers** -- `isinstance(rc, Dog)` for `Box[Pet]`/`Rc[Pet]` narrows the polymorphic payload reached through the wrapper's reference-returning `__deref__`, so `rc.bark()` resolves against `Dog` while `rc.clone()` stays an Rc method (the wrapper type is never narrowed). Rides `narrowed_types` under a distinct key (`prescan.deref_view_key`); `TpyCall.isinstance_deref_depth` + the access nodes' `deref_narrowed_to` tag the AST; codegen casts the deref payload pointer. A non-owning handle (`Weak[Pet]`) is rejected with an `upgrade()`-first hint.
  - **Adapter-aware `isinstance` for structural conformers** -- `tpy::dyn_adapter_cast<P,U>` (in `dynamic.hpp`) tries both `Adapter`/`RefAdapter` shapes and projects `.inner`; `ProtocolGenerator.dynamic_narrow_cast_rhs` picks `dynamic_cast` (inheritance) vs `dyn_adapter_cast` (structural) for all sources (bare/Ptr/Optional/wrapper). Flipped `error_isinstance_dyn_structural` to positive `isinstance_dyn_structural`.
  - **`Own[Pet]` rvalue param** (inheritance + structural) works; locked in by `own_polymorphic_param`.
  - **`Optional[Pet]` init-only function-local** allowed (lowers to `const Pet*`); rvalue rebind stays rejected (shared slot can't retype). Unified `polymorphic_subclass_into_optional` to cover direct @dynamic inners.

  Remaining (deferred, tracked here):
  - **`Own[Optional[Polymorphic]]`** stays sema-rejected at `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py::validate_type` (item 6); the `unique_ptr<P>`-nullable lowering is a separate pass. Use `Optional[Box[Pet]]`.
  - **`Optional[Own[Polymorphic]]`** now sema-rejected (was a miscompile): the inner Own lowers to `unique_ptr<P>` so the slot is `optional<unique_ptr<P>>`, a double indirection the member-access/call-site/isinstance lowerings don't thread. Threading it through is the follow-up; use `Optional[Box[Pet]]`.
  - **Unify the deref-view / pointer-source cast-emit read path** (residual of the deref-view cast-and-cache work, now mostly done). The deref-view if-init cast-and-cache landed (`deref_view_init_locals`: the condition + every narrowed access in a branch share one `Sub*`, same shape as the bare/Ptr/Optional sources). Two related cleanups remain, both the same silent-divergence hazard (a fix to one emit block missing the other -- the shape that produced this branch's only Critical):
    - The isinstance-condition codegen in `expressions.py` has two near-identical ~18-line blocks (the `expr.isinstance_deref_depth > 0` deref-view block vs the `polymorphic_source_inner` pointer block): same if-init null-check shortcut, same `is_const_src`, same `checks` comprehension, differing only in cast-input/source-inner derivation and which init-locals map is consulted. Extract a shared helper.
    - A *structural* conformer narrowed inside a compound `isinstance(p, Cat) and p.purr()` expression: `_collect_inline_isinstance_facts` re-runs `dyn_adapter_cast` for the inline read instead of reusing the if-init's `isinstance_init_locals[p]`. (Inheritance conformers there use `static_cast`, cheap and validated by the `&&` LHS; only the structural inline read re-casts.) Reuse the cached local when present -- naturally folds into the same read-path helper.
  - **Invariant: any prefix-scan of `narrowed_types` must also account for deref-view keys.** Deref-view facts ride `narrowed_types` under a `\x00deref` suffix (`prescan.deref_view_key`), which is NOT a `name.`-dotted path, so the field-path prefix sweeps (`_invalidate_field_facts` etc.) silently don't cover it -- `update_after_write` needs a bespoke `pop(deref_view_key(name))` (that omission was this branch's Critical). A new invalidation site that prefix-scans `narrowed_types` will silently reopen the stale-fact bug. Recorded as a comment at `prescan.deref_view_key`; the durable fix is the THIR migration making this a first-class `Place` variant rather than a string-keyed side fact.
  - **`_is_non_deref_handle` is a structural heuristic** (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) -- it rejects `isinstance` on a record that exposes `upgrade()` but no `__deref__` (i.e. `Weak`). A user record that happens to expose `upgrade()` without a deref would get the same Weak-specific diagnostic. Replace with a library-declared TypeDef flag if a second weak-like type appears.
  - **Phase 2 test-coverage gaps** (surfaced by /tpy-review): subclass-only *field* access (not method) through `deref_narrowed_to` codegen (receiver is a narrowed local); tuple-form `isinstance(rc, (A, B))` through a deref view; `readonly[Rc[Pet]]` receiver (const deref path); structural `isinstance(p, Cat)` inside an `Own[Pet]` body. (A wrapper *field* as the isinstance subject -- `isinstance(self.rc, Dog)` -- is NOT a test gap: the subject must be a bare name, see the bare-name-only limitation below.)
  - **`isinstance` subjects are bare-name-only -- a field or expression subject is rejected** (general, pre-existing). `isinstance(self.pet, Dog)` (or any `isinstance(<expr>, T)` where `<expr>` isn't a bare local/param) fails sema with "isinstance() first argument must be a variable name". Holds for *all* sources (union fields too -- `isinstance(self.x, A)` where `self.x: A | B`), not just wrappers. Workaround is clean: bind to a local first (`p = self.pet; if isinstance(p, Dog): ...`) -- for a `@nocopy` `Rc`/`Box` field that's a borrow, and the deref-view narrowing then applies to the local. Lifting it means extending `_analyze_isinstance` to accept a field-path narrowing key (which the flow facts already key on dotted paths), plus a stable codegen binding for the subject expression. Medium; the bare-name restriction is a sane default, so this is ergonomics, not correctness. (NOTE: `match` no longer shares this -- polymorphic `match` accepts arbitrary expression subjects: it re-evaluates the subject rather than narrowing a name in place, so it needs no dotted-path key.)
  - **`match` field-condition predicate duplication -- materialize `has_field_condition` on the AST.** `MatchAnalyzer._field_constraint_key` (sema, `tpyc/sema/match.py`) and `MatchGenerator._pattern_has_field_condition` / `_sub_has_field_condition` (codegen, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py`) independently walk the same `TpyClassPattern.keywords` structure to answer "does this arm carry a field-value condition?" and must be kept in manual lockstep. Both also mirror `_record_field_conditions`. Set a `has_field_condition: bool` flag on `TpyClassPattern` during sema (the `_field_constraint_key` path already computes it) and have codegen read it -- per CLAUDE.md's "materialize sema->codegen facts on AST nodes" guideline, cutting a future THIR lowering line. The predicates already drifted from the emitter once (`_record_field_conditions` silently dropped `None` field tests while the predicates treated `None` as constraining -- since fixed); a single source of truth prevents a repeat. A related mirror to fold in at the same time: the nullable-repr decision for `field=None` is computed in *both* sema (`_check_none_field_nullable`: optional vs union-with-None) and codegen (`_record_field_conditions`: `has_value` vs `monostate`); tagging the repr kind on the pattern node at sema time would remove that second re-derivation too. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of `feat-poly-match-field-value` and `fix-match-none-field-pattern`.
  - **`match` polymorphic dispatch: materialize `poly_depth` on the AST** (low priority). Sema computes the deref depth in `_poly_dispatch_source` but only sets a `polymorphic_dispatch: bool` flag on `TpyMatch`; codegen re-derives depth via `ctx.deref_dispatch_source`. Storing the depth (and source-inner) on the node would cut a future THIR lowering line, per CLAUDE.md's "materialize sema->codegen facts on AST nodes" guideline. Consistent with isinstance today (which also recomputes), so not urgent.
  - **`match` polymorphic expression-subject test-coverage gaps** (surfaced by /tpy-review of `feat-poly-match-expr-subject`): (a) a *non-deref* (depth-0, bare polymorphic) **expression** subject -- exercises `_poly_match_cast_arg`'s `&__match_subject` / `__match_subject` branches for an expression rather than a name. Currently unwritable: a bare polymorphic field needs `Box` (slicing), and a bare `list[Animal]` element is covariance-rejected (the genuinely-slicing literal case, see the resolved-list-literal entry below). This is also why the latent unwrap mismatch in `_gen_match_polymorphic` (now fixed by using `stmt.subject_type`) couldn't be triggered. (b) an or-pattern (`case Dog() | Snake():`) with an expression subject -- `_poly_or_condition` doesn't reference `subject_name`, so low risk, but the combination is unexercised. Both are ergonomics/coverage, not correctness.
  - **`list[Box[Pet]]` literal element upcast -- RESOLVED** (`covariant-list-literal-upcast`; test `list/covariant_wrapper_literal`): covariant-generic wrapper elements now upcast to the annotated element type in list literals + comprehensions via `CompatibilityChecker.is_covariant_generic_upcast`, which exempts the per-element anti-slicing guard; value/reference slicing + invariant generics stay rejected. Original analysis kept for history -- `pets: list[Box[Pet]] = [Box(Dog()), Box(Cat())]` is rejected ("List literal element 1 has type Box[Dog], incompatible with annotated element type Box[Pet]"). Root cause: the contextual list-literal element check (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py:2072-2077`) has a deliberate anti-slicing guard that rejects *any* user-record `Child -> Base` element ("same invariance as dict/set"). That guard fires on `Box[Dog] -> Box[Pet]` because `Box` is a user record -- but the upcast is NOT slicing: `Box`/`Rc` are `Covariant[T]` (pointer-steal that preserves the payload's dynamic type). The covariant coercion already exists (`compatibility.py:813`) but the guard short-circuits before reaching it. Tell: `.append(Box(Dog()))` into `list[Box[Pet]]` already works (append routes through `check_type_compatible` and accepts the covariant upcast), so the list *literal* is stricter than `append` for no reason -- that inconsistency is the bug. Fix: narrow the guard so covariant-generic wrappers fall through to the existing coercion, while invariant value/reference records stay rejected (`list[Animal] = [Dog(), Cat()]` must still error -- that one really slices). Not a blind patch: it relaxes a slicing-safety guard and must draw the covariant-vs-slicing line consistently across literal / append / dict / set -- run it through `/tpy-add-feature`. Workaround: `.append(...)`, or annotate elements. Sibling: any covariant-element list literal (`list[Rc[Pet]]`, `list[Ptr[Parent]]`) hits the same guard.
  - **`dict`/`set` covariant value-literal upcast -- RESOLVED** (`covariant-list-literal-upcast`; test `dict/covariant_value_literal`): a `dict` value literal whose annotated value type is a covariant-generic wrapper now coerces each value to it (`dict[str, Box[Pet]] = {"a": Box(Dog()), "b": Box(Cat())}` builds at `dict[str, Box[Pet]]` directly), via the same `is_covariant_generic_upcast` routing the list literal uses -- the value-unification takes the per-value-against-hint branch instead of peer-unifying. `set` got the symmetric change (moot for `@nocopy` Box/Rc, which are rejected as set elements; live for a hashable covariant value-type element). Container-level invariance is unchanged (a whole `dict[str, Box[Dog]]` still can't be passed where `dict[str, Box[Pet]]` is expected). Original analysis kept for history: the `dict` literal entered contextual mode with `expected_value` but the value-unification's else-branch peer-unified the values (`Box[Dog]` vs `Box[Cat]` -> "mixed value types"; single value -> `dict[str, Box[Dog]]` -> container mismatch) instead of checking each against the annotated value type.
  Filed during /tpy-review of `isinstance-subclass-narrowing`; Phase 1 on `fix-forward-typeparam-bound`; Phase 2 on `dyn-protocol-wrapper-compose`.
  - **Phase 1 review follow-ups (low priority):** (a) no test for tuple-form `isinstance(p, (A, B))` on a bare `@dynamic` protocol / `Ptr[P]` param; (b) no test directly exercising the generic-callee `Own[T] -> T` erasure (the relaxation's second call site -- `rc_new_arg_passing` only hits `Own[U] -> Own[U]`); (c) no `readonly[Pet]` param + isinstance test on the protocol branch; (d) `_validate_polymorphic_subclass_dispatch` (`calls.py`) guards on `is_protocol_type(inner)` -- tighten to `is_dynamic_dispatch_inner` so a static-protocol inner can't reach `directly_implements_dynamic` with an opaque error (latent: callers are gated by `polymorphic_source_inner`, which only yields @dynamic). Surfaced by /tpy-review of Phase 1.
- **Phase 20 cleanup: `cpp_concept` semantic overloading.** `ProtocolInfo.cpp_concept` now carries two meanings: C++ concept name for `@native`-only structural protocols, and C++ class name for `@native + @dynamic` protocols (Throwable). `get_concept_name` returns `cpp_concept` unconditionally; if Throwable were ever used as a type-param bound (`class Foo[T: Throwable]`), the emitter would put `"::tpy::Throwable"` in a concept-constraint position -- ill-formed C++. Sema doesn't guard against `@dynamic` protocols as bounds today. Cleanup: split into a separate `cpp_class` / `native_base` field on `ProtocolInfo` for the `@native + @dynamic` case so callsites are unambiguous. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Phase 20 branch; also flagged by Codex during planning.
- **Phase 20 test coverage gap: `raise self.exc` (Box[Throwable] field narrowing) at snippet level.** The pattern is the live production code path in `lib/tpy/asyncio/_executor.py::TaskState[T].__poll__` -- narrow `self.exc` from `Box[Throwable] | None` and rethrow. Exercised transitively by `tests/cases/async/` cases via CancelledError, but no snippet test under `tests/cases/exceptions/` directly exercises the narrowed-Box-field raise shape with a user-defined exception subclass. A dedicated `tests/cases/exceptions/box_throwable_field_raise/` (or equivalent under `tests/cases/async/task_raises_user_exception/`) would lock down dynamic-type preservation through the Task boundary for user exceptions specifically. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Phase 20 branch.
- **Phase 20 test coverage gap: concrete-subclass borrow rejection.** `tests/cases/exceptions/error_slicing_borrow_into_field/` covers the same-type case (`def store(self, e: BaseException)` storing into a `BaseException | None` field). The concrete-subclass case (`def store(self, e: AppError)` storing into a `BaseException | None` field) exercises a different source-type branch in `_check_polymorphic_slicing` and has no dedicated error test. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Phase 20 branch.
