- **STDLIB_ROADMAP.md async section: residual stale rows.** Most of the original backlog is now reconciled -- M3-M6 (line ~1079), M8, M9 carry SHIPPED rows, the M1 note was corrected, and M2 (class-based exc dispatch) now has a SHIPPED row. Remaining: M7 (generic async free funcs + methods), M10 (`Task.cancel()` runnable-mark hook), and M11 (`gather` variadic-positional + `gather_list_settled`) still have no explicit SHIPPED rows though they're all in `docs/ASYNC_PROGRESS.md`. Add SHIPPED rows for M7, M10, M11. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Phase 20 branch.
- **Test coverage gaps for the new isinstance / dynamic_cast path.** (1) Deeper inheritance chain (4+ levels under a `@dynamic` root) -- guards `iter_ancestor_records` MRO walk depth. (2) ~~isinstance on a stored `Optional[Polymorphic]` field~~ -- struck: `isinstance` on a field is rejected outright (bare-name-only subject), so this is not a writable test. Lifting field subjects is tracked under the "isinstance / match subjects are bare-name-only" entry above; a test follows that, not this. (3) Positive regression for the 2-level direct case (e.g. `protocol_dynamic_inherit`) that no "hides" warning fires after the new `_method_is_transitively_dynamic_protocol_member` suppression -- relies on implicit empty diag.txt today.
- **Recursive-union wrapper test-coverage gaps (post-migration).** (1) The migration's core goal -- mutation through a wrapper *local* alias propagates to the source -- is untested for the plain (non-error_return) `v = h.get()` local binding; the only mutation test (`generic_recursive_field_alias`) exercises a `match` *subject* (`error_return/borrow_local_alias` covers the @error_return slice for list/record, not wrappers). Add a case that mutates through a wrapper local and reads back from the field. (2) No test exercises a record *method* with a *non-generic* wrapper param under Part B const-inference (free-function + generic-method are covered; `gen_params_with_protocols` / method path is not) -- include the self-mutating-method-returns-the-param shape (verified working, was BUGS.md residual). (3) A generic-type (`Tree[Int32]`) parallel of `wrapper_param_const`. (4) `generic_recursive_return_readonly_error_return` is readonly-masked; a mutable GENERIC-wrapper `@error_return` borrow variant would guard the alias binding with an actual write-through (the borrow-copy bug itself is fixed; the non-generic-wrapper residual is the `_call_returns_cpp_ref` BUGS.md entry). (5) `match`-subject coverage: `match_hoisted_wrapper_local` covers only the generic hoist-via-if/else -- add a non-generic wrapper-union hoist and a while-loop-reassignment hoist; and a ptr-variant-union mutation-through-arm aliasing guard for `match_ptr_variant_call` (currently read-only because a read-only-return ptr-variant is `const`, so a natural mutating case needs a mutable union source). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the wrapper-reference migration.
- **Tuple-form `isinstance` member reordering via `make_union`.** `isinstance(x, (RuntimeError, OSError))` reorders alphabetically before codegen iterates `check_members`, so the emitted dispatch tries `OSError` first then `RuntimeError`. Semantically neutral for single-inheritance hierarchies (any concrete type matches at most one alternative) but observable for multi-inherited types and changes the source-order semantics CPython preserves. Tracked here as a watch-item; not blocking. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the v1.5 M2 polymorphic dispatch landing.
- **Auto-readonly inference for never-mutated local-var aliases.** Local-var bindings emit non-const references by default, even when the variable is never written. For polymorphic-isinstance narrowing this propagates non-const back through the cast chain and demotes the source param from `const T*` to `T*`. Reproducer: `def classify(r: Optional[Root]) -> str: if isinstance(r, Sub): narrowed = r; return narrowed.name` -- the `narrowed = r` alias emits `Sub& narrowed = (*__r_ptr);` (non-const), forcing `__r_ptr` to `Sub*`, the `dynamic_cast` to non-const, and the param signature to `Root*` (not `const Root*`). `narrowed` itself is only read, so a mutation-propagation analysis on locals could mark it `const Sub&` and preserve the const-borrow chain. Lives in sema's local-binding analysis; sibling to the existing `@auto_readonly` machinery for methods/fields (which doesn't fire on locals today). Visible as a snapshot delta in `protocol_dynamic_marker` / `protocol_dynamic_optional_isinstance` after adding `narrowed = r  # tpyc: type(Sub)` annotations. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the `isinstance-subclass-narrowing` branch. **Also gates `@auto_readonly` accessor alias-read const:** `x = o.b.get(); return x.v` keeps `o` mutable today because the non-const local `x` requires a mutable source (`auto-readonly-usage-clone-selection` marks the receiver mutable when an accessor result is bound to a local, precisely to avoid an ill-formed `Foo& x = const Foo&`). Once locals can bind const, relax that mark so aliased reads also get a const receiver -- the same change unblocks the aliased-read variant of the field-path element-borrow case in BUGS.md.
- **Implicit-readonly stub dependency on `@dynamic` protocol methods.** `BaseException.__str__` satisfies `Throwable.__str__`'s `@readonly` declaration only because `__str__` is in `IMPLICIT_READONLY_METHODS`. Any future `@dynamic` protocol method declared on an `@native` class hierarchy requires either an entry in that list or an explicit `@readonly` on the stub, with no diagnostic if missing. Should be a registration-time sema check that flags mismatch between protocol-method `@readonly` and stub-method readonly status. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the Throwable activation.
- `__stream__` / `__write__` dunder: efficient stream-based output for records (avoids allocating a string just to print). Needed for no-alloc logging.
- default macro protocols to implement hash/eq/compare/str/etc? -- rust inspired?
- python dict lookup with string_view? (no need to create std::string)
- KeyInterrupt exception?
- optmize build, split headers
- Finish per-compilation-state-on-Compiler migration: address `_dynamic_attached_qnames` (`tpyc/type_def_registry.py`). The other six globals (`_namespace_map`, `_include_path_map`, `_native_cpp_names`, `_union_alias_names`, `_protocol_modules`, `_return_exception_names`, plus the dynamic slice of `_type_defs` and `_dynamic_created_qnames`) have moved onto `Compiler` and are read via `tpyc/compilation_context.py`'s `ContextVar`. `_dynamic_attached_qnames` stays module-level because it tracks mutations to the SHARED static `_type_defs` entries (e.g. `builtins.list` getting its `RecordInfo` attached at sema time) -- the rollback has to outlive the originating Compiler so the next compilation's startup can clean up. The proper fix is to stop writing payloads back into the static registry from sema (so static TypeDefs become genuinely read-only post-`_populate()`); requires reshaping how `attach_dynamic_type_def` handles the static-overlay case. Also unblocks parallel in-process compilation. See `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` for the architectural direction; the conftest autouse fixture (`_TestCompilerContext`) can also be retired once tests stop relying on module-level state. Side cleanup: add a dedicated `tpyc/test_compilation_context.py` covering (a) `require_current_compiler()` raises outside any active context, (b) nested `activate_compiler` restores the outer compiler on exit, (c) an exception thrown inside `with activate_compiler(...)` still restores the prior context. And add a note to `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` about the static-vs-dynamic TypeDef split (the rationale is currently inline in `type_def_registry.py` only).
- Function-scope vs module-scope sema unification: `_analyze_top_level` already reuses `FunctionTrackingState` and `deduction.resolve_all()` for module init, so the two paths are mostly the same. The `_ModuleInitSentinel` (in `sema/context.py`) lets code distinguish a real `TpyFunction` from the module-init host. Legitimate uses rely on `TpyFunction`-specific attributes (`.params`, `.return_type`, `.type_params`, `.is_method`, `super()`); but several gates just ask "am I in a function body?" and reject the sentinel by accident -- empty list/dict literals at module scope were two of those (fixed; `is_body_like_scope` helper in `sema/context.py` names the predicate). Remaining suspect: `calls.py::_can_defer_generic_inference` at ~`:3375` reads *"Only in function bodies (resolve_all runs there)"* -- same mistaken rationale, needs a real repro before changing. A broader pass should: (1) audit every `isinstance(current_function, TpyFunction)` in `sema/` (~15 sites) and classify each as legitimate vs accidental; (2) migrate accidental sites to `is_body_like_scope`; (3) consider making `MODULE_INIT_CONTEXT` a real `TpyFunction` with empty params/return/type_params so the sentinel can be retired entirely. Medium effort, independent of other refactors.
- Strict-warning hardening follow-ups (the test suite already runs under the downstream-mirroring flag set; see `STRICT_WARN_FLAGS` in `tpyc/compiler.py`):
  - `-Wshadow` not in the strict set. Record/dataclass constructors emit the idiomatic `T(P p) : p(p) {}` pattern which always shadows. Either rename the ctor parameter (e.g. `T(P p_) : p(p_) {}`, snapshot churn across every record) or document `-Wno-shadow` for downstream tpy-gen TUs (current state -- documented next to STRICT_WARN_FLAGS).
  - **Low-prio:** TPy-level diagnostic for unread local variables. `-Wno-unused-variable` is in the strict set because user TPy code can legitimately write `x = compute()` with no read (Python idiom), and per the "users never see C++ errors" invariant we can't let that surface as a C++ `-Werror`. Adding a sema-level info/warning diagnostic would let the user learn about unused locals in TPy terms while the C++ side stays silent (so the suppression stays). Convention to consider: skip the diagnostic when the var name starts with `_`.
  - Sign-mixed comparison handling: codegen routes `Int32 < UInt32` and friends through `std::cmp_*` (`expressions.py:_gen_binop`) so the result is mathematically correct without the "promote signed to unsigned" reinterpretation; sema additionally emits a warning so the user knows they're hitting that path (skip-list: literal-seeded locals still pending retro-widen, since the compare may resolve to same-sign once the seed is widened). Future tightening: extend the "literal seed" gate so retro-widening of *both* operands together (rare today) doesn't false-positive either. Lower priority since false-positives currently surface only for user code that intentionally compares mixed-sign.
  - **Consolidate async-for `_build_async_for` loop-stack lifetime.** `_build_async_for` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/resumable_cfg.py:740`) appends to `_loop_stack` before one try/finally pair (which only pops `_region_stack`) and pops in a separate try/finally pair around the body build. A `CodeGenError` thrown from the handler-body build between the two would leak the loop ctx for subsequent compilation in the same session. Currently unreachable in practice (the synthesized handler body is a single `TpyBreak` that can't fail given the just-pushed loop ctx), but fragile. Mirror the sync `_build_for`'s single-try pair around the loop stack. Surfaced by /tpy-review of M6.
  - **Elide async-return copy for lvalue sources.** Async-def returns lower uniformly as `T __tpy_async_ret = <expr>; return Poll<T>::ready(std::move(__tpy_async_ret));` (e.g. `tests/cases/async/async_for_break/expected/src/main.cpp:38`, `await_in_for_range`, `asyncio_run_returns_value`). For value types this is free, but for owning non-value return types like `BigInt`, `std::string`, `list[T]`, etc., the assignment from a named source (frame field / local / param) is a real copy at every async return. The temporary local exists so the `std::move` into `Poll::ready` is unconditional. **Fix:** when the return expression is an lvalue at last use (frame field, local, or param), `std::move` directly into `Poll::ready` instead of going through the materialized local; when it's an rvalue (literal, call result), pass it through without the intermediate. Mirrors the sync-return value-categorization that codegen already does. Pre-existing async codegen pattern -- broader than M6 -- but worth tracking since it touches every async function returning a non-value type. Surfaced while reviewing M6 snapshots.
  - **Med-prio:** TPy-level diagnostic for `__init__` field-assignment order out of declaration order. C++ runs MIL entries in field-declaration order regardless of MIL syntax, so `-Wreorder` (in the strict set) fires when `__init__` writes `self.f = ...` lines in a different order than the `class.fields` list. Today the user sees a C++ `-Werror=reorder` blob with no TPy-level fingerprint -- and only on a fresh (cache-miss) rebuild, since cached `.o` files mask the warning. Fix: a sema check on `__init__` walking the leading `self.X = ...` chain, comparing index against the record's field declaration order, emitting a TPy diagnostic with line numbers + a fix-it hint ("reorder the assignments to match field declarations, or reorder the field declarations themselves"). Surfaced while writing `lib/tpy/io.py` (StringIO/BytesIO had `_pos`/`_total` swapped relative to their `__init__` body); the failure was opaque enough that I had to track the codegen path manually before realising the cause. Half-day, lives next to the existing init-validation passes in `sema/`. Don't auto-reorder the MIL silently -- TPy's "generated C++ is readable" invariant relies on source-vs-output staying 1:1, and the user-facing fix is small.
- **Bidir-hint inference follow-ups (post `feat-bidir-hint-inference`).** Surfaced by /tpy-review and /code-review of the shipped feature; none blocking, none worth shipping alone, but worth tracking so they don't rot:
  - **`partial_inferred` Fn-hint poisoning** (`tpyc/sema/calls.py:2923`). Phase-2 of `_infer_arg_types` seeds `partial_inferred` from `seed_subst`. When a non-Fn arg's inferred type disagrees with the seed, `match_type_with_inference` returns False and the seed binding stays. The Fn-arg hint then reflects the seed, not the arg-derived type. Design question: should seed or arg evidence win in the Phase-2 partial_inferred when they disagree?
  - **Wildcard partial-explicit merge semantics** (function-call site `tpyc/sema/calls.py:4247`; record-constructor site `tpyc/sema/calls.py:4571`). Both merges do `for tp, ta in zip(...): if ta is not None: merged_seed[tp] = ta`, so a user-written wildcard at a given position doesn't opt-out of LHS-seed influence. Design question: should `_` mean "infer from args only" (clear seed) or "infer from args + LHS" (current)? Whatever answer wins, apply it at both sites together.
  - **kwargs not iterated by the methods.py seed closure** (`tpyc/sema/methods.py`). The closure iterates only `expr.args` (positional). Kwargs at seed-eligible positions silently miss the contextual hint that the seed would have produced. Pair with the two-phase-Fn alignment work; `_analyze_generic_method_call` should pre-flatten kwargs before the closure (or the closure should consult `expr.kwargs` directly). Same gap in the overload-probe siblings: `_probe_analyze_method_args` (methods.py) and `_probe_analyze_args` (calls.py) iterate only `expr.args`, and `candidate_arg_hints` (type_ops.py) takes only positional `n_args`.
  - **Union not stripped from seed-derived hints.** `post_substitute_hint` now strips Own/Readonly/Ref/Optional, but `UnionType` (e.g. `T | A | None` constructor params with multiple non-None members) still doesn't unwrap. A param `value: T | A` with seed `T=Concrete` propagates a `Union[Concrete, A]` hint that the inner record-ctor / function-call seed match rejects. Needs design: try each Union member against the inner pattern? Pick the matching member? Skip seeding for Union params?
  - **`IntLiteralType` / `FloatLiteralType` filter affects pre-existing function/method seed paths too.** The filter additions live on the shared `_seed_subst` helper, so they apply to `seed_subst_from_return_hint` (function and method-call seeders) as well as the new record-pattern sibling. Pre-this-PR, a function/method call whose return-type match pinned T to `IntLiteralType` would propagate that binding via `seeded_arg_hint` to per-arg seeded hints. Now the filter drops it; per-arg hint falls back to None. Theoretical (no current trigger -- annotations don't carry literal markers), but the behavior shift across two previously-shipped paths is undocumented. Decide intent: keep filtered or restore unfiltered with explicit comment.
  - **Same-name cross-module records can mis-seed in `seed_subst_from_record_pattern`.** `_match_record_with_inference` compares NominalType by simple name, so a hint of `other_module.Box[Pet]` matched against a pattern built from `my_module.Box`'s record can yield a bogus seed binding before the assignment-level cross-module mismatch is reported. Fix: gate the pattern match on `qualified_name()` equality.
  - **kwargs not iterated by the record-ctor seed loop** (`tpyc/sema/calls.py:4578`). Parallel to the methods.py kwarg gap above. Pair the fix across both sites (and the overload-probe siblings `_probe_analyze_args` / `_probe_analyze_method_args` / `candidate_arg_hints`) so the rule is uniform.
  - **Bound check runs after `_apply_dyn_hint_at_position` uplift.** Pre-existing in both function-call and record-ctor paths: bound validation operates on the inferred dict after the @dynamic uplift overwrites T=Concrete with T=Protocol, so `T: Hashable` + non-Hashable Concrete + Hashable LHS Protocol type-checks despite the heap payload failing the bound at runtime. Restructure bound validation to run twice -- once against arg-derived T pre-uplift, once against the uplifted T -- or restrict uplift to bound-respecting positions.
  - **Defer path consumes seeded arg cache.** `_create_pending_generic_instance` re-iterates args via bare `analyze_expr`; any inner record-ctor arg that was seeded by a failed outer call returns its cached seeded type. The pending instance therefore carries arg types influenced by an LHS hint the deferred resolver may never see. Worth a focused reproducer (outer ctor with wildcards leaving a TP unresolvable from args alone but inner ctor's seed pinning a flippable T).
  - **Macro re-entry inherits outer `ctx.expr_type_hint`.** Synthetic constructor calls emitted by class macros run inside the enclosing analyze_expr_with_hint scope, so the seed fires using the outer site's hint even when the macro author intended T to come purely from explicit args. Pre-existing risk in the function-call path; the new record-ctor seed extends the surface. Fix: clear `expr_type_hint` at macro expansion boundaries, or gate the seed on a per-call opt-in.
  - **Test gap: type-mismatch diagnostic shape for nested-generic regressions.** The deleted `error_rc_box_box_pet_nested` was the only test pinning the 'Type mismatch in variable r: expected ... got ...' shape for 3-level chains. Replaced by a conformance-failure test, but a partial-degradation regression (seed silently drops one nesting level) would re-trigger the old shape with nothing catching it. Add a regression test that pins the type-mismatch shape via a setup that bypasses the seed deliberately (e.g. via an opt-out flag if one exists, or via a known limit-case).
  - **Probe / `_infer_pending_container_element` interaction** (`tpyc/sema/methods.py:_probe_analyze_method_args`). `_infer_pending_container_element` pre-analyzes args into the `pre_analyzed_method_args[id(expr)]` side table; the new overload probe then re-analyzes the same args. For inner generic-ctor/call args, the cache short-circuit at `_analyze_record_constructor` / `_analyze_generic_function_call` returns the previously-cached type. `_check_and_coerce_args` pops the pre-analyzed cache but uses probe's `arg_types`, so the element-type inference work done in the pending path is duplicated and the ordering of `info.coerced_element_type` updates becomes path-dependent. Worth a focused reproducer.
  - **Direction-swap admits LHS=Optional[T] vs return=T** (`tpyc/sema/type_ops.py:candidate_arg_hints` non-generic gate). The current swap (LHS-side as param) correctly rejects `return=Optional[Foo]` vs `lhs=Foo`, but symmetrically accepts `lhs=Optional[Foo]` vs `return=Foo` -- a bare-T-returning candidate matches against an Optional-annotated LHS via the matcher's param-side Optional unwrap. Likely intentional (T flows into Optional[T] slot via narrowing), but undocumented. Either tighten (direction-symmetric structural match) or add a code comment.
  - **Routing divergence: calls.py vs methods.py probe inputs** (`tpyc/sema/calls.py:_probe_analyze_args` vs `tpyc/sema/methods.py:_probe_analyze_method_args`). `_probe_analyze_args` passes UNSUBSTITUTED `func_infos`; `_probe_analyze_method_args` passes SUBSTITUTED `resolved_overloads`. `candidate_arg_hints` routes generic-vs-non-generic on `func.type_params`, so a class-generic-only method (e.g. `class Box[T]: def push(self, x: T)`) post-substitution goes through the non-generic branch in methods.py, while the equivalent free function in calls.py would go through the generic branch. Branches are independently correct but the asymmetry isn't documented.
  - **Audit `substitute_method_type_params`'s dropped FunctionInfo fields** (`tpyc/sema/type_ops.py:1554`). The constructor explicitly passes ~24 fields; the FunctionInfo dataclass has ~46. Dropped fields include `is_pure`, `is_inline`, `is_property_getter` / `is_property_setter` / `property_name`, `native_cpp_return_type`, `type_param_defaults`, `is_constructor`, `special_handling`, `builtin_decorator_key`, `owning_type_qname`, `addr_escapes_params`, `direct_mutated_params`, `direct_structural_mutated_params`, `call_edges`, `direct_self_mutated`, `self_mutated`, `is_auto_readonly_mutable_clone`, `inline_body`, `originating_module`, `const_borrow_params`, `deep_const_borrow_params`. Some are presumably defaulted on purpose (mutation-tracking state that's per-original-function), but others (`is_pure`, `is_inline`, `inline_body`, `originating_module`) look call-site-relevant. `kwarg_name` was confirmed-and-fixed in `bidir-hint-quick-wins`; the same shape may bite for other fields when a future caller consults them on a substituted method. Audit each field: should it propagate, or is the default correct? Add a comment block explaining the per-field policy so future maintenance doesn't accidentally drop more without justification.
  - **Per-candidate trial-and-rollback at the overload probe** (`tpyc/sema/calls.py:_probe_analyze_args`, `tpyc/sema/methods.py:_probe_analyze_method_args`). The current single-LHS-matching gate over-rejects calls where 2+ candidates LHS-match and all of them would accept the user's args via per-candidate hint propagation. Concrete shape: overloads `f(Own[Box[Pet]], str)` and `f(Own[Box[Pet]], Own[Box[Pet]])`, called as `f(Box(Dog()), Box(Dog()))` under `r: Result = ...`. Both returns match; pre-fix B-first ordering succeeded via Adapter wrap, A-first failed -- a real declaration-order-dependent bug. Post-fix consistent rejection (see `error_overload_cross_candidate_hint`). Principled fix is regime-C-style per-candidate trial: each LHS-matching candidate gets a scratch-space analysis under its own hints; the candidate whose scratch result type-checks wins. Today's regime-C path already handles this for Fn-bearing args (2+ Fn-bearing-supplied candidates -> per-candidate trial). Extend to non-Fn LHS-matching candidates. ~100+ lines + analysis-state save/restore.

- **Pin the `owns_fresh_idx` dual-branch tie-break with a regression test.** `merge_binding_provenance` keeps the MIN element index when both merged branches flag the same tuple local owns-fresh at different indices; no test exercises both-branches-non-None (the existing `tests/cases/tuple/error_tuple_local_fresh_member_branch_merge` has one branch None). Diagnostic-text only (the reject fires regardless), so this is a low-priority guard against a future merge-policy change silently flipping which index the error names. Add a 3-element-tuple case (then fresh at idx 0, else fresh at idx 1). Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the consolidation.
- **Replace the `__for_tup_` name-prefix check in the coro frame-local emitter with a prescan fact.** `gen_async.py`'s borrow-form tuple frame-field branch excludes synthetic tuple-unpack loop holders by `lname.startswith("__for_tup_")`; the for-loop prescan that already builds `pointer_form_names` has the AST (`TpyForEach.is_tuple_unpack`) and should publish a dedicated loop-managed-names set instead of a string heuristic. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the tuple borrow-pointer unification.
- **Skip the `tuple_to_storage` wrap for fully VALUE-captured tuple literals at storage destinations.** The wrap sites key on the destination TYPE having a pointer-repr element, so an all-VALUE-captured literal (whose fresh rvalue members already render the storage form) still routes through `tuple_to_storage` -- a runtime identity (the same-shape branch forwards), but one needless move per element plus template noise. Two known sites: `records.py`'s field-init wrap, and the container-literal element path (e.g. `[("a", Node(1))]` in `warn_dict_ctor_copy`'s generated code). Gate additionally on the source literal's elem_capture. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the tuple borrow-pointer unification.
- **[LOW] Emit `copy(lvalue)` as a bare in-place copy when it's a tuple-literal element with a matching slot type.** `(77, copy(b))` lowers `copy(b)` via `_gen_copy_expr` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:632`) to the prvalue `Box(b)`; as a `std::tuple{...}` element that prvalue binds to the ctor's forwarding-ref `Box&&` and is MOVED into the member -- one extra move vs the bare `(77, b)` form, which copy-constructs the member in place from `const Box&`. Fix: at the tuple-literal element emit site, when the element is `copy(x)` (lvalue/field source) and the slot type equals `x`'s type, emit the bare `x` and let the normal element copy-construct path run (the warning stays suppressed -- it's a sema fact keyed on the `copy()` wrapper, independent of codegen). Saves the move, keeps explicit-copy semantics. **Low value:** the extra move is elided at -O2 for trivial-move types (the common case) and only bites non-trivial-move *copyable* tuple elements (`@nocopy` types don't use `copy()`); it's also a sub-case of the broader "prvalue element into a `std::tuple` literal incurs a move" (`(77, Box(5))` has it too), so the general fix is in-place tuple-element construction, not a `copy()`-specific unwrap. Pairs with the value-captured `tuple_to_storage`-skip item above (that targets the other move in the chain). Surfaced reviewing the per-element-Own rebind test field-store copy.
- **[LOW] Consolidate the per-element-Own walrus collapse across its sema + codegen sites.** The walrus per-element-Own collapse is applied at two sema sites (`_analyze_named_expr`: collapse `resolved`, and override the walrus expression `result_type` to the collapsed type for a pointer-repr tuple) plus one codegen site (`_gen_named_expr`: collapse `value_type` for reassigned targets). For a pointer-repr tuple sema already carries the collapsed type as the walrus result, so the codegen collapse is a no-op there; it is load-bearing only for the exotic Own-of-value-element tuple (`tuple[Int32, Own[Int32]]`), where sema deliberately returns the uncollapsed raw `value_type` to preserve literal-ness. A clean unification would have sema carry the collapsed form for that edge too so the codegen collapse can be dropped -- but NOT by unconditionally setting `result_type = resolved` (that drops scalar-literal preservation for `(n := 5)`); it needs a tuple-scoped path that collapses without losing the literal types of value elements. Low value (current code is correct; the duplication is a parallel-mechanism smell, not a bug). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the walrus per-element-Own fix.
- **Upgrade `gen_resumable_mixed_tuple_yield` to mutate-and-observe.** The case is read-only, so it is parity-blind for the aliasing of its yielded reference member (covered indirectly by `gen_ref` / `gen_tuple_yield_ref`); add a post-yield mutation + observation so the resumable mixed-tuple shape guards its own aliasing. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity).
- **Anchor the rvalue generic tuple-element gap with a compile-fail case.** BUGS.md records `(i, fn(x))` under a generic `tuple[Int32, T]` slot at a non-value instantiation as a C++-error-caught gap; an `error_`/compile-fail anchor keeps the boundary pinned if the gap is later (partially) closed. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage).
- **Add a method-receiver cross-module `@error_return` auto-propagation test.** The qname-based `error_return_matches` gate (`fix-error-return-identity`) is covered for free-function propagation (`error_cross_module_same_name`, `reexport_propagate`), but the same `calls.py:4798` path also fires when the propagating caller is a *method* (`current` is a `TpyFunction` from method registration). Low risk (shared code path), but a method-receiver case across modules would close the coverage gap. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the B113 fix.
- **Subclass `__init__`-field-inference follow-ups (from `feat-subclass-init-fields` review).** (a) The sema gate in `validate_record_inheritance` keys on `record.bases` (the parse-time base list, which includes protocol-only / `Generic[T]` entries) to complement the parser's `if not bases`; gating on the sema-resolved `record_info.parents` would be strictly accurate and self-documenting (harmless today -- such a class has no MRO field ancestors, so `get_all_fields` returns own fields only). (b) Add a grandparent / 3-level MRO inference test (`C(B(A))`, A has `x`, child `C.__init__` assigns `self.x` + a new name) -- the grandparent-skip relies on `get_all_fields` walking the full `mro_ancestors`, currently only 2-level-tested. (c) Add a `@native`-subclass regression guard: a `@native`-derived class with a `self.f = param` in `__init__` must NOT get an inferred field (the `linkage == DEFAULT` gate is the only barrier; needs native companion files). Surfaced by /tpy-review of `feat-subclass-init-fields`.
- **Walrus-reassignment test hardening.** Two coverage gaps from the walrus-reassign fix: (a) a walrus reassign of a var-decl-introduced name *inside an if/else arm* -- locks the invariant that `declared_vars` is snapshot/restored across branches while `walrus_pre_declared` is monotonic, so the `need_predecl` `or` in `_gen_named_expr` backstops both; (b) a value-tuple walrus reassignment (`t = (1,2); (t := (3,4))`) -- exercises the shared tuple-facts tail that the value/error/nonvalue cases don't. Add under `tests/cases/control_flow/`. Surfaced by /tpy-ready.
- **Extend `coerce_reassignment` to the remaining reassignment sites.** `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py::coerce_reassignment` unified the var-decl and walrus paths, but two sites still inline the same `mark_list_different_size` / `link_list_literals` / view-tracking / `resolve_reassignment_target_type` calls: `_analyze_assign`'s `TpyName` branch (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`, apparently unreachable -- the parser routes every `name = expr` to `TpyVarDecl`) and the augmented-assign write-back (`+=` etc., a distinct operation whose semantics may not fit the plain-reassignment shape). Route the `TpyName` branch through `coerce_reassignment` if it ever becomes reachable; decide separately whether augmented-assign should share it (the RHS is the binop result, not a bare rebind). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit).
- **Send/Sync `Send[Iterator[T]]` / `Send[Cancellable[T]]` annotation surface (gated on Phase 5).** The per-value frame chain now lands at the `Send[T]` / `Sync[T]` conversion site for *lambda* and *function-reference* values (names the offending capture; `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py::_value_frame` + `send_chain.why_not_frame`). The remaining surface is per-value Send/Sync checking of *generator / coroutine call results* via `Send[Iterator[T]]` / `Send[Cancellable[T]]` annotations: requires `_value_frame_traits` / `_value_frame` to resolve a `TpyCall` whose callee is an async/generator function to that callee's `FrameType`, and `Iterator[T]` to persist as a marker (`Cancellable[T]` already does, being `@dynamic`). Gated on **Phase 5** -- its only enforcement consumer is the multi-threaded executor's `Task` migration site, which does not exist yet, so the annotation would be testable only via a hand-written local with no runtime meaning. Two residual diagnostic gaps fold in here: a function-ref whose non-Send cause is an awaited *sub-frame* (not an own slot) still falls back to the static-type chain (the conversion-site chain walks own-slots only); and a lambda passed to a `Send[Callable[...]]` slot is classified capture-by-ref (slot type dropped), so its chain names the capture but not its type. The sub-frame-fallback path has no `error_` test (a coroutine ref whose own slots conform but whose awaited sub-frame is non-Send, passed to a `Send[Callable[..., Cancellable[...]]]` slot); add one when the generator/coroutine-call resolution lands, since that is what makes the case constructible. See `docs/SEND_SYNC_DESIGN.md` Phase 3 notes.
- **Unify import-aware record resolution in `tpyc/sema/calls.py`.** The cross-module-identity fix added `_record_for_local_name` (resolves a possibly-aliased local name to its record, overriding the short-name `get_record` only when the import table proves a different defining module), used at the constructor sites. The `isinstance` second-arg path (`_resolve_isinstance_type`) does its own import-table-first resolution inline with different shape (unconditional import lookup, no collision guard). Two parallel patterns for the same sub-problem; fold the isinstance site onto the helper (or a shared resolver) so same-name disambiguation lives in one place. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of `fix-cross-module-type-identity`.
- **[cleanup] Remove the now-redundant `__del__` from asyncio `TaskState`/`Future` and channel `_Send`.** Each `__del__` only drops its cached-result slot (`result`/`_result`/`_value`), which is now a `tpy::UninitStorage[T]` whose own dtor already `reset()`s a live payload -- so the explicit `if slot.has(): slot.reset()` is redundant (it existed when the slot was `UninitArrayStorage`, whose debug dtor panics on a live element). Removing `__del__` is the clean end-state but NOT a mechanical edit: it drops the codegen-emitted custom move ctor + `__tpy_owned_` guard (the types fall to C++-defaulted move/dtor) and may shift their TPy value/reference classification, so it needs verification that the defaulted member-wise move stays correct (all members -- `Box`/`Rc`/`UninitStorage` -- are correctly movable) and the pass-by classification is unchanged, plus a full suite run. Surfaced by /tpy-review (convention/safety) of the liveness-flag cleanup.
- **[test-coverage] `_Send.__del__` live-slot branch is untested.** The channel send awaitable (`tpy/channel.py` `_Send`) drops its still-held value in `__del__` when the send was never pushed -- a task cancelled mid-suspension while blocked inside `await tx.send(...)` on a full channel. The only channel-cancel path in the suite is receiver-side, so this branch has no coverage. Pre-existing (predates the liveness-flag cleanup, which only touched the branch). Add a `panic_`/output case that cancels a task parked in `send` on a full channel with a `@nocopy`/`__del__`-tracked payload so a leak or double-drop would surface. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the liveness-flag cleanup.
- **[test-coverage] No `@nocopy`-payload `Mutex`/`RwLock`/`unsafe_store` case makes the `set()` move vs copy observable.** The `set()`/`unsafe_store` `Own[T]` fix (branch `mutex-set-alias`) is exercised by `threading/mutex_set_move` (list payload, mutate-through-guard) and `threading/warn_mutex_set_copy` (read-back shows the lock's independent copy), but no case uses a `@nocopy` payload (e.g. `Mutex[Box[Int32]]`) where a silent copy would be a hard compile error rather than merely a value divergence. `tests/cases/pointers/mem_unsafe_store_object` stays parity-blind for the same reason (`Point` has no owned resource, so copy vs move print identically -- only the byte-diffed snapshot pins the `std::move`). Add a `Mutex[@nocopy]` / `unsafe_store` of a `@nocopy` value case (rvalue/last-use move admitted; a still-live-lvalue `set` a hard non-copyable-into-owned-storage error) so the consumption is proven at the type level, and consider hardening `mem_unsafe_store_object` to mutate-after-the-boundary. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of `mutex-set-alias`.

## Deferred to THIR/MIR & workload-gated

Items below are blocked on the THIR/MIR migration (the `cpp_shape` /
place-model end-state) or explicitly gated on a real workload / reproducer
/ named prerequisite -- not schedulable in isolation. `## Next` above still
carries some THIR-aligned *cleanups* that are cheap to do opportunistically
alongside related feature work; only the big-rock deferrals live here.

- **`cpp_shape` redesign (THIR-aligned, post-IR-migration).** TPy types currently decide their C++ boundary form (value / borrow / pointer / storage) through three uncoordinated predicates -- `is_value_type()`, `needs_wrapper()`, `uses_pointer_repr()` -- plus 5+ consumer sites that spell `is_value_type() or needs_wrapper()` disjunctions, plus per-site specialcases (`functions.py:557-568` for wrapper unions, the `RefType.to_cpp_return` qualifier delegation, the `_gen_array_literal` ReadonlyType peel, `OptionalType.uses_pointer_repr`'s force-pointer-repr flag). Every new type-shape concept (recursive-alias wrappers, future variant-form unions, MIR places) re-discovers the decomposition and adds another consumer-site disjunction. The /tpy-review medium pass on `generic-type-aliases` flagged this as the underlying altitude issue. Right shape: a single `cpp_shape` property per `TpyType` (VALUE / BORROW / POINTER / STORAGE) that consumers ask once; lowering methods (`to_cpp_param`, `to_cpp_return`, `to_cpp_storage`) read it and emit. Qualifier types (`ReadonlyType`, `OptionalType`, `RefType`, `OwnType`) compute their shape from their inner uniformly. Codegen never spells the disjunction. Touches typesys.py + sema (the auto-Ref insertion sites must refuse VALUE-shape inners) + every codegen consumer. **Deferred to THIR/MIR migration** -- exactly the kind of refactor THIR is designed to host (per CLAUDE.md's perf section: "THIR is explicitly designed as the natural cache boundary"). Surfaced by the iterative bug-fix passes on `generic-type-aliases` (the recurring "consumer site missed wrapper-aware predicate" pattern is what this redesign eliminates).
  - **Attempted standalone 2026-06 (branch `cpp-shape-redesign`), then the classifier was REVERTED -- the premise didn't hold.** A 3-way `ReprKind {VALUE,BORROW,POINTER}` classifier (design via /tpy-add-feature + Codex review) was built and most consumer reroutes were undone in review: the global-storage sites ask a *storage* axis (where `force_pointer_repr` Optional-of-value diverges from the boundary axis), not the boundary form; and the remaining look-alike predicates (`not is_value_type() and not isinstance(x, (OwnType, TypeParamRef))` etc., ~6 sites) turned out to be **distinct context-specific decisions** (return-Own-wrapping, slot-mutability, has-ref-elements, capture-mode, vararg-is-ref), not one drifted boundary-form predicate. With only one trivial consumer left and no real unification to enable, the classifier was a half-used abstraction and was removed. **Lesson: the "~38 disjunctions" repeatedly dissolved on inspection -- mostly storage-axis, multi-axis (repr+ownership), single-predicate, or structural unwraps; there is little genuine boundary-form duplication to unify.** The full contextual `cpp_form(type, context)` end-state (Codex's sketch) remains THIR's job (`lower.py`); no near-term driver justifies building it on the AST first.
  - **Genuine wins kept from that branch (independent of the classifier):** (1) the wrapper-union return special case was dissolved by making `UnionType.to_cpp_return`/`to_cpp_return_const` wrapper-aware (the `recursive-union-ref-return` merge settled this on the Family C convention -- wrapper returns `X&`/`const X&` by reference, and functions.`_return_type_cpp` no longer special-cases wrappers; the standalone `cpp-shape-redesign` branch's by-value variant was overridden); (2) the motivating tuple-local fresh-member dangle (`t = (i, Box(i)); yield t`) is now **diagnosed** via a per-local dataflow fact `owns_fresh_tuple_member_vars` (sema/context + flow_facts UNION merge + init_tracker; set at the tuple-literal assignment, rejected at the yield/return boundary, pointing at the `Own[T]` element escape) -- NOT via the classifier. Only the *fresh*-member face is fixed; the *durable*-member face (tuple local holding a reference-type member is storage-form and silently COPIES it -- CPython divergence, plus return-build-fail and loop garbage) is a distinct pre-existing codegen bug, now one MED entry in BUGS.md, needing `/tpy-fix-bug`. **Effort 2 follow-ups (deferred, non-blocking):** the UNION branch-merge conservatively over-rejects a mixed `if/else` fresh/safe tuple assign (escape is `Own[...]`); no `tuple_local_own_member_return` happy case yet (settle the return-ctor gap first).
- **Param-const verdict: collapse the body-side re-derivation onto the materialized fact.** `decide_param_const`'s per-param const verdict is now materialized on `FunctionInfo.deep_const_borrow_params`/`const_borrow_params` (`populate_const_borrow_params`, post-Phase-2) and read by the call site, resumable factory field, and union signature. But `compute_body_const_sets` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/functions.py`) still *re-derives* name-keyed `(const_ref, deep)` sets by re-calling `decide_param_const` -- it needs body-local, name-keyed, `reassigned_params`-aware facts the index-keyed FI verdict doesn't carry. Kept in lockstep only by both paths passing the same `use_readonly_params`; will silently drift if they diverge. Also `param_const._is_ptr_variant_union` hand-copies `CodeGenContext.is_ptr_variant_union` (justified -- no Compiler ctx at Phase-2 time -- but a duplicate predicate). THIR-aligned final form: a single per-param ABI descriptor (the `ParamABI` Codex sketched) consumed by the signature emitter, body, call site, and frame field, so there's one code path, not one fact read by N sites plus a parallel body derivation. Fits with the `cpp_shape` redesign above. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-readonly-union-param-const`.
- **Tag the resolved constructor `RecordInfo` on `TpyCall` instead of re-deriving it in codegen.** The cross-module-identity fix left codegen's record-constructor path (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) re-resolving the record from the expr's sema-assigned type (`get_record_for_type(get_expr_type(expr))`) to override the short-name `get_record(func_name)` for same-name cross-module collisions. Sema already resolves the authoritative record in `_analyze_record_constructor` / `_set_record_constructor_info` -- it should stamp the `RecordInfo` (or its qname) on the node so codegen reads it directly, eliminating the consumer-side re-derivation (the THIR node-fact pattern CLAUDE.md flags). The qualified-call path (`_gen_method_call`, `expr.user_module_call is not None`) is a second instance of the same re-derivation: `fix-qualified-ctor-import-shadow` made it probe `find_record_by_qname` / `fi.originating_module` at emit time to recover the authoritative module; sema should stamp `defining_module` on the `TpyMethodCall` node (like `user_module_call` already is). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of `fix-cross-module-type-identity` and `fix-qualified-ctor-import-shadow`.
- **`mark_param_mutated`'s `through_field` field-path climb -> MIR place projection.** The `through_field=True` path (`tpyc/sema/context.py`) strips one dot off a storage key via `_storage_root` (`o.items` -> `o`) to attribute a genuine through-reference write back to the owning param. It's a second string-split climb alongside `effective_storage_through_borrows`, exactly the BorrowTracker string-key shape CLAUDE.md says should be expressed as a `Place::Field` projection traversal under the MIR place/loan model. Works today; fold into the place-model migration. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of `auto-readonly-usage-clone-selection`.
- **`return_borrows_from` receiver-borrow fact: make it precise (does-borrow), audit-enforced -- not a capability heuristic.** Bodyless native/`@cpp_template` stubs get no body-derived borrow facts, so registration (`tpyc/sema/registration.py`) stamps `return_borrows_from={-1}` ("result borrows the receiver") for any such stub whose return passes `signature_may_return_borrow` (`tpyc/sema/type_ops.py`). That predicate is a *capability* check ("could this type carry a borrow") -- true for `str`, views, tuples, generics, and any non-value reference -- so it OVER-stamps owned-returning stubs (`str.upper -> str`, etc.) with a *literally false* "borrows self" fact. Inert today only because all three consumers (`is_dangling_return`, `_register_call_result_borrow`, the auto-move gate) re-filter on the *binding's* storage form, so the false fact never acts. The hazard is a future consumer that treats `return_borrows_from` as authoritative -- the MIR/THIR loan generator most of all -- producing phantom loans of the receiver. **Principle (from the co-brainstorm): the stored fact should mean "does borrow," not "might, unless a later filter disproves it"; the signature heuristic should be a coverage GUARDRAIL, not the source of truth.** Design (synthesis of two brainstorm passes): (1) split the predicate -- keep `signature_may_return_borrow` as the broad capability check, add a precise `call_declares_return_borrow` that becomes `return_borrows_from`; (2) auto-stamp only the unambiguous cases (borrowing view return -> borrows receiver; `Own`/void/value/owned-`str`/owned-`bytes` -> fresh, no stamp); (3) for the ambiguous middle (non-value reference / tuple / generic returns: `dict.get`, `list[i]`, `setdefault`, ...) REQUIRE an explicit contract -- `@borrows_self` (re-add) or a new `@returns_fresh` opt-out -- and an audit at registration that FAILS the `lib/tpy` stub tests if a bodyless ambiguous stub declares neither. That gives the can't-forget property without polluting the fact with false positives, and mirrors the `@readonly`/`auto_readonly` duality (inference where a body exists; declared contract at the bodyless library boundary). **Context:** branch `fix-view-lifetime-tracking` removed the redundant `@borrows_self` decorator (master's heuristic already covered every stub it was on), accepting the heuristic's inert imprecision until this lands. Best done WITH the MIR loan model (the consumer that makes precision pay off); independently buildable but not worth the churn before then. Surfaced by the /co-brainstorm on the view-lifetime fix.
- **Consolidate the three view-source provenance predicates into one classifier.** `_view_source_is_temporary` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`, new), `is_dangling_return` (`tpyc/sema/compatibility.py`), and `is_view_compatible_source` (`tpyc/sema/local_deduction.py`) each walk the same expression tree to answer a provenance question, and the first two re-implement view-constructor detection and `return_borrows_from` traversal independently -- when a new node kind or `fi` attribute is added, all three need lockstep updates (the method-call-arm `return_borrows_from` gap filed in BUGS.md Safety is exactly this drift). They differ only in the boundary policy: `is_dangling_return` treats a local name/field as DANGLING (function-return scope), while `_view_source_is_temporary` treats it as STABLE (same-scope binding). Right shape: one stateless `view_source_provenance(expr)` classifier parameterized by the stable-vs-dangling policy (or returning a provenance category the callers map to their policy), so the walk and the view-ctor/`return_borrows_from`/walrus/ternary recursion live once. This is the provenance classifier the IR migration's place/loan model wants; folds with the `return_borrows_from` precision item above. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit, safety-model) of the view-lifetime fix.
- **Phase 20 endpoint design (Stage 4c) -- captured, not planned.** Phase 20 shipped Stages 1, 2 (ABI + sema rules + invariant tests), 3 (`raise <expr>` desugar with fresh-construction peephole keeping idiomatic `throw X(args)`), 4a-b (`what()` auto-emit + runtime `raise_X(msg)` helpers replacing the `raise<E>` template), and 5 (slicing-site sema rejection + asyncio `TaskState[T]` / `Future[T]` migration to `Box[Throwable] | None`). The headline BUGS.md slicing bug is closed: mechanism fixed, stdlib workaround removed, new occurrences rejected at compile time. **Stage 4c is design-captured-but-not-planned**: it adds no user-facing behavior (the mechanism is already shipping), and the design walls below give it a poor cost/benefit ratio against current needs. The right trigger to revisit is a concrete future feature that *needs* exception classes to be TPy-defined -- e.g. `@dataclass` on user exception classes, a macro that introspects the exception hierarchy, or some other capability the macro can't accommodate. Until that emerges, the `TPY_THROWABLE_VIRTUALS` macro is small (~20 lines), stable, and approximately zero maintenance.

  **What 4c would complete:** move the 17 exception classes (`BaseException`, `Exception`, `ValueError`, ..., `CancelledError`) from `core.hpp` / `async.hpp` to pure TPy code in `lib/tpy/tpy/_builtins/_exceptions.py`. Drop `TPY_THROWABLE_VIRTUALS` (auto-emit covers all Throwable subclasses now). Replace the inline `raise_X(msg)` helpers in `core.hpp` with forward declarations in `throwable.hpp` + definitions in a new `lib/tpy/tpy/_builtins/_raise.py`. Rewrite the ~40 format-using runtime call sites to bake `std::format(...)` inline (the linker-resolved helpers take `std::string_view` only).

  **Design walls (why this is more than a mechanical move):**

  1. **StopIteration tier-coupling.** StopIteration is a value-type `ReturnException` marker used by the iterator protocol (`std::expected<T, StopIteration>`) in 15+ runtime headers that construct `StopIteration{}` by value. Forward decl is insufficient -- needs a complete C++ type at runtime-header parse time. Forces either special-casing StopIteration as `@native` (with the awkward consequence of dropping `Exception` inheritance, breaking the "StopIteration is an Exception" relationship), or introducing a runtime stand-in marker type with a sema/codegen bridge at the user-visible boundary.
  2. **Method `...` body restriction on non-native classes.** Sema rejects `def clone(self) -> Own[Throwable]: ...` on regular classes with "only allowed on @native classes". After dropping `@native` from `_exceptions.py`, the Throwable protocol's clone/__raise__/what methods need a sema-visibility declaration on BaseException for conformance checking, but the C++ implementations come from the codegen auto-emit -- the stub body is intentionally synthetic. A name-based carve-out for `("clone", "__raise__", "what")` works but smells like the wrong layer; the right design is a "compiler-synthesized method" category with explicit AST attestation.
  3. **BaseException auto-emit symmetry.** Today `_needs_throwable_auto_emit` in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/records.py` excludes `builtins.BaseException` (the historical macro handled it). After 4c that exclusion needs to flip (auto-emit fires uniformly including the root). Small change, interacts with item 2.

  **Cost:** ~2-3 days focused work + ~100 snapshot regenerations. The headline downside is that 4c's payoff is purely architectural -- if it never lands, nothing breaks.
- **State-aware destruction for `frame_slot<T>`, drop the runtime alive bool.** `tpy::frame_slot<T>` (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/frame_slot.hpp`) currently carries a runtime `alive_` bool that the helper's destructor checks before running `~T()`. The bool is redundant: codegen already knows which frame fields are live at each suspension point. Replace with: (a) liveness analysis emitting an alive-set per `__state` value; (b) the generator/coro struct's destructor switches on `__state` and calls `slot.destroy()` on each live slot; (c) `frame_slot::~frame_slot()` becomes a no-op (caller-driven). Saves one byte + one branch per slot and makes "uninitialized until source-level write" a fully compile-time invariant. Care points: exception thrown mid-suspension must destroy the partial frame correctly (catch path needs the throwing state's alive-set); interaction with pointer-form (`T*`) slots and synthetic optional slots stays consistent. Defer until a workload needs the cycles.
- **Pack `__cancel_pending` into `__state`'s high bit on coro frames.** Every async-def coro struct emits parallel `int32_t __state` + `bool __cancel_pending` fields (see e.g. `tests/cases/async/asyncio_cancel_unblocks_future_wait/expected/include/main.hpp`). With alignment this is 4-8 bytes per frame the bool effectively costs. The bit-packing alternative: states never exceed a few dozen so the int32_t has 25+ free high bits; encode cancel as `__state |= CANCEL_BIT` on cancel, mask via `__state & STATE_MASK` on dispatch in the resumable switch. Codegen sites: `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py` (field emit + every state transition + every cancel-check). Cost: every dispatch needs the mask, every state write needs `& ~CANCEL_BIT` somewhere. If multi-threaded async ever lands the read/write would need atomic ordering (today both are plain single-threaded writes, ordering is implicit). Single-byte saving per frame is small in absolute terms but cumulative across long-running programs with many tasks. Touches every async-def expected snapshot in the test corpus -- defer until a workload demands the cycles, or until a broader resumable-frame refactor lands.
- **Heap-allocated slots for `Optional[Polymorphic]` locals with rvalue rebinds.** Local declarations of `Optional[Polymorphic] = ValErr(...)` work for the init-only case (codegen types the slot at the rvalue's actual class type so `&slot` upcasts), but the rebind path raises a clean `CodeGenError` because the shared rebind slot is typed at the polymorphic base and would slice. Phase 20 (above) rejects this shape at sema entirely, pointing users at `Box[Throwable]` for owned polymorphic locals. This entry is preserved as the *alternative* direction (transparent `std::unique_ptr<Base>` slot, one heap allocation per rebind, no source-level change required) -- not planned unless Phase 20's explicit-Box ergonomics turn out to be too heavy in practice. Lives in `_gen_pointer_local_init` and `_gen_pointer_local_rebind` in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`.
- **Multi-protocol-diamond const-merge in `_get_dynamic_override_info`.** Current behavior is first-wins (most-derived MRO ancestor's protocol decides const-ness for a given method name). In a diamond where two sibling `@dynamic` protocols declare the same method with conflicting `is_readonly`, the first-visited one wins and the override emitted by the concrete record may silently fail to override the sibling's virtual slot (the sibling's pure virtual stays abstract, derived class stays abstract). Latent today -- no stdlib or test exercises this shape. Fix when a real reproducer surfaces; the right resolution is probably to reject the conflicting-const declaration at registration time, not to silently pick one.
- Per-slot const inference for tuple borrow params. Today `decide_param_const` is per-param all-or-nothing: if any tuple slot is mutated, all slots stay non-const. Per-slot precision (`tuple[mut_slot, readonly_slot]` -> `const tuple<T*, const T*>&`) requires extending `mutated_params: frozenset[int]` to a slot-aware map (`frozenset[tuple[int, int]]` of (param_index, slot_index)?) plus matching propagation in `mutation_propagation.py` and tracking through `_analyze_tuple_unpack`'s borrow edges. Codegen already supports per-slot const through `TupleType._element_to_cpp_param(t, const)`. Worth doing once a real user case emerges where the per-tuple coarseness forces awkward signature splits; defer until then.
- Unify `TypeDef.cpp_formatter` and `@native` / `_native_cpp_names`. They're two implementations of "how to render this type in C++ when the Python name isn't the answer": `cpp_formatter` handles builtins (parameterized: `list[T]` -> `std::vector<T>`), `_native_cpp_names` is populated per-compilation in `codegen_cpp/generator.py::generate` with four kinds of entries: (1) `@native` records with `native_name` -- static per-qname; (2) nested types (`Outer.Inner` -> `Outer::Inner`) -- static per-qname; (3) **cross-module canonical qualification** -- `ArrayList` in one module's view resolves to `::tpystd::tplib::array_list::ArrayList`, but the resolved path depends on the compiling module's import graph (facade routing, etc.), so this is **per-compilation, not per-qname**; (4) builtin stub records (`TextIO` -> `tpy::TextFile`). Cases 1, 2, 4 could move to `TypeDef.cpp_formatter`; case 3 cannot because `TypeDef` is keyed by qname and the answer depends on compilation context. A proper unification requires first splitting `_native_cpp_names` into "static per-qname" and "dynamic per-compilation" tables, then migrating only the static part. Naturally pairs with the per-compilation-state-onto-Compiler refactor below, since both case 3 and the Compiler-instance move require the same "which module am I compiling right now" context plumbing. Also blocked on `@cpp_template` composition for parameterized native records. Defer until the Compiler-instance refactor lands.
- Explicit variant-alternative construction at recursive-union value sites. Codegen currently emits `return 42;` / `return 3.14;` / `return "hi";` and relies on the wrapper struct's forwarding constructor + `std::variant`'s overload resolution to land on the right alternative. Sema already classified the source's TpyType (after the int->float coercion fix), but that decision isn't transmitted to C++ -- it gets re-derived from the literal's C++ type. Fragile: a future variant-member addition or reordering can silently shift selection (same class as the int->float regression, one layer down). Cleanup: thread the resolved alternative through codegen and emit explicit construction at every recursive-union value site -- function returns, list/dict element initializers, field assignments. Forms to consider: `return ::tpy::BigInt(42);` (typed temp; works because the wrapper's template ctor accepts BigInt unambiguously), or `return V{std::in_place_type<::tpy::BigInt>, 42};` (most transparent, no overload-resolution dependency). Skip the wrap when the literal is already typed correctly (`3.14` is `double`; `"hi"` is `string_view`/`string`). Scope is wide: every value-into-recursive-union site, not just returns. Defer until a real bug forces it; current behavior happens to be correct for our existing tests after sema's natural-member fix.

## Frontend-plugin v1 follow-ups
Surfaced by the v1-shipped review; none block existing functionality
but each is a real architectural / hygiene gap worth fixing before a
second concrete plugin lands.
- Plugin-emitted `StarImport` is fully wired through `frontend_ir/` and `compiler._expand_star_imports_for_module`, but `docs/FRONTEND_PLUGIN_DESIGN.md` "Imports (v1)" explicitly says plugin-emitted modules may NOT use star imports. The Pascal frontend relies on it for `uses` clauses, so the doc is the side that's wrong -- either update the spec to allow star imports under the constraints actually in force, or carve out a narrower form.
- `tpyc/compiler.py:32` imports `FrontendDiagnosticCategory` and uses it nowhere; `_discover_via_plugin` strips `FrontendDiagnostic.diagnostic` immediately, so the category-aware banner path in `FrontendDiagnostic.format()` is permanently unreachable. Either route plugin diagnostics through `FrontendDiagnostic.format()` (preserving category) or remove the unused machinery.
- `get_module_name` is implemented twice with identical logic: as a standalone function in `tpyc/cli.py:179` (consumed by conftest) and as a static method on `ModuleResolver` (consumed by the compiler). The two will drift. Fix: have `cli.get_module_name` delegate to `ModuleResolver.get_module_name`.
- `_all_plugin_entry_extensions` uses `lru_cache(maxsize=1)` while the sibling `discover_cases` uses `@functools.cache`. Both are no-arg caches; pick one decorator and use it consistently.
- `_frontend_registry_for(src_file)` derives the case dir as `src_file.parent.parent` without asserting the immediate parent is `src/`. Fragile implicit contract. Fix: assert (or document) the layout.
- `lib_dirs` ordering between CLI (`_run_cli` slots plugin libs at `len(lib_dirs) - 1` so the final order is `user_libs + plugin_libs + stdlib`) and conftest (`extra_lib_dirs + DEFAULT_LIB_DIRS`, no user-libs channel) is asserted by mirroring rather than shared logic. The next CLI ordering tweak (e.g. adding env-var lib dirs) will silently drift from the test harness. Fix: extract `compose_lib_dirs(user_libs, plugin_libs, stdlib_dir, no_stdlib)` in `tpyc/cli.py` and call it from both sites.
- `Record.base` -> `TpyRecord.bases` lowering (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py:_lower_record`) has only unit coverage of the lowered shape (`tpyc/test_frontend_lower_records.py`); nothing exercises the full pipeline (lowering -> sema registration -> inherited field/method resolution -> codegen). No frontend plugin emits `Record(base=...)` yet, so the integration regression guard for the original "inherited members unresolved" bug can't be added until one does. Add an end-to-end case when the first plugin emits a subclass. (Note: a fixing commit message claimed an end-to-end record-inheritance case exercises this; no such case exists -- the claim is unsubstantiated.) Surfaced by `/tpy-review` test-coverage.
- `Decorator` -> native lowering (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py:_route_decorators`, `BUILTIN_LOWERING(tpy.native)`) has only unit coverage of the lowered shape (`tpyc/test_frontend_lower_macros.py` -- native fields on `TpyFunction`/`TpyRecord`, registry/target/conflict errors, `macro_data`); nothing exercises the full pipeline (lowering -> sema -> codegen -> C++ link/run). No in-tree frontend plugin emits a native decl yet, and a full native-interop case needs a C++ companion + include/link directives. Add an end-to-end case when the first plugin emits native -- a downstream plugin's native-binding cases are the intended integration guard. (Same deferral shape as the `Record.base` note above.) The `macro_data` channel (`FrontendModule.macro_data` -> `TpyModule.macro_data` -> `FunctionMacroContext.module_data`) likewise has only unit coverage until that plugin lands.
- `Call` kwargs -> `TpyMethodCall.kwargs` lowering for an attribute (method) callee (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py:_lower_expr`) has only unit coverage of the lowered shape (`tpyc/test_frontend_lower_method_kwargs.py`); nothing exercises the full pipeline (lowering -> sema kwarg binding). The original bug was sema reporting "too few arguments" because kwargs were dropped, but the unit test stops at the lowering boundary and can't reach sema. No frontend plugin emits `obj.method(key=value)` yet, so the sema-level regression guard can't be added until one does. Add an end-to-end case when the first plugin emits a method call with keyword args. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` test-coverage.
- `BUILTIN_LOWERING` dispatch in `_route_decorators` (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py`) hard-codes `if dec.name != "tpy.native"` in parallel to the registry that is supposed to own routing. Adding a second `BUILTIN_LOWERING` entry to `CORE_DECORATORS` requires editing both `decorators.py` and `lower.py`. Unify the dispatch (e.g. a per-entry lowering handler keyed off `DecoratorEntry`) when the second builtin decorator lands. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` architecture-fit.
- `Conditional` -> `TpyIfExpr` lowering (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py:_lower_expr`) has only unit coverage of the lowered shape (`tpyc/test_frontend_lower_conditional.py`); nothing exercises the full pipeline (lowering -> sema -> codegen). No in-tree frontend plugin emits a ternary yet -- a downstream plugin's branch-selection lowering is the intended integration guard. Add an end-to-end case when the first plugin emits `Conditional`. (Same deferral shape as the `Record.base` / `Decorator` / `Call`-kwargs notes above.)
- `Param.default` -> `TpyFunction.defaults` lowering (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py:_lower_function`) has no constant-expression gate, unlike the parser path: the parser validates every default via `_validate_const_default` (`tpyc/parse/parser.py`), sema does NOT re-validate (its `_validate_param_default_enums` defers "other forms" to the parser), and codegen `default_to_cpp` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/functions.py`) falls back to a bare `return "0"`. So a plugin supplying a non-constant default (a `Call`, `BinOp`, or arbitrary `Name`) would silently miscompile to `0` instead of erroring. Latent -- no in-tree plugin emits `Param.default` yet, no test triggers it. Fix needs a design decision on where to share the constant-default predicate (reuse `expr_to_cpp_default` / `_validate_const_field_default` in `tpyc/sema/registration.py` so both producers are gated at the point sema owns, or factor the parser's `_validate_const_default` into a loc-agnostic predicate callable from lowering) -- run `/tpy-add-feature`, present the placement decision before code. Fold in the related edge case: an untyped param (bare `self`) with a default is now newly reachable and accepted silently (a defaulted `self` is nonsensical) -- decide reject-vs-accept as part of the same validation work. Also add an end-to-end case exercising lowering -> sema -> codegen when the first plugin emits a defaulted param (same deferral shape as the `Record.base` / `Conditional` notes above). Surfaced by `/tpy-review` architecture-fit / test-coverage.
- (really low prio) `AugAssign` -> `TpyAugAssign` lowering (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py:_lower_stmt`) has only unit coverage of the lowered shape (`tpyc/test_frontend_lower_aug_assign.py`); nothing in-tree exercises lowering -> sema -> codegen. The in-tree path to a real end-to-end guard is the Pascal frontend: its `DESIGN.md` specs `inc(x)`/`dec(x)` as `AugAssign`, but `pascal_translator/translate.py:_lower_inc_dec_stmt` actually emits `Assign(x, BinOp(x + n))` (the `x = x + n` form). Aligning the plain-`Ident` path of that emit with the spec (the var-param path routes through `unsafe_store`, which has no augmented form, so it stays as-is) both fixes the spec-vs-impl drift and provides the guard. Confirm first whether `x += n` vs `x = x + n` changes generated C++ for `int` (`__iadd__` vs `__add__`); if so it churns any Pascal snapshot using `inc`/`dec`. Same deferral shape as the `Conditional` note above.
- `docs/FRONTEND_PLUGIN_DESIGN.md` "Expressions" advertises several IR nodes that are NOT implemented in `tpyc/frontend_ir/nodes.py`: `TupleLit`, `DictLit`, `Slice`, `CharLit`, `BytesLit`, `Lambda`, `FormatString`, and the comprehension nodes (`ListComp`/`DictComp`/`SetComp`/`GeneratorExpr`). A plugin emitting any of them gets an unrecognized-node lowering failure, not the documented behavior. (`Conditional` was in this set until this branch implemented it.) Either implement them on demand (as `Conditional` was) or mark them planned in the doc so the contract matches the implementation. Spec-vs-impl drift, same kind as the `StarImport` note above.
- `module_data` is threaded to `FunctionMacroContext` but not to `ClassInfo` (`tpyc/macro_api.py`). A `MACRO`-routed decorator on a `record` (via a plugin `decorator_manifest`) would see `module_data == None` in its class macro. Currently latent -- records only collect `pending_macros` and the function-macro phase doesn't scan methods -- but thread `module_data` to `ClassInfo` when class macros on plugin-emitted records become reachable. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` architecture-fit.
- `_TARGET_KINDS` (`tpyc/frontend_ir/decorators.py`) is an over-broad `build_registry` validation whitelist: it accepts `enum`, `enum_value`, `field`, and `constant`, but `_route_decorators` is only ever called with `"record"` / `"function"` (`_lower_enum` and field/constant lowering never route decorators at all). So a plugin manifest entry targeting one of those four kinds *registers* successfully yet can never fire -- a plugin-authoring trap (a decorator that registers but silently never runs), not a miscompile of user code. Remove the unroutable kinds from the whitelist until their lowering is wired (leaving only `function`/`record`), or wire the routing. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` architecture-fit.
- `_route_decorators` / `_native_spec` reject branches lack unit coverage: MACRO-route positional args, `BUILTIN_LOWERING` with an unimplemented name, unknown `target_kind` in a manifest, `@native` with more than one positional arg, and a non-bool `@native(function=...)`. The `module_data` channel is also only verified to land on `TpyModule.macro_data`, not end-to-end through `run_function_macros` -> `FunctionMacroContext.module_data`. Add these to `tpyc/test_frontend_lower_macros.py`. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` test-coverage.
- `CallMacroContext.module_data` (now exposed via `ctx.macro_data`, set in `bind_imports`) has only unit coverage (`tpyc/test_compiler.py::TestCallMacroModuleData` wires `bind_imports -> ctx -> CallMacroContext.module_data` directly, bypassing the `FrontendModule.macro_data -> lower.py -> TpyModule.macro_data` seam). Add an end-to-end snapshot case under `tests/cases/macros/` when a plugin first sets `FrontendModule.macro_data` -- ideally covering `FunctionMacroContext.module_data` parity in the same case (that sibling channel is also only unit-tested; see the `Decorator`-native note above). Surfaced by `/tpy-review` test-coverage.
- The same `module.macro_data` payload now reaches macros via two channels: `FunctionMacroContext` gets it as an explicit ctor kwarg threaded through `run_function_macros` / `run_deferred_sema_macros` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`), while `CallMacroContext` reads `ctx.macro_data` (always set in `bind_imports`). Since the ctx slot is now live before any macro expansion, the kwarg threading is redundant -- `FunctionMacroContext` could read `ctx.macro_data` directly, unifying the two paths so they can't drift. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` architecture-fit.
- The new `module_name()` plugin hook renames the entry module inside the compiler (`Compiler._module_name_for` -> `compiled.name`), but `tpyc/cli.py:501` still derives the build identity (`BuildLayout` output dir, CMake target, binary name, and the `Compiler.from_source` name) from the path-based `get_module_name()`, which never consults the hook. A plugin whose `module_name()` returns a name differing from the file stem gets `compiled.name` and the output/binary name out of sync (latent: no in-tree plugin overrides `module_name()` yet). Fix: route the CLI's entry name through the same plugin-aware path. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` architecture-fit.
- `compiler._discover_module_via_plugin` (`tpyc/compiler.py:1610-1612`) builds `(resolver.base_dir, *resolver.extra_dirs)` inline -- the same construction now provided by the new `_plugin_search_dirs()` helper, differing only by an explicit `resolver is None` guard. Fold the inline copy into the helper (giving the helper the None-guard it already has) so the search-dir set has one source of truth. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` architecture-fit.
- `Compiler._module_name_for`'s fallback branch (a plugin claims the extension but its `module_name()` returns `None`, so the path-based name is used) is untested. `tpyc/test_frontend_plugin_naming.py` only covers the plugin-provides-a-name path. Add a case with a plugin whose `module_name()` returns `None` and assert the entry is named by its path stem. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` test-coverage.
- The `module_name()`/`resolve_module()` contract documents the invariant `resolve_module(module_name(p)) == p` for every claimed file, but nothing enforces it -- no assertion in the compiler, no round-trip test. A plugin that violates it (e.g. `module_name` and `resolve_module` disagreeing on a name) fails in a confusing downstream way rather than at the boundary. Add a round-trip test (and consider a debug-time assertion) once a second plugin exercises the pair. Surfaced by `/tpy-ready` retrospective.
- IR overload groups (`Function.is_overload` -> `is_overload_stub`, `tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py`) have no IR-path regression test for the two overload error diagnostics that the lowered flag makes reachable: `_reject_same_param_overloads` (two `is_overload` members with identical param-type sequences) and `_reject_bodied_overloads_with_impl` (a bodied `is_overload` member paired with a same-named non-overload impl). Both are exercised for source-level `@overload` (`error_overload_same_params_diff_return`, `error_overload_body_with_impl`) and key purely on `is_overload_stub`, so the IR path can't diverge today -- these are regression anchors, not gap closures. Add them via a plugin emitting a bad group (the existing `_OverloadPlugin` in `tpyc/test_frontend_lower_overload.py` is the template). Surfaced by `/tpy-review` test-coverage.
- No `tests/cases/` snapshot test exists for a plugin-emitted overload group; `test_ir_overload_group_resolves_by_arg_type` exercises sema but produces no generated-C++ snapshot, so codegen drift for IR-synthesised overloads would be invisible. Codegen is unchanged by the `is_overload` work (the lowered group is identical to a source group), so this is low urgency -- add an end-to-end case when the first concrete plugin emits an overload group, alongside the other "first plugin emits X" deferrals above. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` test-coverage.

## Open feature gaps
- **Relax the nested-type / same-class-member collision rejection using the shadow-qualification mechanism.** A class declaring both a nested type and a same-named field/method is still a hard sema error (`Class 'X' declares both a nested type 'Y' and a field/method 'Y'`, `analyzer.py`) -- distinct from the sibling-type collision (now fixed by fully-qualifying the shadowed type inside the record's emission). The same mechanism (`_qualify_shadowed_nominals` + `local_qualified_cpp_names`, which already registers nested records by qname) could likely qualify `::tpyapp::mod::Outer::Inner` and lift the rejection, matching CPython (which allows it). Extra subtlety: the nested type is declared *inside* the struct, so verify a member declared before it doesn't still shadow the in-struct declaration point. Low value (rare shape, clean diagnostic today). Surfaced fixing the member-name/type-name sibling collision.
- **Optional-typed fields on `@native` records; then upgrade OSError's attrs to close the `None` divergence.** No `@native` record carries an `X | None` field today -- the C++ member would be `std::optional<...>`, `native_field` access codegen would need optional-member handling, and Optional STRING fields hit the borrow-form/storage-form duality for views (the recurring bug-class area). If that capability lands, upgrade `OSError.errno/strerror/filename/filename2` from the `0`/`""` sentinels to true Optionals: the only observable divergences the sentinels leave are `e.errno is None` (currently a loud compile error; falsiness is the documented spelling) and printing the raw unset attribute (`0` vs `None`) -- equality and truthiness already behave CPython-identically (probe-verified: `Optional[Int32] == Int32` compiles and returns False when empty). Declared in LANGUAGE_FEATURES until then. Surfaced re-examining the sentinel decision on the oserror-exact branch.
- **User-defined `__raise__` on Throwable subclasses (pure-TPy dispatching raises).** Sema currently rejects a user `__raise__` (`error_throwable_user_override_raise`: the Throwable ABI methods are codegen-auto-emitted and an override would collide) -- but the collision is mechanical, not principled: codegen could emit the user's body instead of the auto `throw *this`. Allowing it would let pure-TPy code express the OSError-style ctor-time-decision raise dispatch (`e = MyError(code, msg); raise e` surfacing a subclass) that today needs `@native` interop + `@virtual_raise`; a raising factory function covers only the direct-raise half. Design considerations captured now so they aren't rediscovered: (a+c, one design move) codegen appends an implicit `throw *this;` fallthrough to the emitted override, so a body path that returns normally means "throw myself as-is" -- this satisfies the C++ `[[noreturn]]` contract with no raise-only termination analysis AND gives the non-dispatch fallback a spelling that doesn't recurse (`raise self` would re-enter __raise__; falling off the end doesn't); (b) presence of a user `__raise__` implicitly sets the record's `virtual_raise` flag so the fresh-throw peephole is skipped (the explicit `@virtual_raise` decorator stays for `@native` classes, where the compiler can't see the C++ body -- and is now REJECTED on non-native classes as accepted-then-meaningless, see `error_virtual_raise_non_native`); (d) the body must be effectively readonly (const receiver); (e) `clone`/`what` stay auto-emitted (only `__raise__` opens up). CPython has no `__raise__` at all, so this is TPy-only surface -- no parity contract, but the semantics should be documented in EXCEPTION_DESIGN.md when built. Surfaced by the @virtual_raise design discussion on the oserror-exact branch.
- **Bound-coroutines v1 exclusions** (from the create_task/run de-intrinsic + bound-coroutine-handle feature, zero-alloc concrete representation; each is a follow-up, none is silent): (1) *top-level (module-scope) coroutine binding* -- `c = f()` at module level is rejected at the consumption site ("cannot copy non-copyable type 'Cancellable[...]'": globals are not last-use-movable); usable but the diagnostic should name the coroutine and suggest binding inside a function. (2) *All-paths must-consume* -- the "bound coroutine is never consumed" warning fires only for handles never read after binding; a conditionally-consumed handle (consumed in one branch, dropped in the other) gets no diagnostic, and any NON-cancel read clears it (a `.cancel()` receiver read is carved out and no longer silences it; the `.cancel`-on-coroutine type conflation itself remains BUGS-filed). Upgrade to a flow_facts intersection over true consumption points. (3) *Nested defs* -- `unread_coro_locals` drains at each body end, so a nested def's analysis can clear an enclosing body's pending entries (missed warning, never a false one). (4) *Container/field storage* -- coroutines in `list[...]`/fields stay rejected; `Box[Cancellable[T]]` is the owned-storage spelling (unchanged). (4b) *No elegant "await one of two bound coroutines"* -- the ternary over handles is rejected (borrow) and the if/else form leaves the un-awaited handle warning as unconsumed; CPython allows the alias form freely (the loser gets a GC RuntimeWarning). Loud, but a real expressiveness corner of the move-only model.
