This blocks the canonical context-manager `__exit__(self, exc_type: type | None, exc_val: BaseException | None, exc_tb: object | None) -> bool` signature -- users must leave those params unannotated. `object` could map to `Any`; `type`-as-value is gated on the same first-class-class-object work as the `## Open feature gaps` `type()` items. At minimum, document the unannotated `__exit__` workaround. Surfaced by /tpy-fuzzy-test (tplib-advanced), 0.3.0 smoke test.
- Enum name lookup: `Kind["TEXT"]` -> `Kind.TEXT`. CPython uses `EnumMeta.__getitem__` with a string key. C++ codegen: `EnumUtil<Kind>::from_name("TEXT")` (already exists in runtime). Needs subscript support on enum types in sema + codegen.
- properties master
- **[LOW] Async: support recursive inline `await` via an opt-in heap-allocated sub-future.** Self- and mutually-recursive inline `await` (`async def f(n): ... return await f(n-1)`; `ping`/`pong`) are rejected today (`async/error_async_{self,mutual,3cycle}_recursive_await`): an inline await stores the callee coro by value in the awaiter's frame (`std::optional<__coro_callee>`), so a cycle is infinite-size. The cycle is already detected by the topological coro-struct emit (`_emit_async_coro_structs`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/generator.py`). Supporting recursion means heap-allocating the *cyclic* sub-future edge -- a pointer member (`std::unique_ptr<__coro_callee>` or a `Box[...]`) is fixed-size and works off the forward decl, breaking the cycle; emplace becomes a heap alloc, reads deref through the pointer. Only the back-edge that closes a cycle needs heaping; acyclic awaits stay by-value (zero-alloc preserved). **Allocation must be opt-in / non-silent** (it crosses the `@noalloc` zero-heap invariant; recursion inherently needs heap, like CPython frames). Open design question -- candidate spellings: (a) **`await Box(f(n-1))`** [lead -- reuse the existing `Box[T]` explicit-heap primitive at the exact await site; non-recursive `await f()` stays inline]; (b) a coro-level decorator `@recursive` / `@heap_recursion` [coarser, doesn't mark the alloc site]; (c) a `# tpy: heap_await` directive on the await line. Existing no-new-syntax escape hatch: drive the recursion through a `Task` (`asyncio.create_task` already heap-boxes the coro) -- which the reject diagnostic points to; this feature is the lighter-weight direct form. Runtime caveat: the poll chain nests (`poll(outer)`->`poll(sub)`->...), so deep recursion recurses through C++ `poll()` calls -> stack overflow at large depth (bounded recursion fine). Low priority; Task workaround exists. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` (the spelling is a real design decision). Surfaced by user review of the recursive-await reject.
- Generator: `yield from`, `send()`, `throw()`, `close()`
- Generator: protocol-typed params -- residual gaps. Multi-yield resumable-frame generators/coroutines with a static-protocol param (`def gen(it: Iterable[T])`) are DONE (H4, 2026-06-03): the for-loop iterator frame field is typed against the deduced template arg `T_<pname>` (the captured param), not the abstract concept. Follow-ups remain: (1) a protocol param *aliased into a local* then iterated across a suspension (`xs = it; for x in xs: yield x`) is now DONE for the resumable (multi-yield) path -- sema records a forwarding fact (`TpyFunction.forwarded_locals`) for a single-assignment bare-param alias, the local occupies no frame field, and codegen resolves every use to the captured param via `ctx.generator_storage_name`, reusing its `T_<pname>` deduction (generator: `iterators/gen_proto_param_aliased_local` + method variant; coroutine: `async/async_proto_param_aliased_local`; reassignment still rejected: `iterators/error_gen_proto_param_realiased_local`). Remaining sibling: aliasing an `Own[Awaitable[T]]` param then `await`-ing the alias (`aw = coro_param; await aw`) is moot today -- the await-operand restriction rejects awaiting a bare name (only a direct call / Task / Future / `__poll__` value is accepted), so the forwarding path is unreachable there; revisit if await-of-bare-name is ever supported. The non-param-sourced rejection branch and the alias-of-alias chain (`ys = xs`) are now covered by `iterators/error_gen_proto_param_alias_chain`. (2) simple (single-yield) proto-param generators are a separate concern (the lambda peephole tries `begin()/end()` instead of `__next__()`), and the alias case there is buggy (copies / miscompiles -- see BUGS.md); (3) `await consume(<collection literal>)` -- awaiting a protocol-param coroutine with a `[...]`/`{...}` literal arg directly -- is cleanly rejected (`async/error_async_await_proto_param_literal`); awaiting a *named* iterable works, and the **workaround now works** (bind the literal to a local first, then await -- `async/async_await_proto_param_local_bind`; the local-collection-in-coro crash that previously blocked it is fixed). The remaining gap is only the direct-literal ergonomic form, which would need the await arg hoisted into a frame field (extending the rvalue-temp await-arg hoist); the materialization target now exists.
- Generator: liveness optimization -- only promote yield-crossing variables to struct fields, keep others as stack locals in `__next__()` (currently all locals are promoted)
- Closures: `std::move_only_function` for `Callable` with `Own[T]` params/returns (design doc says to use it, currently uses `std::function` with rvalue params which works but is incorrect per C++23 semantics)
- Closures: `@noalloc` enforcement (Phase 5) -- `Callable` in `@noalloc` context should be rejected. Currently compiles without error.
- `@noalloc` does not flag heap allocations generally: a plain list literal (`xs = [1, 2, 3]`) and the coroutine erasure-boundary `make_adapter` allocation (create_task args, `-> Own[Cancellable[T]]` factory returns) both compile clean under `@noalloc` (probe-verified 2026-07-03). The Level-1 warn-on-allocation profile is the designed home for making the (signature-declared, deliberately marker-free) erasure-boundary allocations visible on demand -- when profile enforcement is built, include the make_adapter boundary class.
- `in` operator without `__contains__`: warn in `@hotpath`/`@noalloc` contexts. Falls back to O(n) linear iteration (`std::ranges::contains` for built-in NativeIterables, `::tpy::__iter__` + `__next__` for others), which is almost certainly not intended in perf-critical code. Users should define `__contains__` for O(1) lookup or be aware of the cost.
- Closures: method references (`obj.method` as a value) -- needs partial application binding `self`. Currently gives "has no field" error.
- Protocol isinstance in ternary expressions: `x = a.foo() if isinstance(a, P1) else a.bar()` generates a runtime `?:` but both branches must be valid C++ at template instantiation time. Fix: generate an IIFE with `if constexpr` inside, e.g. `[&]() -> T { if constexpr (P1<T_a>) { return a.foo(); } else { return a.bar(); } }()`. This also enables single-line field init in `__init__` (goes into the C++ member initializer list instead of requiring unconditional pre-assignment + reassignment in branches).
- Allow `@runtime_checkable` decorator on protocols (no-op in tpyc, enables CPython compatibility for isinstance checks on user-defined protocols)
- Nested patterns in match/case: `case Box(value=str() as v)` for disambiguating parameterized union members like `Box[str] | Box[int]`. Uses field guards with type sub-patterns -- standard Python match syntax, CPython-compatible.
- allow type annotation to use "" (forward decl)
- C-style for loop: reassigning loop variable affects iteration (differs from Python)

## Documentation
- **[LOW] Reconcile isinstance status in FEATURE_ROADMAP / UNION_TYPES_DESIGN.** `docs/FEATURE_ROADMAP.md` (~line 208, ~520) and `docs/UNION_TYPES_DESIGN.md` (~line 52) still list the `isinstance(x, (A, B))` tuple form (and isinstance-on-alias) as "not done" / "design only" -- stale pre-existing drift: the tuple form has long shipped, and the inline-union form (`isinstance(x, A | B)`) plus the union-alias-name rejection now exist too. `LANGUAGE_FEATURES.md` is current; these design docs lag. Surfaced by /tpy-review (docs-sync) of the inline-union isinstance feature.
- language restriction documentation
- `tpy --print-types`: internal modules (`tpy._bootstrap.*`, `tpy._builtins.*`, `tpy._core.*`, `tpy._typing`) are shown as top-level sections because that's where the real class/function bodies live. Users import names like `Int32`, `Own`, `Span` from `tpy` (via re-exports in `tpy/__init__.py`), so seeing them under `tpy._core._types` is both noisy and slightly misleading (the import path is "wrong"). Hiding the `_*` modules outright removes visibility of every builtin type -- fix properly requires walking re-export chains: when `tpy/__init__.py` does `from ._core import Int32`, attribute `Int32` to the `tpy` section (with a note on where it's defined) and hide the underlying `_core/_types` entry. Non-trivial: needs import resolution across the package tree in `tpyc/dump_types.py`.

## Type System
- **[design] Sub-default-int arithmetic: promote, or keep width-preserving?** Today fixed-int arithmetic is width-preserving and checked: `Int8 + Int8 -> Int8` via `add_check<int8_t>` (panics on overflow), with explicit `add_wrap`/`shl_wrap`/... statics as the deliberate-wraparound escape hatch (`lib/tpy/tpy/_core/_types.py` stub dunders). That is Rust's model; C++ instead promotes anything narrower than `int` to `int` before arithmetic, so C++ never computes in int8. The user-visible consequence: narrow-typed values are hostile to ordinary arithmetic (`dt.hour * 3600` in Int8 needs explicit `int(...)` widening), which blocks packing value-type fields (datetime/time could shrink 28->12 / 16->8 bytes with Int8/Int16 fields, like CPython's C datetime does internally -- the motivating case; boundaries/ctor args stay `int` regardless, the validate-in-BigInt-then-store contract is orthogonal). Three candidate shapes: (a) full C++-style promotion of sub-default-int operands to the default int -- but C++'s own experience says this is hated in byte-manipulation code (`b[i] & 0x0F` promoting forces casts back into byte buffers; TPy's stdlib -- base64/hashlib/struct/socket -- is full of width-preserving UInt8 ops), and bitwise-vs-arithmetic split rules would be a third rulebook; (b) promote only MIXED-width operands (`Int8 + Int32 -> Int32`), keeping same-width ops closed and byte code cast-free; (c) status quo plus range-proven implicit narrowing on assignment (value_range), which fixes the packed-field read ergonomics without touching arithmetic rules. Churn if (a): retypes stub dunders -> stdlib emitted C++ changes (whole-suite exec re-key), ~42 test cases + 13 stdlib files use sub-Int32 types, dozens-100+ snapshots, plus a rejects-valid ripple (`c: Int8 = a + b` needs a narrowing story, aug-assign too). THIR: not structurally blocked (typing is sema-side, upstream of both codegen paths; the byte-diff gate moves in lockstep) but THIR unit tests pin current `add_check<intN_t>` emits and the value-scalar slice is under active development -- sequence this after the current THIR increments settle. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` (typing-rule change across all fixed-int code). Surfaced by the datetime v2 field-packing question.
- **[feature] Class type-param defaults with an arbitrary default type** -- support `@type_param_default(C=int) class Counter[T: Hashable, C: AnyInt]: ...` (or the PEP 696 native `class Counter[T: Hashable, C: AnyInt = int]`). Two gaps today: (1) `@type_param_default` is FUNCTION-only -- on a class it errors `Unknown macro 'type_param_default'` (the `RecordInfo.type_param_defaults` field exists but isn't wired through class registration/resolution); (2) the default can only be the `DefaultInt` sentinel -> the *configured* default int (Int32), since `type_ops.py` (~1385) handles only `qnames.DEFAULT_INT` and raises on anything else -- so it cannot default to a concrete type like `int`/BigInt. The PEP 696 native syntax `[T, C = int]` is a Python 3.13 feature (env is 3.12 -> parse error), so it's blocked until the min-Python bump. Motivating use: a parametric *count type* for `collections.Counter` (`Counter[Object, Int32]` for cheap fixed-width histograms) while `Counter[T]` keeps `C` defaulting to `int` (arbitrary precision, CPython-matching -- defaulting to Int32 would silently change overflow behavior). Needs the `AnyInt` protocol (above) for the `C` bound. Benefits any generic container wanting a defaulted param (`Box`, `ArrayList`, future `defaultdict`), not just Counter. Surfaced by the Counter parametric-count-type question. Needs `/tpy-add-feature`.
- `is_trivially_destructible()`: current approximation uses `is_value_type() and not is_expensive_copy()`, which is conservative for non-value types (e.g. Span, SpanIter are trivially destructible but return False). Proper implementation: primitives/views/pointers always trivial; records trivial if no `__del__` and all fields/bases are trivially destructible; tuples/optionals trivial if elements are. Used by `del` codegen to elide move-sink.

## Refactor
- **[LOW] `_validate_cpp_template` re-implements `expand_cpp_template`'s brace-scanning grammar -- a soundness coupling, not just duplication.** The cpp_template value-repeat rejection (`tpyc/parse/parser.py:_validate_cpp_template`) hand-copies the `{{`/`}}` escape + matched-`}` + field-extraction scan (and the digit-index normalization) that `expand_cpp_template` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`) uses. The whole safe-by-construction guarantee holds ONLY while the two grammars agree: if they drift (a new placeholder kind, a different escape rule), the validator green-lights a template the expander then double-evaluates -- reintroducing exactly the bug this closed. The parser can't import `codegen_cpp`, so extract the scan into a neutral shared field-iterator both import; and add a shared-corpus regression test that feeds the same template strings to both and asserts their field decompositions agree. Not a merge blocker (both are tested today and the classifier is index-normalized to match `int(field)`). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) + /tpy-ready second-opinion of the cpp_template-safe-by-construction feature.
- **[LOW] No THIR unit test asserts a `THIRMethodCall` carrying a non-None `cpp_template`.** `list.sort`/`Span.sort` were the sole construct that lowered as a `THIRMethodCall` carrying a `cpp_template`, and both migrated to `@native`, so `tpyc/thir/test_thir_containers.py` now only asserts `native_function_name`/`method_cpp` routing. This path is currently unwitnessable in the THIR slice: any method carrying a `@cpp_template` must be on a `@native` class (sema rejects `...` bodies on regular classes), and `@native`-class method calls do not route to THIR at all -- so a synthetic witness cannot be built today, and there is no registered THIR gate face for it (the only cpp_template face is `call.template_free`, a *free* callee -> `THIRCall`), so nothing in the zero-witness tracker regressed. Restore a direct assertion if/when the THIR slice admits a `@native`-class receiver whose method carries a cpp_template. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) + /tpy-ready second-opinion.
- **[LOW] Extract `_gen_method_call`'s argument-rendering into its own method.** The function is ~600 lines and now has two arg-entry shapes -- the lazy `_args()` fallback closure (for the branches that interpolate a fallback string) and the specialized per-param `gen_call_arg` loop in the user-record tail (assigned to `args` before the shared epilogue), reconciled by an `args: str | None = None` sentinel + `if args is None: args = _args()` guard. This works and fits the function's existing "chain of guarded early-returns feeding a shared epilogue" shape, but the arg-rendering is now the natural extraction seam. Hygiene, not a defect. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the value-union-arg double-eval fix.
- **Point `PropertyInfo.getter` at the canonical (surviving) overload.** It currently grabs the first entry of the getter's overload list -- the auto_readonly MUTABLE clone, which `prune_value_property_clones` later removes from `record.methods` for value-typed returns; no observed miscompile (codegen emits the single const overload regardless), but a dangling metadata reference is debt that resurfaces. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of datetime v3.
- **Align the implicit auto_readonly dunder gate with the deferred ValueType pass.** `_apply_implicit_auto_readonly` (method_expansion.py) checks `rtype.is_value_type()` before user-record ValueType flags are set, so `__deref__`/`__getitem__`/`__span__` returning a value record emit a redundant (but VALID -- the pair differs in self const-ness, probe-verified benign) mutable overload. Consistency cleanup, not a defect; mirror the property-prune deferral. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of datetime v3.
- **[LOW] The stale-view-coerce fact is re-derived at two consumer sites instead of stamped on the coerce node.** The `fix-enum-name-strview` repair (a str/bytes view->owned coerce renders identity when its sink resolved VIEW storage) is decided independently in gen_expr's TpyCoerce arm (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`, keyed on the threaded sink type via `_resolve_pending_view`) and in THIR's `_peel_stale_view_owned_coerce` (`tpyc/thir/lower.py`, via `_resolved_str_value`/`_resolved_bytes_value`). The fact is sema-derivable once the pending-view resolution lands -- stamp it on the TpyCoerce node (or replace the coerce) at/after resolution so both paths dispatch on the stamp, per the CLAUDE.md first-class-facts rule. Byte-diff-guarded today; hygiene, not a defect. Surfaced by /tpy-ready second-opinion of `fix-enum-name-strview`.
- **[LOW] Generator/async frame-field decl inits thread a gen_expr target only for TpyCoerce inits.** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` (~2022) threads `var_types.get(name)` into `gen_expr` only when `stmt.init` is a TpyCoerce (the stale-coerce fix), because unconditional threading would make target-sensitive literal renders (bytes/char literals) suddenly target-aware in frames. The trap is not self-guarding: a future target-sensitive render reachable through frame-field inits silently misses the threading the sync decl path gets. Unify the frame-field init emit with the sync decl path's target threading (deliberately, with a full snapshot run). Surfaced by /tpy-ready second-opinion of `fix-enum-name-strview`.
- **[LOW] asyncio.run/create_task interception is a hardcoded qname gate copied at three dispatch sites + the temp-call idiom is at five.** The coroutine-only arg contract (`_analyze_asyncio_spawn_call`) is triggered by `qname in (ASYNCIO_RUN, ASYNCIO_CREATE_TASK)` checks in `tpyc/sema/methods.py` (module-attribute path), `tpyc/sema/calls.py` (IMPORTED_NAME qname branch), and `calls.py` (bare-name registry path keyed on `FunctionInfo.qualified_name`) -- a parallel mechanism to the generalized `special_handling` dispatch (which is coupled to `is_builtin_function`, so not a drop-in for plain TPy library functions). A fourth asyncio-shaped special function would need a fourth copy. Generalize to a FunctionInfo-level post-analysis hook consumed once at the shared dispatch points. SAME refactor family: the borrowed-receiver ESCAPE warning for bound method-coroutines is hardcoded at two sites (the create_task/run contract check + `return c`) -- but ANY consumption that moves a receiver-borrowing handle out of the receiver's scope is the same hazard (e.g. an arg to a user fn taking Own[Cancellable[T]] that stores/spawns it gets no warning). Generalize by keying the warning on the ConcreteCoroType-with-coro_owner -> Own[Cancellable[T]] consumption chokepoint (which sees every escape) instead of asyncio qnames. Also: `_analyze_asyncio_spawn_call`'s internal three-way dispatch is dead generality today (both callees are plain TPy functions), and `methods.py::_analyze_module_method_call` now has FIVE near-verbatim copies of the "synthetic TpyCall -> delegate -> copy 5 fields back" idiom (4 pre-existing) -- extract a `_dispatch_via_temp_call` helper in the same pass. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the bound-coroutines feature.
- **[LOW] `tpy::truediv` integer overloads are ambiguous for non-exact integer arguments.** The overload set (`truediv(double,double)` in `core.hpp`; `truediv(int64_t,int64_t)`, `truediv(uint64_t,uint64_t)`, `truediv(BigInt,BigInt)` in `bigint.hpp`) makes a call with plain `int`/`int32_t`/`size_t` arguments ambiguous (verified with g++). Every codegen `cpp_template` call site casts to an exact match, so no generated code is affected -- this is a landmine only for future hand-written runtime or native-interop code calling `tpy::truediv` directly. If that ever bites, add exact-match thin wrappers (or a constrained template) rather than relying on callers to cast. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness) of the correctly-rounded-division fix.
- **[LOW] Enum qname resolution -- generalized (`enum-qname-index` branch); residual noted.** `TypeRegistry.find_enum_by_qname` now gives a collision-safe qname-keyed enum lookup, implemented by routing through the declaring module's own `ModuleInfo.enums` dict + a bare-name fallback (mirrors `find_record_by_qname`'s tail). No dedicated `_enum_qname_index` was added: unlike records, a local enum always wins its own bare `registry.enums` slot (an aliased import's compensating write runs in an earlier analyze sub-phase), so the current-module index records need is dead weight for enums. A survey of the ~15 `registry.enums`/`get_enum(name)` consumers found all but one safe by construction (local-decl-wins-slot, dotted/nested keys, source-spelling keys, or type-object reads); the one theoretical site (`sema/statements.py` enum for-each over an `IMPORTED_NAME` binding) was migrated to `find_enum_by_qname` -- belt-and-suspenders, since aliased enums actually bind as `BindingKind.ENUM` and take the safe branch, so that fallback branch fires zero times in the corpus. **Residual (intentional, not a bug):** the two sites the merged `fix-enum-alias-collision` patched still re-derive qnames inline rather than routing through the helper -- `parse/type_resolver.py`'s annotation resolver (parse-time NominalType minting; consulting the index there is registration-order-sensitive) and `codegen_cpp/generator.py`'s `native_cpp_names` population (a *producer* of qname spellings, not a lookup consumer). Both are correct as-is; convert only if a future consumer needs it. A third natural consumer exists: `codegen_cpp/expressions.py:4152` builds a dotted `f"{src_mod}.{src_name}"` and passes it to `get_enum(dotted)`, but `registry.enums` is only keyed by bare names, so that disjunct is effectively dead for enums today -- route it through `find_enum_by_qname(dotted)` if that path is ever exercised. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` (architecture-fit) of `enum-qname-index`.
- **[LOW] "`__floordiv__`/`__rfloordiv__` have no C++ `//` operator" is re-derived at four sites.** The datetime v1 P2 fix (decouple `//` from `/`) now encodes this fact in: `tpyc/sema/operators.py` (`DUNDER_CPP_TEMPLATES` method-call template), `tpyc/codegen_cpp/records.py` (`_gen_binary_operators` friend-operator skip), and `tpyc/codegen_cpp/protocols.py` x2 (the structural-concept-check and `@dynamic`-adapter forwarding paths both exclude floordiv from the `DUNDER_TO_BINARY_OP` branch). This is the CLAUDE.md "consumer re-derives the fact" anti-pattern. Clean fix is likely just **dropping `__floordiv__`/`__rfloordiv__` from `DUNDER_TO_BINARY_OP`/`DUNDER_TO_REVERSE_BINARY_OP` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`)** -- then both `protocols.py` exclusions fall out automatically (the `in DUNDER_TO_BINARY_OP` guards go false) and the friend-operator skip in `records.py` becomes redundant too. Not `unify two dicts` (the `modules/defs.py` `DUNDER_CPP_TEMPLATES` is the runtime-helper set and correctly lacks floordiv). Works and is tested today; this is hygiene. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective of the datetime v1 branch.
- **[LOW] Owned-param recognition hand-copies the `unwrap_optional_own(unwrap_readonly(unwrap_send_sync(ptype)))` peel at 6+ sites.** After the Send/Sync-marker-peel fix, this idiom is repeated in `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py` (`_warn_unconsumed_own_params`), `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py` (`_is_owned_var` + is-param-derived), `tpyc/sema/calls.py` (`check_own_param`), and `tpyc/codegen_cpp/{records,statements}.py` -- **plus two untouched twins in `tpyc/thir/lower.py` (`_ctor_param_eligible` / ctor `own_param_names`, ~:1924, ~:1978)** that were NOT given the peel. UNREACHABLE today, not a present miss: `_f1_param_eligible` admits only value scalars / F1-records / scalar tuples / scalar-element containers, so a `Send[Own[T]]` marker param fails that gate and stays on the AST path. It becomes a live AST/THIR divergence the moment the THIR slice is widened to admit marker/own params -- peel there at the same time. Factor a single `own_param_type(ptype) -> OwnType | None` chokepoint (readonly + marker peel + `unwrap_optional_own`) and route all sites through it, including the THIR twins, so a future miss is impossible. Aligns with CLAUDE.md "prefer first-class facts over consumer-side re-derivation". A correct helper should also handle the `Send[Own[T]] | None` shape (see the BUGS.md entry -- the marker distributes inside the Optional today). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit + safety-model) of the threading V1 marker-peel fix.
- **[LOW] Generator `Iterator[T]` validation + `generator_yield_type` assignment + copyable check lives at three parallel sites.** `Registrar._set_generator_yield_type` (`tpyc/sema/registration.py`, added for the @overload-impl path), the inline block in `register_function` (~3102-3117), and the inline block in method registration (~1265-1280) all run the same three steps. The two inline sites are NOT routed through the helper, so they can drift. They differ subtly (the function site nests in the `is_protocol_type` branch with an `else` arm for the non-generator protocol-return error; the method site adds an `isinstance(NominalType)` guard and uses `method.loc or record.loc`; the helper says "Generator function" in its message), so a clean dedup must generalize the message/loc and preserve each site's guard -- it touches the generator-validation path for all functions and methods, so do it as its own commit with a full snapshot run, not bundled into an unrelated fix. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the overloaded-generator fix. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` scope assessment or a careful standalone refactor.
- **[LOW] Cross-module record qualification is resolved at three call sites.** `find_record_by_qname` + `record_qualification` are called in the same sequence at the bare-name ctor path (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py` ~3353), the static-method path (~3557), and the qualified-call path (~3614, added by `fix-qualified-ctor-import-shadow`). The three differ subtly (static-method also handles `is_native`/`native_name` and template args; the ctor paths differ in fallback), so a naive extraction risks regressing one -- factor a shared `_cross_module_record_qual(module, short_name)` helper carefully, with its own review. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of `fix-qualified-ctor-import-shadow`.
- **[LOW] Two `|`-chain flatteners.** `CallAnalyzer._flatten_union_operands` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`, added for inline-union isinstance) and codegen's `_flatten_chain(node, "|")` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) both recursively flatten a left-leaning `TpyBinOp(op="|")` tree over the same node type. They can't import across the phase boundary; the clean fix is one shared module-level helper (e.g. in `tpyc/parse/nodes.py`). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the inline-union isinstance feature.
- **[LOW] Subscript analysis has two parallel record-`__getitem__` dispatch blocks** (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`): a new pre-int-gate block for non-integer keys and the existing post-int-gate block, split by `is_any_int_type(index_type)`. Both reproject readonly onto the result independently, so the readonly rule can drift between them. Unify into one record-`__getitem__` path that validates the index against the key param for both int and non-int keys, falling through to the integer-gate error only when there is no matching `__getitem__`. Two follow-ons fold in here: (a) a non-int key that does NOT match the `__getitem__` key param currently falls through to the misleading "Subscript index must be an integer type" message -- the unified path should emit a key-type-specific diagnostic; (b) an int index on a non-int-keyed `__getitem__` (e.g. `Scores()[42]` with `__getitem__(key: str)`) is wrongly accepted today because the post-int-gate dispatch does not validate the key. Add an `error_` test for the mismatch message when this lands. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit + test-coverage) of the readonly-dict-reads fix.
- **[LOW] `get_iterable_element_type`'s readonly unwrap+reproject is dead for its two primary callers** (`tpyc/modules/type_resolution.py`): the for-loop (`sema/statements.py`) and comprehension paths pre-unwrap `ReadonlyType` and reproject onto the element themselves before calling, so the reprojection rule now lives in both the wrapper and each caller and must be kept in lockstep. Have those callers pass the raw readonly-wrapped type and rely on the wrapper. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the readonly-dict-reads fix.
- **[LOW] `_get_record_getitem_key_ret` duplicates `_get_record_getitem_type`** (`tpyc/sema/narrowing.py`): same record lookup + `build_type_substitution` + `substitute_type_params`. Dedup by having the key-ret helper call `_get_record_getitem_type` for the return-type half (NOT the reverse -- `_get_record_getitem_type` has no param-count gate, so making it delegate to the `len==1`-gated key-ret helper would regress multi-param / slice-only `__getitem__` on the int-index path). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the readonly-dict-reads fix.
- **[LOW] `_get_record_getitem_key_ret` (`tpyc/sema/narrowing.py`) reads only the first `__getitem__` overload** (`lookup_record_method`), so a record with multiple non-integer `__getitem__` overloads dispatches only the first for `obj[key]`. The non-int gate makes this reachable only for 2+ non-int-keyed overloads (exotic). Scan overloads for a key-param match instead. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the readonly-dict-reads fix.
- **[LOW] No `error_` test for `pop(k)` / `del d[k]` rejection on a `readonly[dict]`.** The mutation-rejection path is pre-existing (this branch added read support), so it is a regression-guard gap, not new behavior; add a small `error_readonly_dict_mutate` case. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the readonly-dict-reads fix.
- **[LOW] A `str`-keyed user `operator[]` emits `std::string key` by value, not `std::string_view`** (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/records.py` `_gen_const_subscript_operator` uses `to_cpp()` instead of a param-form spelling); similarly a literal string key on a `readonly[dict[StrView, V]]` emits `std::string` instead of `std::string_view` because `view_key_target` / the subscript key-target hint (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`, `expressions.py`) don't strip `ReadonlyType` before the `is_dict`/`is_set` test. Both are pre-existing perf-only misses (extra heap alloc, temporary lives for the expression -- no dangle); the readonly-dict-reads snapshots first expose the `operator[]` one. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness, safety-model) of the readonly-dict-reads fix.
- **[LOW test-coverage] `BytesView`-keyed dict/set membership (`k in d` / `k in s`).** `dict/view_key_membership` covers the str-view-key path of the new heterogeneous `ordered_map`/`ordered_set::contains` overload; the parallel `BytesView`-against-`bytes`-key path (same `requires { K(k) }` template, `view_key_target` already routes BytesView keys) is untested. Add a case if the bytes-key family is exercised. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the view-key contains fix.
- **[perf] Transparent (heterogeneous) hashing for `ordered_map`/`ordered_set` -- zero-copy view-key lookups.** Every view-key *lookup* on a str/bytes-keyed dict/set materializes a temporary owned key for the comparison: `contains`/`__getitem__`/`__delitem__`/`find`/`pop` all do `find(K(key))` / `erase(K(key))`, and the heterogeneous `contains` overload added for view-key membership does `contains(K(key))`. The underlying `table_` is a non-transparent `std::unordered_map<K, Node*>`, so a `string_view` needle can't probe it without a temp `std::string` (one heap alloc per non-SSO key). Fix: give `table_` a transparent hash + `std::equal_to<>` (C++20 `is_transparent`) so `table_.find(view)` probes directly, then route the lookup ops through it. This **supersedes/consolidates** the heterogeneous `contains` overloads (the separate `const K&` + `KeyArg` overloads collapse into one transparent template) and removes the `K(key)` materialization in the lookup dunders -- do NOT preserve them. `insert_or_assign`/`__setitem__` still own the key (storing it is inherent, matches CPython). Touches `ordered_map.hpp`/`ordered_set.hpp` (the `table_` type + find/contains/erase + the intrusive-node hashing) and `dunder.hpp`'s lookup overloads. Perf-only (no behavior change); aligns the dict/set view-key surface with the project's zero-copy principle. Needs care on hash consistency with the intrusive linked-list node storage + a full suite. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the view-key contains fix.
- **[LOW test-coverage] Reference-type VALUE stored through a non-int-key user `__setitem__`.** `records/setitem_nonint_key` exercises the non-int-key write dispatch (the fix) with an `Int32` value only; a reference-type value (list / user record) forwarded via the `V&&` path is parity-blind per CLAUDE.md (a read-only ref-type test hides a silent copy-vs-alias at the store). Orthogonal to the key-dispatch fix (the `V&&` value forwarding is unchanged and already exercised by int-key `__setitem__`), so not a regression risk -- a completeness add: store a ref-type value via `m["k"]=v`, mutate the original, observe through the stored value (with `copy()` or `no_cpython` to acknowledge TPy's field-store copy). Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage, cpython-parity) of the write-subscript dispatch fix.
- **[LOW] The overload-trial Fn-hint guard (`tpyc/sema/calls.py` ~line 4018) uses a bare `contains_type_param(p)` and was not narrowed like the `_infer_arg_types` guard (which now scopes to the callee's own un-inferred params).** A lambda hint whose param types contain an *enclosing-scope* type param still bails the overload trial there (`return None` = candidate doesn't match -- fail-safe, never a miscompile). Propagating the `callee_unresolved` narrowing would broaden what the overload-trial path accepts, so it wants its own consideration, not a drive-by. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the generic-key-lambda fix.
- **[LOW] A `for x in <Own-yielding generator>` loop copies each yielded element into the consumer frame instead of moving it; and `x = list_field[i]` materializes an owned copy where a const ref would do.** Confirmed in csv `DictReader.__iter__` codegen: `row = ::tpy::unwrap_ref(*(*__for_r_0))` copies the `Own[list[str]]` row per iteration, and `std::string name = ::tpy::__getitem__(fieldnames, i)` copies each field name per row. Both are perf-only for copyable elements (csv uses str), but the loop-var copy is the *for-loop sibling* of the comprehension Own-copy in BUGS.md ("A comprehension over a generator yielding `Own[T]` copy-inserts...") -- and would be a hard `@nocopy` build error the same way if the element type were move-only. Fix shape (shared with that BUGS entry): `std::move` the bound element when the loop var's type is owned/move-only; bind `name` as `const str&` when it doesn't outlive the iteration. General, not csv-specific. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of csv.DictReader.
- **[LOW] `_gen_subscript` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) unwraps `obj_type` independently at several branch points; the `view_key_target(obj_type)` call (~line 5712) still uses the raw form.** The `__getitem__` lookup, tuple fast-path, and bounds-safe path each handle `obj_type` separately. `view_key_target` on a qualified `Own[dict[StrView, V]]` returns None (it needs a bare `NominalType`), so a str/bytes literal key in a generator-yielded dict subscript uses heap storage instead of static -- a perf pessimization (NOT a correctness bug; same blind-spot class as the const-read fix, one line up). A clean fix extracts `bare_obj_type = unwrap_qualifiers(obj_type)` once and routes the type-dispatch branches through it -- but the tuple/Optional-narrowing/bounds-safe branches use raw `obj_type` deliberately, so this is a careful refactor, not a drive-by. Related: `get_record_for_type` (`tpyc/typesys.py`) is called from ~15 sites without caller-side qualifier stripping; unwrapping inside it would close the whole class at the source, but sema callers may rely on it returning None for `Own[T]` to distinguish qualified from bare -- needs an audit before landing. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the const-container subscript-read fix.
- **[LOW] Unify the two address-of (value -> Ptr) coercion branches in `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py`.** The `TypeParamRef -> Ptr[W]` branch sits beside the `NominalType` address-of branch (`actual_is_addr_taker`); both select `UPCAST_TO_PTR`/`UPCAST_TO_CONST_PTR` for the same semantic op on different carrier types. Could widen `actual_is_addr_taker` to also accept a non-INT `TypeParamRef` and generalize the inner `isinstance(expected.inner_pointee, NominalType)` guard to accept `TypeParamRef` identity, giving one address-of block instead of two parallel ones (so future lvalue-enforcement / `forbid_return_local` changes touch one site). Also: `UPCAST_TO_PTR`/`UPCAST_TO_CONST_PTR` (`tpyc/coercions.py`) carry `from_type=_is(NominalType)`, now stale since both are applied directly to `TypeParamRef` -- the annotation is dead (these coercions are used directly, never via `resolve_coercion`) but misleading; drop or correct it. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the generic-param address-of fix.
- **[LOW] Route the stored-copy simple-generator iterator fields through the iter aliases too.** The for-loop-iter-aliases cleanup routed the resumable strategies through `iter_type_t`/`iter_next_t`/`iter_result_t`/`begin_iter_t`/`aiter_type_t`, but left the simple-generator (lambda-peephole) path. Two of its sites are NOT pure expression-form and CAN use the aliases: `gen_generators.py:383` (stored-copy begin: `decltype(std::declval<std::decay_t<decltype(expr)>&>().begin())` == `begin_iter_t<std::decay_t<decltype(expr)>>`) and `:434` (stored-copy `__iter__` == `iter_type_t<std::decay_t<decltype(expr)>>`). Partial flatten only (the `std::decay_t<decltype(expr)>` core remains because the simple path works from a source *expression*, not a type). The `else` branches (`:386`, `:438`) stay inline -- they apply `.begin()`/`__iter__` to a value directly (genuinely expression-form). Low payoff + another corpus regen; capture so the partial win isn't lost. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the for-loop-iter-aliases branch.
- **[LOW] Deduce `create_task`'s `T` from its argument to drop the repeated type at call sites.** `create_task<T>(make_adapter<Cancellable<T>>(coro()))` spells `T` twice -- once as the explicit template arg, once inside `Cancellable<T>` -- e.g. `create_task<std::vector<Settled<int32_t>>>(make_adapter<Cancellable<std::vector<Settled<int32_t>>>>(gather_helper()))`. Since the argument is a `unique_ptr<Cancellable<T>>`, `create_task` could deduce `T` from it (a `template<typename T> Task<T> create_task(unique_ptr<Cancellable<T>>)` overload), letting codegen drop the explicit `<T>` so only the `Cancellable<T>` inside `make_adapter` spells it. Unlike the iter/adapter alias hoists, this is a runtime *signature* change, NOT behavior-identical-by-construction -- it needs real verification (overload resolution at every `create_task` site, the `run`/`task_from_coro` siblings, and the asyncio stdlib callers in `lib/tpy/asyncio/`). Cosmetic payoff (one fewer `T` repetition) for non-trivial risk, so low priority; do it as its own scoped task with a behavior pass, not bundled into a cosmetic-aliases branch. Surfaced reviewing the create_task call-site verbosity after the make_adapter hoist.
- **[LOW] (precision, not unsound) A post-sema function-macro `replace_expr` leaves the replaced call's stale `call_edges` entry in place, blocking readonly inference.** When `_drain_deferred_sema_macros` rewrites a call node sema already recorded a `call_edge` for (e.g. `sentinel(a, b)` -> `a + b`), the now-removed call's edge survives in `FunctionInfo.call_edges` through Phase-2 propagation. Conservative -- it over-reports mutation, so the enclosing function can never be auto-inferred `@readonly` even when its post-macro body is pure; never unsound. Fix shape: have `replace_expr` (post-sema context) drop the replaced subtree's `call_edges` entries, or rebuild the edge set for the function after a post-sema rewrite. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the post-sema function-macro phase.
- **[LOW] (diagnostics) A deferred post-sema function-macro callback that forgets `set_expr_type` on an emitted node produces a CODEGEN crash with no pointer back to the macro.** `run_deferred_sema_macros` (`tpyc/sema/function_macros.py`) wraps a callback's unexpected exceptions onto the decorated function's `loc`, but the common author error -- emitting a node and not typing it -- escapes that try/except entirely: it surfaces far later in codegen (which reads `expr_types` per node), with no reference to the callback or the emitted node. Unlike a pre-sema macro, where a bad node surfaces as a normal sema error at its own span. Fix shape: after a callback runs, validate that every node it emitted via `replace_expr` carries an `expr_types` entry and raise a macro-attributed `SemanticError` if not; or have `set_expr_type`/`replace_expr` track emitted nodes for a post-callback completeness check. Until then, MACRO_DESIGN.md warns authors that an untyped emitted node fails in codegen, not at the macro site. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective of the post-sema function-macro phase.
- **Sema/codegen static-protocol predicate mirror.** `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py::_is_static_protocol_type` is a phase-appropriate mirror of `tpyc/codegen_cpp/protocols.py::is_static_protocol_param` (both: bare protocol / `Optional[proto]` / protocol-union, excluding `@dynamic`). The codegen version interleaves `resolve_type_for_codegen` per sub-type (union members, optional inner) for nominals not yet marked `is_protocol` at parse time; the sema version omits it because sema types are already alias-resolved. They will silently diverge if a new protocol shape is added to one. Not a clean lift (the per-member resolve interleaving means a shared helper needs a resolver callback or restructuring codegen's loop), so scope it separately: extract an `is_static_protocol_type(typ, resolve=identity)` core into `typesys.py` (next to `is_protocol_union`) that both call, codegen passing its resolver. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the proto-param-alias feature.
- Consolidate the two hand-rolled brace-init prefixes onto `TypeResolver.typed_brace_init`. `_gen_slice_assign` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:~1227`) and the `+=` list-extend aug-assign path (`statements.py:~2596`) each hand-roll `f"std::vector<{elem_cpp}>{value}"` to give a collection-literal value a deducible C++ type -- the same brace-init-deduction problem the shared helper `TypeResolver.typed_brace_init` centralizes (used by the subscript-set and dict-comp-value paths). Route both through the helper to drop the parallel hand-written prefixes. The aug-assign site guards on `isinstance(..., TpyArrayLiteral)` (narrower than the helper's `startswith("{")`) -- confirm that guard is still correct (or that `startswith("{")` is safe there) before routing. Verify byte-identical output first (the helper uses `type_to_cpp(target)` = `std::vector<int32_t>` vs the hand-rolled `std::vector<{elem}>`; confirm no snapshot churn before/after). Pre-existing; surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the subscript-set collection-literal fix + the dict-comp-value fix. Effort: XS.
- **[LOW] Let the tuple borrow/storage conversion helpers take element types as a parameter pack instead of the full `std::tuple<...>`.** `tuple_to_pointer<std::tuple<int32_t, Box*>>(h.pair)` spells `std::tuple` in the explicit template arg; a variadic form (`template<typename... Es, typename FromTuple> std::tuple<Es...> tuple_to_pointer(FromTuple&&)`) lets codegen emit `tuple_to_pointer<int32_t, Box*>(h.pair)` -- the pack is supplied explicitly, `FromTuple` still deduces from the arg. Purely cosmetic; same instantiation. Two reasons it's the full-tuple form today: (1) codegen builds the destination once via `tuple_borrow_cpp(...)` and reuses that one string for both the local's decl (`std::tuple<int32_t, Box*> t = ...`) and the conversion target, so the pack form needs codegen to emit the bare element list separately (or strip the `std::tuple<>` wrapper); (2) the siblings `tuple_to_storage`, `tuple_value_to_borrow`, and the storage helpers all use the full-tuple form, so a clean change converts all of them together for symmetry. Same "wrap/relax the spelled-out C++ type" family as the `box_dyn` and brace-init-helper items. Surfaced reviewing the per-element-Own rebind generated code.
- **Dual local-type binding tables (`current_scope` + `current_ns`) are hand-synced at every late-resolution sink.** Deduced local types live in two parallel tables: `func.current_scope` (a `Scope`, flow analysis) and `func.current_ns` (a `Namespace`, read by the resumable-frame local hoist `generator_locals`). The deferred `Pending*` resolution sinks in `tpyc/sema/local_deduction.py` (`_apply_container_resolution`, `_update_resolved_binding`, `_resolve_pending_view_types`) must each remember to write BOTH -- the `_sync_resolved_to_ns` helper closes the ns leg today, and `_assert_no_pending_locals` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`) guards against a future sink forgetting it, but the design is still "update one, must remember the other." The same split is why `try_retro_widen_literal_arg` writes `var_types` + `declared_var_types` + scope + ns by hand. Proper fix: a single source of truth for a local's resolved type so resolution writes once. Sema-internal cleanup -- NOT auto-dissolved by the THIR/MIR migration (orthogonal to lowering); scope it as its own branch. Surfaced by the retrospective on `fix-pending-collection-in-coro`.
- **[LOW] Consolidate the `Pending*`-leaf type set across the two recursors.** `_find_pending_leaf` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`, returns the leaf, checks `_PENDING_LOCAL_TYPES` incl. `PendingViewType`) and `contains_pending_leaf` (`tpyc/typesys.py`, re-exported via `tpyc/sema/context.py`; returns bool, checks `PENDING_CONTAINER_TYPES` -- NO `PendingViewType`) share the same recurse-over-`inner_types()` shape but **already disagree on which types count as a pending leaf**. The risk is divergence, not the duplication itself: a new `Pending*` subtype learned by one predicate and not the other silently changes which leaves get caught (the frame assert) vs finalized (the bindings pass). Fix by backing both with one `_first_pending_leaf(typ, types)` core AND reconciling the type set (decide whether view pendings belong in the finalize path; they currently can't reach a stored composite, so the divergence is latent). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit, convention-compliance) + /tpy-ready retrospective of `fix-comp-pending-elem-type`.
- **[LOW] `_deep_resolve_pending` and `resolve_int_literals` are the same leaf-replace-via-`map_inner_types` shape.** `_deep_resolve_pending` (`tpyc/sema/local_deduction.py`) intercepts Pending* container leaves; `resolve_int_literals` (`tpyc/typesys.py`) intercepts IntLiteral leaves -- both recurse identically. A shared `map_leaf_types(typ, predicate, resolver)` in `typesys.py` would unify them. Speculative scope; do as its own cleanup. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of `fix-comp-pending-elem-type`.
- **[LOW] No machine-check that the two loop-var resolved-type stores agree.** After `_finalize_pending_in_bindings`, `pending_loop_vars[name][0]` (bare element type, feeds the frame hoist) and the `current_ns` binding for the same loop var (`make_ref(elem_type)`) are resolved by separate paths with no cross-check; a divergence would mismatch a frame field against the borrow-form emission. Not shown to occur (the alias-propagation pass runs before both records), so low priority: add a post-finalization assert `unwrap_ref_type(binding.type) == pending_loop_vars[name][0]`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of `fix-comp-pending-elem-type`.
- **[LOW test-coverage] Genexpr whose element is a nested composite is untested.** `(r for r in [[i, i + 1] for i in range(3)])` would exercise the genexpr `result_elem_type` finalization (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`); confirm it's reachable first (a genexpr rejects yielding a freshly-constructed list literal, but here it yields a borrow of an existing inner list). Add a case if reachable. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of `fix-comp-pending-elem-type`.
- **[LOW] The composite-pending completeness net is partial.** `_assert_no_pending_locals` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`, frame locals only) plus the recorded-composite raise in `_finalize_pending_in_bindings` (`tpyc/sema/local_deduction.py`) together catch a missed element resolution for frame locals and for any expr node recorded at `set_expr_type`. NOT covered: a bare `Pending*` cached for a node that bypasses both `_apply_container_resolution` and the composite recording (e.g. a future binding sink). That residual would still surface as the opaque `to_cpp()` codegen crash. A cheap-but-not-free deep check over a plain function's name bindings at end of `resolve_all` would close it; deferred because it touches the module-wide-store-scan tradeoff the recording approach was chosen to avoid. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective of `fix-comp-pending-elem-type`.
- **[LOW perf-watch] `contains_pending_leaf` runs on every `set_expr_type` write.** The composite-pending recording hook (`tpyc/sema/context.py`) is unconditional on the hottest sema cache-write sink. It fast-returns on leaf types (empty `inner_types()`), so it is bounded and not a cliff today, but it walks the full type tree for every composite store. Becomes a THIR lowering-time concern (the recording is a fact codegen consumes), not a runtime hook, once the IR migration lands -- capture so it is reconsidered there rather than entrenched. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) + /tpy-ready retrospective of `fix-comp-pending-elem-type`.
- Dead `std::optional<T>` rebind slot for `@nocopy` locals re-bound inside a loop body. When codegen emits `entry = ClassName()` for a `@nocopy` local inside a `while`/`for` body (e.g. the `Settled[T] entry` binding in `_GatherSettledFuture.__poll__`'s assembly loop), it emits BOTH `ClassName __slot_N{...}` and an unused `std::optional<ClassName> __slot_M;` -- the optional is never assigned (the local is bound by pointer to the direct slot), so it fires `-Wunused-variable` at every template instantiation and scales to one dead optional per such binding per loop iteration. Not a correctness issue (`@nocopy` -> empty optional dtor is a no-op; tests build clean since `-Wunused` isn't `-Werror`), just codegen noise. Fix: suppress the optional-slot emission when the rebind target is `@nocopy` and the direct slot suffices. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `gather-list-settled-clean` (codegen-correctness + safety-model). Effort: XS.
- `directly_implements_dynamic` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/protocols.py`) only checks DIRECT `implemented_protocols`, so a user class that inherits a `@dynamic` protocol transitively through a concrete ancestor (`class Dog(Animal)` where `class Animal(Cloneable)`) is NOT recognized as implementing `Cloneable` directly. Codegen then wraps `Dog()` into an `Own[Cloneable]` slot via `make_unique<Adapter<Cloneable, Dog>>(...)` instead of the leaner `make_unique<Dog>(...)` -- both compile and run correctly (Dog IS-A Cloneable through C++ inheritance, so the Adapter indirection is unnecessary), but the Adapter form is a wasted allocation + indirection layer. Fix: walk `(record, *iter_ancestor_records(record)).implemented_protocols` (transitively) and drop the `is_native` exclusion (the comment claiming `@native` C++ structs don't inherit their declared protocol is stale -- `tpy::BaseException : tpy::Throwable` in core.hpp:35 does inherit). Pure optimization; changes the snapshot for `infer_const_dynamic_transitive` (Adapter -> concrete). Surfaced during the covariant-return exploration (dropped); this widening was the one independently-valuable codegen finding from it. Effort: XS + snapshot regen.
- Unify comparison operator codegen with arithmetic: comparisons (`==`, `!=`, `<`, etc.) now resolve through `BINOP_TO_METHOD` + `resolved_binop` like arithmetic ops, but two issues remain: (1) `DUNDER_CPP_TEMPLATES` in `operators.py` duplicates `DUNDER_TO_BINARY_OP` in `codegen_cpp/context.py` -- both map dunder names to C++ operators. Consolidate into one table or derive templates from the operator character. (2) The comparison codegen section still has its own special cases (BigInt/float cast, IntEnum coercion, Optional unwrap, literal folding) that run before `resolved_binop` dispatch. Ideally these would be handled uniformly in the binop result path.
- Extract shared arg-binding helper between `_gen_call` and `_gen_method_call` in `codegen_cpp/expressions.py`. Today each dispatcher has its own loop over `(arg, param)` pairs that applies coercion, covariant wrapping, `@dynamic` adapter insertion, `TpyTypeParamConstruct` / `TpyVarargPack` materialization, TypeParamRef substitution, etc. The loops have diverged repeatedly: `TpyVarargPack` was first handled only in `_gen_call` (broke `math.hypot(*coords)` -> mirrored into `_gen_method_call`'s first-pass loop), and then again only in that first-pass loop (broke method `*args` user-record calls -> mirrored into the user-record branch at the bottom of `_gen_method_call`). `_gen_method_call` alone has at least two arg-emission loops (first-pass + per-record-type fallback) that drift independently. Fix: pull the per-arg transformation into a helper `_bind_args(args: list[TpyExpr], params: list[(str, TpyType)], type_subst) -> list[str]` and call it from all paths. Keeps the dispatch split (the paths resolve very differently -- registry lookup vs receiver-type method resolution + TypedDict/callable-field/nested-constructor/property/dunder special cases) while eliminating the duplicated arg-list logic that has been the source of "feature X added to one path, not the other" bugs. Pairs with: consider having sema rewrite module-qualified calls (`math.hypot(x, y)` is currently `TpyMethodCall(obj=Name("math"), ...)`) into bare `TpyCall` with a qualified name, so they route through the simpler dispatcher -- `math` is a compile-time namespace, not a runtime object with methods.
- **[XS] `FieldInfo.has_in_class_init` property for the "field has an in-class initializer" fact.** Four sites now inline the same `fld.default_expr is not None` guard to mean "this field is default-constructible regardless of its type": `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py` (`_explain_default_ctor_deletion` + `_record_default_ctor_is_deleted`), `tpyc/sema/protocols.py` (`_is_default_constructible`), `tpyc/sema/calls.py`, and `tpyc/codegen_cpp/records.py::_all_fields_default_constructible`. Add a `has_in_class_init` property on `FieldInfo` (`tpyc/typesys.py`) so the fact is authoritative and a future site can't silently omit a leg. First-class type fact, moves cleanly across the THIR boundary. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` (architecture-fit) of the enum-field-default branch.
- Inplace operator C++ return type: `__iadd__`/`__ior__` etc. declare returning self type (Python semantics) but the C++ helpers (`list_extend`, `dict_update`) return `void`. Add thin wrappers that return `T&` (e.g. `list_extend_inplace`, `dict_update_inplace`) so the declared return type matches the C++ signature. Affects list and dict.
- `tpy::ptr_variant<Ts...>` wrapper: replace `std::variant<Ts*...>` in param signatures with a thin wrapper that has an implicit converting constructor from `ptr_variant<T*...>` to `ptr_variant<const T*...>`. This would let deduced-const (non-mutated) params use deep const consistently with `@readonly`, without breaking callers. Currently deduced-const uses shallow const (`const variant<T*...>`) which doesn't prevent mutation through the pointer at the C++ level. Start with param signatures only; locals/fields/returns can stay `std::variant`.
- Inline `and`/`or` chains for side-effect-free operands: `a or b or c` currently emits an intermediate temp (`auto&& __tmp = (a ? a : b); result = (__tmp ? __tmp : c)`) to avoid double-evaluation. When all operands are provably side-effect-free (variables, literals), the temp is unnecessary and the chain can be emitted as a single nested ternary (`!a.empty() ? a : !b.empty() ? b : c`), which is more readable.
- `tpy::tpy_callable<F, R(Args...)>` concept: replace verbose `requires requires(__F0& __fn, ...) { { __fn(...) } -> std::convertible_to<R>; }` blocks with a compact `tpy::tpy_callable<__F0, R(Args...)>` concept in the runtime. Same `R(Args...)` notation as `std::function`. Needs a helper trait with partial specialization (`std::invocable` + `std::convertible_to` for return, void special case). Cosmetic improvement -- only worth prioritizing if it measurably speeds up C++ compilation for template-heavy Fn usage.
- Unify rvalue materialization: two overlapping mechanisms exist for extending rvalue lifetimes -- the slot system (`std::optional<T>` slots in `_gen_pointer_local_init`, tied to var decl infrastructure) and TempState (`auto&&` temps, expression-level). Both solve the same problem at different abstraction levels. Consider exposing a lower-level "materialize this rvalue" API that both var decls and expression-level temps (e.g. `and`/`or` ternaries) can share.
- Transparent coercion codegen: `TpyCoerce` in codegen has a growing list of special cases for when to forward `coerce_target` vs `actual_type` to the inner `gen_expr` (literals need the target to pick C++ representation; view coercions need it for zero-copy paths). Add a `transparent: bool` flag on coercion definitions so the decision is made at coercion definition time in `coercions.py`, not with `isinstance` checks in codegen.
- Deferred type resolution in parser: the parser currently resolves type names eagerly during parsing, but its per-module TypeRegistry lacks cross-module types. Two workarounds exist: (1) a `resolved` guard that accepts any import-resolved name as a NominalType in annotation context (sema validates later), and (2) speculative `_parse_type_annotation` at call-parsing sites with a sema fix to clear `call_type` when the name turns out to be a function. The proper fix: the parser should produce unresolved type reference nodes and let sema (which has full cross-module information) resolve them. This would also eliminate `_resolve_primitive_type` special cases for `slice`/`int`/`float`/etc. -- sema would resolve all type names uniformly through type factories.
- Push marker-protocol metadata from compiler hardcoding into library-declared `@builtin_type` annotations + add docstrings. Today `tpy.ValueType` / `tpy.Copyable` / `tpy.Default` / `tpy.NativeIterable` / `tpy.Send` / `tpy.Sync` / `tpy.ReturnException` / `tpy.Covariant` / `tpy.AnyFixedInt` / etc. are declared as bare `@native("tpy::X") class X(Protocol): ...` stubs with no docstring -- compiler dispatch on them lives in `tpyc/sema/protocols.py`'s marker arm (an `if qname == qnames.X` chain), with the qname strings centralized in `qnames.py`. Same per-type-knowledge-in-compiler-if-chains shape as the sibling "Push subscript metadata" entry below. Migration shape: (1) add `@builtin_type("tpy.X", is_marker=True, cpp_concept="tpy::X", marker_predicate="value_type" | "copyable" | "default" | None, ...)` to each marker so the metadata lands on its static TypeDef payload; (2) replace the marker arm's qname-chain with a single TypeDef-property lookup (`marker_predicate == "copyable" -> not is_type_non_copyable(actual)` etc.); (3) retire the now-dead qname constants from `qnames.py` (VALUE_TYPE / COPYABLE / DEFAULT / ...) and the per-marker fields in `qnames.py`'s "tpy protocols" block. Also add one-line docstrings to each protocol stub in `lib/tpy/tpy/_core/_types.py` (and sync `lib/cpy/tpy/__init__.py`) explaining what conformance grants (e.g. "passes to range-for codegen", "static `T{}` construction", "implicit `Box[Parent] <- Box[Child]` upcast") and how conformance is established (implicit predicate / explicit `extends`). The docstrings are useful even without the migration; folding them into the same pass keeps the touch local. Two known gaps in the existing predicates that the migration should also close: (a) `_is_default_constructible` returns False unconditionally for `TypeParamRef` parents in the aggregate-record walk (lines 502-504) -- doesn't consult `get_type_param_bound(name)` to check whether the bound conforms to Default, so `class Holder[T: Default](T): ...` (aggregate, extends T) is silently rejected; (b) regression-test coverage on `error_copyable_extends_contradiction` and `error_default_extends_contradiction` only exercises the `__init__ + required-params` denial branch -- the `del_suppresses_default_ctor` paths (@nocopy+__del__, __del__+no-__init__+indeterminate-init-field) and the aggregate-record-with-non-default-field path are unguarded.
- Push subscript metadata from compiler hardcoding into library-declared type metadata. Today the codegen bounds-safe path needs to know whether a type's C++ `operator[]` takes `size_t` (built-in containers: `vector`/`array`/`span`/`string`/`bytes` families) or the user's declared param type (typically `int32_t` for user records). The decision lives in `_is_concrete_user_record` (`codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) gated on `is_list/is_array/is_span/is_str_category/is_bytes_category` predicates from `type_def_registry.py` -- "STL types use size_t" is a fact about the library that's currently baked into the compiler. Cleaner: declare each type's `operator[]` parameter type as part of its TPy stub metadata (and similar properties: bounds-checked vs unchecked, supports negative indices, etc.) and have codegen query it. Pairs with the broader "Unify `TypeDef.cpp_formatter` and `@native` / `_native_cpp_names`" entry in the strict-warning hardening section -- both are about pushing per-type C++ properties from compiler-side hardcoding into library-declared metadata. Same shape applies to other ad-hoc per-type knowledge in codegen (e.g. the `iter_range` adapter dispatch, Python-int polymorphism per-type rules).
- **Generator resumable-frame migration: remaining work.** The migration's **structural** goal (Phases A-F: one struct-shape codegen + the simple-generator peephole; legacy `gen_generators.py` struct path deleted) is DONE. Full phase record: `docs/GENERATOR_RESUMABLE_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`. **User-visible completeness** is **Phase H** (close the rejects -- shapes the resumable frame can structurally lower that still emit clean "not yet supported" diagnostics): the blanket suspension-shape sema reject is now fully retired (**H1** -- `match`+suspension -- done 2026-05-29; **H2** done 2026-05-28; **H3** done 2026-05-28). **H4** (multi-yield proto-param generators) done 2026-06-03; **H5** (narrowing-across-suspension -- `if`/`while`/`assert` and `match` arms) done 2026-06-03; **H6** done 2026-05-28. Remaining: **H7** (multi-item `async with`) is low-priority / workaround exists. Out of Phase H scope (new surface, distinct features): **G** (`yield from`/`send`/`throw`/`close`), async generators (PEP 525), `@contextlib.contextmanager`. Plus the test-coverage follow-ups below.
  - **Phase D test-coverage follow-ups** (surfaced by /tpy-review): add resumable-path generator tests for (a) `range(start, stop)` 2-arg form (distinct field count in `_analyze_for_strategy` from the 1-arg and 3-arg forms already covered); (b) a temp/rvalue iterable with a *non-value* element type (the `__for_src` + pointer-form-`T*`-loop-var combination -- `gen_resumable_for_temp` is value-element, `gen_resumable_for_nonvalue` is a named param); (c) a `readonly`-element container, guarding the pointer-form -> `frame_slot` fallback (`pointer_form_loop_var=None` for `ReadonlyType` elements); (d) a `test_resumable_cfg.py` unit test asserting `CFGBuilder().build(body_without_finally)` produces `cfg.finally_helpers == []`, so the `gen_coro_finally_top_def` no-op gate (`if not cfg.finally_helpers: return`) has direct unit-level coverage in addition to today's indirect snapshot coverage. Also: `tests/cases/iterators/gen_resumable_param_own` carries `no_cpython.txt` only because the `lib/cpy` Box stub models `.get()` as a `_Ptr` proxy (`_Ptr * int` fails) -- fix the stub to allow CPython parity instead of permanently skipping.
  - **H1 test-coverage follow-up** (surfaced by /tpy-review): `test_resumable_cfg.py` `TestMatchDecomposition` only asserts the `join_bb is not None` shape; add a CFG-unit case where every arm `return`s (exhaustive + all-terminate) so `_build_match` yields `join_bb is None` (the `__builtin_unreachable()` dispatch tail) -- today only end-to-end snapshot coverage via `async/async_match_await`.
  - **isinstance-narrowed frame-union coverage + invariant (surfaced by /tpy-review of the frame_slot-unwrap fix).** `tests/cases/generators/isinstance_union_frame` covers the `if`/`assert` narrowing of a frame-resident union local. Two coverage gaps to add when next touching this: (a) the narrowing inside an `async def` body (same `in_generator_body` + `generator_optional_fields` guard as a generator; no async case exists); (b) a 3+-member frame union (`A | B | C` local narrowed to one alternative -- only 2-member is covered). A `while isinstance` variant is currently blocked by the separate `while isinstance` static-fold-to-`while(true)` bug (BUGS.md). INVARIANT to preserve: the per-resume re-extraction of the narrowing alias (`auto& __t = std::get<M>((*name))` re-emitted at each resume case) is sound only because nothing holds the alias across a suspension -- `frame_slot::emplace` invalidates prior `operator*` results, so a future optimization that elides the per-resume re-extraction and caches the alias across states would silently dangle. The unwrap decision is centralized in `ctx.frame_slot_deref`.
  - **[LOW] Make the pointer-form match-binding reject root-aware (lift the over-rejection).** The W1 reject (`match.py` `_emit_binding`, `resumable_match_subject_is_lvalue`) rejects a pointer-repr `Optional` arm binding when the subject is non-lvalue OR a narrowed name (`error_gen_match_nested_narrowed_ptr_bind`). The narrowed-name half is **over-conservative**: a narrowing alias is a C++ *reference* (`auto& __case_0 = std::get<0>(__match_subject)`), so when the narrowed subject roots at a *frame-resident* lvalue (param/local/field, e.g. `this->u`), `optional_to_ptr(__case_0.maybe)` points into frame storage and is SAFE across the suspension -- the reject (and the /tpy-review safety "Critical") wrongly treated the reference variable as owned storage. The precise rule: reject only when the subject's storage *root* is a dispatch-local copy (a non-lvalue root like `match foo():`); a narrowing alias is transparent (safe iff its source is frame-resident, unsafe iff it narrows a non-lvalue). Proper fix: track root-frame-residency through `narrowed_vars` rather than the blunt syntactic check. Over-rejection is currently HARMLESS -- the only shape that hits it (nested union match) can't compile anyway due to the pre-existing union-match codegen bugs (the `*std::get` operator + union-param issues in BUGS.md). Sequence this AFTER those union bugs are fixed (lifting it sooner just swaps a clean reject for an opaque C++ error). Low priority. Surfaced by user review of the W1 fix.
  - **[LOW] `match` parity matrix: normal function vs generator vs async.** The generator/async path reuses `gen_match` for the dispatch but diverges from the normal-function path in how it routes arm bodies (the H1 `_emit_case_body` arm-emitter hook -> resumable state machine) and how it materializes pattern bindings (owned *frame field* vs owned function-scope local). Today's coverage exercises each context separately; nothing asserts the two routes produce the *same* observable behavior. Add a parity test matrix -- the same `match` (subject kinds: int/enum/str/union/record/optional; binding shapes: capture / field / `as` / or-pattern; with guards; exhaustive + non-exhaustive) run in (a) a normal function, (b) a generator, (c) an `async def`, asserting identical output across all three and against CPython. Catches drift in binding ownership/copy, narrowing, exhaustiveness, and arm-body control flow between the routes. Motivated by the W1 investigation (binding storage differs between routes -- owned function-scope local vs owned frame field -- same result today, but the two emit paths could diverge silently). Low priority; hardening, not a known defect.
  - **H4 follow-ups** (surfaced by /tpy-review): (a) [hardening] `_CoroParam.ctor_param_type` doubles as the `std::forward<...>` template-arg name for `STATIC_PROTOCOL` params (`gen_async.py` `ctor_init` / `_factory_args_forwarded`); it is coincidentally also the field/ctor C++ type spelling, so a future change that adds `const`/`&` to the ctor-param type would silently break reference-collapsing for lvalue args. Give the forward template-arg its own accessor (or store the deduced-template name separately) so the two roles can't drift. (b) [coverage] add a multi-protocol-param generator (`def zip2(a: Iterable[A], b: Iterable[B])` -- distinct `T_a`/`T_b` template args) and a protocol-union-param generator (`it: Iterable[T] | NativeIterable[T]`, which `is_static_protocol_param` accepts and `protocol_param_template_name` still names `T_<pname>`); current coverage is single-bare-protocol-param only. (c) [coverage, optional] a generator *method* with a protocol param -- shares the `proto_param_names` path but goes through the method-emit arm; add only if the method path proves materially distinct from the free-function form.
  - **H6 test-coverage gaps** (surfaced by /tpy-review): (a) a class with TWO protocol-bounded type params (`class Pair[K: Hashable, V: Iterable[Int32]]`) plus a generator/async method -- the existing `gen_method_generic_multi_param` uses unbounded params; (b) a generator/async method that is itself templated (own `[U]`) on a bounded class -- the existing `async_method_typeparam_on_generic_class` is unbounded; (c) a `@readonly` generator/async method on a bounded generic class. All exercise paths the H6 fix touched; current coverage is the single-bounded-param shape only. Also: the simple-generator path (`gen_generators._setup_body_scope`) doesn't thread `record_type_param_bounds` -- latent, user-visibility near-zero (simple generators use the lambda peephole through the enclosing sync method's codegen path), worth folding in only if a real workload hits it. And: the resumable for-loop prescan (`gen_async._prescan_resumable_for_loops` -> `gen_generators._analyze_for_strategy`) ignores `current_type_param_bounds` -- a *suspending* for-loop over `T: NativeIterable[X]` / `T: Spannable[X]` still falls back to the universal `::tpy::__iter__` shape even though the H6 fix made the *non-suspending* sister case pick the begin/end peephole. Asymmetric perf gap; fix is to call `_resolve_iterable_type` (or inline its bounds lookup) from `_analyze_for_strategy` before the strategy dispatch.
  - **H2 follow-ups** (surfaced by /tpy-review): (a) `finally_exit_bb` is unconditionally allocated for every CFG-based finally even when no `return` is reachable in try/handlers/finally (`resumable_cfg.py:1117`). Spot-checks across the 14 regenerated suspending-finally snapshots confirm the case-label count is unchanged (the new exit BB absorbs the post-try join via case merging -- same region_stack, single-pred), so this is a purely internal CFG simplification (one BB per finally), not state-machine bloat. Optional cleanup: gate on `pending_return_flag is not None` and fall back to the pre-H2 inline-append shape. (b) The 3-way `_stmts_have_any_return(try OR handlers OR finally)` predicate is duplicated between `gen_async.py:1755-1758` (the field-allocation prescan) and `resumable_cfg.py:1095-1098` (the CFG builder's slot allocation). H2 extended both copies in lockstep; pre-existing in a 2-way form. Extract a single helper (likely on `TpyTry` in `parse/nodes.py`, paralleling the existing `stmts_have_any_suspension` family) so future extensions don't drift.
  - **H3 follow-ups** (surfaced by /tpy-review): (a) generator-side coverage for nested suspending `finally` is thin -- only `iterators/gen_nested_yield_finally` (normal exit) exists. The async side has 4 cases (normal exit, return-in-inner-finally, return-in-inner-try, raise-in-inner-try). The forwarding code in `_emit_async_finally_exit` is shape-neutral, so the gen path is exercised structurally, but belt-and-suspenders gen tests for return-in-inner-finally and raise-in-inner-try would lock the gen-shape `make_unexpected(StopIteration)` replay path. (b) void async def with nested suspending `finally` and `return` in the inner finally body -- exercises the `outer_slot is None and inner_slot is None` branch of the forwarding guard at `_emit_async_finally_exit:2644`. Untested. (c) `async with X: async with Y:` direct-nesting (multi-item OR two stacked single-item `async with` regions both suspending) is still rejected by the `_build_async_with` guard at `resumable_cfg.py:1370-1379`. H3 lifted the equivalent restriction for plain `try`/`finally` nesting via the AsyncFinallyExit forwarding; the async-with case needs additional care for `__aexit__` call-ordering through the forwarding path. Symmetric follow-up to H3.
  - **Phase E follow-up test gaps** (surfaced by /tpy-review): (a) a resumable generator whose `with ... as v` target is a **pointer-form Optional** (`__enter__(self) -> T | None`) used across a yield -- exercises the `else` (`pointer_locals`, plain `=`) branch of the new `_emit_with_enter` dispatch; `gen_resumable_with_as` only covers the `frame_slot` (non-value) branch. (b) a multi-yield `readonly[tuple[Int32, P]]` (mixed value + reference) generator -- exercises the `target_readonly and mode == REF -> CONST_REF` promotion in `_tuple_literal_slot_info` alongside a VALUE slot; `gen_resumable_readonly_tuple_yield` covers only all-ref `readonly[tuple[P, P]]`.
  - **`readonly[tuple[Ref, ...]]` for-loop consumer copies each element per iteration.** Consuming `for pair in gen()` where `gen` yields `readonly[tuple[P, P]]` materializes the loop var as a value tuple (`std::tuple<P, P> pair = ::tpy::unwrap_ref(*__r)`), copying both `P` elements every iteration, where the non-readonly mixed-tuple consumer binds by `auto&&` (zero copy). Semantically correct (copies of copyable records), perf-only -- noticeable for large reference element types / tight loops. Fix: bind the readonly-tuple loop var by `const auto&` / borrow form at the consumer boundary instead of materializing a value tuple. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Phase E follow-up work (`gen_resumable_readonly_tuple_yield`).
  - **Strip the stray blank line after no-op `gen_coro_finally_top_def`.** Both the async-coro and (now) resumable-generator orchestration in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/generator.py` unconditionally emit `cpp.write("\n")` after `gen_coro_finally_top_def`, even when the CFG produced no finally helpers (the method early-returns). Every async coro / resumable generator without a try/finally carries a double blank line between the body and the factory in main.cpp. Behavior-neutral; fix is either (a) have `gen_coro_finally_top_def` return a "did emit" bool and gate the trailing newline on it, or (b) inline-check `cfg.finally_helpers` at the call site. Touches both async and generator snapshots (one blank line removed per file with no finally). Small follow-up to the finally-top wiring; left as its own item because the original pair partner (`frame_slot` unification) has shipped.
  - **Resumable frame-field size: drop the engaged-bool for default-constructible field types.** The synthetic for-loop fields (`__for_i`/`__for_stop`/`__for_itr`/`__for_r`/`__for_src`/...) and hoisted locals are emitted as `std::optional<T>` / `tpy::frame_slot<T>`, both carrying an `alive_`/engaged bool, because they're constructed in the setup state (not the struct ctor) and may be non-default-constructible. For default-constructible field types (range counters `int32_t`, `std::vector` sources, most iterators) the bool is pure waste -- e.g. a range counter is `optional<int32_t>` (8 bytes) vs a bare `int32_t` (4). Bare storage is correct there (default-construct in the struct ctor, assign in setup; the struct dtor handles non-trivial destruction). The compiler can't see C++ default-constructibility, so it needs a `std::conditional`-style wrapper (`tpy::frame_field<T>` = `T` when `is_default_constructible_v<T>` else deferred storage) selected at the C++ level. Frame-size win for hot generators; pre-existing (the legacy generator path wraps these in `std::optional` too). Pairs with the "unify resumable frame fields on `frame_slot`" cleanup in `docs/GENERATOR_RESUMABLE_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`. Surfaced reviewing the `gen_resumable_for_temp` `__for_src` field.
  - **Defer construction of conditionally-assigned value-type frame fields.** Value-type generator/coro frame locals emit as bare `T name;` (default-constructed in the frame ctor), even when only assigned on some control-flow paths -- e.g. `async_match_binding/expected/include/main.hpp` carries `BigInt tag; BigInt v; BigInt r;` where `v`/`r` are each assigned in only one match arm. So every frame instance pays a default-construction per such field regardless of which branch runs. Non-value locals already avoid this (deferred `tpy::frame_slot<T>`, constructed on first write, destructed only if engaged); value locals don't. Fix shape: extend the deferred-slot storage to value fields that are NOT assigned-before-use on all paths (liveness-gated), so construction is skipped on branches that don't reach the assignment, with destruction conditioned on the alive flag. Caveat: only a win when default-construction is non-trivial -- for cheap-default value types (`int`, small `BigInt`) bare storage + the no-bool form is better, so this must be gated on default-construction cost, not applied blanket. The unified answer is a per-field storage strategy driven by liveness + default-construction cost; pairs with the "drop the engaged-bool for default-constructible types" (`frame_field<T>` wrapper) entry above and the state-aware-destruction entry (which removes the alive bool). Low priority. Surfaced by user review of the `async_match_binding` frame layout.
