  - **Resumable state-machine: jump-thread empty resume states.** A `yield`/`await` whose resume BB is empty and unconditionally `Fall`s to another case-entry emits a no-op forwarding state, e.g. `case S_RESUME_1: { __state = S_JOIN_0; continue; }` -- so resuming costs two switch dispatches (S_RESUME_1, then S_JOIN_0) where one would do. Common when a `yield` is the last statement in a loop body (resume just loops back to the condition). Fix: a CFG simplification before emit -- when a Yield terminator's `resume_bb` is an empty BB with a single `Fall` to BB X, retarget the suspension's resume state directly to X and drop the dead BB (standard empty-block elimination / jump threading). Benefits both generators and async coroutines (per-suspension dispatch overhead, relevant for hot loops). Surfaced reviewing `gen_for_else`'s `S_RESUME_1`.
  - **F3 tuple-unpack test/coverage gaps** (surfaced by /tpy-review): add resumable-path cases for (a) a `_`-wildcard on the *reference* unpack position (e.g. `for _, item in rows:` with `item` a record), exercising the `tname is not None` skip in the `pointer_form_unpack_targets` set-comp; (b) a 3+-target unpack with a reference element; (c) a `list[readonly[tuple[P, P]]]` consumed via for-unpack, guarding the readonly -> value-copy fallback (no aliasing). Also: a `test_resumable_cfg.py`-level unit test for the `pointer_form_unpack_targets` selection in `_analyze_for_strategy` (the two gate-exclusion unit tests were removed with the gate; the selection logic now has only end-to-end snapshot coverage).
- Resolve `@native(cpp_return_type=T)` annotation from string to `TpyType`. The user-supplied type name (`UInt64` etc.) is parsed into `_NameArg`, stored as a string on `TpyFunction.native_cpp_return_type` and `FunctionInfo.native_cpp_return_type`, and never read for its content -- codegen only checks `is not None` and casts to the *declared TPy return* (`fi.return_type`). The "true type" intent of the annotation is lost. Resolve the name in sema (registry lookup, mirror how `error_return(StopIteration)` resolves to a qualified record name) and store the resolved `TpyType`. Unlocks: (a) validation that the declared `cpp_return_type` is wider than `fi.return_type` (catches accidental `cpp_return_type=Int8` on a method declared `-> Int64`), (b) using the source type to drive smarter codegen (e.g. choosing between `static_cast` and `tpy::checked_cast` based on the conversion direction), (c) better error messages when the name doesn't resolve (today: silently accepted at parse time, breaks at codegen lookup if anything reads it).
- Reject `@error_return(E)` + `@native("name", cpp_return_type=T)` on the same method/function at sema time. The combination is meaningful (a C++ function returning `std::expected<T, E>` where T is wider than declared) but codegen short-circuits the cast in `_maybe_native_return_cast` (the `error_return_type is None` guard) -- so the user's `cpp_return_type` is silently ignored and `-Wconversion` may fire from inside the unwrap expression. Either implement proper composition (cast inside the unwrap statement-expression) or reject at sema with a clear error pointing the user at `@cpp_template`.
- Generalize set/dict element/key slot constraints into per-slot TypeDef metadata. Today `TypeOperations.validate_hashable_container_elem` (in `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py`) is the single source of truth (called from 4 literal/comprehension sites in `expressions.py` and from `validate_type`'s annotation-time gate), but it's hard-coded for `builtins.set` (slot 0) and `builtins.dict` (slot 0) and combines two distinct checks (@nocopy + Hashable + Equatable). The cleaner shape mirrors `TypeDef.param_kinds`: add per-slot metadata describing the constraints each slot's substituted type must satisfy. Two flavors needed: (a) protocol bounds, which the existing user-record bound-check machinery already handles -- could be expressed as `class set[T: Hashable]` / `class dict[K: Hashable, V]` in the `.py` stubs at `lib/tpy/tpy/_builtins/{_set,_dict}.py` and `validate_record_type_args` would enforce them automatically; (b) negative properties like copy-constructibility, which aren't a protocol the user implements (`is_type_non_copyable` is the absence of @nocopy + has_del + parent-chain propagation) and would need a non-protocol slot constraint -- e.g. a per-slot `must_be_copyable: bool` on TypeDef plus a diagnostic-label tuple so the generic validator can produce "set element"/"dict key" messages. Open: how to fold in the user-record dunder-validation hint (`missing __hash__; use @dataclass(frozen=True) or define it explicitly`) -- the literal-path check has more nuance than just protocol conformance, so either keep that as a separate specialized validator at literal sites, or expose a registry-driven extension. Generalizes naturally to future hash-table-backed containers (frozenset if added, custom user maps); once landed, drop the two special-case validators. Surfaced when reviewing the `set/dict[Rc[T]]` sema-gate fix. Effort: S-M.
- Consolidate `_substitute_type_params` / `_subst_type_params` / `_partial_substitute`. Four copies of the same "replace TypeParamRef from a name->TpyType map, pass through unknowns" walk now exist: `tpyc/sema/operators.py`, `tpyc/sema/match.py`, `tpyc/sema/calls.py::_partial_substitute`, and `tpyc/cycle_detection.py` (the new one, which adds NominalType int-slot preservation for Array[T, N]). The canonical version is `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py::substitute_type_params` on the type-resolution context, but it lives on a sema-only object and not all sites can reach it. Extract a stateless helper (e.g. `typesys.substitute_type_params(typ, subst)` taking `dict[str, TpyType | int]`) that all four call sites consume; the int-slot handling becomes default rather than the special case it is in cycle_detection's copy. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the structural-cycle walker.
- Migrate `_gen_copy_expr`'s inline `ptr_variant_locals` check (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:577`) to the centralized `ctx.is_ptr_variant_source` helper. The duplicate `_to_value_variant_if_needed` was already migrated; `_gen_copy_expr` is the remaining inline site. Same shared-helper principle. Surfaced during /tpy-review followup of the union ctor-init fix. Effort: XS.
- Promote `_get_cpp_declared_type` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:473`) to public API or relocate to `CodeGenContext`. Currently `_`-prefixed on `ExpressionGenerator` but called cross-class from `StatementGenerator._gen_assign_code` (3 sites added by the LHS-narrowing-leak fix). The `_` prefix is misleading: this helper is effectively the public way to ask "what's the storage-shape type of this expression, ignoring narrowing?". Two options: (a) drop the underscore (rename to `get_cpp_declared_type`); (b) move it to `CodeGenContext` next to `is_ptr_variant_source` -- aligns with the recent centralization of write-side-affecting helpers. Surfaced during /simplify review of the LHS-narrowing-leak fix. Effort: XS.
- Add test coverage for `None` at less-common value-bearing slots: `*args: None`, `**kwargs: None`, and an `error_none_annotation_positions` case for `def f(x: None) -> int: return x` (should be rejected by sema since NoneType can't satisfy a non-None return). Both are low-probability shapes but structurally distinct from the existing `cases/generics/none_annotation_positions` coverage (named param / local / field). Effort: XS. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the value-bearing-slot generalization.
- Typed-kwargs broken on `@overload` groups. `tpyc/sema/registration.py:2654-2665` (`register_overload_group`, which builds `stub_param_infos`) handles `vararg_type` but not `kwarg_type` -- so an `@overload`-decorated stub with `**kw: Unpack[Opts]` registers a FunctionInfo whose `params` list omits the kwarg slot, then overload dispatch at `tpyc/sema/calls.py:3825-3837` fails the call with `'<name>' got unexpected keyword argument 'a'`. Non-overload free functions go through `register_function` (line 2481-2496) which DOES handle `kwarg_type`; methods at line 1096 also do. Repro: two `@overload @native def call_native(...)` stubs where the second uses `**kw: Unpack[Opts]`; `call_native(a=1)` is rejected even though `a` is a valid Opts field. The fix has two layers: (1) mirror the kwarg block from `register_function` into `register_overload_group` so the ParamInfo exists, (2) plumb `_pack_kwargs_into_typed_dict` (or equivalent) into the overload candidate scorer so per-candidate kwarg matching considers TypedDict fields -- the scorer at `calls.py:resolve_overload` currently builds `accepted_names` from `p.name for p in f.params` only, so even with (1) the kwarg-typed param doesn't auto-expand its TypedDict field names. (1) alone is necessary-but-insufficient (verified: applied (1) in isolation, user-visible bug persists). Effort: S. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the kwarg_type-leak fix and verified by direct repro.
- Add a macro-fragment regression test covering `def(**kw: Unpack[TypedDict]) -> ...` in a fragment body. The FragmentParser path through `_finalize_function_refs` is now resolving `kwarg_type` (after the kwarg_type-leak fix), but no macro test exercises it directly. Effort: XS. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the kwarg_type-leak fix.
- Add a unit test for `cycle_detection._walk`'s `AliasRef` branch (`tpyc/cycle_detection.py:72-74`). The recursive-union snippet tests at `tests/cases/union/error_union_recursive_*` cover the end-to-end path but `tpyc/test_cycle_detection.py` has no isolated case using `AliasRef("Tree")` as the self-reference placeholder. Effort: XS. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the union/variant unification (test-coverage).
- Add a cross-module diagnostic test for `union_display_names`. Module A defines `type Pet = Dog | Cat`; module B imports `Pet` and triggers a type mismatch using a `Pet` value. The error should print `Pet` (the canonical alias name registered by A's sema) rather than `Dog | Cat`. The current `tests/cases/union/error_union_*` snapshots cover only single-module diagnostics; the cross-module display-name code path (`setdefault` first-write-wins behavior across module-sema passes) is exercised only implicitly. Effort: XS. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the union/variant unification (test-coverage).
- Extract the "capture return value into a typed temp before the finally chain" pattern shared by `_make_return` and `_make_async_return` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`) into one helper -- the two copies differ only in temp-name prefix and which ctx field supplies the C++ return spelling (`current_return_cpp` vs `async_coro_return_cpp`). Parallel-mechanism drift risk if one grows a rule the other misses. Effort: XS. Surfaced by /tpy-review of fix-finally-lowering.
- Extract the try-tier binding-hoist pattern (`all_bindings = dict(try_bindings); update(finally bindings); populate if_branch_decls + hoisted_vars`) now duplicated across `_analyze_try_finally_only`, `_analyze_try_return`, and `_analyze_try_throw` in `tpyc/sema/statements.py` into a shared helper. Effort: XS. Surfaced by /tpy-review of fix-finally-lowering.
- Migrate `_gen_while` / `_gen_for_each` (and the explicit narrowed_vars/declared_persistent_aliases snapshot blocks in `_gen_with` / `_emit_case_body` / `_gen_try_throw` / `_gen_try_finally_only`) to use `snapshot_local_scope` / `restore_local_scope` for the body-scope save/restore. Today each construct that opens a fresh C++ scope save/restores the two narrowing maps manually, in parallel to LocalScopeSnap which already covers them. Two-mechanism risk: when LocalScopeSnap gains another scope-bound field, every manual site has to be touched too. The blockers are (a) save_local_scope also tracks user-declared variables (`declared_vars`, `var_types`, etc.) which should NOT be restored at loop-body / try-body close (Python scoping makes them visible after), so the migration needs a narrower "narrowing-only" snapshot variant; or (b) the existing if-body pattern that does `restore_local_scope` at body close demonstrates that the broader restoration is in fact correct for narrowing-bearing scopes (worth verifying). Either way, consolidating onto one mechanism is the right shape post-fix-poly-early-return-narrowing. Effort: S. Surfaced by /tpy-review.
- Split `@nocopy`'s two meanings. The decorator currently covers two distinct intents: (a) **move-only at the C++ level** -- the C++ class actually deletes its copy ctor (explicit `@nocopy`: `Task[T]`, `Rc[T]`, `Box[T]`, user TPy classes that opt in); the TPy-level rejection mirrors the C++ ABI; pairs with `is_value_type=False` because copies are physically impossible. (b) **TPy-level ergonomics gate only** -- the C++ class is freely copyable but the TPy side rejects user-visible copies for safety (e.g. `SpanIter[T]`: a freely-copyable struct over `std::span + index`, but copying gives two iterators advancing independently and is almost always a bug); pairs with `is_value_type=True` because the C++ side is copy-friendly. Conflating (a) and (b) blocks a drift guard between explicit-stub `@nocopy` and `TypeDef.is_value_type` (the contradiction we hit on `tpy.Task` -- `is_value_type=True` despite the deleted copy ctor -- shipped silently for months). Possible shapes: (i) introduce `@no_implicit_copy` as the ergonomics-only sibling and reserve `@nocopy` strictly for C++-deleted-copy-ctor cases; (ii) add a `cpp_copyable: bool = False` knob on `@nocopy` so authors opt into the ergonomics-only meaning explicitly. Either lets the registry assert `@nocopy + not cpp_copyable + is_value_type=True` is impossible. Also re-opens the question of whether `SpanIter[T]` should be `@nocopy` at all -- the current footgun-prevention argument is real but might be better served by switching `is_value_type=False` on iterators (so `it2 = it` is a reference-binding, matching Python's mental model) and dropping the decorator. Effort: S; touches `lib/tpy/tpy/_core/_containers.py`, `lib/tpy/tplib/rc.py`, the @nocopy parser hook, and the registry. Surfaced after the `tpy.Task is_value_type=False` fix.
- **[LOW] `DUNDER_TO_REVERSE_BINARY_OP` is a static parallel table that is the filtered inverse of the adjacent `DUNDER_TO_BINARY_OP`** (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`): derivable as `{"__r" + k[2:]: v for k, v in DUNDER_TO_BINARY_OP.items() if k not in <comparison dunders>}`. Keeping it a separate literal means any future `DUNDER_TO_BINARY_OP` addition (e.g. `__matmul__`) needs a matching manual edit in the reverse table. Unify into one derived constant to drop the lockstep. Effort: XS. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit, codegen-correctness) of `feat-reflected-operators`.
- **[LOW test-coverage] Reflected-operator codegen exercises only `__radd__`/`__rmul__`/`__ror__`.** The other reverse ops in `DUNDER_TO_REVERSE_BINARY_OP` (`__rsub__`, `__rtruediv__`, `__rfloordiv__`, `__rmod__`, `__rand__`, `__rxor__`, `__rlshift__`, `__rrshift__`) share the one friend-emit code path, so a single added case (e.g. `__rsub__`) proves the loop handles the rest; and `__rpow__` (deliberately excluded from friend-op emission, dispatched via `std::pow`) has no test confirming a user-defined `__rpow__` still works. Add as a completeness guard. Effort: XS. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of `feat-reflected-operators`.

## Random items
- AddressSanitizer test mode: add `--asan` flag to compile exec tests with `-fsanitize=address` to detect memory leaks, double-free, and use-after-free. Important before serious usage of `__copy__` + `__del__` patterns (e.g. CopyableBox[T]).
- Large value-type copy warning: estimate record sizes from fields and warn when Own[T] passes a large type by value (e.g. >128 bytes). For inline data there's no cheap move -- the bits are the object. Suggest Own[Box[T]] for cheap ownership transfer (moves a pointer) or Ptr[T]/Ptr[readonly[T]] for borrowing. Stricter threshold in @noalloc contexts.
- use this as source of examples: https://github.com/shedskin/shedskin/tree/master/examples (at some point we would like to make them all work)
- ultimate goal: make tpyc compile with tpyc
- game of life benchmark TPy vs CPy (two version: idiomatic python, optimized TPy types)
- **Division semantics switch: Python way vs C way for `/` and `//` (and `%`).** One compiler switch (or per-scope opt-in) selecting between parity and raw-C++ cost, covering both operators:
  - `//`/`%`: Python floors (round toward -inf; `-7 // 2` = `-4`), C++ truncates (`-3`). Codegen emits `div_floor`/`mod_floor` (a branch per op) plus the zero-divisor check (`div_check`/`mod_check`, elided when `divisor_non_zero` range tracking proves it). C mode = raw `/`/`%`, no floor adjustment, no zero check (UB on zero, like C++).
  - `/`: Python raises catchable `ZeroDivisionError` and defines int/int as the exact quotient rounded ONCE (the `truediv` overloads in `bigint.hpp`; magnitudes >= 2^53 take a BigInt slow path). C mode = plain `(double)a / (double)b` -- no zero check (yields inf/NaN), double-rounding above 2^53 accepted.
  - Implementation shape: the semantics live entirely in the stub `cpp_template`s (`lib/tpy/tpy/_builtins/_types.py`, `_core/_types.py`) and the runtime helpers, so the switch swaps templates like `--default-int` does; no sema change. Sub-options from the original entry remain live: default-to-C for fixed-width types only (Int32 users already accept C++ semantics) with BigInt always Python; and range-tracking elision (already partly in place via `divisor_non_zero`) as the no-semantic-change fallback. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` (mode naming, scope granularity -- global flag vs `# tpy:` directive vs decorator -- and the documented-divergence story).
- c++ generation profiles: utf8 strings vs char strings
- static_cast<char> -- should rather use checked cast (policy based)
- `Own[T]` for argument passing: passing an object from a pointer to an `Own[T]` param -- require explicit copy?
- `DefaultInt` type alias: currently a sentinel class in `tpy.extern` used only by `@type_param_default`. Make it a real type alias with compiler support so it can be used in type annotations (e.g. `x: DefaultInt = 42`).
- `iter()` non-native: currently `@native("tpy::__iter__")` because regular functions can't return protocol types (`Iterator[T]`). Relax the protocol return type restriction for generic functions so `iter()` can have a real body (`return x.__iter__()`).
- update char semantics (e.g. passing str to a function accepting Char should throw if len != 1)
- formatting/linting like in genweb
- existing C++ interoperability: when we want to call existing C++ we need to declare types/functions in TPy files, but without generation, only annotating how to use them in code
- auto-declare fields from `__init__`: extend beyond param-only to `self.f = expr` and `self.f = Constructor()` (requires expression type inference at parse/registration time, see `docs/CONSTRUCTOR_DESIGN.md`)
- `__int__` equivalent for Int32 etc types (e.g. `__int32__` etc or prefixed: `__tpy_int32__`)
- hoisted variable slots: keep them at the lowest scope that satisfies lifetime, instead of always hoisting to function scope
- runtime `using` declarations in global namespace (`tpy.hpp`): generated code should use `tpy::` prefix instead of relying on `using tpy::BigInt` etc.
- refactor: consider merging `gen_module_init()` body generation into `gen_body()` helper (functions and methods already use it, but module init has too many special cases currently)
- panic show line number?
- decorator qualified syntax: @tpy.extern.native (3-level attribute access not yet supported)
- "did you mean" import hints for unresolved decorators (needs library resolution)
- `# tpy: range-check=off`
- extend int type configuration to AddressType/SizeType/PtrDiff (e.g. UInt32, Int32, Int32)
- diagnostics: trace "float spill" origin across assignments/expressions (e.g. accidental `/` instead of `//`) and surface root cause in downstream type mismatch errors
- extract c++ compiler interface
- analysis: when an object is passed to a function by reference but then copied, should we suggest passing as Own[]?
- sizeof() function
- logging, stream object printing, to log instead of __str__

## tplib (TPy standard library)
- Protobuf macro (tplib.protobuf) — designed in the doc, would be the next real use case for the macro system

## Standard Library

See `docs/STDLIB_ROADMAP.md` for the per-module tracker (status, priority, items, blockers). Update that doc when adding or changing stdlib coverage.

- **datetime -- v1 through v4 landed (`timedelta` + `date` + `time` + `datetime` with fixed-offset `timezone` AND `ZoneInfo` awareness, PEP 495 fold, strftime/strptime/fromisoformat, timestamp/astimezone, TZ-honoring Hinnant backend, `zoneinfo` module).** See `docs/DATETIME_DESIGN.md` for the full phased roadmap and divergences. Remaining deferred surface: (a) DONE -- `dt.date()`/`dt.time()` accessors landed once the member-name/type-name C++ collision was fixed (test `stdlib/datetime_accessors`); (b) `timedelta` float OPERATORS -- `timedelta / number`, `timedelta * float`, `timedelta / float` (round-half-to-even, via the new `float.as_integer_ratio()` builtin) -- DONE (test `stdlib/datetime_timedelta_float`); float CONSTRUCTOR args (`timedelta(hours=1.5)`) intentionally DECLINED (would need an `int | float`-union ctor that boxes integer construction and `@overload __init__` support fighting the THIR ctor migration -- integer equivalent `timedelta(minutes=90)` exists; a symmetric-ctor-overloads impl was built then reverted for this reason -- see DATETIME_DESIGN.md); (c) `timedelta + datetime` / `timedelta + date` reflected forms (need `__radd__`-style dispatch on the record operand); (d) `zoneinfo` residual surface (v4.1 added `available_timezones()` + `ZoneInfo.fromutc`): `from_file` + `TZPATH`/`reset_tzpath` are deferred backend work (no public TZif-stream parser in the vendored provider); `no_cache`/`clear_cache` are PERMANENTLY unsupported (identity semantics + mid-run db reload conflict with the value-typed pin-once model -- see DATETIME_DESIGN.md); (e) aware `time` incl. `time.fold` (tzinfo field on `time`, `time.fromisoformat` offset suffix -- currently a loud documented rejection); (f) `f"{dt:%Y}"` strftime delegation -- blocked on the `__format__` protocol feature below; (g) `timezone.utc` spelling -- blocked on Final/class-constants-of-record-type below (module-level `UTC` alias is the supported spelling); (h) aware-arithmetic micro-opt, only if profiling ever shows it: `.tzinfo` reconstructs the tz value via a zone_key+zone_lookup round-trip (mutex + hash lookup) and `datetime.__add__` re-ingests tzinfo per operation -- a raw tz-block copy constructor would skip both; cold today, do not build speculatively.
- **Make `time.tzset()` genuinely re-resolve TZ (currently a CPython-parity no-op).** The tz provider pins at first local-time use, so a SECOND `os.environ["TZ"]`+`tzset()` switch mid-run is silently ignored (acknowledged divergence in DATETIME_DESIGN.md; set-once-then-use is fully correct). Fix shape (meta-review-verified sound): `date_shim.cpp` swaps the pinned provider through an `std::atomic<const TzProvider*>` (readers stay lock-free -- one acquire load on the per-conversion hot path; replaced providers deliberately leak since an in-flight reader may hold one and tzset frequency is ~never), `tzset()` forwards to a shim `retzset()`. Wrinkle to solve: `time.tzset()` lives in header-only `time.hpp` while the provider lives behind the managed `date` lib -- keep the no-op fallback when the backend isn't linked. Unlocks a deterministic mid-run-TZ-switch parity test. Deferred by user decision at the datetime v3 review (option (a) now, (b) filed).
- **`__format__` protocol: record format specs in f-strings.** CPython dispatches `f"{x:spec}"` to `x.__format__(spec)`; TPy has no `__format__` dunder -- today the spec is misapplied to the `__str__` result (silent-divergence defect filed in BUGS.md; the sema-side rejection is the bug-fix half). The feature half: recognize a user-defined `__format__(self, spec: str) -> str` and route record format specs through it; datetime then adds the CPython strftime delegation (`__format__` = strftime for non-empty specs). Needs `/tpy-add-feature`. Surfaced by the datetime v3 design survey.
- **`Final[T]` (module + class constants) for value-type records.** `Final[timezone]` is rejected (constexpr model: primitives + tuple only), so a record constant is spelled as a plain annotated global (`UTC: timezone = ...`) -- exportable but not Final-frozen, and a class-level `utc: Final[timezone]` inside the class (CPython's `timezone.utc`) is inexpressible. Design (worked out at the datetime v3 review follow-up): the CONSTEXPR arm is permanently out of reach for records (ctors run BigInt validation -- not a literal type -- and named timezones call `intern_name`, a mutex'd table), so ride Final's EXISTING non-constexpr arm (`extern const T NAME;` header / `const T NAME = ...;` source -- exactly how `Final[int]`/tuple lower today, dynamic init at startup). Prereqs verified on the packed layout: value records are trivially copyable, readonly methods emit const member fns (calls on a `const timezone` compile), and dynamic-init interning is SIOF-safe (`tz_intern`'s table is a function-local magic static, constructed on demand; `UTC` itself is unnamed and never interns). Two halves: (1) module-level -- small: admit value records in `is_final_allowed_inner` (no flag-ordering trap: globals analyze after the ValueType pass), route record Finals to the extern-const codegen arm, frozen-binding sema generalizes as-is (~1d + tests); (2) class-level `utc: Final[timezone]` -- fiddlier: a new class-constant emission arm (in-class `static const timezone utc;` decl + out-of-line definition; the current class-const path only emits inline `static constexpr`), incl. the self-referential static-member-of-own-type sequencing. Half (2) is what makes CPython's `timezone.utc` spellable and shrinks the DATETIME_DESIGN divergence entry (the `error_datetime_timezone_utc_attr` case then flips to a happy case). The ALL_CAPS-without-Final warning already keys on `is_final_allowed_inner`, so widening the predicate auto-revives the suggestion for record constants. Needs `/tpy-add-feature`. Surfaced by datetime v3's UTC constant.
- **collections.Counter -- deferred v1 surface.** Shipped v1: `Counter()` / `Counter(iterable)`, `c[key]` (missing -> 0), `__setitem__`, `len`, `in`, `total()`, `most_common(n)`, `update`/`subtract`. Deferred: (a) `elements()` -- blocked on the generator-iterating-and-subscripting-a-dict-field const bug (BUGS.md); a non-generator eager `list[T]` form is the fallback if the generator fix lands later; (b) the `+`/`-`/`&`/`|` operators -- blocked on the field-target subscript-assignment readonly-key bug (BUGS.md); (c) bare `most_common()` (no arg = all) -- v1 requires `n` (the no-arg/`n=None` form needs an overload or sentinel; pass a large `n` for all); (d) `Counter(mapping)` / `Counter(**kwargs)` construction (mapping currently counts keys, not values -- see the dict-as-Iterable warning item below and the docstring divergence note); (e) `update`/`subtract` accepting an arbitrary iterable / kwargs (v1 takes a `Counter` only); (f) minor: `most_common(n)` takes `Int32` so `n > Int32.MAX` panics (CPython accepts any int); `total()` emits a per-iteration `BigInt` copy; (g) a parametric count type -- `Counter[T: Hashable, C: AnyInt]` with `C` defaulting to `int` (so `Counter[Object, Int32]` gives cheap fixed-width counts) -- gated on class type-param defaults with an arbitrary default type (see the Type System entry). Once the two BUGS blockers are fixed, elements() and the operators are mechanical. Surfaced building Counter.
- **[feature] Warn when a `dict` is passed where an `Iterable[T]` (element-iterable) parameter is expected.** A `dict[K,V]` conforms to `Iterable[K]`, so `f(some_dict)` (for `f(xs: Iterable[K])`) silently iterates the KEYS -- a common CPython-habit bug (e.g. `Counter(mapping)` counts keys instead of reading values, with no diagnostic). Add a compile-time warning at the coercion site ("iterating a dict yields its keys; use `.items()`/`.values()`/`.keys()`") with the explicit-spelling escape hatch. General (not Counter-specific) and turns a silent divergence loud. New warning => needs `/tpy-add-feature` (diagnostic + escape hatch design). Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of `collections.Counter`.
- **collections: the rest of the module.** `OrderedDict` (thin alias/subclass over `tpy::ordered_map` -- dict is already insertion-ordered), `defaultdict` (store a factory; macro-friendly), `deque` (needs a C++ backing), `namedtuple` (class macro, ~`@dataclass(frozen=True)`), `ChainMap` (pure-TPy over a list of dicts; aliasing semantics need care). Each is its own `/tpy-add-feature`.
- **csv: deduplicate the row parser.** `reader` (a `Readable` protocol-param generator) and `_parse_rows` (a `Ptr[R]` generic generator used by `DictReader`) carry identical ~55-line CSV state machines. They can't share: `reader` must be protocol-param because a cross-module `csv.reader(io.StringIO(...))` must bind an rvalue temporary (a generic `R&` param rejects it), while `_parse_rows` must be `Ptr[R]` because `DictReader.__iter__` drives it from a borrowed-`Ptr` field inside a resumable frame -- and a protocol value can't form a `Ptr`, nor can a `Ptr`-param generator take a protocol. Collapse to one impl if/when either constraint lifts (cross-module generic-generator rvalue binding, or protocol-param generators nesting in a resumable frame). Surfaced building `csv.DictReader`.
- **csv `escapechar`, quoting constants, `Dialect`/`register_dialect`, `Sniffer`, DictReader `restval`/`restkey`, and DictWriter `extrasaction='ignore'`.** Deferred from the dict-row surface. DictWriter already matches CPython's write-side defaults (restval="" for a missing field, ValueError for an extra key); the `extrasaction='ignore'` opt-out is what's missing. DictReader read-side divergences are structural (`dict[str, str]` can't hold None or a list): a short row pads missing fields with `""` (CPython restval=None); a long row drops trailing extras (CPython restkey).
- **os module -- M2 remainder (pwd-dependent paths + environ iteration).** Shipped so far: filesystem queries (`getcwd`/`chdir`/`listdir`(+`"."` default)/`scandir`->`DirEntry`/`getenv` (now 2 typeshed-style overloads)), mutating ops (`mkdir`(+`mode=`)/`makedirs`(+`mode=`/`exist_ok=`)/`rmdir`/`removedirs`/`remove`/`rename`/`replace`/`symlink`/`readlink`) + full errno->OSError-subclass table, `stat`/`lstat` -> `stat_result` with the `os.path` stat queries (`getmtime`/`getatime`/`getctime`/`samefile`/`ismount`), `os.path.expandvars`, and `os.environ`/`putenv`/`unsetenv` (snapshot mapping; `getenv`/`expandvars` read the snapshot). Remaining, grouped by the binding each needs:
  - **`os.environ` view-semantics parity (low priority).** `keys()`/`values()`/`items()` return owned snapshot `list`s, not CPython's live set-like views -- iteration matches but set ops (`keys() & {...}`) and live reflection are unsupported; the faithful views fight the `auto_readonly[V]` dict-view return type + a field-borrow lifetime. Also: `_Environ.get` is a single `get(key, default=None) -> str|None`, not the typeshed str / str|None overload pair, because cross-module method overloads don't resolve (BUGS.md) -- restore the overload pair once that's fixed, for narrowing parity.
  - **`os.path.realpath` follow-ups:** the `strict=` kwarg (raise on a missing path; today only the `strict=False` default ships, and passing `strict=` is a compile error); exact symlink-loop / mid-path-permission parity (CPython `strict=False` returns a partial best-effort at the loop point, TPy returns the lexical absolute path -- a possible byte-divergence, rare).
  - **Full `pwd` stdlib module.** `os.path.expanduser` is done with an internal `getpwnam`/`getpwuid` home-dir helper (`tpy::stdlib::os::user_home`/`current_home`); the public `pwd` module (`struct_passwd` with `pw_name`/`pw_uid`/`pw_gid`/`pw_dir`/`pw_shell`/..., `getpwnam`/`getpwuid`/`getpwall`) is a separate feature. `grp` is the sibling.
  - **stat-tier follow-ups:** `os.path.samestat` (takes two `stat_result`, so it can't live in `os.path` without re-hitting the os<->os.path cycle -- needs stat_result reachable there, or relocate to `os`); `stat_result.st_blksize`/`st_blocks`/`st_rdev` (platform-specific extras); `stat_result` sequence/structseq protocol (`st[0]`..`st[9]`, `len`, iteration, unpacking -- CPython structseq; today attribute-access-only, indexing is a clean compile error) and a CPython-style `__repr__`; `os.fstat` (needs an fd); `os.mkdir`/`makedirs` `mode=` deterministic test via `os.umask` (current test uses the umask-independent `& 0o077 == 0` check).
  - **os coverage backlog (cheap test adds):** `readlink` error path (non-symlink/missing -> OSError); `os.path.getatime`/`getctime` missing-path errors (identical code path to the tested `getmtime`); assert the un-asserted `stat_result` fields (`st_uid`/`st_gid`/`st_dev`/`st_ino`/`st_atime_ns`/`st_ctime_ns` -- computed but never read in a test); `expandvars("abc$")` (`$` at end, distinct loop boundary); per-syscall error paths for the newer ops -- `os.link` to an existing dst (FileExistsError), `os.ftruncate`/`os.fsync` on a bad fd, `os.truncate` on a missing path (the errno->OSError table is already covered by `os_fd_errors`/`os_mutate_errors`, so these just pin the per-call wiring). Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of os M2 + the os tail.
  - **Mutating-op follow-ups:** `dir_fd`/`follow_symlinks` kwargs; `os.utime` no-arg (current time) and `ns=`/`times=None` forms (today `times` is a required tuple).
  - **fd-I/O follow-ups.** `O_NONBLOCK`/`O_CLOEXEC`/`O_DIRECTORY`/... (the rest of the `O_*` family -- add via the same `native_global`-to-a-C++-global pattern as the shipped flags); `os.get_blocking`/`set_blocking`; `openat`/`dir_fd`. None blocked -- all extend the shipped fd layer.
  - **Ergonomic: a `native_const("MACRO")` helper (also closes a const-correctness gap).** The shipped flag/whence/mode constants each need a hand-written **non-const** C++ global (`kc_o_rdonly = O_RDONLY;` ...) plus a `native_global` binding -- a TPy module constant named like a libc macro can't be emitted as a C++ symbol, and `native_global` only binds an existing symbol AND emits a non-const `extern`, so the C++ globals can't be `const` without a declaration mismatch. That leaves the TPy `Final` guarantee unenforced at the C++ storage level (latent: only hand-written C++ could write them). A thin `native_const("O_RDONLY")` auto-generating a safe-named `const` global + binding would remove the boilerplate, make the storage `const`, and serve the recurring need (fcntl/stat/termios/errno all expose macro-named int constants). Pure ergonomics + hardening; `/tpy-add-feature`-sized when a second module needs it.
  - **`os.scandir` / `DirEntry` follow-ups.** (a) `DirEntry.stat()` returns a fresh `stat_result` each call -- CPython caches it; add caching once returning a cached reference-type field is clean (today it would copy or fight the borrow checker). (b) `is_dir`/`is_file`/`stat`'s `follow_symlinks=False` variant (and `DirEntry.stat(follow_symlinks=False)` -> lstat) -- deferred; passing the kwarg is a compile error, not silent. (c) `scandir` returns an eager `list[DirEntry]`, not CPython's lazy iterator + context manager (`with os.scandir(p) as it`); the lazy/`__enter__` form needs an iterator type holding the open `DIR*` with deterministic close -- `with os.scandir()` is a clean compile error today.
  - Also still deferred: `os.path.commonpath` (path-aware) -- needs `min`/`max` over `list[list[str]]`, unsupported today (`min`/`max` over `list[str]` already errors -- `commonprefix` works around it with an explicit loop); `os.path.altsep` constant omitted (`None` on POSIX, no clean str-or-None constant).
- **Yield-by-reference of frame-resident values -- conservative for-loop form SHIPPED; general consumption IR-gated.** A generator may now yield a borrow of a frame-resident local: `for root, dirs, files in walk(p): dirs[:] = [...]` propagates the caller's in-place prune to the generator on resume (the `os.walk` contract). The shipped form rests on the existing consumer-side machinery: the for-loop loop var is a live borrow whose escapes (return / field-store / container-insert) the ephemeral-borrow rules govern (insert copies with the `copies ... into owned storage` warning -- memory-safe). Two follow-ups left open: (1) **Manual-`next()` consumption** of a borrow-yield silently COPIES where CPython aliases, with no warning (BUGS.md, Safety) -- pre-existing for param/self roots too; the rigorous fix (bind `next()` results as ephemeral borrows under a region rule, or warn) is region-flavored. (2) **Yield of a `Span`/view of a frame-local** (the strict "yield a mutable view" form) is not enabled -- the view path (`check_view_return_dangle`) does not pass `for_yield`, so a `Span` into a frame-local list yielded under `Iterator[Span[T]]` still rejects; enabling it needs the same view-lifetime/region reasoning as the captured-view-generator dangle (BUGS.md). Both are THIR/MIR region work. The `os.walk` stdlib function itself is now buildable (see STDLIB_ROADMAP). Test-coverage gaps to add when next touching this (none changes behavior, just guards): an `error_` case for a value-typed frame-local yield (the `_local_has_borrowable_storage` reject branch -- distinct, currently no negative test), and a happy case for a `dict`/`set` frame-local yield (only `list`/`tuple` are covered today). (The generator-*method* and ternary/walrus-wrapped loop-body-local yields are now covered by `tests/cases/generators/yield_loop_body_local_borrow`.) LATENT COUPLING: `yield_rescued_by_frame_local`'s `leaf_unsafe` walk (`sema/compatibility.py`) must mirror `is_dangling_return`'s wrapper recursion (coerce/field/subscript/ternary/walrus) -- a wrapper node added to `is_dangling_return` but not here silently re-opens the peephole UAF (this was the C1 review finding). Unify the two walks when the IR lands; until then, any new wrapper-recursion in `is_dangling_return` must be mirrored in `leaf_unsafe`.
- **Test gap: a view-family (str/bytes) for-loop var in an `async def`.** The resumable-frame view-loop-var pending-resolution fix (`_sync_resolved_to_ns` -> `pending_loop_vars`) is regression-tested for sync generators (`tests/cases/generators/resumable_view_loopvar`: str/bytes/tuple-unpack) but not for `async def`, which shares the exact `_collect_generator_locals` / `pending_loop_vars` path -- verified to compile, just needs a driven (`asyncio.run`) happy case to guard it. Cheap; bundle with the next asyncio test work.
- **View-family `*args` use storage-form elements by default (intentional; borrow-form deferred to THIR).** A `*args: str` / `*args: bytes` param lowers its ELEMENTS to the owned storage form (`varargs<std::string>` / `varargs<std::vector<uint8_t>>`, via `varargs_elem_cpp` -> `type_to_cpp`), so each individual arg is copied into the pack -- unlike the scalar `str`/`bytes` param, which is already the borrow form (`std::string_view` / `std::span<const uint8_t>`). This was an implicit default of the original `*args` implementation (`8f211f572`, refined by `ebfc4246c`'s body-view type), not a deliberated decision; it is memory-safe. The zero-copy borrow form is attractive for the `os.path.join("a","b")` / `f(*paths: str)` hot path (the win is modest -- short strings are SSO, so the copy is cheap; the gain is long strings + a 16B-vs-32B pack), but it is **all-paths-or-nothing**: a varargs param has one element type, so every consumer (iteration, comprehension, subscript, slice, forwarding, star-unpack, `list(parts)`, returns/stores, generator frame capture) must agree, and several element-type derivations in codegen currently disagree. It is a textbook instance of the borrow-form-vs-storage-form duality, so it is **deferred to the THIR/MIR migration** -- where it lands as one element-as-borrow + materialize-at-owned-sink rule instead of ~10 scattered consumer-side dispatch patches plus frame-owning generator capture and a star-unpack `vector<view>` helper. The full consumption / sink / lifetime inventory (the spec the migration inherits) is in `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` under the borrow/storage-form ledger (Open Questions item 9). Surfaced reviewing the varargs view->owned fix; confirmed all-paths-or-nothing by a per-shape probe + Codex co-validation (2026-06).
- **Share the condition boolean-tree walk between `narrowing._isinstance_facts` and `statements._unfold_loop_killed_isinstance`.** Both recurse the same `TpyCall(isinstance) / TpyBinOp(&&,||) / TpyUnaryOp(!)` structure -- one to collect narrowing facts, one to clear the static fold on a loop-killed isinstance subject. They must stay in lockstep if a new connective/short-circuit form is added. Extract a small visitor (a leaf callback over `isinstance_var`-carrying nodes) in `narrowing.py` and route both through it. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the while-isinstance fold fix.
- **Test-coverage: `os.walk(topdown=False)` with `followlinks=True` and a broken-symlink entry.** The bottomup followlinks/symlink handling (`followlinks or not path.islink(bchild)`, per-entry `is_dir()` try/except) is character-identical to topdown's (covered by `os_walk`), so bottomup currently relies on shared-mechanism coverage. Add a bottomup symlink case when next touching os.walk. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the bottomup feature.
- **Optional-`Callable` param: excluded shapes (from the optional-callable-param feature).** A lambda/function-name arg now binds to a `Callable[...] | None` param (the arg-hint unwraps `OptionalType`). NOT covered: (a) `readonly[Callable[...]] | None` -- the unwrap peels `Optional` but not the inner `readonly`, so a lambda arg still hits "cannot infer" (verified `/tmp/agents/v2probe/exc_readonly.py`); a readonly callback is exotic. (b) a broader `Union` with a Callable member (`A | Callable[...] | None`, or two members one of which is Callable) -- only the exact `Callable | None` (single-member Optional) is handled; a multi-member union arm doesn't expose the callable shape to lambda/name inference. (c) `Send[Callable[...]] | None` -- canonicalizes to `Optional[Send[Callable]]`, so the marker sits *inside* the Optional. Peeling send/sync after the Optional makes arg-inference pass, but then narrowing + the call path also need to unwrap the Send (else the narrowed call hits `optional<function>(args)` -> C++ build error); since that's more than the arg-hint and a pre-fix `Send[Callable]|None` lambda was already a clean sema reject, it was deliberately left a clean reject rather than advanced into a codegen failure (verified `/tmp/agents/v2probe/send_callable_optional.py`). (a)/(b) are extensions of the same `expressions.py` arg-hint unwrap (add the `readonly` peel / union-arm scan); (c) additionally needs send/sync unwrap in narrowing.py + the callable-call path. Add if a real use surfaces.
- **Widen `io` seek/tell offsets to Int64.** `io.StringIO`/`BytesIO` `seek(pos, whence)`/`tell()` and the `Seekable` protocol (`tpy/_core/_types.py`) are all `Int32`, so a buffer position past 2GB overflows. CPython positions are unbounded ints; Int64 is the natural width. The `io.SEEK_*` constants are Int32 to match the current `whence`; widening would let them be Int64 and reuse os's int64 seek globals (retiring the parallel `kc_seek_*32` globals added for the Int32 form). Touches the seek/tell signatures on both buffers + the protocol + snapshot churn -- a focused "64-bit io offsets" change, deferred from the io.SEEK_* constant work. `/tpy-add-feature`-sized.
- **`io.TextIOWrapper`** -- wrap a binary buffer (`BytesIO` / `open(..., "rb")`'s `BinaryIO`) to present decoded text with newline translation. Deferred from the `read(size)` + `json.load/dump` effort because it's *not* a prerequisite for json/csv/configparser (those operate over text file objects, which already satisfy `Readable`/`Writable`). The real design call is how `TextIOWrapper` holds its underlying binary buffer: a generic `TextIOWrapper[B]` owning the buffer via `Own[B]`, a `@dynamic`-protocol field, or a borrowed `Ptr[B]` -- no existing stdlib precedent. Plus an encoding story (TPy `str` is UTF-8 internally, so non-UTF-8 codecs would be a documented divergence) and `newline=` translation. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` (the buffer-ownership model is a genuine design decision).

## Other
- Docstrings: silently skipped in codegen (harmless, but no introspection support)
- make a doc with TPy vs Python differences

## C++ Codegen Review Findings (2026-03-12, updated 2026-03-18)

### Systematic suboptimalities
- **[HIGH effort]** Double subscript in augmented assignment: `a[i] += v` emits `__setitem__(a, i, add_check(__getitem__(a, i), v))` -- two separate bounds checks + index normalization. Hand-written C++ would use a single indexed access. Fix needs a reference-based codegen path (e.g. `__getitem_ref__` or direct `operator[]`).
- **[HIGH effort]** Inherited fields body-assigned instead of member initializer list: child constructors without `super()` assign inherited `std::string`/container fields in the body (default-construct then assign) instead of via MIL or base-class constructor delegation. Adds an extra default construction per non-trivial inherited field.
- **[MED effort]** Double `__deref__()` call in auto-deref field access: `r.x + r.y` where `r` has user-defined `__deref__()` emits `r.__deref__().x + r.__deref__().y` -- two separate deref calls. Should generate a temporary for the deref result when multiple field/method accesses share the same deref base in one expression.
### Minor type mismatches
- **[LOW]** Generator yield tuple uses `T&` instead of `T`: generic for-generators emit `auto __val = std::tuple<int32_t, T&>{...}` because the loop variable is bound as `auto&&`. The return wraps it in `std::optional<std::tuple<int32_t, val_or_ref_t<T>>>`, so the implicit tuple converting constructor strips the ref and it compiles correctly. Cosmetic -- the intermediate type is technically wrong but has no runtime impact.
- **[LOW]** `val_or_ref_t<T>` / `param_val_or_ref_t<T>` wrappers are redundant when the corresponding type-param is bounded by `ValueType`. Example: `def identity[T: ValueType](x: T) -> T` emits `template<::tpy::ValueType T> ::tpy::val_or_ref_t<T> identity(::tpy::param_val_or_ref_t<T> x)`. With the `ValueType` bound, `is_value_type<T>` is always `true_type` so both traits reduce to `T` -- the signature could collapse to `T identity(T x)`. Same ABI, cleaner generated C++. Fix: when emitting a generic function/method param/return and the corresponding type-param has a `ValueType` bound, skip the trait wrap. Affects the small handful of `[T: ValueType]` cases in the test corpus (snapshot regen on those alone). Surfaced during /tpy-review of the native ValueType spec-emission fix.
- **[LOW]** Generic-tuple slot emission is needlessly verbose: `to_val_or_ptr<::tpy::val_or_ptr_t<T>>(item)` spells the trait twice because `Dest` must be explicit (the source alone can't distinguish a `val_or_ref<U>` instantiation from a plain non-value `U` -- see the helper comment in `type_traits.hpp`), but `Dest` is always `val_or_ptr_t<T>` / `val_or_cptr_t<T>` OF the type param. Add type-param-keyed siblings `to_val_or_ptr_t<T>(x)` / `to_val_or_cptr_t<T>(x)` returning the trait type, halving the call noise. Optionally also emit one per-generator `using` alias for the yield-slot tuple type, which is currently spelled three times per simple-gen lambda (make_generator arg, lambda return, optional wrap). Same ABI, cleaner generated C++; snapshot regen on generic-tuple cases. Surfaced reading `gen_generic` output during the tuple-unification merge review.

### Performance: JSON parser codegen overhead
Benchmarked with CME MBO order book (15MB JSON, 20K messages). Library-level optimizations (skip_value rewrite, redundant _skip_ws elimination) brought 68ms->59ms. Hand-optimizing the generated C++ (removing checked arithmetic, direct pointer indexing) brought it to 23ms (7.6x vs CPython). The gap is:
- **[MED effort]** StrView/str direct indexing: `sv[i]` where `i` is non-negative Int32 guarded by `i < len` should emit `sv.data()[i]` instead of `__getitem__(sv, i)` with `normalize_index`. The pattern `while pos < len: c = data[pos]` is ubiquitous in parsers.
- **[MED effort]** Checked arithmetic elision for bounded loop counters: `pos += 1` where `pos < len` was just checked, `pos` is Int32, and `len` is Int32 -- the increment can't overflow. Extend `value_range.py` to propagate bounds from loop/if guards.

### Missed optimizations
- **[Open, no work planned]** Generic `T=str`/`T=bytes` param ABI doesn't match non-generic. Direct `str` params lower to `std::string_view`; generic-T routes through `param_val_or_ref_t<T>` keyed on the C++ storage type and gives `const std::string&` -- the trait can't distinguish TPy `str` from TPy `String` since they share `std::string` storage. Same conflation for `bytes`/`bytearray`. Cost: generic-T-over-str pays a `std::string` materialization at call sites (SSO covers short literals; long literals heap-allocate once per call). No fix is unambiguously best; every idiomatic option pays a real cost (distinct types via inheritance breaks `vector<auto_string>` <-> `vector<std::string>` interop; codegen monomorphization is heavy compiler internals; descriptor templates compromise readable-C++ output). Recommended fix *if* the perf gap ever justifies the work: codegen monomorphization (per-call function emission, output stays idiomatic, vector interop preserved). See `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` "Open Questions" item 8 for the full landscape. Surfaced on the generic-U-over-str dangling-view bug.
- **[MED effort]** Proven-safe `T=StrView` inference for pass-through generics. Until the U-over-str dangling-view fix landed, `prefer_strview_for_literals` auto-downgraded `T=str` to `T=StrView` when bare-T params received string literals, on the safety theory that string literals have static lifetime. The downgrade was zero-cost for pure pass-through (`def transform[U](x: U) -> U: return x` with `transform("hello")` produced `transform<string_view>("hello")` -- no materialization). Removed during the dangling-view fix because the signature-level safety check (excluding `Fn[..., T]` / `Own[T]` / record fields) couldn't soundly cover the bug class -- a generic body can construct/store/forward T values, and signature scanning misses that. To claw back the perf, the right shape is **body analysis**: only auto-infer `T=StrView` when the generic is *proven* borrow/pass-through safe -- body returns/yields exactly one of the bare-T literal-backed params, no construction/storage/forwarding of new T values. Use `return_borrows_from` facts if they can express it; otherwise it's an explicit body pass. Today the perf is recovered by writing `transform[StrView]("...")` explicitly. Surfaced when test diffs showed `transform<std::string_view>` -> `transform<std::string>` after dropping the downgrade.
- **[LOW]** Gratuitous named-temp hoisting for non-identifier call args. `_gen_call` / `_gen_method_call` hoist every non-bare-identifier argument to a named local, even when no materialization, lifetime extension, or last-use analysis is needed: `heappush(h, 0)` emits `int32_t __tmp_1 = 0; heappush(h, __tmp_1);` instead of `heappush(h, 0);`. Observed across `cases/stdlib/heapq/expected/src/main.cpp` for trivial int literals, BigInt constructor calls, list literals, and the string case above. Pure source-level bloat -- RVO/dead-store-elim optimizes it at runtime, but it noises up generated C++ and adds work to the C++ frontend (already a compile-time bottleneck). Likely a uniformity decision in codegen to simplify evaluation-order / auto-move analysis. Fix: only hoist when the arg needs materialization (list literal -> vector, literal -> type-conversion), a slot for auto-move, or when two args could alias; let trivial literals pass through inline.
- **[MED effort]** Dead branch elision in codegen: when a condition folds to a constant (`if (true)` / `if (false)`), emit only the live branch body instead of the entire if/else structure. Currently the folded constant is emitted and the C++ compiler optimizes it away, but this adds unnecessary work to C++ compilation which is already a bottleneck. Applies to Literal narrowing (`if mode == "rb":` inside a branch where mode is known), union overload specialization (`isinstance` checks), and any future constant-folding path. Pattern exists in `_gen_if_overload_specialized` for union isinstance -- generalize to all if-conditions.
- **[LOW]** String concat chain produces N-1 intermediate allocations: `a + b + c + d` emits left-associative nested `str_concat` calls, each allocating a temporary `std::string`. A codegen optimization detecting a chain of `+` on string-view operands could emit a single `reserve` + N `append` calls.
- **[LOW]** BigInt method return copies: any method (including property getters) returning `int`/BigInt returns by value (`::tpy::BigInt`), copying on every call. Not property-specific -- `is_value_type()=True` makes `_resolve_return_type` use value return. `str` has `StrView` as a zero-copy alternative; BigInt has no equivalent view type. Fix options: (A) add a `BigIntView` / `const BigInt&` return convention for non-mutating methods, (B) extend `_resolve_return_type` to use `const T&` for `is_expensive_copy()` types in const methods.
- **[LOW]** `__param_` copy for reassigned parameters: when a parameter is reassigned in the function body, codegen takes it by `const&` then copies into a mutable local. For BigInt/string params, taking by value instead would let the caller move. Only helps when caller passes an rvalue; for lvalue calls it's worse (forces copy at call site vs zero-cost `const&`). Also changes ABI (not API).
- **[LOW]** `print()` routes *every* union arg through `::tpy::__str__`, including kinds that already have a cheaper direct stream path. A recursive-union *wrapper struct* has its own `operator<<`, and an isinstance-narrowed union local is emitted as its concrete member type -- both stream directly with `os << x`, but the `UnionType` branch in `gen_print` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/builtins.py`) wraps them in `__str__`, which materializes a `std::string` via `ostringstream`. Output is byte-identical; cost is one alloc per such print (negligible in an I/O path). Kept simple deliberately (matches the f-string path, which also routes all unions through `__str__`). The proper fix is *not* mechanical: `get_resolved_type` ignores narrowing so `gen_print` can't cheaply tell a narrowed local from a bare variant, and gating on `narrowed_vars` would miss assignment-narrowed unions (`a: int|str = 5`, still stored as a variant) -- risking a regression of the original no-`operator<<` build failure. Revisit only if print perf matters; the narrowing-aware path f-string uses (`gen_expr_narrowed`) is the model. The intended end-state is to *stream* a union instead of building a string: a `VariantPrinter` wrapper with `operator<<` (paralleling `ListPrinter`/`DictPrinter`/`TuplePrinter`) that `std::visit`s the variant and streams each member through its Python-faithful form -- scalars (`int|str`, `int|float`) stream directly with zero string alloc; only container members stringify, as they already do. A bare `operator<<(ostream&, std::variant<Ts...>&)` is *not* viable: ADL won't find a `tpy`-namespace overload for a union with no `tpy`-namespace member (`float | str` -> `variant<double, std::string>`, both `std`), and adding the overload to namespace `std` is UB. `VariantPrinter` is the ADL-safe form (it's a `tpy` type). It is gated on the same narrowing-precision prerequisite as above: unlike the universal `__str__`, a variant-specific printer fails to compile when `gen_print` hands it a narrowed concrete value or a recursive-union wrapper struct, so it can only be emitted once `gen_print` fires the union branch on genuinely-bare variants. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the union print/str fix.
- **[LOW]** Dead frame-field slot for awaitless-loop iter vars in resumable bodies. Sema's `pending_loop_vars` adds **every** for-loop iter var to `func.generator_locals`, regardless of whether the loop spans a yield/await. The frame-layout emitter (`gen_coro_struct` / `gen_generator_struct`) then emits a slot per name (e.g. `::tpy::frame_slot<Item> it;` visible in `tests/cases/async/coro_for_loop_ref_iter/expected/include/main.hpp`). The slot is never written or read -- the body uses a C++-local `const auto& it = *__beg_0;` instead. Cost: ~16-24 bytes wasted per coro frame per dead iter var, plus tiny ctor/dtor work per coro instance. Correctness was addressed on `fix-iter-var-shadow` via `frame_field_shadows` (which suppresses the body-emit `(*it)` peel); this entry is the perf follow-up. Fix shape: sema-side, scan each for-loop body for internal yield/await (the scan logic already exists at codegen-time as `_for_body_contains_yield`); only add the iter var to `pending_loop_vars` -> `generator_locals` when the loop actually spans a suspension. Side effect: removes the for-loop case from `frame_field_shadows` (the with-as / tuple-unpack cases still need it). Risk: verify no other consumer of `pending_loop_vars` relies on the over-broad classification -- in particular Phase F3's tuple-unpack frame-store gate (`_gen_tuple_unpack`, `statements.py`) keys off `frame_field_shadows` to tell a suspending loop var (real frame field -> frame-store the targets) from an awaitless one (shadow local -> keep zero-copy `T&` references); the narrowing must keep that distinction intact (ideally it makes the gate unnecessary by not hoisting awaitless loop vars at all).

## Low Priority
- **Test harness: the cpy-phase skip is keyed only on `main.py`, missing sibling `src/*.py` in multi-module cases.** `compute_main_fingerprint` (`tests/conftest.py`) hashes `main.py`'s bytes alone, and `test_case`'s cpy skip gates on that `main` fingerprint + the session `cpy_stubs` hash. For a multi-module case where `main.py` imports a helper `.py` in the same `src/`, editing the helper changes neither hash, so the cpy phase skips and the TPy-vs-CPython parity for that edit goes unverified until `main.py` changes or `--force-exec`. The exec phase is unaffected (its content-addressed fingerprint hashes every local module's generated `.cpp/.hpp`); only the secondary CPython parity check is coarse. (`input.txt` is not a gap -- `run_cpython` doesn't pipe stdin, so the fixture never reaches CPython.) Fix: hash all local `src/*.py` in the main fingerprint -- but it changes the `main` value for every case, forcing a one-time corpus-wide cpy re-run (or a mass `--update-snapshots` to refresh `.fingerprints`). Pre-existing; low severity. Surfaced by /tpy-ready of the local-exec-cache branch.
- **Perf: non-trivially-copyable value types (`BigInt`/`int`, `String`) are *copied* into a `*args` backing array instead of borrowed.** The varargs copy-vs-borrow split keys on `is_value_type`: value types use the contiguous `varargs<T, true>` (direct) mode, so call sites copy-construct each arg into a `std::array<T, N>` (`many(a, b)` -> `{BigInt(a), BigInt(b)}`). That's free for trivially-copyable value types (`Int32`, `Char`, `bool`), but `BigInt`/`String` are const-ref-passed everywhere else (`one(const BigInt&)`) and behave-as-copy without a physical copy -- so a `*args: int` element gets a deep copy of the digit storage it would otherwise borrow. Correctness-neutral; matters only for hot paths passing large `BigInt`/`String` varargs. Fix shape: route non-trivially-copyable value types through the indirect/pointer-array (`varargs<T, false>`) borrow mode like reference types, so the split is "trivially-copyable -> copy, else borrow" rather than "value -> copy". Pre-existing (the packing-mode selection predates the `varargs[T]` body-view type). Surfaced reviewing BigInt vararg copying. The varargs copy-vs-borrow split keys on `is_value_type`: value types use the contiguous `varargs<T, true>` (direct) mode, so call sites copy-construct each arg into a `std::array<T, N>` (`many(a, b)` -> `{BigInt(a), BigInt(b)}`). That's free for trivially-copyable value types (`Int32`, `Char`, `bool`), but `BigInt`/`String` are const-ref-passed everywhere else (`one(const BigInt&)`) and behave-as-copy without a physical copy -- so a `*args: int` element gets a deep copy of the digit storage it would otherwise borrow. Correctness-neutral; matters only for hot paths passing large `BigInt`/`String` varargs. Fix shape: route non-trivially-copyable value types through the indirect/pointer-array (`varargs<T, false>`) borrow mode like reference types, so the split is "trivially-copyable -> copy, else borrow" rather than "value -> copy". Pre-existing (the packing-mode selection predates the `varargs[T]` body-view type). Surfaced reviewing BigInt vararg copying.
- **Test-fixture dedup: the self-signed cert PEM + `handshaken_pair()` helper are copy-pasted across the https test cases** (`ssl/tls_makefile`, `stdlib/https_client`, `stdlib/urlopen_https`, `tplib/requests_https`, `tplib/requests_redirect_https`). Each TPy test case compiles as a standalone module, so there's no shared *compiled* helper -- but the cert blob and the handshake-loop body are plain source text that a regen (cert expiry, a `handshaken_pair` signature change) must touch in ~5 places, where they can silently drift. Options: a committed shared companion `.py` the cases import, or a small generator. Low priority; surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective of SSL Increment 5.
- **Test gap: requests https failure paths are offline-unblocked only for context config.** `tplib/requests_verify_context` unit-tests `_ssl_context_for` (verify True/False/"path" -> verify_mode/check_hostname) directly. Two end-to-end paths remain untested because the `_tls` injection seam supplies a *post-handshake* SSLSocket, so handshake-time failures can't be reached offline: (a) the `ssl.SSLError -> requests.SSLError` wrap in `_request_on` (the wrap + `str(e)` preservation are verified by dumped C++ + a runtime catch-rethrow probe, but no in-suite case drives a real TLS read failure through requests); (b) `verify=False` actually permitting an otherwise-untrusted handshake. Unblock condition: a test seam that injects a *pre-handshake* failing transport (or a loopback TLS server fixture that completes inside the harness). Surfaced by /tpy-review of Increment 5 (test-coverage). Low priority -- the logic is verified by other means.
- **Perf: `requests._connect` copies a freshly-built `SSLContext` instead of moving it.** `_ssl_context_for(verify)` returns `Own[SSLContext]`, but `HTTPSConnection.__init__` takes `context` by reference and `copy()`s it, so a brand-new context is copied (semantically a move). Making `HTTPSConnection.__init__` accept `context: Own[ssl.SSLContext] | None` would let the fresh `Own` move in directly (and still create its own default for the `None` case). Minor -- `SSLContext` is a small config record (the by-value capture was an accepted Inc-4 decision); revisit only if it shows up. Surfaced by /tpy-review of Increment 5 (safety-model).
- **Test gap: `@dynamic`-override reference param -- ptr-variant-union and transitive-inheritance shapes.** `tests/cases/protocols/dyn_inherit_ref_param` covers the override-param const-ness fix for `dict`/`list` ref params on plain (non-`@readonly`) overrides. Two sibling shapes the same fix path touches are untested: (a) a ptr-variant-union param (`A | B` of records) in a `@dynamic` override -- a distinct codegen shape (deep-vs-shallow const, `param_const.py:116/135`); (b) a class that inherits a `@dynamic` protocol *transitively* via a concrete intermediate, overriding a method with a ref param (`override` is derived transitively in `_get_dynamic_override_info`). Both compile under the fix but have no dedicated case. (The `@readonly`-override + ref-param combination is NOT a coverage gap but a separate open bug -- see BUGS.md.) Low value (the const-suppression path is exercised by dict/list); filed for completeness. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-dyn-override-param-const`.
- **Test gap: `varargs[T]` -> `Span[T]` rejection at return / local-binding position.** The `varargs`-body-view tests cover the rejection at the call-argument position (`error_varargs_to_span_param` etc.); the return position (`def f(*a: T) -> Span[T]: return a`) and local-binding (`x: Span[T] = args`) use the same coercion path but have no dedicated test. Add a `error_varargs_return_to_span` case. Low value (same coercion already exercised), filed for completeness. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the varargs body-view type.
- **`ClassInfo.add_method` mutates the passed `TpyFunction`** (`tpyc/macro_api.py:1211`) -- sets `func.is_macro_generated = True` on caller-owned state. Classic Python alias-mutation pattern; no current trigger because all in-tree callers pass freshly-built functions, but a future macro that re-registers an existing method (wrap-and-replace pattern, e.g. trace/logging decorator) would falsely flag user-written code as macro-generated. Fix: accept the flag as an explicit `add_method(..., is_macro_generated: bool = True)` parameter, or copy the function. Surfaced by /code-review of the super-init UX branch.
- **Extract a shared "is the C++ default ctor synthesizable" predicate** in `tpyc/typesys.py`. `tpyc/codegen_cpp/records.py::_all_fields_default_constructible` / `_fld_type_cpp_default_constructible` and `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py::_record_default_ctor_is_deleted` / `_field_type_blocks_default_ctor` express the same C++ rule from opposite angles. Two parallel implementations means a change to either has to be mirrored in the other or they silently diverge. Move the canonical recursive predicate next to `del_suppresses_default_ctor` (already in `typesys.py`), and have both sema and codegen call it. Aligns with CLAUDE.md guidance on pushing per-type properties into library-declared metadata over compiler hardcoding. Defer until the gap above is observed in the wild or until the THIR migration -- the migration is the proper place to land this consolidation. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the super-init rule.
- **Quote code suggestions in diagnostic messages.** Sema/codegen diagnostics that embed code snippets in prose currently render them bare, making code parts visually indistinguishable from English. E.g. `use -> StrView for zero-copy access or -> String to silence this warning` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py:403`); similar patterns at `tpyc/diagnostics.py:43` (Own[...]), `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py:330` (@dataclass(frozen=True)), `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py:1580` (Box[X]), `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:1964` (Own[...]), `tpyc/sema/calls.py:4559,4623` (@dataclass / __init__). Sweep ~10 sites; wrap each embedded snippet in backticks (or another visual marker). ~40-60 existing test snapshots regenerate as a mechanical update. Surfaced reading `rc_dyn_structural`'s `diag.txt`.
- **PEP 696 default-type-params support.** Once min-Python is bumped to 3.13+, replace the implicit
  default-T=U inference rule (`type_ops.py::infer_type_params_for_function`) with explicit signature
  syntax: `def f[U, T = U](...) -> ...`. Migration: when PEP 696 lands in TPy's parser, emit a
  warning at sema for any factory function relying on the implicit rule, suggesting the explicit
  annotation. Eventually drop the implicit rule once all stdlib + tests migrate. Dependency:
  bumping `pyproject.toml` `requires-python` from 3.12 to 3.13. Touches: parser (accept `[T = U]`
  in generic-param lists), sema (resolve the explicit default), and a deprecation warning hook on
  the implicit rule.
- Automatic `Iterable[T]` range-for dispatch via a range-adapter. Codegen emits a single range-for over a helper `::tpy::iter_range(items)` that returns `std::ranges::ref_view(items)` when the concrete type satisfies `NativeIterable` (zero-overhead direct begin/end) or `::tpy::__iter__(items)` otherwise (`native_iterator` or user iterator with `next_iter_mixin`-synthesized begin/end). Single loop body in generated C++; dispatch hides inside the helper via `if constexpr`. Strictly cleaner than both the manual `Iterable[T] | NativeIterable[T]` + isinstance pattern (ugly) and an `if constexpr`-around-body approach (body duplicated). Could collapse the current universal default (`for (;;) { r = __itr.__next__(); ... }`) into the same `for (auto x : iter_range(...))` form for uniformity. Gotchas to resolve: (1) `val_or_ref<T>` unwrapping in the loop variable (needs a deref-hook in `next_iter_mixin::iterator` or an `operator T` decay); (2) move-only iterators (`map`/`filter`/`zip` results) — verify both branches preserve no-copy semantics; (3) rvalue iterables need an lvalue binding first (already done via `auto&& __src`). Infrastructure already in place: protocol narrowing + concrete-type NativeIterable detection both plumbed through codegen. Confirm via benchmark that uniformly routing everything through the adapter doesn't regress anything.
- Native records without `__init__` silently get a positional constructor synthesized from field order. This is fragile -- reordering fields in the `.py` stub breaks callers with no error. Should require an explicit `__init__` stub instead (like non-native records).
- TypedDict per-field `NotRequired[]` / `Required[]` (PEP 655) for finer control than `total=False`.
- `f"{x!r}"` doesn't use the repr() sema fallback: `repr(x)` accepts primitives and user records, but `f"{x!r}"` still requires the `Representable` protocol. Should share the same acceptance logic.
- `= default` semantic gap: records with required `__init__` params currently emit `ClassName() = default;` if their C++ fields are all trivially constructible, bypassing the Python-level construction contract. Should emit `= delete` (or nothing) instead -- `std::optional<T>` and containers don't require default-constructibility, so the impact is limited to direct `T t;` / `T arr[N]` patterns which tpyc doesn't generate anyway. Fix: check `init_params` required count in `_fld_type_cpp_default_constructible` (same as `_is_default_constructible`). See `docs/CONSTRUCTOR_DESIGN.md` open question 4.
- Unnecessary pointer locals for loop-scoped tuple unpack vars: `reassigned_vars` marks a name as reassigned when it appears in two for-loops, even though the C++ declarations are in independent loop-body scopes. This causes `T*` pointer indirection instead of `T&` reference binding. The scopes are independent in C++ so re-declaration as `T&` each iteration is safe.
- None-seeded variable assigned in all branches stays `Optional[T]`: when `x = None` is followed by assignment in both the `if` and `else` branches (so every path guarantees a value), `x` is still typed `std::optional<T>` after the if/else block. Post-dominance analysis could demote it to `T` and skip the optional wrapper. Not a correctness issue -- output is identical -- but adds unnecessary runtime cost and less readable C++.
- Container-literal `any_movable` over-triggers for a last-use `str | None` / `bytes | None` element. A view-form Optional param is in `movable_locals`, so `_is_last_use_movable` fires at a container-literal element site (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py` `_gen_array_literal` / `_gen_dict_literal` / `_gen_set_literal`); the element has already been view-copied to owned storage (`std::string((*a))`), so the resulting `std::move(std::string(...))` is a no-op on a prvalue and the literal needlessly routes through `make_vector` / `make_ordered_map` / `make_ordered_set` (extra `reserve`) instead of brace-init. Output is correct; this is a minor codegen-quality nit. Fix: gate the move / `any_movable` on the element code being an unwrapped pass-through of the movable local (the view-conversion wrap rewrote it, so the move doesn't apply). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the literal-element move fix.
- Test gap: the set-literal / `set.add()` path for the value-tuple-with-pointer-repr-member copy warning is newly wired (`_analyze_set_literal` -> `_warn_storage_element_copy` and the `add` insert -> `T -> Own[T]` branch) but has no test case. Mostly unreachable today (set elements must be hashable + copyable, so the element type is constrained), but a user record with `__hash__`/`__eq__` whose tuple wraps a reference member would hit it. Add a `set[tuple[...]]` literal + `.add()` case once a clean hashable-record exemplar exists. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the tuple-member copy warning.
- Comprehension copy-warning loc guard is inconsistent with the literal sink. The new comprehension warning sites (`_analyze_elem_comprehension` / `_analyze_dict_comprehension` in `tpyc/sema/expressions.py`) guard on the element/key/value sub-expression's `.loc`, while the literal-element sink (`_analyze_array_literal` etc.) guards on the container node's `expr.loc` to suppress macro/compiler-synthesized nodes. No macro synthesizes comprehensions today (the element-level loc is arguably the more precise guard), so this cannot bite yet, but the two sinks should express "is this user-written" the same way. Align to one guard during the THIR migration or when a macro first emits a comprehension. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the comprehension copy-warning fix.
- Test gap: the comprehension copy-warning has no dedicated set-comprehension warn case, no dict-KEY reference-type warn case (the case covers only a value-type key), and no set/dict `@nocopy` error case. The chokepoint is shared (`_warn_storage_element_copy`, routed for list+set elements and dict key+value), so behavior is covered mechanically and the list/dict-value/error-list branches are directly exercised -- these are missing regression guards, not a current hole. Adding the set / dict-key cases needs a hashable reference type (`@dataclass(frozen=True)` or explicit `__hash__`/`__eq__`). Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage, cpython-parity) of the comprehension copy-warning fix.

- Threading V2 shipped `Atomic[T: AnyFixedInt]` (`tpy.atomic`) with the full
  `std::atomic<integral>` op surface. Deliberately sliced for later, on demand:
  (a) the remaining integral fetch ops `fetch_max` / `fetch_min` / `fetch_nand`
  and `fetch_update` (Rust has them; C++ `std::atomic` lacks nand/update); (b)
  `AtomicBool` / `AtomicPtr` equivalents -- `AnyFixedInt` excludes `bool`, `Char`,
  and pointers, so those need their own conformance or a sibling type; (c) a
  lock-free-restriction bound if we ever want to *enforce* (not just document)
  that only lock-free scalars are wrapped. None blocks Arc; add when a real use
  appears.
- **[parser cleanup] Generalize `_schema_from_stub` so an all-defaulted, all-bool decorator stub validates through the generic path instead of a bespoke parser.** `tpyc/parse/parser.py`'s `_conditional_override_markers` hand-rolls bool-type / duplicate / unexpected-keyword validation for `@unsafe_send(if_params_send=..., if_params_sync=...)`, because `_schema_from_stub`'s "first param = positional" rule doesn't fit a kwargs-only signature. Teach the deriver to treat an all-defaulted signature (esp. all-bool) as kwargs-only, then route these decorators through `_validate_decorator_args` like the other builtin decorators. Pre-existing bypass shape (the old `@unsafe_send_if(*traits)` didn't fit the schema system either), so not a regression -- just a missed unification. Side effect worth fixing together: on a *method*/*function*, `@unsafe_send(if_params_send=True)` is (correctly) rejected, but the message is the schema's confusing "got unexpected keyword argument 'if_params_send'" (the all-bool signature makes the deriver treat `if_params_send` as positional). A kwargs-only-aware schema would let this read "if_params_* is generic-classes-only". Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the conditional-decorator syntax reshape.
- Test gaps from the Threading V2 Arc review (non-blocking): (a) the
  `@unsafe_send`/`@unsafe_sync` conditional (`if_params_*`) parser diagnostics
  are only partially exercised. Covered: positional arg, non-generic class,
  kwargs on a method, kwargs on `@nosend`. Still uncovered: a non-bool flag
  value (`if_params_send=1`), an unexpected kwarg, a repeated kwarg, an all-False
  call, kwargs on a free *function* (same guard as the method case), and two
  conditional decorators on the same trait; also the *Sync*-side of the
  conditional-vs-unconditional conflict (`@unsafe_sync` + `@unsafe_sync(if_params_
  sync=True)`) -- only the Send side (`error_conditional_conflict`) is tested.
  (b) No `Arc` analog of `rc_readonly_clone` (readonly-handle `clone`/`downgrade`/
  `upgrade` yielding a readonly payload) -- lower risk since the `auto_readonly`
  mechanism is shared verbatim with Rc. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage).
- **[perf] Inline the Mutex/RwLock cell (fuse `Arc[Mutex[T]]` to one alloc).**
  (Threading V3 `Mutex`/`RwLock` shipped -- see `docs/THREADING_DESIGN.md` V3;
  this is the one open perf follow-up.) A bare `Mutex[T]` is one heap alloc --
  the `_MutexCell` reached via `unsafe_interior_mutable[Ptr[...]]`, same shape and cost as
  `Rc.new`. The "two allocs" is only `Arc[Mutex[T]]`, and that is plain
  composition (`Arc[X]` = the Arc cell + whatever `X` allocs; `Arc[Rc[T]]` is
  two allocs for the same reason), not a Mutex-specific wart. The difference
  from Rc: Rc's cell is *fundamental* (shared ownership needs a cell outliving
  individual handles), whereas Mutex's cell is *incidental* -- a Mutex solely
  owns its `T`; the heap indirection exists only to hand out a mutable payload
  borrow through a readonly handle. So unlike Rc, Mutex could go fully inline
  (`{_RawMutex, T}` by value, Rust's shape), which would make `Arc[Mutex[T]]`
  one alloc. Blocked on a missing primitive: there is no address-of-an-inline-
  field op, and `unsafe_interior_mutable[...]` is `Ptr[T]`-only (`tpyc/sema/registration.py`
  `_strip_interior_field_markers`), so an inline `{lock, payload}` can't be
  reached by a mutable pointer through a readonly handle. Add `unsafe_interior_mutable`
  support on a non-`Ptr` inline field (or an address-of-field op) and drop the
  `_MutexCell`. Correctness-neutral; deferred until a workload cares.

- **[feature] Extend `Deref[T]` transparency to consumption-site dispatch (not
  just method-call + attribute-access).** Today the deref chain
  (`tpyc/sema/methods.py` ~828, `tpyc/sema/expressions.py` ~2004) forwards
  *method calls* and *attribute access* through any `Deref[T]` (depth 8), so
  `box.method()` / `arc.field` / `guard.append(x)` all reach the payload -- but
  other protocol-dispatch sites resolve *directly on the wrapper type* and do
  NOT walk the chain, so a wrapper/smart-pointer type (`Box`/`Rc`/`Arc`/lock
  guards) stops being transparent there. Confirmed gaps: iteration (`for v in
  guard:` -> "Cannot iterate over type ReadGuard"; the for-loop resolves
  iterability on the source type), the `with` context-manager protocol
  (`_analyze_with` looks up `__enter__`/`__exit__` directly, `sema/statements.py`
  ~2545, so `with arc_holding_a_cm:` wouldn't find the payload's `__enter__`),
  and builtin dispatch (`len(guard)` / `len(box)` -> "No matching overload for
  len(MutexGuard[list[Int32]])"). Note CPython rejects the iteration/`with`/`len`
  spellings too -- implicit special-method lookup goes through the *type*, so the
  stubs' `__getattr__` forwarding never fires -- so widening any of these means
  teaching `lib/cpy/tpy/` to mirror it, or the cpy phase diverges.
